# KEYFLOW OS — Implementation Specs

Eleven specs plus the sequencing plan, produced 2026-08-09 against `main`.
Every claim re-derived from the code; where a spec cites a line number, the file was opened.

Companions: `docs/CAPABILITY_MAP_2026-08-09.md` (the audit) and
`docs/OPEN_GAPS_2026-08-09.md` (the defect ledger).

Constraints baked into every spec: MODULAR (explicit module boundary, no new
import cycle — the server needed 68 forwardRef deferrals to boot) and SCALABLE
(BullMQ with job keys, never setInterval; no in-process state; every new model
added to BUSINESS_ID_MODELS).

        "spec": "I read the two audit docs, then verified every claim below against the code. Here is the spec.\n\n---\n\n# The Obligation Clock — Implementation Spec\n\n**Repo:** `C:/kfm` (`/c/kfm`), branch `main`\n**Basis:** `docs/CAPABILITY_MAP_2026-08-09.md` and `docs/OPEN_GAPS_2026-08-09.md`. This builds on both; it does not restate them.\n**Status:** ready to open files against.\n\n---\n\n## 0. The one-sentence problem\n\n`GET \"what is due this week across this business\"` has no answer, because there is no table that means *owed*. There are seven tables that mean *a thing to do* and three that mean *when to fire*, and the union of them is not queryable.\n\nThis spec adds one primitive — **Obligation** — beside the seven, with a recurrence spine (**ObligationSeries**) and a per-business ruleset (**ObligationRule**). Nothing is consolidated. Nothing is deleted. The seven emit into it.\n\n---\n\n## 1. What exists today\n\nEvery line number below was opened.\n\n### 1.1 The seven \"a thing to do\" models\n\n| Model | `schema.prisma` | `businessId` | due field | done field | status vocabulary | tenant-scoped? |\n|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|\n| `Task` | **12382** | yes | `dueDate` | `completedAt` | `\"todo\"` (lowercase) | **no** |\n| `CrmTask` | **12611** | yes | `dueDate` | `completedAt` | `\"todo\"` (lowercase) | **no** |\n| `ContactTask` | **3231** | yes | `dueDate` + `remindAt` | `completedAt` | `\"OPEN\"` (uppercase) | **no** |\n| `ProjectTask` | **4262** | yes | `dueDate`/`startDate`/`endDate` | `isCompleted` **bool** | `ProjectTaskStatus` **enum** | **yes** (`client.ts:85`) |\n| `AutopilotTask` | **4518** | yes | `dueDate` + `scheduledFor` | `executedAt` | `\"PENDING\"` | **no** |\n| `ReviewTask` | **6973** | yes | *(none)* | `resolvedAt` | `\"PENDING\"` | **no** |\n| `TaskAssignment` | **10299** | **no column** | — | `unassignedAt` | — | **deliberately excluded**, `client.ts:77-80` |\n\nFour different spellings of \"done\". Three of \"status\". One has no due date at all. `ProjectTask` is the only one the isolation extension covers.\n\n`apps/server/src/modules/temporal/temporal-overview.service.ts:50-56` is the closest thing to a due-this-week answer today, and it counts `projectTask` only — one of seven — then reports it as `today.tasksDue`.\n\n### 1.2 The three \"when to fire\" models\n\n| Model | line | how it fires | state lives where |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| `TriggerDefinition` | **11173** | `businessId` is **nullable** — a global row scopes to no tenant | — |\n| `AgentTrigger` | **7837** | `schedule` is a cron string; `cron-scheduler.service.ts:20-25` reads them at boot and pushes to BullMQ via `queue.service.ts:113` | Redis (correct) |\n| `KeyCortexTriggerRule` | **7623** | `cooldownMinutes` / `maxDailyFirings` enforced by `key-cortex-trigger.service.ts:27` — **`private readonly firingState = new Map<string, FiringState>()`** | **a module field.** Two replicas = two independent cooldowns |\n\nThere is no shared spine between them and none of them models *what is owed*, only *when to run something*.\n\n### 1.3 What already works and must be reused, not rebuilt\n\n- **BullMQ is a direct dependency** — `apps/server/package.json:42`, `bullmq@^5.76.8`, resolved to `5.76.8`. Three files use it: `ai/queue.service.ts`, `business-events/business-event.queue.ts`, `temporal-flow/temporal-flow-memory.queue.service.ts`. `business-event.queue.ts` is the cleanest Queue+Worker template in the repo.\n- **The modern scheduler API is available.** I checked the shipped typings at `node_modules/.pnpm/bullmq@5.76.8/node_modules/bullmq/dist/esm/classes/queue.d.ts`: `upsertJobScheduler` at **:193**, `removeJobScheduler` at **:293**, `getJobSchedulers` at **:261**. `getRepeatableJobs` — which `ai/queue.service.ts:116` uses — is marked **`@deprecated ... will be removed in v6`** at **:239**. New code uses the scheduler API.\n- **BullMQ is `require()`-safe** — its `package.json` declares `\"main\": \"./dist/cjs/index.js\"` and no `\"type\": \"module\"`. This matters (§7.3).\n- **Four `@Global` modules give us every dependency with zero module imports** — `core/prisma/prisma.module.ts:4`, `core/event-bus/event-bus.module.ts:4`, `core/redis/redis.module.ts` (`@Global` on the class), `modules/business-events/business-event.module.ts:8`. This is the whole architectural trick in §2.\n- **Event names we can listen to already exist.** `booking.completed` is emitted at `bookings.service.ts:214` with payload `{ booking, contact, businessId }`. `contact_task.created` at `crm-timeline.service.ts:276` and `contact_task.completed` at `:364`, both `{ task, businessId }`. Also live: `invoice.created`, `invoice.paid`, `quote.accepted`, `crm.deal.won`, `booking.cancelled`, `booking.no_show`.\n- **`runWithTenant` is exported** — `core/tenant/tenant-context.ts:13`. Usable from a worker.\n- **`BusinessEventService.emit()`** — `business-events/business-event.service.ts:24` — is the audit ledger, `@Global`, and never throws. Discharge audit goes here. We do not build a second audit table.\n\n### 1.4 The two constraints, measured today\n\n**Scale.** I re-derived these rather than quoting the brief:\n\n```\n@Cron decorators (non-spec)          26      (17 of them in modules/key-cortex)\nsetInterval call sites (non-spec)    52\ndistributed locks / leader election   0\n```\n\n`commerce/recurring-invoice.service.ts:20-26` is the representative case: an hourly `setInterval` in `onModuleInit`, no lock. Two replicas issue every recurring invoice twice. `flow/flow-runner.service.ts:283-284` is the other: the comment `// In production, this should queue to BullMQ` sits directly above `if (totalMs > 0 && totalMs <= 30000)`.\n\n**Modularity.** `grep -rn forwardRef apps/server/src --include=*.ts | grep -v spec` returns **294 occurrences across 94 files** (this counts imports and use sites, not distinct deferrals — the brief's figure of 68 deferrals is the deduplicated count). `app.module.ts` registers ~110 feature modules. `ai/flow-orchestrator.service.ts` imports roughly fifty other modules' services directly (lines 47-76 alone import fifteen).\n\n**Tenancy.** Re-derived against `packages/db/src/client.ts` and `schema.prisma` this session:\n\n```\nPrisma models                        439\nmodels with a businessId column      347\nmodels listed in BUSINESS_ID_MODELS   77\nmodels with businessId, UNLISTED     270\n```\n\n`core/prisma/tenant-model-list.spec.ts` checks two directions — listed→exists (**:58**) and listed→has-a-column (**:68**). It never checks *has-a-column → listed*. **That is why a new model is unscoped by default and no test fails.** Confirmed by reading the spec, not by assumption.\n\nAnd two facts about the extension that constrain the worker design, both stated in `client.ts` itself:\n- **`create`, `createMany`, `upsert`, `aggregate`, `groupBy` are not hooked** (`client.ts:239-241`). The extension cannot stop a cross-tenant create.\n- The tenant context comes from `core/tenant/tenant.interceptor.ts:16-18`, which reads `req.params/body/query`. **It is HTTP-only.** In a BullMQ worker `activeBusinessId()` is `undefined` and the extension is inert.\n\n---\n\n## 2. The module boundary\n\n```\napps/server/src/modules/obligations/\n  obligation.module.ts               <- imports: []   (the load-bearing line)\n  obligation.service.ts              <- the public interface\n  obligation-series.service.ts       <- RRULE materialisation\n  obligation-rule.service.ts         <- the genome ruleset\n  obligation-queue.service.ts        <- three BullMQ queues + workers\n  obligation.listener.ts             <- @OnEvent bindings\n  obligation.controller.ts\n  obligation-genome-packs.ts         <- static data, zero imports\n  obligation.types.ts\n  dto/{open-obligation,discharge-obligation,create-series,upsert-rule,list-obligations}.dto.ts\n```\n\n### 2.1 What it owns\n\nThe three tables in §3 and nothing else. It never reads `contact_tasks`, `project_tasks`, `invoices`, `contracts`, `genome_facts` or any other module's table. Everything it knows about the rest of the business arrives as an event payload or as an argument to a public method.\n\n### 2.2 Public interface\n\n`ObligationModule` exports exactly three providers. This is the declared contract; nothing else in the module is importable.\n\n```ts\n// obligation.service.ts\nexport type ObligationStatus =\n  | 'PENDING' | 'DUE' | 'OVERDUE' | 'DISCHARGED' | 'WAIVED' | 'SUPERSEDED' | 'CANCELLED';\n\nexport interface OpenObligationInput {\n  businessId: string;\n  /** Vocabulary word from the genome pack: REBOOK, VAT_RETURN, RENEWAL, SERVICE_VISIT... */\n  obligationType: string;\n  label: string;                          // what the owner reads\n  dueAt: Date;\n  /** Idempotency. Unique per business. Re-opening with the same key is a no-op. */\n  occurrenceKey: string;\n  owedToType?: 'CONTACT' | 'BUSINESS' | 'AUTHORITY' | 'STAFF' | 'SELF';\n  owedToId?: string;\n  owedToLabel?: string;                   // \"Board of Inland Revenue\"\n  responsibleType?: 'USER' | 'STAFF' | 'KEY' | 'UNASSIGNED';\n  responsibleId?: string;\n  subjectType?: string;                   // 'ContactTask' | 'Invoice' | 'Contract' | ...\n  subjectId?: string;\n  sourceModule: string;                   // 'crm' | 'bookings' | 'obligations:rule'\n  amountDue?: string;                     // decimal string; never a JS number\n  severity?: 'LOW' | 'NORMAL' | 'HIGH' | 'STATUTORY';\n  seriesId?: string;\n  ruleId?: string;\n  metadata?: Record<string, unknown>;\n}\n\nexport interface DischargeInput {\n  businessId: string;\n  obligationId?: string;                  // either this...\n  occurrenceKey?: string;                 // ...or this\n  dischargedBy: string;\n  evidenceType?: string;                  // 'ContactTask' | 'Payment' | 'Booking'\n  evidenceId?: string;\n  note?: string;\n}\n\n@Injectable()\nexport class ObligationService {\n  open(input: OpenObligationInput): Promise<Obligation>;\n  discharge(input: DischargeInput): Promise<Obligation | null>;\n  waive(input: { businessId: string; obligationId: string; by: string; reason: string }): Promise<Obligation>;\n  supersede(input: { businessId: string; occurrenceKey: string; by: string }): Promise<Obligation | null>;\n  list(q: { businessId: string; dueFrom?: Date; dueTo?: Date; status?: ObligationStatus[];\n            owedToId?: string; responsibleId?: string; obligationType?: string;\n            limit?: number; offset?: number }): Promise<{ rows: Obligation[]; total: number }>;\n  /** The question the product could not answer. One index scan. */\n  dueBetween(businessId: string, from: Date, to: Date): Promise<ObligationDigest>;\n}\n```\n\n`ObligationSeriesService` exposes `createSeries`, `pauseSeries`, `previewOccurrences(rrule, dtstart, tz, n)`.\n`ObligationRuleService` exposes `upsertRule`, `listRules`, `setEnabled`, `applyPack(businessId, packKey)`, `proposeRule(...)`.\n\n### 2.3 What it listens to\n\nAll via `EventEmitter2` `@OnEvent` in `obligation.listener.ts`. A listener creates **no import edge** — the emitter does not know the listener exists.\n\n| Event | emitted at | obligation effect |\n|---|---|---|\n| `contact_task.created` | `crm/crm-timeline.service.ts:276` | open, `occurrenceKey = task:ContactTask:{id}` |\n| `contact_task.completed` | `crm/crm-timeline.service.ts:364` | discharge, evidence `ContactTask/{id}` |\n| `contact_task.rescheduled` | crm-timeline | reschedule `dueAt` |\n| `booking.completed` | `bookings/bookings.service.ts:214` | evaluate rules → e.g. open `REBOOK` at `+P6W` |\n| `booking.cancelled` / `booking.no_show` | bookings.service | supersede any rebook obligation for that booking |\n| `invoice.created` / `invoice.paid` | commerce | open / discharge a `PAYMENT_DUE` obligation |\n| `crm.deal.won` | `crm/crm-deals.service.ts` | evaluate rules (onboarding obligations) |\n\nFive more emits must be **added** (one line each, in the owning module, no new import — `EventEmitter2` is already injected in all five):\n\n| Add to | after | emit |\n|---|---|---|\n| `crm/crm.service.ts` (Task CRUD) | create/complete | `task.created` / `task.completed` |\n| `crm/crm.service.ts` (CrmTask CRUD) | create/complete | `crm_task.created` / `crm_task.completed` |\n| `projects/projects.service.ts` | task create / `isCompleted` flip | `project_task.created` / `project_task.completed` |\n| `autopilot/autopilot.service.ts` | create / execute | `autopilot_task.created` / `autopilot_task.completed` |\n| `documents/*` (ReviewTask) | create / resolve | `review_task.created` / `review_task.resolved` |\n\n> **`core/event-bus/event-wiring.spec.ts` will fail if a listener is added without its emitter.** That spec holds the dead-listener count at 10 with a canary proving the detector still fires. Ship the emit line and the listener in the same commit, or the gate — correctly — stops you.\n\n### 2.4 What it emits\n\n`obligation.opened`, `obligation.due`, `obligation.overdue`, `obligation.discharged`, `obligation.waived`, `obligation.breached`, `obligation.series.materialized`, `obligation.rule.applied`. Payload always `{ businessId, obligationId, obligationType, dueAt, ... }`. Plus a `BusinessEvent` row per state change through the `@Global` `BusinessEventService`.\n\nThe module never calls another module's service. If a discharge should send a WhatsApp, `communications` listens to `obligation.due` — obligations does not reach for a sender.\n\n### 2.5 Why this creates no import cycle — the argument, not the assertion\n\n```ts\n// obligation.module.ts\n@Module({\n  imports: [],                       // <- this line is the invariant\n  providers: [ObligationService, ObligationSeriesService, ObligationRuleService,\n              ObligationQueueService, ObligationListener],\n  controllers: [ObligationController],\n  exports: [ObligationService, ObligationSeriesService, ObligationRuleService],\n})\nexport class ObligationModule {}\n```\n\n`imports: []` is legal because every dependency is provided by a `@Global` module, verified this session:\n\n| dependency | provider | `@Global` at |\n|---|---|---|\n| `PrismaService` | `PrismaModule` | `core/prisma/prisma.module.ts:4` |\n| `EventEmitter2` | `EventBusModule` | `core/event-bus/event-bus.module.ts:4` |\n| `REDIS_CLIENT` | `RedisModule` | `core/redis/redis.module.ts` (class decorator) |\n| `BusinessEventService` | `BusinessEventModule` | `modules/business-events/business-event.module.ts:8` |\n\n**`ObligationModule` therefore has out-degree zero in the module import graph.** A vertex with no outgoing edges cannot lie on a cycle. So `AiModule → ObligationModule` (needed in §5.3 so `flow-orchestrator` can call the tools) is provably cycle-free, needs no `forwardRef`, and adds nothing to the 294 occurrences.\n\nThe invariant is not a comment. It is a test — §6.1.\n\n**One rule follows and must be written into the module header:** *if you ever need to `import` another feature module here, you have modelled it wrong.* The correct move is to accept the data as a method argument or an event payload. Reading `GenomeFact` (`schema.prisma:1219`) directly would be exactly this violation; §4.3 says what to do instead.\n\n---\n\n## 3. Data\n\n### 3.1 `Obligation`\n\nHouse style throughout: `snake_case @map`, `businessId` with a cascading `Business` relation, compound `@@index([businessId, ...])`, `String` status with the vocabulary in a doc comment (matching `Task:12390`, not the `ProjectTaskStatus` enum at `:4273` — obligation types are per-business and must not be a Postgres enum).\n\n```prisma\n/// The obligation clock. One row = one thing owed, to someone, by a moment.\n/// This model is BESIDE Task/CrmTask/ContactTask/ProjectTask/AutopilotTask/\n/// ReviewTask, not above them. They emit into it; it never writes back to them.\nmodel Obligation {\n  id         String   @id @default(cuid())\n  businessId String   @map(\"business_id\")\n  business   Business @relation(fields: [businessId], references: [id], onDelete: Cascade)\n\n  /// Vocabulary word supplied by the genome pack: REBOOK, VAT_RETURN,\n  /// POLICY_RENEWAL, SERVICE_VISIT, TERM_REENROL, GRANT_REPORT, PAYMENT_DUE.\n  obligationType String  @map(\"obligation_type\")\n  label          String\n  detail         String? @db.Text\n\n  // ── to whom ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\n  owedToType  String? @map(\"owed_to_type\")   // CONTACT | BUSINESS | AUTHORITY | STAFF | SELF\n  owedToId    String? @map(\"owed_to_id\")\n  owedToLabel String? @map(\"owed_to_label\")  // \"Board of Inland Revenue\"\n\n  // ── by whom ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\n  responsibleType String  @default(\"UNASSIGNED\") @map(\"responsible_type\") // USER|STAFF|KEY|UNASSIGNED\n  responsibleId   String? @map(\"responsible_id\")\n\n  // ── about what ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\n  subjectType String? @map(\"subject_type\")   // 'ContactTask' | 'Invoice' | 'Contract' | ...\n  subjectId   String? @map(\"subject_id\")\n\n  // ── by when ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\n  dueAt       DateTime  @map(\"due_at\")\n  windowStart DateTime? @map(\"window_start\")  // earliest it can be discharged\n  graceUntil  DateTime? @map(\"grace_until\")   // OVERDUE only after this\n\n  /// PENDING | DUE | OVERDUE | DISCHARGED | WAIVED | SUPERSEDED | CANCELLED\n  status   String @default(\"PENDING\")\n  severity String @default(\"NORMAL\")          // LOW | NORMAL | HIGH | STATUTORY\n\n  amountDue Decimal? @map(\"amount_due\") @db.Decimal(18, 4)\n  currency  String?\n\n  // ── what discharged it ────────────────────────────────────────────────\n  dischargedAt      DateTime? @map(\"discharged_at\")\n  dischargedBy      String?   @map(\"discharged_by\")     // userId | 'KEY' | 'SYSTEM'\n  dischargeType     String?   @map(\"discharge_type\")    // COMPLETED | PAID | FILED | WAIVED | SUPERSEDED\n  evidenceType      String?   @map(\"evidence_type\")     // 'ContactTask' | 'Payment' | 'Booking'\n  evidenceId        String?   @map(\"evidence_id\")\n  dischargeNote     String?   @map(\"discharge_note\") @db.Text\n\n  // ── the reminder ladder. Advanced by compare-and-set; see §4.2. ────────\n  noticeLevel  Int       @default(0) @map(\"notice_level\")\n  nextNoticeAt DateTime? @map(\"next_notice_at\")\n\n  // ── provenance ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\n  seriesId     String?  @map(\"series_id\")\n  series       ObligationSeries? @relation(fields: [seriesId], references: [id], onDelete: SetNull)\n  ruleId       String?  @map(\"rule_id\")\n  sourceModule String   @map(\"source_module\")\n  /// Idempotency key. `task:ContactTask:{id}`, `series:{seriesId}:2026-09-25`.\n  occurrenceKey String  @map(\"occurrence_key\")\n\n  metadata Json @default(\"{}\")\n\n  createdAt DateTime @default(now()) @map(\"created_at\")\n  updatedAt DateTime @updatedAt @map(\"updated_at\")\n\n  @@unique([businessId, occurrenceKey])\n  @@index([businessId, status, dueAt])\n  @@index([businessId, dueAt])\n  @@index([businessId, subjectType, subjectId])\n  @@index([businessId, owedToType, owedToId])\n  @@index([businessId, responsibleId, status])\n  @@index([businessId, seriesId, dueAt])\n  @@index([status, nextNoticeAt])\n  @@map(\"obligations\")\n}\n```\n\n**On `@@index([status, nextNoticeAt])` — the one index that is not tenant-first, deliberately.** The clock tick must find work across all tenants. That query lives only in the worker (§4.2), selects `{ id, businessId }` and nothing else, and every follow-up query is `where: { businessId, id: { in: [...] } }`. This is stated here rather than discovered later because an index without `businessId` in front is otherwise a smell.\n\n### 3.2 `ObligationSeries`\n\n```prisma\n/// The recurrence spine. RRULE (RFC 5545) is the grammar — \"every second\n/// Tuesday\", COUNT vs UNTIL and EXDATE are exactly the cases hand-rolled\n/// frequency strings get subtly wrong. RecurringInvoice.frequency (a bare\n/// String) is the existing hand-roll; this model does not replace it.\nmodel ObligationSeries {\n  id         String   @id @default(cuid())\n  businessId String   @map(\"business_id\")\n  business   Business @relation(fields: [businessId], references: [id], onDelete: Cascade)\n\n  obligationType String  @map(\"obligation_type\")\n  label          String\n\n  /// RFC 5545 RRULE, WITHOUT the \"RRULE:\" prefix.\n  /// e.g. FREQ=MONTHLY;INTERVAL=3;BYMONTHDAY=25\n  rrule    String\n  /// Local wall-clock start. Converted to UTC using `timezone` at materialisation.\n  dtstart  DateTime @map(\"dtstart\")\n  /// IANA zone. Defaults from Business.timezone (schema.prisma:156,\n  /// \"America/Port_of_Spain\").\n  timezone String\n  /// ISO-8601 dates to skip, e.g. a public holiday.\n  exdates  DateTime[] @default([]) @map(\"exdates\")\n\n  /// ISO-8601 duration applied to each occurrence to get dueAt, e.g. \"P25D\".\n  dueOffset   String? @map(\"due_offset\")\n  graceOffset String? @map(\"grace_offset\")\n\n  /// Template merged into every materialised Obligation.\n  template Json @default(\"{}\")\n\n  status              String    @default(\"ACTIVE\")   // ACTIVE | PAUSED | ENDED\n  horizonDays         Int       @default(120) @map(\"horizon_days\")\n  lastMaterializedAt  DateTime? @map(\"last_materialized_at\")\n  nextOccurrenceAt    DateTime? @map(\"next_occurrence_at\")\n\n  ruleId String? @map(\"rule_id\")\n\n  obligations Obligation[]\n\n  createdAt DateTime @default(now()) @map(\"created_at\")\n  updatedAt DateTime @updatedAt @map(\"updated_at\")\n\n  @@index([businessId, status])\n  @@index([businessId, nextOccurrenceAt])\n  @@index([status, nextOccurrenceAt])\n  @@map(\"obligation_series\")\n}\n```\n\n### 3.3 `ObligationRule`\n\n```prisma\n/// The genome-driven ruleset. ROWS, not code — a business edits its own.\n/// A pack seeds rows once; it never gates behaviour at runtime.\nmodel ObligationRule {\n  id         String   @id @default(cuid())\n  businessId String   @map(\"business_id\")\n  business   Business @relation(fields: [businessId], references: [id], onDelete: Cascade)\n\n  /// Which vocabulary this came from: 'salon.v1', 'accountant.tt.v1'.\n  packKey String? @map(\"pack_key\")\n  /// Stable within a pack, so re-applying a pack updates instead of duplicating.\n  ruleKey String  @map(\"rule_key\")\n  label   String\n\n  /// EVENT | SERIES | FIELD_WATCH\n  triggerKind String @map(\"trigger_kind\")\n  /// EVENT: the EventEmitter2 name, e.g. 'booking.completed'.\n  /// FIELD_WATCH: 'Contract.renewalDate'.\n  triggerKey  String @map(\"trigger_key\")\n  /// Deterministic predicate over the event payload. NO LLM on this path.\n  /// { \"service.category\": { \"in\": [\"cut\", \"colour\"] } }\n  matchJson   Json   @default(\"{}\")\n\n  /// ISO-8601 duration from the trigger instant to dueAt. \"P6W\", \"-P30D\".\n  dueOffset String? @map(\"due_offset\")\n  /// SERIES rules carry recurrence here instead of an offset.\n  rrule     String?\n  timezone  String?\n\n  /// Merged into the created Obligation (obligationType, label, owedTo*, severity).\n  template Json @default(\"{}\")\n\n  /// Do not open a second one of these within N hours for the same subject.\n  cooldownHours   Int     @default(0) @map(\"cooldown_hours\")\n  maxOpenPerSubject Int   @default(1) @map(\"max_open_per_subject\")\n\n  enabled Boolean @default(true)\n  /// PACK | USER | KEY  — who put this rule here.\n  origin  String  @default(\"PACK\")\n\n  createdAt DateTime @default(now()) @map(\"created_at\")\n  updatedAt DateTime @updatedAt @map(\"updated_at\")\n\n  @@unique([businessId, ruleKey])\n  @@index([businessId, enabled, triggerKey])\n  @@index([businessId, triggerKind])\n  @@map(\"obligation_rules\")\n}\n```\n\n### 3.4 Tenant isolation — the explicit step\n\nAll three models go into `BUSINESS_ID_MODELS` in **`packages/db/src/client.ts`**, in the set that starts at **line 81** and closes at **line 153**, as a new commented block before the closing `]);`:\n\n```ts\n  // ── The obligation clock, added 2026-08-10 ─────────────────────────────\n  // A new model is UNSCOPED BY DEFAULT: tenant-model-list.spec.ts checks\n  // listed->exists (:58) and listed->has-a-column (:68), never\n  // has-a-column->listed. 270 of the 347 models with a businessId are\n  // unlisted today (re-derived 2026-08-09). These three are not among them.\n  //\n  // What this does NOT protect, restated because the workers depend on it:\n  // the clock's BullMQ workers have no HTTP request, so activeBusinessId()\n  // is undefined there and these entries are inert. Their scope is the\n  // explicit `where: { businessId }` in obligation-queue.service.ts. And\n  // create/createMany/upsert are not hooked at all (see :239-241) — every\n  // Obligation is created with a businessId bound from a route param or a\n  // job payload, never from a request body.\n  'Obligation', 'ObligationSeries', 'ObligationRule',\n```\n\nThen extend the spec — §6.5.\n\n### 3.5 The migration\n\n`packages/db/prisma/migrations/20260810090000_add_obligation_clock/migration.sql`\n(House sequence: the latest today is `20260808140000_bank_transaction_external_id_unique`.)\n\nGenerated by `pnpm --filter db exec prisma migrate dev --name add_obligation_clock`, then **hand-appended** with the two things Prisma cannot express:\n\n```sql\n-- Partial index: the clock tick only ever scans live obligations. Full index\n-- on (status, next_notice_at) over a table that accumulates DISCHARGED rows\n-- forever is mostly dead weight; this one stays small in proportion to work\n-- outstanding rather than to work ever done.\nCREATE INDEX CONCURRENTLY IF NOT EXISTS obligations_live_notice_idx\n  ON obligations (next_notice_at)\n  WHERE status IN ('PENDING', 'DUE', 'OVERDUE');\n\nCREATE INDEX CONCURRENTLY IF NOT EXISTS obligation_series_live_idx\n  ON obligation_series (next_occurrence_at)\n  WHERE status = 'ACTIVE';\n```\n\n> `CONCURRENTLY` cannot run inside a transaction block. Prisma wraps each migration in one, so these two statements go in a **separate follow-up migration directory** `20260810090100_obligation_partial_indexes/migration.sql` — or drop `CONCURRENTLY`, which is fine on an empty new table and is the simpler choice for the first deploy. Take the simple one; note it in the file.\n\nDeploy path is unchanged: `pnpm db:deploy` → `prisma migrate deploy`, and note `docs/OPEN_GAPS_2026-08-09.md` records that `_prisma_migrations` was reconciled by hand on 2026-08-09 — check `prisma migrate status` before applying.\n\n---\n\n## 4. The engine\n\n### 4.1 Three queues, zero timers\n\n`obligation-queue.service.ts`, modelled on `business-events/business-event.queue.ts`.\n\n```ts\nconstructor(@Inject(REDIS_CLIENT) private readonly redis: Redis, ...) {\n  this.clockQueue  = new Queue<ClockTickJob>('obligation-clock',  { connection: redis });\n  this.noticeQueue = new Queue<NoticeJob>('obligation-notice',    { connection: redis });\n  this.seriesQueue = new Queue<SeriesJob>('obligation-series',    { connection: redis });\n}\n\nasync onModuleInit() {\n  // ONE scheduler id. BullMQ's job scheduler is Redis-atomic, so N replicas\n  // produce ONE tick — leader election by construction, no advisory lock.\n  await this.clockQueue.upsertJobScheduler(\n    'obligation-clock-tick',\n    { pattern: '*/5 * * * *', tz: 'UTC' },\n    { name: 'tick', data: {}, opts: { removeOnComplete: { count: 50 }, removeOnFail: { count: 50 } } },\n  );\n  await this.seriesQueue.upsertJobScheduler(\n    'obligation-series-sweep',\n    { pattern: '17 * * * *', tz: 'UTC' },\n    { name: 'sweep', data: {} },\n  );\n  // workers...\n}\n```\n\n**No `setInterval`. No `@Cron`.** The `@nestjs/schedule` decorator would give us a 27th unguarded cron; `upsertJobScheduler` gives us a guarded one. `removeJobScheduler` on `onModuleDestroy` is *not* called — the schedule must survive a rolling restart.\n\n### 4.2 The compare-and-set ladder — a distributed lock with no new dependency\n\nThe tick selects candidate ids across tenants, then fans out **one job per business**:\n\n```ts\nprivate async tick() {\n  const now = new Date();\n  const candidates = await this.prisma.client.obligation.findMany({\n    where: { status: { in: ['PENDING', 'DUE', 'OVERDUE'] },\n             OR: [{ dueAt: { lte: now } }, { nextNoticeAt: { lte: now } }] },\n    select: { id: true, businessId: true },      // <- ids only, nothing else\n    take: 5000,\n    orderBy: { nextNoticeAt: 'asc' },\n  });\n  for (const [businessId, ids] of groupByBusiness(candidates)) {\n    await this.noticeQueue.add('advance', { businessId, ids }, {\n      jobId: `advance:${businessId}:${floorToMinute(now, 5)}`,   // dedupe on replay\n      removeOnComplete: { age: 3600, count: 500 },\n      attempts: 3, backoff: { type: 'exponential', delay: 2000 },\n    });\n  }\n}\n```\n\nEvery state advance is a **conditional update whose row count is the lock**:\n\n```ts\n// Two replicas race. Postgres serialises. Exactly one gets count === 1.\nconst claimed = await this.prisma.client.obligation.updateMany({\n  where: { id, businessId, status: 'DUE', noticeLevel: level },   // <- the CAS predicate\n  data:  { status: 'OVERDUE', noticeLevel: level + 1,\n           nextNoticeAt: nextLadderStep(level + 1) },\n});\nif (claimed.count !== 1) return;                                  // someone else won; stop\nthis.events.emit('obligation.overdue', { businessId, obligationId: id, ... });\n```\n\nThis is the pattern that replaces `key-cortex-trigger.service.ts:27`'s in-memory `firingState` Map. Cooldown and daily caps are **columns**, not fields: `noticeLevel`, `nextNoticeAt`, and for rules, `ObligationRule.cooldownHours` checked against the newest matching Obligation's `createdAt`. Nothing about the clock's behaviour changes with replica count.\n\n**Worker tenancy, spelled out.** Inside a worker there is no HTTP request, so `activeBusinessId()` is `undefined` (`core/tenant/tenant.interceptor.ts:16-18`) and the extension does nothing. Therefore:\n\n1. **Every** worker query carries `where: { businessId }` explicitly, taken from the job payload. Never from anywhere else.\n2. Every `create` binds `businessId` from the job payload — `create` is not hooked at all (`client.ts:239`).\n3. The handler body is additionally wrapped in `runWithTenant(businessId, KEY_SYSTEM_ACTOR_ID, () => ...)` (`core/tenant/tenant-context.ts:13`, actor from `core/auth/system-actor.ts`) as a second layer. **This is belt-and-braces, not the control** — say so in the code comment, because a reader who believes it is the control will drop rule 1.\n\n### 4.3 Series materialisation\n\n`obligation-series.service.ts` materialises `horizonDays` ahead, idempotently:\n\n```ts\nimport { RRule } from 'rrule';\n\nconst rule = RRule.fromString(`DTSTART:${toUtcBasic(series.dtstart, series.timezone)}\\nRRULE:${series.rrule}`);\nconst occurrences = rule.between(fromUtc, horizonUtc, true)\n  .filter((d) => !series.exdates.some((x) => sameInstant(x, d)));\n\nfor (const occ of occurrences) {\n  await this.obligations.open({\n    businessId: series.businessId,\n    seriesId: series.id,\n    occurrenceKey: `series:${series.id}:${occ.toISOString().slice(0, 10)}`,   // <- idempotency\n    dueAt: addIsoDuration(occ, series.dueOffset),\n    ...(series.template as object),\n  });\n}\n```\n\n`ObligationService.open` upserts on `@@unique([businessId, occurrenceKey])`, so re-running the sweep — after a crash, a redeploy, or on a second replica — creates nothing new.\n\n**Timezone, honestly.** `rrule`'s native `tzid` support requires `luxon` as an optional peer, and we are not adding luxon. Instead: `dtstart` is stored as a **floating local wall-clock**, converted to UTC at materialisation using the series `timezone` (defaulted from `Business.timezone`, `schema.prisma:156`) via `@date-fns/tz` (MIT). Trinidad observes AST year-round with no DST, so the primary market never exercises the hard path — which is exactly why the conversion must be explicit rather than assumed. `apps/server` has `date-fns@^4.1.0` and does **not** have any tz package; `@date-fns/tz` is the v4-native companion.\n\n---\n\n## 5. The three layers\n\n### 5.1 MANUAL — `/app/obligations`\n\nNew route: `apps/web/src/app/app/obligations/page.tsx` (+ `components/`), plus `apps/web/src/app/app/obligations/rules/page.tsx`.\n\nThe list screen is one table with: **Due this week / Overdue / Next 30 days / Discharged** tabs; columns *what · to whom · by when · who owes it · what would discharge it*; row actions **Mark discharged**, **Waive**, **Reschedule**; and a **New obligation** side sheet. Follow `apps/web/src/app/app/contracts/page.tsx` — it is the repo's own writable fixture (`tool-route-audit.ts:310`) and uses `StandardModuleLayout`, `SideSheet`, `EmptyState`, `SkeletonList`.\n\nNew api module `apps/web/src/lib/api/obligations.ts`, following `lib/api/contracts.ts`:\n\n```ts\nimport { apiGet, apiPost, apiPatch, apiDelete } from \"@/lib/api\";\n\nexport async function createObligation(businessId: string, body: CreateObligationBody) { ... }\nexport async function updateObligation(businessId: string, id: string, body: UpdateObligationBody) { ... }\nexport async function resolveObligation(businessId: string, id: string, body: DischargeBody) { ... }\nexport async function createObligationSeries(businessId: string, body: CreateSeriesBody) { ... }\nexport async function updateObligationRule(businessId: string, ruleKey: string, body: UpsertRuleBody) { ... }\nexport async function applyObligationPack(businessId: string, packKey: string) { ... }\n```\n\n> **These names are load-bearing.** `tool-route-audit.ts:531` `canWrite()` walks imports 3 deep looking for a mutation **imported from the api layer**, matched by `MUTATION_NAME` at **:469-470**: `^(create|update|delete|archive|publish|approve|send|cancel|assign|mark|resolve|submit|verify|remove|revoke|restore|import|generate|merge|take|record|apply|execute|refund|void|reconcile|fulfill|issue|acknowledge)[A-Z]`. `dischargeObligation` would **not** match. `resolveObligation` and `applyObligationPack` do. Name them this way or the parity gate fails.\n\nNav entry in `apps/web/src/lib/nav-config.ts`, in the `schedule` section (lines 220-228) after \"Overview\":\n\n```ts\n{ label: \"Obligations\", href: \"/app/obligations\", icon: Clock },\n```\n\n`Clock` is already imported (`nav-config.ts:21`). Schedule, not Work: an obligation is a clock fact, and the Work section is project-shaped.\n\n### 5.2 SMART — deterministic rules, no LLM\n\n`ObligationRule` rows evaluated by `obligation-rule.service.ts`. `matchJson` is a small, closed predicate language over the event payload — `eq`, `in`, `gt`, `lt`, `exists`, `contains` — and that is all. No expression evaluation, no `eval`, no model call anywhere on this path.\n\nThis layer must not repeat the SMART-layer defect the capability map names (32 triggers advertised, 8 implemented, `outcome:'success'` logged either way). Two structural consequences:\n\n1. **One registry.** `OBLIGATION_TRIGGERS` and `OBLIGATION_MATCHERS` are exported from `obligation.types.ts` and are the *only* source read by (a) the rules screen's dropdowns, (b) the `upsertRule` DTO validator, and (c) `obligation-rule.service.ts`'s dispatcher. A spec asserts all three sets are equal (§6.4).\n2. **A rule that matched nothing says so.** `ObligationRule` evaluation returns `{ matched: boolean; opened: string[] }` and the `BusinessEvent` records the real numbers. A rule that opened zero obligations may never be logged as a success.\n\n### 5.3 AI — six KEY tools\n\nAdded to `FLOW_TOOLS` in `apps/server/src/modules/ai/flow-tool-registry.ts` (array opens at **:81**; the `FlowTool` interface with `family` / `riskTier` / `manualEquivalentRoute` is at **:23-43**).\n\n| tool | family | riskTier | `manualEquivalentRoute` |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| `obligation_list_due` | `read` | 1 | `/app/obligations` |\n| `obligation_open` | `crud` | 2 | `/app/obligations` |\n| `obligation_discharge` | `execute` | 2 | `/app/obligations` |\n| `obligation_waive` | `execute` | **3** | `/app/obligations` |\n| `obligation_create_series` | `crud` | 2 | `/app/obligations` |\n| `obligation_set_rule` | `organize` | 2 | `/app/obligations/rules` |\n\nFour gate facts, each verified by reading the checker:\n\n- **The `obligation_` prefix is required.** `tool-route-audit.ts:118-123` computes `domain = name.split('_')[0]` and demands the route contain it after stripping `/` and `-`. `/app/obligations` contains `obligation`. **No `CROSS_DOMAIN_ROUTES` entry is needed** — and needing one would be the signal we picked the wrong screen.\n- **`crud`/`execute`/`organize` are all write families** (`:258`), so all five write tools need `canWrite()` to pass on `/app/obligations` — which §5.1's api-module names deliver.\n- **`obligation_waive` is T3 on purpose.** Waiving a statutory filing is a decision with legal consequence; it goes to approval. `obligation_discharge` at T2 is the ordinary \"I did it\" and needs `evidenceType`/`evidenceId` — a discharge with no evidence pointer is refused by the DTO, which is the anti-fabricated-success control at the tool layer.\n- **Handlers are `case` clauses.** `flow-tool-honesty.spec.ts:38-44` reads `executeToolAction` in `flow-orchestrator.service.ts` and asserts every declared tool has `case '<name>':`. So: add six cases, `import { ObligationService } from '../obligations/obligation.service'` alongside the ~50 already there (lines 47-76), and add `ObligationModule` to `AiModule`'s `imports`. Per §2.5 that edge points into a sink; no `forwardRef`.\n- **Role reachability.** `role-tool-reachability.spec.ts:56` fails on any tool no role can reach. Add `'obligation_*'` to `operations` and `finance` `approvedTools` in `role-engine.service.ts` (`ROLE_DEFINITIONS` at **:180**), and `'obligation_list_due'` to `sales`, `support` and `general`. `general`'s `maxRiskTier` is 1, so it gets the read tool and nothing else — correct.\n\n**Controller guards.** `@UseGuards(AuthGuard, BusinessGuard, RateLimitGuard)` with routes under `businesses/:businessId/obligations`, mirroring `task-assignments/task-assignment.controller.ts:24-32`. **Do not add `@RequireModuleScope('operations')`.** `core/auth/module-scope.guard.ts:22` lists 13 module keys including `operations`, while `identity.service.ts:411` `PERMISSION_MODULES` lists only 11 and omits it — and `identity.service.ts` writes that short map on every invite, so an invited ADMIN resolves `none` on every `operations` handler. Until that reconciliation ships (Wave 0 #5 in the capability map), a new controller that uses it is a new lockout. Use `projects`, which is in both lists, or no module scope.\n\n**All `@Body()` DTOs are declared classes with `class-validator` decorators.** No inline-typed `@Body()` — `flow.controller.ts:26/61` is the live example of what that costs: the global `ValidationPipe` whitelist does not strip an inline-typed body, so a client can inject fields. In this module the field that must never come from a body is `businessId`; it is bound from the route param, always.\n\n---\n\n## 6. Tests, with the negative controls\n\nEvery gate below is written so that a broken version of it fails visibly. The recurring defect in this repo is a green test whose assertion cannot fail.\n\n### 6.1 `obligations/obligation-module-is-a-sink.spec.ts` — the modularity invariant\n\n```ts\n// The whole cycle-freedom argument is one line of source. Assert the line.\nconst src = readFileSync(join(__dirname, 'obligation.module.ts'), 'utf8');\nconst imports = src.match(/imports:\\s*\\[([^\\]]*)\\]/)?.[1] ?? 'MISSING';\nexpect(imports.trim(), 'ObligationModule must import no module. Every dependency is @Global. '\n  + 'A vertex with out-degree zero cannot lie on a cycle — that is the entire reason '\n  + 'AiModule -> ObligationModule needs no forwardRef.').toBe('');\n\n// It must also not reach INTO another feature module's tables.\nconst FOREIGN = /prisma\\.client\\.(contactTask|projectTask|autopilotTask|reviewTask|crmTask|task|genomeFact|invoice|contract)\\b/;\nfor (const f of walk(__dirname)) expect(FOREIGN.test(read(f)), `${f} reaches into another module's table`).toBe(false);\n```\n\n**Negative control, in the same file:** run the same two matchers over two inline fixture strings — one with `imports: [CrmModule]`, one containing `prisma.client.contactTask.findMany(` — and assert both are rejected. Without this the spec passes identically whether the regexes work or not. This is precisely the shape `tool-route-audit.ts:308` `assertParityCheckWorks` uses on itself, and the reason it exists.\n\n### 6.2 `obligations/obligation-recurrence.spec.ts` — RRULE, unit, no DB\n\nGolden cases, each an expected date list:\n\n| case | rrule | why |\n|---|---|---|\n| T&T VAT quarterly | `FREQ=MONTHLY;INTERVAL=3;BYMONTHDAY=25` from 2026-03-25 | crosses a year boundary |\n| month-end | `FREQ=MONTHLY;BYMONTHDAY=31` | Feb/Apr must be skipped, not clamped |\n| every second Tuesday | `FREQ=WEEKLY;INTERVAL=2;BYDAY=TU` | interval + byday interaction |\n| `COUNT=5` vs `UNTIL=…` | both | the classic off-by-one |\n| EXDATE | series with one exdate | the occurrence disappears and the ones after it do not shift |\n| rebook at +P6W | offset arithmetic across a month boundary | `addIsoDuration` |\n\n**Negative control:** one `it('the golden fixtures can fail')` that feeds the *wrong* expected list to the same comparator and asserts it throws. Otherwise a comparator that always returns true reads identical.\n\n### 6.3 `test/obligation-clock.integration.test.ts` — real Postgres, the CAS race\n\nHouse pattern from `test/cortex-context-v2.integration.test.ts` (dotenv from repo root, `require('@keyflow/db')`, `setTenantContextProvider` stand-in) and `test/task-assignment-tenant.integration.test.ts` (prefixed ids, raw-SQL cleanup).\n\n```ts\n// One obligation. Two \"replicas\" advance it concurrently.\nconst [a, b] = await Promise.all([\n  advanceNotice(obligationId, bizId, 'DUE', 0),\n  advanceNotice(obligationId, bizId, 'DUE', 0),\n]);\nexpect([a.count, b.count].sort()).toEqual([0, 1]);          // exactly one winner\nexpect(await countEvents('obligation.overdue')).toBe(1);     // and one event\n```\n\n**Negative control, in the same file:** repeat the race with the CAS predicate removed (`where: { id, businessId }` only, no `status`/`noticeLevel`) and assert **both** updates report `count === 1`. If that half passes, the assertion above is proving something; if it fails, the test is not measuring what it claims and the file says so out loud.\n\n### 6.4 `obligations/obligation-trigger-registry.spec.ts` — set equality, three ways\n\nAssert `OBLIGATION_TRIGGERS` (the registry) ≡ the trigger names the DTO validator accepts ≡ the names `obligation-rule.service.ts` dispatches on ≡ the names the rules screen offers. Four sets, one expected value. **Negative control:** add a fake trigger to one copy inside the test and assert the comparison fails.\n\nThis is the gate the SMART layer never had. It exists so `/app/obligations/rules` can never advertise a trigger the engine does not implement.\n\n### 6.5 `core/prisma/tenant-model-list.spec.ts` — the missing direction, ratcheted\n\nAdd a fourth `it` to the existing file:\n\n```ts\nit('a model with a businessId column is either scoped or on the exemption ledger', () => {\n  const unlisted = modelsWithBusinessId().filter((n) => !listed.includes(n) && !EXEMPT[n]);\n  // 270 today (re-derived 2026-08-09 against 439 models, 347 with the column).\n  // This is a RATCHET, not a pass. The number may fall; it may never rise.\n  expect(unlisted.length,\n    'a new model with a businessId is unscoped by default and nothing else catches it'\n  ).toBeLessThanOrEqual(270);\n  for (const n of ['Obligation', 'ObligationSeries', 'ObligationRule']) expect(listed).toContain(n);\n});\n```\n\n**Negative control:** a fixture schema string containing `model FakeScoped { businessId String }` fed to the same extractor, asserted to be reported as unlisted. Without it the extractor could return `[]` for a broken reason and the ratchet would read as compliance.\n\nThis is the single highest-leverage line in the spec: it makes \"a new model is unscoped by default\" a thing the build notices, for every future model, not just these three.\n\n### 6.6 Gates that already exist and will now run against this work\n\n| gate | what it will check | action required |\n|---|---|---|\n| `apps/web/src/lib/__tests__/tool-routes.spec.ts` | 6 new tools declare a real, same-domain, writable route | build `/app/obligations` before merging the tools |\n| `ai/flow-tool-honesty.spec.ts` | every declared tool has a `case` | 6 cases in `executeToolAction` |\n| `ai/role-tool-reachability.spec.ts` | no orphan tool | `obligation_*` in two roles |\n| `core/event-bus/event-wiring.spec.ts` | no listener without an emitter | ship the 5 emit lines with the listener |\n| `test/commonjs-compat.test.ts` | `rrule` and `@date-fns/tz` are `require()`-able | §7.3 |\n| `test/app-module-boots.integration.test.ts` | the module graph still boots | `imports: []` guarantees it |\n| `apps/web/src/lib/__tests__/nav-reachability.spec.ts` | the new screen has a door | nav entry in §5.1 |\n\n---\n\n## 7. Effort, and what to adopt\n\n### 7.1 Days\n\n| # | Work | Days |\n|---|---|---|\n| 1 | Three Prisma models, migration, `BUSINESS_ID_MODELS`, the ratchet spec (§6.5) | 2 |\n| 2 | `ObligationService` + controller + DTOs + tenant integration test | 3 |\n| 3 | `ObligationSeriesService` + `rrule` + recurrence spec (§6.2) | 3 |\n| 4 | `ObligationQueueService` — 3 queues, CAS ladder, race test (§6.3) | 3 |\n| 5 | `ObligationRuleService` + `obligation-genome-packs.ts` + registry spec (§6.4) | 3 |\n| 6 | `obligation.listener.ts` + 5 emit lines in owning modules + backfill job | 3 |\n| 7 | `/app/obligations` + `/app/obligations/rules` + `lib/api/obligations.ts` + nav | 4 |\n| 8 | 6 KEY tools, orchestrator cases, role allowlists | 2 |\n| 9 | Module header docs, runbook note, buffer | 1 |\n| | **Total** | **24 days** |\n\nItems 1-4 are the spine and are independently shippable: after day 11 the clock exists, ticks once across N replicas, and `dueBetween()` answers the question — with only the ContactTask listener feeding it.\n\n### 7.2 Adopt\n\n| Adopt | Licence | Why not build |\n|---|---|---|\n| **`bullmq@5.76.8`** — already a dependency | MIT | Repeatable jobs fire once across N workers *by construction*. Redis is in both compose files. `flow-runner.service.ts:283` asks for this in a comment. |\n| **`rrule@^2.8.1`** — new | **BSD-3-Clause** | \"Every second Tuesday\", `COUNT` vs `UNTIL`, `EXDATE`, month-end clamping. Every one of these is easy to get subtly wrong and impossible to notice for a quarter. |\n| **`@date-fns/tz@^1`** — new | MIT | Wall-clock → UTC. `date-fns@^4.1.0` is installed; no tz companion is. |\n\n### 7.3 Pre-flight before adding either dependency\n\n`test/commonjs-compat.test.ts` is a real gate: `apps/server` emits CommonJS against Node 20.18.1, and `isRequireable` at **:93-107** fails any package with `\"type\": \"module\"` and no `require` condition in `exports`. `uuid@14` already took the server down this way. So, before `pnpm add`:\n\n```bash\nnode -p \"JSON.parse(require('fs').readFileSync('node_modules/rrule/package.json')).type ?? 'commonjs'\"\nnode -p \"JSON.parse(require('fs').readFileSync('node_modules/@date-fns/tz/package.json')).type ?? 'commonjs'\"\n```\n\nNeither may print `module`. `rrule@2.8.1` ships `\"main\": \"dist/es5/rrule.js\"` with no `type` field, so it is expected to pass — but check, do not assume, and let the spec be the second check.\n\n### 7.4 Explicitly not adopting\n\n**Temporal**, **Trigger.dev**, **pg-boss**, **Agenda**. BullMQ is installed, already used correctly in three files, and needs no new container. Adding a second job runtime to a repo that has 52 unguarded `setInterval`s would be solving the wrong problem.\n\n---\n\n## 8. What this deliberately does not do\n\n| Not doing | Why |\n|---|---|\n| **Consolidate the seven task models.** | The brief forbids it and the brief is right. Seven migrations, seven screens, seven APIs, and the win is a schema diagram. The clock delivers the actual value — one queryable spine — at a fraction of the risk. The seven keep their own lifecycles; the Obligation carries a `subjectType`/`subjectId` pointer back. |\n| **Touch `TriggerDefinition`, `AgentTrigger` or `KeyCortexTriggerRule`.** | They answer \"when to run something\", not \"what is owed\". Merging them is a separate decision. The one thing worth doing to them — moving `key-cortex-trigger.service.ts:27`'s in-memory `firingState` into Postgres — is demonstrated here and can be copied later. |\n| **Migrate the 52 `setInterval` schedulers.** | Correct and large. This module ships zero new ones and provides the working `upsertJobScheduler` + CAS pattern for whoever does. Do not bundle it. |\n| **Compute Trinidad statutory amounts.** | The accountant pack supplies **dates and labels only** — never a rate, a threshold or a figure. Rates change annually and an engine that is 90% right on VAT is worth less than nothing. Same refusal, same reason, as the capability map's on PAYE/NIS. |\n| **Business-hours / SLA arithmetic.** | `dueAt` is an absolute instant. \"Due in 2 business days, excluding Carnival Monday\" needs a per-business working calendar the product does not have. `graceUntil` is the crude stand-in. |\n| **Emit `.ics`, or sync to Google Calendar.** | `ical-generator` (MIT) is the right answer and the calendar module is the right owner. An obligation is not a calendar event; conflating them is how the clock becomes a second, worse calendar. |\n| **A visual RRULE builder.** | v1 ships a preset picker (daily / weekly / monthly / quarterly / annually / custom-RRULE-string) with a live **\"next 5 occurrences\"** preview computed by the same `previewOccurrences` the server uses. The preview is the safety feature; the builder is not. |\n| **Add an `obligations` key to `PERMISSION_MODULES`.** | `module-scope.guard.ts:22` has 13 keys, `identity.service.ts:411` has 11, and the mismatch already locks invited ADMINs out of `operations`. Adding a 12th/14th key before that reconciliation lands would deepen a live defect. Use `projects`, which both lists contain. |\n| **Write back into the seven tables.** | Discharging an Obligation does **not** complete the ContactTask. The arrow is one-way by design: the task is the system of record for the task, the obligation is the system of record for the clock. Two-way sync between seven heterogeneous status vocabularies is how this becomes unmaintainable. The UI offers \"open the source record\"; it does not pretend to be it. |\n\n---\n\n## 9. Genome packs — the concrete part\n\n`obligation-genome-packs.ts` is static data with **zero imports**. A pack seeds `ObligationRule` rows via `applyPack(businessId, packKey)`; after that, the rows are the business's own and the pack has no runtime authority.\n\nPack selection: `Business.archetype` (`schema.prisma:214`) has only five values — `LOCAL_SERVICE`, `DIGITAL_PRODUCT`, `AGENCY`, `CLINIC`, `ECOMMERCE` — far too coarse to distinguish a hairdresser from an accountant. So the pack is **chosen explicitly** at onboarding (a picker on `/app/obligations/rules`), defaulted from `Business.industry` (`schema.prisma:215`) by a lookup table in the same file. Not inferred silently.\n\n```ts\nexport const OBLIGATION_PACKS: ObligationPack[] = [\n  {\n    key: 'salon.v1',\n    label: 'Salon / barber / spa',\n    vocabulary: { REBOOK: 'Rebook', COLOUR_REFRESH: 'Colour refresh' },\n    rules: [\n      { ruleKey: 'salon.rebook.cut', label: 'Rebook after a cut',\n        triggerKind: 'EVENT', triggerKey: 'booking.completed',\n        matchJson: { 'booking.service.category': { in: ['cut', 'cut_and_style'] } },\n        dueOffset: 'P6W', cooldownHours: 24, maxOpenPerSubject: 1,\n        template: { obligationType: 'REBOOK', severity: 'NORMAL',\n                    owedToType: 'CONTACT', label: 'Rebook {{contact.firstName}} — 6 weeks since last cut' } },\n      { ruleKey: 'salon.rebook.colour', label: 'Rebook after colour',\n        triggerKind: 'EVENT', triggerKey: 'booking.completed',\n        matchJson: { 'booking.service.category': { eq: 'colour' } },\n        dueOffset: 'P4W', template: { obligationType: 'COLOUR_REFRESH', owedToType: 'CONTACT' } },\n    ],\n  },\n  {\n    key: 'accountant.tt.v1',\n    label: 'Accounting practice (Trinidad & Tobago)',\n    vocabulary: { VAT_RETURN: 'VAT return', PAYE_NIS: 'PAYE / NIS remittance',\n                  CORP_TAX_INSTALMENT: 'Corporation tax instalment' },\n    rules: [\n      { ruleKey: 'tt.vat.quarterly', label: 'VAT return — quarterly',\n        triggerKind: 'SERIES', triggerKey: 'series',\n        rrule: 'FREQ=MONTHLY;INTERVAL=3;BYMONTHDAY=25', timezone: 'America/Port_of_Spain',\n        template: { obligationType: 'VAT_RETURN', severity: 'STATUTORY',\n                    owedToType: 'AUTHORITY', owedToLabel: 'Board of Inland Revenue',\n                    label: 'VAT return due' } },\n      { ruleKey: 'tt.paye.monthly', label: 'PAYE / NIS — monthly, by the 15th',\n        triggerKind: 'SERIES', triggerKey: 'series',\n        rrule: 'FREQ=MONTHLY;BYMONTHDAY=15', timezone: 'America/Port_of_Spain',\n        template: { obligationType: 'PAYE_NIS', severity: 'STATUTORY', owedToType: 'AUTHORITY' } },\n      { ruleKey: 'tt.corptax.quarterly', label: 'Corporation tax instalment',\n        triggerKind: 'SERIES', triggerKey: 'series',\n        rrule: 'FREQ=MONTHLY;INTERVAL=3;BYMONTH=3,6,9,12;BYMONTHDAY=31',\n        template: { obligationType: 'CORP_TAX_INSTALMENT', severity: 'STATUTORY' } },\n    ],\n  },\n  // insurance_broker.v1  — POLICY_RENEWAL, FIELD_WATCH on Contract.renewalDate, dueOffset '-P30D'\n  // hvac.v1              — SERVICE_VISIT every P6M after install; WARRANTY_EXPIRY from the asset record\n  // music_academy.v1     — TERM_REENROL, a SERIES per term start; COHORT_START\n  // ngo.v1               — GRANT_REPORT, a SERIES per grant agreement\n  // ministry.v1          — STATUTORY_RETURN, a SERIES; BOARD_PAPER at -P7D before each meeting\n];\n```\n\n**Every pack's statutory dates carry `severity: 'STATUTORY'` and no amount.** The amount field stays null on these rows, permanently, by construction of the pack — that is the §8 refusal made structural rather than remembered.\n\n**How the genome informs this without an import.** Three permitted routes, in order of preference:\n\n1. The owner picks the pack on `/app/obligations/rules`. (Default.)\n2. Another module calls `ObligationRuleService.proposeRule(...)` — an edge into a sink, cycle-free, and the proposal lands with `origin: 'KEY'`, `enabled: false`, awaiting a human.\n3. Another module emits `obligation.rule.proposed`; the listener consumes it. Zero coupling.\n\nWhat is **not** permitted: `obligations` injecting `KeyGenomeService`, or reading `genome_facts` (`schema.prisma:1219`). That would be the module boundary violated on day one, and §6.1's second assertion fails the build if anyone tries.\n\n---\n\n## 10. The first commit\n\nFor the engineer opening files right now, in order:\n\n1. `packages/db/prisma/schema.prisma` — append §3.1-3.3 near the other operational models; add `obligations Obligation[]`, `obligationSeries ObligationSeries[]`, `obligationRules ObligationRule[]` to `model Business` (starts at **:151**).\n2. `packages/db/src/client.ts` — the block from §3.4 before the `]);` at **:153**.\n3. `pnpm --filter db exec prisma migrate dev --name add_obligation_clock`, then hand-edit the SQL per §3.5.\n4. `apps/server/src/core/prisma/tenant-model-list.spec.ts` — add §6.5 and its negative control. **Watch it fail with the models absent, then pass.** A ratchet you never saw red is not a ratchet.\n5. `apps/server/src/modules/obligations/obligation.module.ts` with `imports: []`, and `obligation-module-is-a-sink.spec.ts` beside it.\n6. `obligation.service.ts` — `open` / `discharge` / `list` / `dueBetween` only.\n7. `apps/server/src/app.module.ts` — register `ObligationModule` in the `imports` array (the list starts at **:130**).\n8. `pnpm --filter server test:integration` — `app-module-boots.integration.test.ts` must still pass, with no new `forwardRef` anywhere in the diff."
      },
      {
        "key": "02-ledger-completeness",
        "title": "Every money module posts to the ledger",
        "spec": "# Implementation Spec — Every Money Module Posts to the Ledger\n\n**Basis:** `C:/kfm` @ `main`, read 2026-08-09. Implements `docs/CAPABILITY_MAP_2026-08-09.md` §3.2 items 14, 15, 16 and §3.5 item 1. Every file path and line number below was opened, not inferred.\n\n---\n\n## 0. The claim, re-derived\n\nThe capability map says \"5 of 18 money-touching modules post.\" I re-derived both sides and got the same numerator, a different denominator membership.\n\n**The 5 that post** — established by `grep -rl \"RevenuePostingService\\|ExpensePostingService\\|PostingService\" apps/server/src/modules/<m> --include=*.ts | grep -v spec`, run per module directory. Seven directories return non-zero: `finance` (the engine), `ai` (a `@OnEvent` name only — `ai.listener.ts:151` forwards `purchaseOrder.received`, it does not post), and:\n\n| Module | Call site |\n|---|---|\n| `commerce` | `invoice-workflow.service.ts:157` (`onProductSold`), `:304` (`onInvoiceFinalized`), `:306` (`onInvoiceVoided`); `commerce.service.ts:1407`, `:1524` |\n| `payments` | `payments.service.ts:51`, `:75`, `:102` |\n| `expenses` | `expenses.service.ts:193`, `:195`, `:257` |\n| `marketplace` | `marketplace.service.ts:50`, `:420`, `:1127`; `fulfillment-routing.service.ts:1056` |\n| `site` | `store-order.service.ts:542` |\n\n**The 13 that do not.** The capability map lists `education` and `public-events`. I checked both and they do not mutate money: `education.service.ts:92` `enrollInCourse` writes a `CourseEnrollment` with no amount (`schema.prisma:5299-5314` has no money column), and `public-events` is presence/analytics only. Two modules that *do* move money were missing from the list. Corrected:\n\n| # | Module | The state change that moves money, with no ledger row |\n|---:|---|---|\n| 1 | `payroll` | `payroll.service.ts:218-227` — `markRunPaid` flips `status:'PAID'` + `paidAt`. `totalGross` (`schema.prisma:2377`, `Decimal(14,2)`) leaves the business and nothing records it. |\n| 2 | `procurement` | `procurement.service.ts:250-265` — `markInvoiced` writes `status:'INVOICED'` and a timeline row. No `Bill`, no AP. `issuePO` (`:181-193`) creates a `PurchaseOrder` with `total` from `estimatedBudget`. |\n| 3 | `continental-ops` | `goods-receipt.service.ts:118-139` — `post()` increments `InventoryStock.quantity` and writes **no** `StockMovement` and no ledger row. `stock-count.service.ts:99-119` — `adjust()` **overwrites** `quantity: item.countedQty`, same silence. `delivery-note.service.ts:103-121` writes a movement but no COGS. `payment-receipt.service.ts:63-78` records cash received against an invoice with no `Payment` and no posting. |\n| 4 | `retainers` | `retainer.service.ts:116-143` — `createPeriod` computes `amountBilled` and stores it. `updatePeriod` (`:166-173`) can set `status:'INVOICED'` with `invoiceId` still null. `getSummary` (`:180-183`) then sums that into `totalRevenue`. |\n| 5 | `change-orders` | `change-order.service.ts:114-128` — `update` accepts `status:'APPROVED'` and `additionalAmount` (`schema.prisma:10598`) with no invoice and no posting. |\n| 6 | `contracts` | `contracts.service.ts:120`, `:199`, `:358` write `contractValue`. Nothing reads it into the books. |\n| 7 | `subscriptions` | `subscriptions.service.ts:87`, `:126`, `:154` write `Subscription.priceMonthly`; `SubscriptionPayment` (`schema.prisma:4687-4698`) carries `amount`. The business pays KEYFLOW OS and its own P&L never sees it. |\n| 8 | `bookings` | `bookings.service.ts:828-841` creates deposit and full invoices through `commerce` (so those *do* post). What does not: cancellation forfeiture and no-show fees. `booking-no-show.listener.ts` charges nothing. |\n| 9 | `keystore` | `KeystoreServiceOrder.finalPrice` / `depositAmount` / `depositPaid` (`schema.prisma:11905-11908`); `keystore.service.ts:586` sums `finalPrice` into `totalRevenue` for delivered orders that never became an invoice. |\n| 10 | `supplier` | `supplier.service.ts:463-514` computes `landedCostEstimate`, `grossMargin` and writes a `MarginSnapshot` with `snapshotReason:'cost_update'`. Inventory carrying value on the balance sheet never moves. |\n| 11 | `events` | `attendee.service.ts:212-224` `claimTicketSlot` increments `EventTicketType.quantitySold`. `EventTicketType.price` is `Decimal(12,2)` (`schema.prisma:769`). A ticket is sold and the ledger is not told. |\n| 12 | `time-tracking` | `time-entry.controller.ts:286` → `service.markAsBilled`. Billable hours with an `hourlyRate` are stamped billed with no invoice and no WIP. |\n| 13 | `community` | `resource-marketplace.service.ts:143` writes `ResourceDownload.pricePaid` (`schema.prisma:8540`). A paid download is revenue. |\n\n**How the denominator was fixed:** a module is money-touching if a non-spec service in it creates or updates a row on a model carrying a monetary column or a costed quantity. That rule admits `events` and `time-tracking` and excludes `education` and `public-events`. If you prefer the capability map's membership, the spec below is unaffected — the two swapped entries are Class B/C work, not new recipes.\n\n---\n\n## 1. What exists today — reuse before you add\n\n### 1.1 The engine is correct. Do not touch it.\n\n`apps/server/src/modules/finance/posting.service.ts` (328 lines).\n\n- `validatePosting` (`:79-104`) — pure, ≥2 entries, debit XOR credit per line, non-negative, `Decimal` balance within `0.0001`.\n- `buildExternalRef(sourceType, sourceId, kind)` (`:48-56`) — the idempotency key. `null` when the caller cannot dedupe, which is the escape hatch you must never take.\n- `post()` (`:115-236`) — checks `businessId_externalRef` first (`:124-131`), verifies every `accountId` belongs to the business with a deliberate `skipTenantIsolation` so the cross-business row is *visible in order to be rejected* (`:136-160`), verifies `contactId` (`:165-175`), then one nested create. P2002 race falls back to returning the existing row (`:217-235`).\n- `reverse()` (`:242-327`) — offsetting entries, never an update or delete, refuses when an entry is `lockedByReconciliationId`.\n- Accepts an optional `txClient` so the posting commits or rolls back **with** the source-row write. `finance.module.ts:47-55` states this as the FIN3 rule: \"no async listeners — atomic ledger + source commit.\"\n\n### 1.2 The reference implementation of a correct caller\n\n`marketplace/fulfillment-routing.service.ts:978-1075`. Inside one `$transaction`: update the PO, recompute weighted-average `costPerUnit` (`:1003-1016`), upsert `InventoryStock`, write the `StockMovement` (`:1038-1049`), then `onPurchaseOrderReceived` in the same `tx` (`:1055-1075`) with the comment that says why. Everything specified below is this shape.\n\nThe contrast worth naming: `ExpensePostingService.onExpensePaid` takes a **value object** (`expense-posting.service.ts:148-167`); `RevenuePostingService.onInvoiceFinalized` takes an **id and reads the Invoice itself** (`revenue-posting.service.ts:105-125`). Copy the first. The second is why the finance module has to know about `commerce`'s tables, and it is one of the two edges in the cycle described in §2.3.\n\n### 1.3 Chart of accounts is lazily self-seeding\n\n`chart-of-accounts-seeder.service.ts:18-63` is the `SYSTEM_COA` table; `getBySystemKey` (`:179-190`) calls `ensureDefaults` on a miss and re-reads. **Adding a system key needs no data migration** — the account appears on first use, per business, via the `(businessId, systemKey)` upsert at `:108-124`.\n\n### 1.4 What already exists and is simply not called\n\n- `ExpensePostingService.onPurchaseOrderReceived` (`expense-posting.service.ts:359-402`) — Dr Inventory / Cr AP-or-Cash. One caller.\n- `ExpensePostingService.onProductSold` (`:412-486`) — weighted-average COGS with the rule stated in its own docstring: *\"Skipped silently when no inventory tracking exists — never fabricate cost values.\"* Three callers, none of them `continental-ops`.\n- `StockMovement` writes done correctly in four places: `delivery-note.service.ts:111`, `marketplace.service.ts:1555`/`:1616`/`:1619`, `fulfillment-routing.service.ts:1038`, `store-order.service.ts:593`. Goods receipt and stock count are the two that skipped it.\n- `POST /finance/businesses/:businessId/journal-entries` (`finance.controller.ts:82-113`) — a real manual journal endpoint, wired to `PostingService.post` with `type:'ADJUSTMENT'`. The composer that would call it, `apps/web/src/app/app/finance/components/books-journal-tab.tsx`, has **zero importers** (grepped: the only hit is its own `export function BooksJournalTab()` on line 24).\n- `AccountingPeriodService.checkClosed` (`accounting-period.service.ts:158-168`) exists and is called by **nothing outside its own controller**. Posting into a closed month is currently possible. §5.4 handles this.\n\n### 1.5 Two facts about tenancy you must not get wrong\n\n- `BUSINESS_ID_MODELS` is `packages/db/src/client.ts:81-153`. Grepped for each: **`StockMovement`, `InventoryStock`, `GoodsReceipt`, `StockCount`, `PayrollRun`, `PayrollItem`, `RetainerAgreement`, `RetainerPeriod`, `ChangeOrder`, `Warehouse` are all absent.** `PurchaseOrder` is present (`:132`).\n- `core/prisma/tenant-model-list.spec.ts` checks list→schema (`:58-66`) and list→has-`businessId` (`:68-84`). **It does not check schema→list.** A new model is unscoped by default and this spec is green. Verified by reading the file, not by assuming.\n- `RetainerPeriod` and `ChangeOrder` have **no `businessId` column** (`schema.prisma:10542-10562`, `:10589-10611`). They cannot be added to the set — the spec's third assertion would fail. They are scoped through their parent, and any posting must derive `businessId` from that parent, never from a body.\n\n---\n\n## 2. The module boundary\n\n### 2.1 Name and location\n\n`apps/server/src/modules/ledger/` — `LedgerModule`.\n\n```\nledger/\n  ledger.module.ts\n  money-event.contract.ts        NEW  the public type contract, imports only @prisma/client\n  posting.service.ts             MOVED from finance/ (git mv, unchanged)\n  chart-of-accounts.service.ts   MOVED from finance/chart-of-accounts-seeder.service.ts\n  inventory-cost.service.ts      NEW  weighted-average cost, extracted from expense-posting.service.ts:427-450\n  money-event-posting.service.ts NEW  the 11 recipes\n  posting-attempt.service.ts     NEW  intent record + unposted query\n  ledger-repair.queue.ts         NEW  BullMQ\n  ledger.controller.ts           NEW  /ledger/businesses/:businessId/unposted\n```\n\n### 2.2 What it owns\n\nThe ledger module owns **the mapping from a typed money event to balanced entries**, the **record that the mapping was attempted**, and **nothing else**. It owns two tables: `financial_transactions` and `ledger_entries` (already), plus one new one, `ledger_posting_attempts`.\n\nIt **never queries another module's tables.** Every recipe takes a value object. `payroll` reads `PayrollRun`; `continental-ops` reads `GoodsReceipt`; each hands over a `MoneyEvent`. This is the `onExpensePaid` shape, not the `onInvoiceFinalized` shape, and it is the whole reason the boundary holds.\n\n### 2.3 Public interface\n\n```ts\n// apps/server/src/modules/ledger/money-event.contract.ts\n// Imports: 'type { Prisma } from @prisma/client' and nothing else.\n\nexport type MoneyEventKind =\n  | 'payroll_accrued' | 'payroll_paid'\n  | 'goods_received'  | 'purchase_invoice_matched'\n  | 'stock_variance'  | 'goods_delivered'\n  | 'platform_fee_paid'\n  | 'inventory_revalued'\n  | 'ticket_sold'     | 'ticket_recognised'\n  | 'digital_goods_sold';\n\nexport interface MoneyEvent {\n  businessId: string;\n  kind: MoneyEventKind;\n  /** Prisma model name of the row that caused this. Half of the idempotency key. */\n  sourceType: string;\n  sourceId: string;\n  occurredAt: Date;\n  currency: string;\n  /** Gross magnitude, for the FinancialTransaction header and the attempt row. */\n  amount: number;\n  description: string;\n  contactId?: string | null;\n  reference?: string | null;\n  createdById?: string | null;\n  /** Per-kind payload. Discriminated on `kind`; see money-event.contract.ts. */\n  detail: MoneyEventDetail;\n}\n\nexport interface LedgerPosting {\n  /** Post now, inside the caller's transaction. Throws on a bad recipe. */\n  post(e: MoneyEvent, tx?: Prisma.TransactionClient): Promise<PostingResult>;\n\n  /** Record the intent, then post. Never throws; on failure the attempt row\n   *  is FAILED and the repair queue owns the retry. Use from webhooks and\n   *  schedulers where there is no user to show an error to. */\n  postOrRecord(e: MoneyEvent, tx?: Prisma.TransactionClient): Promise<PostingOutcome>;\n\n  listUnposted(businessId: string, o?: { status?: AttemptStatus; limit?: number }): Promise<UnpostedRow[]>;\n\n  /** Complete one FAILED/SKIPPED attempt. The only mutation the tool layer gets. */\n  resolveAttempt(businessId: string, attemptId: string, actorId?: string): Promise<PostingOutcome>;\n}\n```\n\n`LedgerModule` exports `PostingService`, `ChartOfAccountsService`, `InventoryCostService`, and `LedgerPostingService` (the `LedgerPosting` implementation).\n\n### 2.4 What it listens to, what it emits\n\n**Listens to: nothing.** Deliberate. A listener is asynchronous, and `finance.module.ts:47-55` already decided that the posting must commit in the same transaction as the state change. An `@OnEvent` posting path would reintroduce exactly the failure this spec exists to close: the state changes, the listener throws, and the books are quietly wrong. Callers call the service.\n\n**Emits:**\n\n| Event | When | Who cares |\n|---|---|---|\n| `ledger.posted` | a `MoneyEvent` produced a transaction | key-cortex context slices, timeline |\n| `ledger.posting_failed` | an attempt went FAILED | notifications, the unposted screen badge |\n| `ledger.attempt_skipped` | a recipe returned \"no cost basis\" | the unposted screen |\n\n### 2.5 Why this creates no import cycle — and why the obvious approach does\n\n**The obvious approach adds a cycle, and I can name it.**\n\n`ai.module.ts:97` imports `PayrollModule`, `SubscriptionsModule` and `StaffPerformanceModule` **without `forwardRef`**, and `forwardRef(() => ContractsModule)`, `forwardRef(() => ExpensesModule)`, `forwardRef(() => FinanceModule)`. `finance.module.ts:57` imports `forwardRef(() => AiModule)` and `forwardRef(() => CommerceModule)`.\n\nSo if `PayrollModule` imports `FinanceModule` to reach `PostingService`:\n\n```\nPayrollModule → FinanceModule → forwardRef(AiModule) → PayrollModule\n```\n\nThat is a new cycle, and because `ai.module.ts:97` does not wrap `PayrollModule` in `forwardRef`, closing it requires adding one — `forwardRef` number 103 (current count: `grep -rn \"forwardRef\" apps/server/src --include=*.module.ts | wc -l` → 102). The same argument holds for `SubscriptionsModule` (also unwrapped) and `ContractsModule`.\n\n**The leaf breaks it.** `LedgerModule`'s `imports` array is `[]`. `PrismaModule` (`core/prisma/prisma.module.ts`) and `RedisModule` (`core/redis/redis.module.ts`) are both `@Global`, so `PrismaService` and `REDIS_CLIENT` are injectable with no module edge at all. `EventBusModule` (`core/event-bus/event-bus.module.ts`) is `@Global` too.\n\nOut-degree zero means **no path from `LedgerModule` back to any importer exists, for any importer.** Thirteen new in-edges, zero cycles, by construction rather than by inspection.\n\nThe move of `posting.service.ts` and `chart-of-accounts-seeder.service.ts` out of `finance/` is what makes this true at the TypeScript level as well as the Nest level: leaving them in place and having `ledger` import them by path would create the file-graph edge `ledger → finance` against the existing `finance → ledger`. Cost of the move: 21 import lines for `posting.service` and 11 for the seeder (enumerated by `grep -rn \"posting.service'\"` / `\"chart-of-accounts-seeder.service'\"`). Mechanical, one commit, no behaviour change. `FinanceModule` then does `imports: [LedgerModule, ...], exports: [LedgerModule, ...]` so its 21 existing consumers keep working unchanged.\n\n`InventoryCostService` is a genuine deletion, not an addition: the weighted-average block at `expense-posting.service.ts:427-450` moves into it and `onProductSold` delegates. Two callers of the same arithmetic become one.\n\n---\n\n## 3. Data\n\n### 3.1 New model\n\n```prisma\n/// The record that a money event was supposed to reach the ledger.\n/// Written BEFORE the posting is attempted, so a failure is a queryable row\n/// rather than a log line. This is the whole point of the model: without it,\n/// \"money moved and the books do not show it\" is invisible.\nmodel LedgerPostingAttempt {\n  id         String   @id @default(cuid())\n  businessId String   @map(\"business_id\")\n  business   Business @relation(fields: [businessId], references: [id], onDelete: Cascade)\n\n  sourceType String  @map(\"source_type\")\n  sourceId   String  @map(\"source_id\")\n  kind       String\n  /// Mirrors buildExternalRef(sourceType, sourceId, kind). Never null here —\n  /// an event with no dedupe key is rejected at the contract boundary.\n  externalRef String @map(\"external_ref\")\n\n  status      String  @default(\"PENDING\") // PENDING | POSTED | FAILED | SKIPPED\n  attempts    Int     @default(0)\n  lastError   String? @map(\"last_error\")\n  /// Why a SKIPPED attempt was skipped. e.g. 'no_cost_basis', 'period_closed'.\n  skipReason  String? @map(\"skip_reason\")\n\n  amount   Decimal @db.Decimal(18, 4)\n  currency String  @default(\"TTD\")\n\n  transactionId String?              @map(\"transaction_id\")\n  transaction   FinancialTransaction? @relation(fields: [transactionId], references: [id], onDelete: SetNull)\n\n  /// The MoneyEvent as submitted. Lets the repair queue replay without\n  /// re-reading the source module's tables — which it must not do.\n  payload Json\n\n  occurredAt DateTime  @map(\"occurred_at\")\n  postedAt   DateTime? @map(\"posted_at\")\n  createdAt  DateTime  @default(now()) @map(\"created_at\")\n  updatedAt  DateTime  @updatedAt @map(\"updated_at\")\n\n  @@unique([businessId, externalRef])\n  @@index([businessId, status, occurredAt])\n  @@index([businessId, sourceType, sourceId])\n  @@map(\"ledger_posting_attempts\")\n}\n```\n\nHouse style honoured: `snake_case @map`, `businessId` with a cascading `Business` relation, compound `@@index([businessId, ...])`.\n\n### 3.2 Field additions\n\n```prisma\nmodel PayrollRun {\n  // ...\n  /// Which account the run was paid from. Without it the posting has to guess\n  /// the credit side, and RevenuePostingService.depositSystemKey's guess is\n  /// wrong for payroll — it defaults to CASH.\n  paymentMethod String? @map(\"payment_method\")\n}\n```\n\n`FinancialTransaction` gains the back-relation `postingAttempt LedgerPostingAttempt?`.\n\n### 3.3 Chart of accounts additions\n\nAppended to `SYSTEM_COA` in `chart-of-accounts.service.ts`. No data migration (see §1.3).\n\n```ts\n{ systemKey: 'GRNI',                    name: 'Goods Received Not Invoiced', type: 'LIABILITY', code: '2050' },\n{ systemKey: 'PAYROLL_PAYABLE',         name: 'Payroll Payable',             type: 'LIABILITY', code: '2400' },\n{ systemKey: 'DEFERRED_REVENUE',        name: 'Deferred Revenue',            type: 'LIABILITY', code: '2500' },\n{ systemKey: 'INVENTORY_SHRINKAGE',     name: 'Inventory Shrinkage',         type: 'EXPENSE',   code: '6970', parentSystemKey: 'EXPENSE_GENERAL' },\n{ systemKey: 'PURCHASE_PRICE_VARIANCE', name: 'Purchase Price Variance',     type: 'EXPENSE',   code: '6980', parentSystemKey: 'EXPENSE_GENERAL' },\n```\n\n### 3.4 Migration\n\n`packages/db/prisma/migrations/20260810120000_add_ledger_posting_attempt/migration.sql`\n\n```sql\nCREATE TABLE \"ledger_posting_attempts\" (\n  \"id\"             TEXT NOT NULL,\n  \"business_id\"    TEXT NOT NULL,\n  \"source_type\"    TEXT NOT NULL,\n  \"source_id\"      TEXT NOT NULL,\n  \"kind\"           TEXT NOT NULL,\n  \"external_ref\"   TEXT NOT NULL,\n  \"status\"         TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT 'PENDING',\n  \"attempts\"       INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,\n  \"last_error\"     TEXT,\n  \"skip_reason\"    TEXT,\n  \"amount\"         DECIMAL(18,4) NOT NULL,\n  \"currency\"       TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT 'TTD',\n  \"transaction_id\" TEXT,\n  \"payload\"        JSONB NOT NULL,\n  \"occurred_at\"    TIMESTAMP(3) NOT NULL,\n  \"posted_at\"      TIMESTAMP(3),\n  \"created_at\"     TIMESTAMP(3) NOT NULL DEFAULT CURRENT_TIMESTAMP,\n  \"updated_at\"     TIMESTAMP(3) NOT NULL,\n  CONSTRAINT \"ledger_posting_attempts_pkey\" PRIMARY KEY (\"id\")\n);\n\nCREATE UNIQUE INDEX \"ledger_posting_attempts_business_id_external_ref_key\"\n  ON \"ledger_posting_attempts\"(\"business_id\", \"external_ref\");\nCREATE INDEX \"ledger_posting_attempts_business_id_status_occurred_at_idx\"\n  ON \"ledger_posting_attempts\"(\"business_id\", \"status\", \"occurred_at\");\nCREATE INDEX \"ledger_posting_attempts_business_id_source_type_source_id_idx\"\n  ON \"ledger_posting_attempts\"(\"business_id\", \"source_type\", \"source_id\");\n\nALTER TABLE \"ledger_posting_attempts\"\n  ADD CONSTRAINT \"ledger_posting_attempts_business_id_fkey\"\n  FOREIGN KEY (\"business_id\") REFERENCES \"businesses\"(\"id\") ON DELETE CASCADE ON UPDATE CASCADE;\nALTER TABLE \"ledger_posting_attempts\"\n  ADD CONSTRAINT \"ledger_posting_attempts_transaction_id_fkey\"\n  FOREIGN KEY (\"transaction_id\") REFERENCES \"financial_transactions\"(\"id\") ON DELETE SET NULL ON UPDATE CASCADE;\n\nALTER TABLE \"payroll_runs\" ADD COLUMN \"payment_method\" TEXT;\n```\n\n### 3.5 Tenant isolation — the step that is skipped by default\n\nAdd **`'LedgerPostingAttempt'`** to `BUSINESS_ID_MODELS` in `packages/db/src/client.ts`, in the finance cluster block that begins at line 128, next to `'LedgerEntry'`.\n\nStated explicitly because `tenant-model-list.spec.ts` **will not catch the omission** — it only walks list→schema, never schema→list (verified by reading `:49-95` on 2026-08-09). A new model is unscoped by default and CI stays green. §6.1 adds the assertion that closes this for this model.\n\nWhile adding it, also add `'StockMovement'`, `'InventoryStock'`, `'GoodsReceipt'`, `'StockCount'`, `'PayrollRun'`, `'PayrollItem'` and `'RetainerAgreement'` — all seven have a `businessId String` column and all seven are read on HTTP paths this spec adds. `RetainerPeriod`, `ChangeOrder` and `Warehouse` **must not** be added: the first two have no `businessId` column, so the third assertion at `tenant-model-list.spec.ts:68-84` would fail and the extension would throw `PrismaClientValidationError` at runtime.\n\n---\n\n## 4. The postings — all 13, by class\n\nThree classes, because \"post from every module\" is the wrong instruction for five of them.
