Reuse before you add applies here too: if a module's money already has a document that posts, route it to the document.\n\n### Class A — a direct `MoneyEvent` (7 modules, 11 recipes)\n\nMoney moved, or an asset/liability changed, with no invoice document in play.\n\n| # | Module · call site to change | Event `kind` | `sourceType:sourceId:kind` | Debit | Credit |\n|---:|---|---|---|---|---|\n| A1 | `payroll.service.ts:207-216` `approveRun` | `payroll_accrued` | `PayrollRun:<id>:payroll_accrued` | `EXPENSE_PAYROLL` `totalGross` | `PAYROLL_PAYABLE` `totalGross` |\n| A2 | `payroll.service.ts:218-227` `markRunPaid` | `payroll_paid` | `PayrollRun:<id>:payroll_paid` | `PAYROLL_PAYABLE` | `BANK`/`CASH` per `paymentMethod` |\n| A3 | `goods-receipt.service.ts:118-139` `post` | `goods_received` | `GoodsReceipt:<id>:goods_received` | `INVENTORY_ASSET` Σ(`qtyAccepted`×unitCost) | `GRNI` same |\n| A4 | `procurement.service.ts:250-265` `markInvoiced` | `purchase_invoice_matched` | `PurchaseOrder:<id>:purchase_invoice_matched` | `GRNI` receipt value **+** `PURCHASE_PRICE_VARIANCE` if invoice > receipt | `ACCOUNTS_PAYABLE` invoice value |\n| A5 | `stock-count.service.ts:99-119` `adjust` | `stock_variance` | `StockCount:<id>:stock_variance` | V<0: `INVENTORY_SHRINKAGE` \\|V\\| · V>0: `INVENTORY_ASSET` V | V<0: `INVENTORY_ASSET` · V>0: `INVENTORY_SHRINKAGE` |\n| A6 | `delivery-note.service.ts:103-135` `fulfill` | `goods_delivered` | `DeliveryNote:<id>:goods_delivered` | `COGS` at weighted-avg | `INVENTORY_ASSET` |\n| A7 | `subscriptions.service.ts` payment record | `platform_fee_paid` | `SubscriptionPayment:<id>:platform_fee_paid` | `EXPENSE_SOFTWARE` | `BANK`/`PAYMENT_PROCESSOR` per `method` |\n| A8 | `supplier.service.ts:463-514` cost update | `inventory_revalued` | `ProductCostProfile:<id>:inventory_revalued` | Δ>0: `INVENTORY_ASSET` | Δ>0: `PURCHASE_PRICE_VARIANCE` (reversed when Δ<0) |\n| A9 | `events/attendee.service.ts:212-224` `claimTicketSlot` | `ticket_sold` | `EventAttendee:<id>:ticket_sold` | `ACCOUNTS_RECEIVABLE` or `CASH` at `ticketType.price` | `DEFERRED_REVENUE` |\n| A10 | scheduled, on event start | `ticket_recognised` | `Event:<id>:ticket_recognised` | `DEFERRED_REVENUE` Σ sold | `REVENUE_SERVICE` |\n| A11 | `community/resource-marketplace.service.ts:143` | `digital_goods_sold` | `ResourceDownload:<id>:digital_goods_sold` | `PAYMENT_PROCESSOR` `pricePaid` | `REVENUE_PRODUCT` |\n\n**A3 valuation, and the rule that keeps it honest.** `GoodsReceipt.items` is a JSON array with no `unitCost` (`schema.prisma:4942`). Cost basis, in order: (1) the matching line on `purchaseOrder.items` when `purchaseOrderId` is set; (2) `InventoryStock.costPerUnit` for the product; (3) **none — do not fabricate.** Case (3) writes the `StockMovement`, marks the attempt `SKIPPED` with `skipReason:'no_cost_basis'`, and shows on the unposted screen. This is the rule `onProductSold` already states in its own docstring (`expense-posting.service.ts:406-408`, enforced at `:461-462`) — the alternative is a balance sheet that looks complete and is fiction.\n\n**A3 also fixes a silent bug in the same method.** `goods-receipt.service.ts:128-131` calls `inventoryStock.updateMany({ where: { productId, businessId }, ... })` and never checks the returned `count`. A product with no `InventoryStock` row increments nothing, silently, and returns `status:'POSTED'`. The rewrite reads the stock rows first, and a receipt for a product with no stock row **creates** the row (as `fulfillment-routing.service.ts:1024-1036` already does) rather than pretending. Same defect and same fix at `stock-count.service.ts:108-111`, which additionally uses `updateMany` with a bare assignment — a count against a product held in two warehouses currently sets **both** to the counted quantity.\n\n**A9/A10 deferred revenue.** A ticket sold in March for an event in June is not March revenue. The recognition leg (A10) is not a listener and not a `setInterval` — it is a BullMQ delayed job keyed `ledger-recognise:<eventId>`, enqueued when the first ticket sells and re-enqueued idempotently on each subsequent sale. See §5.2.\n\n### Class B — route to the document that already posts (5 modules, 0 new recipes)\n\nThese modules' money has, or should have, an invoice or payment behind it. Posting them directly would double-count against the existing `commerce`/`payments` paths.\n\n| # | Module | Change | Why not a direct posting |\n|---:|---|---|---|\n| B1 | `retainers` | `retainer.service.ts:166-173` — refuse `status:'INVOICED'` unless `invoiceId` is set. Emit `retainer.period.billable` when a period closes with `amountBilled > 0`; `CommerceModule` listens and calls its existing `createInvoice`. | `getSummary` (`:180-183`) already counts `INVOICED` periods as revenue. The bug is revenue claimed with no document, not a missing journal. Fix the claim. |\n| B2 | `change-orders` | `change-order.service.ts:114-128` — on `status:'APPROVED'` with `additionalAmount > 0`, emit `project.change_order.approved`; commerce raises the invoice line. `businessId` derives from `project.businessId` (the model has none). | An approved change order is a contract amendment. Revenue recognises when it invoices, which is what commerce already does correctly. |\n| B3 | `keystore` | On transition to `DELIVERED` with `finalPrice > 0` and `invoiceId` null, emit `keystore.order.deliverable`. | `keystore.service.ts:586` sums `finalPrice` into `totalRevenue` for orders that never became an invoice. Same shape as B1. |\n| B4 | `bookings` | No-show / late-cancel fee raises a fee **invoice** via the existing `commerce.createInvoiceForService(..., { kind:'FEE', amountOverride })` path used at `bookings.service.ts:832-837`. | A forfeited deposit is already recognised — the deposit invoice posted on payment. Posting the forfeiture again is a double count. The only *new* money is the fee, and a fee needs a document the customer can see. |\n| B5 | `continental-ops` receipt | `payment-receipt.service.ts:63-78` — emit `receipt.issued`; `PaymentsModule` listens and records the `Payment`, which posts through `payments.service.ts:51`. | A receipt is evidence of a payment. Give it the `Payment` row it is evidence *of*, and one posting path stays one posting path. |\n| B6 | `time-tracking` | `time-entry.controller.ts:286` `markAsBilled` — require an `invoiceId` argument; refuse the flag without one. | This is capability-map item #19 (\"time → invoice\"). Marking hours billed with no invoice is the WIP hole; a journal would not close it. |\n\nClass B is where \"reuse before you add\" pays: five modules, no new recipe, no new COA key, and each fix deletes an incorrect revenue claim rather than adding a journal on top of one.\n\n### Class C — deliberately no posting (1 module)\n\n**`contracts`.** `contractValue` (`contracts.service.ts:120`, `:199`, `:358`) is a *forecast of future consideration*, not a transaction. Posting it would put unearned, uninvoiced money on the balance sheet and it would be wrong under any basis. The contracts gap is that `renewalDate` fires nothing — a scheduling defect, addressed by capability-map item #2, not by this spec. Stated here so nobody \"finishes the list\" by posting it.\n\n---\n\n## 5. The three layers\n\n### 5.1 MANUAL — the screen\n\nTwo pieces, both small, both closing an existing hole.\n\n**(a) `/app/finance/unposted`** — new. `apps/web/src/app/app/finance/unposted/page.tsx`. A table of `LedgerPostingAttempt` rows where `status != 'POSTED'`: what happened, when, how much, which module, why it did not post, and a **Post it** button per row. New api functions in the existing `apps/web/src/lib/api/finance.ts`:\n\n```ts\nexport async function fetchUnpostedMoney(businessId: string, params?: { status?: string; limit?: number })\nexport async function resolvePostingAttempt(businessId: string, attemptId: string)\n```\n\nTwo mechanical constraints, both verified against `apps/web/src/lib/tool-route-audit.ts`:\n\n- The mutation must be named to match `MUTATION_NAME` (`:469-470`). **`post*` is not in that list.** `resolvePostingAttempt` matches `resolve`; `postPendingEntry` would silently fail the parity gate.\n- `/app/finance/unposted` survives `followConfigRedirects` — `apps/web/next.config.ts` redirects `/app/finance` exactly and the four sub-paths `revenue|expenses|reports|actions` (with `/:path*`), and there is no `/app/finance/:path*` catch-all. Checked line by line.\n\nNav entry in `apps/web/src/lib/nav-config.ts`, in the `money` group next to `{ label: \"General ledger\", href: \"/app/finance/ledger\" }` at line 178: `{ label: \"Unposted money\", href: \"/app/finance/unposted\", icon: AlertTriangle }`.\n\n**(b) Re-mount the journal composer.** `apps/web/src/app/app/finance/journal/page.tsx` is a 5-line `redirect(\"/app/finance/ledger\")`, and `/app/finance/ledger/page.tsx` (151 lines) is read-only — it imports only `fetchGeneralLedger`. `books-journal-tab.tsx` is a complete balanced double-entry composer with **zero importers** and `POST /finance/businesses/:id/journal-entries` (`finance.controller.ts:82-113`) already accepts what it produces. Mount it at `/app/finance/journal` and delete the redirect. This is capability-map Wave 0 #1; it is in scope here because without it no human can correct a posting this spec creates, and manual parity for the new tools would be a claim rather than a fact.\n\n### 5.2 SMART — the deterministic path, on BullMQ\n\nThree jobs. **No `setInterval`, no `@Cron`, no module field.** State lives in `ledger_posting_attempts` and in Redis.\n\n`apps/server/src/modules/ledger/ledger-repair.queue.ts`, modelled on `modules/business-events/business-event.queue.ts:26-77` (the smallest correct BullMQ service in the repo — injects `REDIS_CLIENT` from `core/redis/redis.constants`, one `Queue`, one `Worker`, `onModuleDestroy` closes both).\n\n| Job | Trigger | Job key | Why it cannot double-fire |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| `ledger:repair` | repeatable, `pattern: '*/15 * * * *'` | `jobId: 'ledger-repair'` | A BullMQ repeatable job with a fixed `jobId` produces one delayed job per window across N workers. This is the same construction `queue.service.ts:113-125` already uses for `ai-cron-triggers`. |\n| `ledger:recognise` | delayed, enqueued on first ticket sale | `jobId: 'ledger-recognise:<eventId>'` | Explicit `jobId` — a second sale enqueuing the same key is a no-op, not a second job. |\n| `ledger:backfill` | manual, once per business | `jobId: 'ledger-backfill:<businessId>'` | Same. |\n\n`ledger:repair` selects `LedgerPostingAttempt where businessId != null and status in ('PENDING','FAILED') and attempts < 5`, ordered by `occurredAt`, `take: 200`, and replays each from its stored `payload` — never by re-reading the source module's tables, which it is not allowed to touch. Backoff is `attempts`-driven in the row, not in memory. Anything at `attempts >= 5` stays on the screen for a human.\n\n`ledger:backfill` replays historical rows for the eleven Class A recipes so a business that has been running for a year gets a complete ledger rather than one that starts today. It is idempotent by `externalRef` and **respects `AccountingPeriodService.checkClosed`** (`accounting-period.service.ts:158-168`) — an event dated inside a CLOSED period is written `SKIPPED` with `skipReason:'period_closed'` and surfaced, never forced.\n\n### 5.3 AI — the KEY tools\n\nThree tools in `apps/server/src/modules/ai/flow-tool-registry.ts`, with `case` clauses in `flow-orchestrator.service.ts` `executeToolAction` — `flow-tool-honesty.spec.ts:45-60` fails the build for a declared tool with no handler.\n\n| Tool | family | riskTier | manualEquivalentRoute |\n|---|---|---:|---|\n| `finance_list_unposted` | `read` | 1 | `/app/finance/unposted` |\n| `finance_resolve_posting` | `execute` | 2 | `/app/finance/unposted` |\n| `finance_explain_posting` | `read` | 1 | `/app/finance/ledger` |\n\nThe `finance_` prefix is load-bearing twice over:\n\n- `tool-route-audit.ts:118-123` takes the tool name's prefix before the first `_` and requires it to appear in the route with `/` and `-` stripped. `\"appfinanceunposted\".includes(\"finance\")` → passes with no `CROSS_DOMAIN_ROUTES` entry.\n- `role-engine.service.ts:214` gives the `finance` role `'finance_*'` with `maxRiskTier: 2` (`:216`). A `ledger_*` prefix would be in **no** role's `approvedTools` and the tools would be structurally unreachable — the failure mode the capability map describes for `JobRole`.\n\n`finance_resolve_posting` at T2 is defensible precisely because of what it cannot do: it completes an attempt row **the system already decided was owed**, replaying a stored payload. It cannot invent a journal, choose accounts, or change an amount. That is the bookkeeper-at-T2 boundary from capability map §2.4, drawn at the one place it is safe.\n\n`finance_list_unposted` is also the first money slice KEY gets for free: \"what money has moved that the books do not show\" is one query against `ledger_posting_attempts`, and it belongs in the standing context (capability-map item #17) the day it exists.\n\n---\n\n## 6. Tests\n\nEvery test below names its negative control, because in this repo the recurring failure is a green assertion that could not have gone red.\n\n### 6.1 The coverage gate — `apps/server/src/modules/ledger/money-module-coverage.spec.ts`\n\nSame shape as `tenant-model-list.spec.ts` and `event-wiring.spec.ts`: a hand-maintained list, diffed against the source every build.\n\n```\nMONEY_MODULES = the 18 from §0, each with { class: 'A'|'B'|'C', evidence: string }\n```\n\nAssertions:\n1. Every Class A module's directory contains at least one call to `ledgerPosting.post(` or `.postOrRecord(`.\n2. Every Class B module emits its named event, and some module has an `@OnEvent` for it. (Reuse `apps/server/scripts/event-wiring.ts`, which already distinguishes dead from unverified.)\n3. Every Class C entry carries a non-empty `reason`.\n4. Every `MoneyEventKind` in `money-event.contract.ts` has a branch in `money-event-posting.service.ts`.\n5. `'LedgerPostingAttempt'` is in `BUSINESS_ID_MODELS`.\n\n**Negative control (runs every time, like `assertParityCheckWorks` at `tool-route-audit.ts:308-334`):** the spec points its own scanner at two fixtures under `__fixtures__/` — `posts.service.ts` which calls `ledgerPosting.post`, and `silent.service.ts` which mutates a money field and does not. It asserts the scanner flags the second and not the first. If either result flips, the spec **throws** with \"the coverage gate is broken\" rather than reporting a finding — a broken checker is a broken invocation, not a fact about the modules.\n\n### 6.2 Real-database integration — `ledger-goods-receipt.integration.spec.ts`\n\nAgainst real Postgres, following the pattern the capability map credits CI with running. Seed business, product, warehouse, `InventoryStock` with `costPerUnit`, `PurchaseOrder` with a matching line. Call `GoodsReceiptService.post`. Assert:\n\n1. `InventoryStock.quantity` increased by `qtyAccepted`.\n2. A `StockMovement` exists with `type:'RECEIPT'`, `reasonCode:'PO_RECEIPT'`, `referenceId = gr.id`.\n3. A `FinancialTransaction` exists with `externalRef = 'GoodsReceipt:<id>:goods_received'`, and Σdebit = Σcredit = `qtyAccepted × unitCost`.\n4. `LedgerPostingAttempt.status = 'POSTED'` with `transactionId` set.\n5. Calling `post` again throws `Already posted`; calling `LedgerPostingService.post` again returns `alreadyPosted: true` and `FinancialTransaction.count` is unchanged.\n\n**Negative controls, three, because each assertion needs to be shown capable of failing:**\n- **The assertion discriminates.** Insert a second `GoodsReceipt` directly with Prisma, bypassing the service. Assert `expectPostedFor(gr2)` **throws**. This proves assertion 3 is not vacuously satisfied by any transaction in the table.\n- **The balance check discriminates.** Hand the balance helper a hand-built unbalanced entry set and assert it returns false. `validatePosting` (`posting.service.ts:79-104`) would reject it at write time, so the helper must be exercised directly or it is never proved to fail.\n- **The no-cost-basis path is real.** A receipt for a product with no PO line and no `costPerUnit` must produce a `StockMovement`, **no** `FinancialTransaction`, and an attempt row with `status:'SKIPPED'`, `skipReason:'no_cost_basis'`. Asserting the absence of a transaction is only meaningful next to the positive case above, which is why both live in one file.\n\n### 6.3 Tenant isolation — `ledger-posting-attempt.tenancy.spec.ts`\n\nPost in business A. With the tenant context set to B: `listUnposted` returns `[]`, `resolveAttempt(B, attemptFromA)` throws, and B's trial balance is unchanged.\n\n**Negative control:** the same test with `'LedgerPostingAttempt'` removed from `BUSINESS_ID_MODELS` must **fail**. Run once by hand at implementation time and record the observation in the file header — the repo's own convention (`tenant-model-list.spec.ts:15-19` records exactly this kind of observation). Without it, \"the tenant test passes\" is compatible with the model never having been in the set.\n\n### 6.4 Single-fire under two replicas — `ledger-repair.queue.spec.ts`\n\nTwo `Worker` instances on the same queue against a real Redis. Enqueue the repeatable job. Assert the processed counter is exactly 1 per window.\n\n**Negative control:** the same test with a `setInterval` in place of the queue must count 2. This is the assertion that makes \"scalable\" a measurement rather than a claim, and it is currently untrue of all 52 schedulers.\n\n### 6.5 Unit — `money-event-posting.service.spec.ts`\n\nOne case per `MoneyEventKind`: correct COA `systemKey` on each side, correct sign, correct `externalRef`. Stubbed Prisma, following `expense-posting.service.spec.ts:70-210`.\n\n**Negative control:** a deliberately reversed recipe (credit where debit belongs) must fail its own assertion. Written as an `expect(() => assertRecipe(...)).toThrow()` so the assertion helper is proved to discriminate — not as a commented-out test.\n\n---\n\n## 7. Effort, and what to adopt\n\n| Wave | Work | Days |\n|---|---|---:|\n| A | Extract `LedgerModule` leaf: `git mv` two services (32 import lines), `InventoryCostService` extraction, `money-event.contract.ts`, `LedgerPostingAttempt` model + migration + `BUSINESS_ID_MODELS`, 5 COA keys | 5 |\n| B | The 11 Class A recipes and their 7 callers, each inside the caller's existing `$transaction`; fix the two unchecked `updateMany` calls; `StockMovement` on receipt and count | 9 |\n| C | The 6 Class B redirections — guards, events, listeners on the existing commerce/payments invoice paths | 5 |\n| D | `ledger-repair.queue.ts`: repair sweep, deferred-revenue recognition, backfill, period-lock guard | 4 |\n| E | `/app/finance/unposted` + nav + api; re-mount `books-journal-tab`; 3 KEY tools + orchestrator cases | 4 |\n| F | The five test files in §6, including every negative control | 4 |\n| | **Total** | **31 days ≈ 6 weeks** |\n\nSequencing note: Wave A must land alone and green. It is a pure move with zero behaviour change, and it is the only wave that touches 32 files — mixing it with a recipe makes the diff unreviewable and the boot gate unhelpful.\n\n**Adopt, do not build:**\n\n- **BullMQ 5.76.8** — already in `apps/server/package.json:42`, already used correctly with `repeat` + fixed `jobId` at `ai/queue.service.ts:113-125`. MIT. Zero new dependencies, zero new containers; Redis is in both compose files.\n- **`decimal.js` via `Prisma.Decimal`** — already the arithmetic in `posting.service.ts:39-41`. Do not introduce a second money type. Apache-2.0 (bundled with Prisma).\n- **`ExpensePostingService.onProductSold`'s cost resolution** — extracted, not rewritten.\n\nNothing else needs buying. This is a wiring problem, and the capability map is right that the engine is eighteen months of competitor work already paid for.\n\n---\n\n## 8. What this spec deliberately does not do\n\n| Not doing | Why |\n|---|---|\n| **T&T statutory payroll** — PAYE, NIS, Health Surcharge, TD4, net pay | Capability map §3.4 refuses it and the refusal is correct: a compliance product with an annual rates obligation. The posting here is **gross only** — Dr `EXPENSE_PAYROLL` / Cr `PAYROLL_PAYABLE` / Cr `BANK`. Deductions become additional credit legs the day someone maintains a rates table; the recipe shape does not change. |\n| **Posting `Contract.contractValue`** | Class C. A forecast on the balance sheet is worse than no balance sheet. |\n| **A three-way-match resolution UI** | A4 computes and posts the variance to `PURCHASE_PRICE_VARIANCE` and surfaces it. Deciding what to *do* about a variance is a workflow, and it belongs after the numbers exist. |\n| **Multi-currency revaluation** | `ExchangeRateService` exists; period-end FX revaluation is a separate recipe with its own correctness argument. Every posting here uses the source row's own currency. |\n| **Refactoring `RevenuePostingService` to value objects** | It reads `Invoice` and `Payment` by id (`revenue-posting.service.ts:105-125`, `:272-288`), which is the boundary violation this spec avoids for new code. Changing it would touch all five working callers for no behavioural gain. Named as debt; not paid here. |\n| **A posting listener on the event bus** | Rejected on purpose (§2.4). Asynchronous posting reintroduces exactly the divergence being closed. |\n| **Touching `education` or `public-events`** | Re-derived (§0): neither mutates a monetary column. Adding a posting to a module that has no money is how a list of 18 becomes a list of 22 that means less. |\n| **Money slices in the standing AI context** | `finance_list_unposted` makes the query trivially available, and capability-map item #17 is the right home for the context work. Out of scope, one line away. |\n| **Fixing the 68 `forwardRef`s** | This spec adds zero and says how (§2.5). Removing existing ones is a separate, falsifiable exercise the open-gaps doc already scopes. |\n\n---\n\n## 9. Where an engineer starts, in order\n\n1. `apps/server/src/modules/finance/posting.service.ts` — read all 328 lines. Nothing below makes sense without `buildExternalRef` and the two `skipTenantIsolation` comments.\n2. `apps/server/src/modules/marketplace/fulfillment-routing.service.ts:978-1075` — the shape every Class A caller becomes.\n3. `apps/server/src/modules/ai/ai.module.ts:97` and `apps/server/src/modules/finance/finance.module.ts:57` — read them together and the cycle in §2.5 is visible in ten seconds.\n4. `packages/db/src/client.ts:81-153` — add the model name before you write the model.\n5. `apps/server/src/modules/continental-ops/goods-receipt.service.ts:118-139` — the smallest complete instance of the defect. Fix it first, end to end, with §6.2's three negative controls, and the remaining ten recipes are the same twenty lines with different accounts."

      {
        "key": "03-manual-layer",
        "title": "The missing manual layers",
        "spec": "# Implementation Spec — The Missing Manual Layers\n\n**Repo:** `C:/kfm` @ `main` (`aea1aab2`). **Read-only pass, 2026-08-09.**\n**Builds on:** `docs/CAPABILITY_MAP_2026-08-09.md` §1.3(3) and `docs/OPEN_GAPS_2026-08-09.md`.\n**Item:** every function where the KEY tool exists and the manual screen is absent or fake; the screens that close it; and the parity gate that stops \"a page exists\" from counting.\n\n---\n\n## 0. The claim being repaired\n\n`check-tool-routes` prints **142/142 write tools have manual parity**. I re-derived the denominator today by parsing `apps/server/src/modules/ai/flow-tool-registry.ts`: 245 tools — `read 95 · draft 8 · organize 42 · execute 35 · crud 65` — so 142 write tools across **40 distinct declared routes**. The count is right. The verdict is not.\n\nThe gate proves *a page exists and imports at least one mutation from the api layer*. It never asks whether the page imports **the mutation this tool performs**. Ten write tools have no manual path anywhere in the product; three more point at a screen that cannot do the thing while a screen that can sits elsewhere; one \"manual equivalent\" is a button whose server handler silently discards the fields it sends.\n\n---\n\n## 1. What exists today\n\n### 1.1 The gate\n\n| File | Lines | Role |\n|---|---:|---|\n| `apps/web/src/lib/tool-route-audit.ts` | 595 | the analysis — six checks, a self-test, a `next.config` redirect follower |\n| `apps/web/src/lib/__tests__/tool-routes.spec.ts` | 68 | the CI half (`pnpm --filter web test:unit`, `.github/workflows/ci-cd.yml:456`) |\n| `apps/web/scripts/check-tool-routes.ts` | 36 | CLI wrapper, `TOOL_ROUTES_WARN_ONLY=1` downgrades |\n\nIt is a careful piece of work — it follows `redirect()` shims (`resolvePage`, :418), reads `next.config.ts`'s redirect table (`nextConfigRedirects`, :351), walks imports to depth 3, refuses to recurse into `lib/` because that made an earlier version unfailable (:544-553), and runs its own negative control every time (`assertParityCheckWorks`, :308). Nothing below is a criticism of its construction. It measures the wrong quantity.\n\n### 1.2 The five ways it passes while parity is absent\n\n**(a) Any mutation counts as every mutation.** `canWrite()` (:531) returns `true` on the *first* imported name matching `MUTATION_NAME` (:470) or `GENERIC_WRITE` (:478). Five finance write tools declare `/app/finance`; `resolvePage` follows `finance/page.tsx:6 redirect(\"/app/financial-flow\")` and evaluates `financial-flow/page.tsx`, which imports `createReserveBucket`, `deleteReserveBucket` (:14) and `apiPost` (:16, used at :79 to send an invoice reminder). **A savings-bucket button is the evidence that a human can post a journal entry.**\n\n**(b) The write test is a hand-maintained verb list.** `MUTATION_NAME` has 30 prefixes. `select*`, `enroll*`, `unenroll*`, `pause*`, `post*`, `adjust*`, `transfer*`, `reply*`, `push*`, `start*`, `stop*`, `sync*`, `void*`, `close*`, `reopen*`, `depreciate*` are not among them. `GENERIC_WRITE` is `^(apiPost|apiPatch|apiPut|apiDelete)$` — exact, so `apiPostSimple` and `apiPostForm` are invisible. Two consequences, both live:\n  - `/app/goals` writes exclusively through `apiPostSimple` (`goals/page.tsx:17,86,129,147`). It passes only because it also imports `apiDelete`.\n  - The self-test's \"read-only\" fixture `/app/accounting` calls `pushInvoicesToAccounting` and `pushCustomersToAccounting` (`accounting/page.tsx:15-16`). It is *not* read-only. It reads as read-only because `push` is not on the list. Rename that function to `sendInvoicesToAccounting` and `assertParityCheckWorks` throws and takes the whole suite with it.\n\n**(c) `crossDomain` is a substring test.** `:118-123` lowercases the route, strips `/` and `-`, and asks whether the tool's name prefix appears. `finance_post_journal_entry → /app/finance` passes on the word \"finance\". Every route under `/app/finance/*` passes for every `finance_*` tool regardless of what the screen does.\n\n**(d) Next.js routing is not an import.** The comment at `:270-283` is correct: a list page does not import its own detail page, so the 7 `procurement_*` entries in `MANUAL_PARITY_EXEMPTIONS` are a workaround for the instrument, not a real gap. But the exemption is un-audited — see §2.4, where one of the seven is untrue.\n\n**(e) The gate is static; the defect can be dynamic.** Nothing here can see a screen whose button reaches a server handler that drops the field. See §2.3.\n\n### 1.3 The orphan\n\n`git show 4420c4aa` (\"refactor(redundancy): Remove 20+ redundant pages\") replaced 110 lines of `apps/web/src/app/app/finance/page.tsx` with `redirect(\"/app/financial-flow\")`. It left four tab components with **zero importers** — verified by grepping both file names and exported symbols across `apps/web/src`:\n\n```\nbooks-journal-tab.tsx    256   the balanced double-entry composer\nbooks-snapshot-tab.tsx   450   duplicates /app/financial-flow\nbooks-accounts-tab.tsx   110   duplicates /app/finance/settings (createFinanceAccount)\nbooks-cashflow-tab.tsx    89   duplicates /app/finance/cashflow\n```\n\nOnly the first is not a duplicate. Everything it needs still exists: `fetchJournalEntries` / `createJournalEntry` (`client.ts:13163,13171`), `fetchFinanceCoa` (`:13129`), `POST /finance/businesses/:businessId/journal-entries` (`finance.controller.ts:82` → `PostingService.post`). It composes lines, enforces `Math.abs(totalDebit - totalCredit) < 0.001` before enabling **Post Entry** (:69,216), and rejects fewer than two active lines (:80). It works. Nothing renders it.\n\n`/app/finance/journal/page.tsx` is a 5-line `redirect(\"/app/finance/ledger\")`, and `/app/finance/ledger` reaches no write.\n\n---\n\n## 2. The inventory\n\n**Method, so it can be re-run.** (1) Parse the registry for `name / family / riskTier / manualEquivalentRoute` (245 rows, 142 write). (2) Re-implement `canWrite` to *collect* the mutation names it finds instead of returning a boolean, and run it over all 40 declared routes plus every dynamic detail route. (3) For each write tool, read its `case` in `flow-orchestrator.service.ts` to get the service method it calls, then grep for (a) a controller route reaching that method and (b) a caller of the corresponding web api function under `apps/web/src/app`. Three tiers fall out.\n\n### 2.1 Tier A — no HTTP endpoint at all. KEY is the only caller.\n\n| Tool | Family / Tier | Declared route | Evidence |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| `payroll_set_rate` | organize T2 | `/app/payroll` | `PayrollService.setRate` (`payroll.service.ts:30`). Only caller in the repo: `flow-orchestrator.service.ts:4802`. `payroll.controller.ts` exposes `Get rates`, `Get runs`, `Get runs/:runId`, `Post runs/:runId/approve`, `Post runs/:runId/mark-paid`. There is no `Post rates`. |\n| `payroll_generate_run` | execute T3 | `/app/payroll` | `PayrollService.generateRun` (`payroll.service.ts:85`). Only caller: `flow-orchestrator.service.ts:4811`. No route. |\n| `helpdesk_reply_to_ticket` | organize T2 | `/app/helpdesk` | `HelpdeskService.replyToTicket` (`helpdesk.service.ts:117`). `helpdesk.controller.ts` has only `Post tickets`, `Patch tickets/:id`, `Delete tickets/:id`. No route, and no reply control on `/app/helpdesk/page.tsx` (it imports `createSupportTicket`, `updateSupportTicket` only). |\n\n`/app/payroll/page.tsx:143` states the inversion in the product's own words:\n\n> `description=\"Set staff pay rates via the KEY chat tools (payroll_set_rate) to start running payroll.\"`\n\nThe empty state instructs the user to use the AI because there is no other way. That is the rule \"KEY is optional, never mandatory\" failing out loud on a nav-linked screen, while the gate reports parity — because the page imports `approveRun` and `markRunPaid`.\n\n**And `helpdesk_reply_to_ticket` cannot succeed for anyone.** `replyToTicket` loads the ticket from `prisma.client.supportTicket` (`:123`, table `support_tickets`, created in `0_baseline`) and then inserts `supportTicketMessage` with that id (`:128-137`). `SupportTicketMessage.ticketId` is a foreign key to a **different table**: `schema.prisma:12689` `ticket HelpdeskTicket @relation(...)`, and `migrations/20260721044726_support_ticket_messages/migration.sql:22` — `FOREIGN KEY (\"ticket_id\") REFERENCES \"helpdesk_tickets\"(\"id\")`. Two ticket models exist (`SupportTicket` :4467, `HelpdeskTicket` :12657). The insert raises `23503` → Prisma `P2003`. No honesty spec covers `helpdesk/`. (Adjacent and already known: `:140` compares `'resolved' | 'closed'` while schema and UI both use `RESOLVED`/`CLOSED` — `schema.prisma:4471`, `helpdesk/page.tsx:123`.)\n\n### 2.2 Tier B — endpoint exists, zero web callers\n\n| Tool | Family / Tier | Server route (exists) | Web caller |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| `finance_post_journal_entry` | organize **T3** | `finance.controller.ts:82` | `createJournalEntry` `client.ts:13171` — sole importer is the orphaned `books-journal-tab.tsx:17` |\n| `finance_create_bill` | organize T2 | `bills.controller.ts:28` | **none** — no client function exists at all |\n| `finance_pay_bill` | execute **T3** | `bills.controller.ts:66` | **none** |\n| `evidence_submit` | crud T2 | via `lib/api/evidence.ts:44 submitEvidence` | **zero callers** under `apps/web/src/app` |\n| `content_upload_deliverables` | execute **T3** | `content-ops` controller `:150` | `uploadDeliverables` `client.ts:13908` — zero callers |\n| `time_mark_billed` | organize T2 | `time-entry.controller.ts:280` | none. `/app/time-tracking` renders a \"Billed\" badge (`:558`) and offers only a billable toggle (`:546`) |\n| `sequence_unenroll` | organize T2 | `crm` `/sequences/:id/enrollments/:id/unenroll` | `unenrollFromSequence` `client.ts:8652` — zero callers |\n\nGrep note: `grep -rn \"/finance/businesses/.*bills\" apps/web/src` returns nothing. The whole accounts-payable surface — list, create, pay, void (`bills.controller.ts:19,28,66,81`) — has never been called from the app.\n\n### 2.3 Tier C — the manual path is fake\n\n**`/app/expenses` \"mark as paid\" is a no-op that reports success.** `expenses/components/expense-pipeline.tsx:136` calls `updateExpense(businessId, id, { status: \"PAID\", paidAt: ... })` and toasts `\"Bill marked as paid\"`. The server's `PATCH /expenses/businesses/:businessId/expenses/:expenseId` (`expenses.controller.ts:175`) declares an inline `@Body()` with no `status` and no `paidAt`; `ExpensesService.updateExpense` (`expenses.service.ts:502-570`) enumerates 17 fields into `updateData` and neither appears. The request returns 200 with the row unchanged — and it bypasses `markBillPaid` (`:230`), the only path that runs `posting.onBillPaid` (Dr AP / Cr Cash, `:257`).\n\nTwo further facts make it worse: `ExpensePipeline` has **zero importers**, and `expenses/page.tsx:331 _handleMarkPaid` — the same call — is declared and never referenced. So the AP loop is unreachable, and would silently fail if reached.\n\n**`commerce_update_product` (crud T2) declares `/app/store`,** justified in `CROSS_DOMAIN_ROUTES` (`tool-route-audit.ts:234`) as *\"products are edited in the Store catalog\"*. Verified false: `updateProduct` (`client.ts:2008`) is imported only by `app/commerce/hooks/use-products.ts:8` (used `:167,259,284`). `/app/store` reaches `createService`, `updateService`, `updateStorefrontConfig`, `updateQualificationConfig`, `updateBusiness`, `createCampaign` — no product mutation. A written-down reason is only as good as the check behind it; nothing checks these.\n\n### 2.4 Mis-declared, but the manual path genuinely exists elsewhere (repoint, no build)\n\n| Tool | Declared | Real manual path |\n|---|---|---|\n| `finance_create_coa_account` | `/app/finance` | `/app/finance/settings` — `createFinanceCoa`, `settings/page.tsx:327` |\n| `finance_auto_match_bank` | `/app/finance` | `/app/finance/reconciliation` — \"Re-run auto-match\", `reconciliation/page.tsx:203` |\n| `commerce_update_product` | `/app/store` | `/app/commerce` |\n| `sequence_enroll`, `sequence_enroll_overdue` | `/app/crm/sequences` | `crm/pipeline/sequences-section.tsx:152` (`enrollContactsInSequence`) |\n| `procurement_update_request` | `/app/procurement` + exemption | **none** — `updateProcurementRequest` (`client.ts:13561`) has no caller. The other six exemptions are true: `procurement/[requestId]/page.tsx:22-35` imports submit / approve / reject / selectVendor / issuePO / acknowledge / fulfill / invoice. |\n\nAlso verified **sound**, so nobody re-audits them: reconciliation (4 tools, `StatementSourcesPanel` :418 and sweep :441), inventory (adjust / transfer / PO at `inventory/page.tsx:154,166`), content-ops except upload (`[id]/page.tsx:40-43,166,174,194`), key-inbox (`replyToThread`, `key-inbox/page.tsx:29,221`), approvals, calendar, structure, payments, bookings, contracts, crm/*, projects, marketing, commerce.\n\n**Total: 10 write tools with no manual path, 2 whose declared basis is fabricated, 5 mis-declared with a path elsewhere, 1 false exemption — inside a green 142/142.**\n\n---\n\n## 3. The screens\n\nHouse pattern for every screen below: `WorkspaceShell` (`components/ui/workspace-shell.tsx`) or `StandardModuleLayout`, `SectionCard`, `EmptyState`, `sonner` toasts, `getStoredBusinessId()`, an api module under `apps/web/src/lib/api/`. `react-hook-form@^7.77` + `zod@^3.24` + `@hookform/resolvers@^5.4` are installed and used in one file; use them here rather than another `useState` ladder.\n\n### S1 — `/app/finance/books` · General Journal *(the headline)*\n\n**Build.** New `apps/web/src/app/app/finance/books/page.tsx`, ~60 lines, mounting the existing composer:\n\n```tsx\n// apps/web/src/app/app/finance/books/page.tsx\nimport { BooksJournalTab } from \"../components/books-journal-tab\";\n```\n\n- **Delete** `books-snapshot-tab.tsx`, `books-cashflow-tab.tsx`, `books-accounts-tab.tsx` (649 lines). Each duplicates a live screen (`/app/financial-flow`, `/app/finance/cashflow`, `/app/finance/settings`). Re-mounting them would create two chart-of-accounts editors.\n- **Delete** `/app/finance/journal/page.tsx` (the redirect to `ledger`) and let `/app/finance/journal` 404, **or** repoint it to `/app/finance/books`. Prefer the repoint — the word \"journal\" is what an accountant types.\n- **Nav:** add to `lib/nav-config.ts`, money section, immediately after \"General ledger\" (`:178`): `{ label: \"Journal entries\", href: \"/app/finance/books\", icon: BookOpen }`. Required — `nav-reachability.spec.ts`'s prefix rule does not make `/app/finance/books` reachable from `/app/finance/settings`, and an unlisted route needs a typed allowlist entry.\n- **Two additions to the composer** (it is otherwise complete):\n  1. Surface the period lock. `PostingService.post` throws when the `AccountingPeriod` is closed; today the toast shows the raw `res.error`. Render it as \"September is closed — reopen it in Close the month\" with a link to `/app/finance/accounting-periods`.\n  2. Show the `type` selector. The tool accepts `INCOME | EXPENSE | TRANSFER | REFUND | TAX | ADJUSTMENT | OPENING_BALANCE`; the controller hard-codes `type: 'ADJUSTMENT'` (`finance.controller.ts:94`) and `GET journal-entries` filters on `type: 'ADJUSTMENT'` (`:121`). Either widen both, or narrow the tool's enum to `ADJUSTMENT`. **Do the second** — one type, both layers, no new UI. `OPENING_BALANCE` belongs to onboarding, not a composer.\n- **Repoint:** `finance_post_journal_entry.manualEquivalent = { route: '/app/finance/books', api: 'createJournalEntry' }`.\n\n### S2 — `/app/finance/bills` · Accounts payable\n\n**Build.** New screen + `apps/web/src/lib/api/bills.ts`. Server unchanged — `BillsController` already has all four routes.\n\n```ts\n// apps/web/src/lib/api/bills.ts\nexport async function fetchBills(businessId: string)                       // GET  /finance/businesses/:b/bills\nexport async function createBill(businessId: string, body: {...})          // POST /finance/businesses/:b/bills\nexport async function payBill(businessId: string, billId: string, body)    // POST /finance/businesses/:b/bills/:id/pay\nexport async function voidBill(businessId: string, billId: string, body)   // POST /finance/businesses/:b/bills/:id/void\n```\n\n- List grouped by due bucket (the aging buckets `ExpensesService.getPayablesAging` already computes), vendor, amount, due date, status.\n- \"Record a bill\" form → `createBill`. \"Pay\" → `payBill` (which posts Dr AP / Cr Cash and is idempotent by `external_ref`). \"Void\" → `voidBill` (reverses postings).\n- **Fix Tier C at the same time**, otherwise this ships beside a screen that lies: replace `updateExpense({status:'PAID'})` in `expense-pipeline.tsx:136` with `payBill`, delete the dead `_handleMarkPaid` (`expenses/page.tsx:331`), and either render `ExpensePipeline` or delete it. Then add `status`/`paidAt` **rejection** to the expenses PATCH so the mistake cannot recur silently: `if ('status' in body || 'paidAt' in body) throw new BadRequestException('Use POST /finance/businesses/:id/bills/:id/pay')`.\n- **Nav:** `{ label: \"Bills to pay\", href: \"/app/finance/bills\", icon: Receipt }` after \"Expenses\".\n- **Repoint:** `finance_create_bill` → `{ route: '/app/finance/bills', api: 'createBill' }`; `finance_pay_bill` → `{ route: '/app/finance/bills', api: 'payBill' }`.\n\n### S3 — `/app/payroll` · pay rates and run generation\n\n**Build, both layers.** Two routes on the existing `PayrollController`, using the service it already injects:\n\n```ts\n@Post('rates')            @RequireModuleScope('operations','write')  // -> payroll.setRate\n@Post('runs')             @RequireModuleScope('operations','write')  // -> payroll.generateRun\n```\n\nThen `apps/web/src/lib/api/payroll.ts` gains `setPayRate` and `generatePayrollRun`, and the screen gains a \"Set rate\" row-action on the rates table (assignment picker from `structure`'s existing `OrgAssignment` list) and a \"Generate run\" period picker. **Delete `payroll/page.tsx:143`** — the sentence telling users to go to KEY chat.\n\n⚠️ `@RequireModuleScope('operations', ...)` is on every payroll route. Per `CAPABILITY_MAP §1.5`, `identity.service.ts:506` writes a `permissionScopes` map with no `operations` key on every invite, so an invited ADMIN resolves `none` here. Building this screen without Wave-0 #5 gives you a screen the second user of a business cannot open. **Sequence #5 before S3, or S3 lands unreachable.**\n\n### S4 — `/app/helpdesk/[ticketId]` · reply *(the largest, and it needs a data fix first)*\n\nThree defects stacked: no route, no UI, and a foreign key pointing at the wrong table.\n\n1. **Migration** (see §5) — repoint `support_ticket_messages.ticket_id` at `support_tickets`, which is the table the service and the UI actually use.\n2. **Route:** `@Post('businesses/:businessId/tickets/:ticketId/messages')` on `HelpdeskController` → `HelpdeskService.replyToTicket`. Fix the casing at `helpdesk.service.ts:140` while open.\n3. **Screen:** new detail route `/app/helpdesk/[ticketId]/page.tsx` — thread, status control, reply composer with a channel selector defaulting to the ticket's `source` (`schema.prisma:4473`: `MANUAL | EMAIL | PORTAL | WHATSAPP`).\n4. **Delivery.** `replyToTicket` writes `channel: 'internal'` and calls no sender. A reply box that stores a row and shows \"sent\" is the exact defect this spec exists to remove. See §4 for how it is delivered without an import edge, and §6 for what the UI must show when delivery fails.\n\n### S5 — four small ones\n\n| Screen | Change | Repoint |\n|---|---|---|\n| `/app/evidence` | \"Submit evidence\" control (upload + `linkedType`/`linkedId` picker) calling the existing `submitEvidence` | `evidence_submit → { route:'/app/evidence', api:'submitEvidence' }` |\n| `/app/content-ops/[id]` | Upload control calling the existing `uploadDeliverables` | `content_upload_deliverables → { route:'/app/content-ops/[id]', api:'uploadDeliverables' }` |\n| `/app/time-tracking` | Bulk \"Mark billed\" over selected entries + invoice picker → existing `POST .../bill` | `time_mark_billed → { route:'/app/time-tracking', api:'markTimeEntriesBilled' }` |\n| `/app/crm/sequences/[id]` | \"Remove from sequence\" on each row of the existing enrollments list (`[id]/page.tsx:560`) → `unenrollFromSequence` | `sequence_unenroll → { route:'/app/crm/sequences/[id]', api:'unenrollFromSequence' }` |\n\n### S6 — repoints only, zero code\n\n`finance_create_coa_account → /app/finance/settings · createFinanceCoa` · `finance_auto_match_bank → /app/finance/reconciliation · runAutoMatch` · `commerce_update_product → /app/commerce · updateProduct` · `sequence_enroll`, `sequence_enroll_overdue → /app/crm/pipeline · enrollContactsInSequence` · `procurement_update_request` → build the edit control on `[requestId]` (its exemption is untrue) and drop that one entry from the waiver list.\n\n---\n\n## 4. Module boundary\n\n**No new NestJS module.** Adding one here would be the wrong instinct: the audit's own measurement is 110 modules, 76 with no cron, no loop and no listener, and 64 `forwardRef(() => …)` deferrals in `*.module.ts` (re-derived today: `grep -rn \"forwardRef(() =>\" --include=*.module.ts apps/server/src | wc -l`). This item is 95% a web-app gap over server capability that already exists.\n\n**Where each server change lands, and why it adds no edge:**\n\n| Change | Module | New import in the module file? |\n|---|---|---|\n| `POST payroll/…/rates`, `POST payroll/…/runs` | `payroll` | **No.** `PayrollController` already injects `PayrollService`; `payroll.module.ts` is untouched. |\n| `POST helpdesk/…/tickets/:id/messages` | `helpdesk` | **No.** Same controller, same service. |\n| Reject `status`/`paidAt` in the expenses PATCH | `expenses` | **No.** |\n| Bills, journal entries | none | **No server change.** `BillsController` and `FinanceController` already expose them. |\n\n**The one cross-module need: delivering a helpdesk reply.** Injecting a comms/outbound service into `HelpdeskModule` is precisely how the 64 deferrals were earned. Instead:\n\n- `HelpdeskService` writes the `SupportTicketMessage` with `deliveryStatus: 'PENDING'` and emits `helpdesk.reply.requested { businessId, ticketId, messageId, channel, contactId, body }` on `EventEmitter2`.\n- `EventBusModule` is `@Global()` (`core/event-bus/event-bus.module.ts:4`), so `EventEmitter2` is injectable with **no module import**. Emitting adds no node and no edge to the module graph.\n- The existing outbound module owns the `@OnEvent('helpdesk.reply.requested')` listener and enqueues the send. Helpdesk depends on a **string**, not a class.\n- The listener writes back via a single declared contract — `HelpdeskService.recordDelivery(messageId, { status, providerMessageId, error })` — reached through the existing `helpdesk.deliveryRecorded` event or a one-way `HelpdeskModule` export. Either direction is one-way; there is no return import.\n\n**Do not copy `flow-orchestrator`'s `moduleRef.get(PayrollService, { strict: false })` pattern** (`flow-orchestrator.service.ts:468-494, 775`). It resolves 40+ services out of the global container at runtime. It is why the orchestrator has no visible edges — the edges exist, they are simply invisible to the module graph and to every tool that reads it. New code declares its dependencies.\n\n**Emits:** `helpdesk.reply.requested`, `helpdesk.reply.delivered`, `helpdesk.reply.failed`, `bill.paid` (already emitted, `expenses.service.ts:272`).\n**Listens to:** nothing new.\n\n---\n\n## 5. Data\n\n### 5.1 No new Prisma model\n\n439 models exist. Every screen above writes to a model that already does: `FinancialTransaction` + `LedgerEntry` (journal), `Expense` with `status='BILL'` (bills — `dueDate`, `paidAt`, `vendor`, `contactId` all present, `schema.prisma:4808-4810`), `PayRate` (`:2348`) and `PayrollRun` (`:2368`), `Evidence`, `TimeEntry`, `SequenceEnrollment`, `SupportTicket` + `SupportTicketMessage`. Adding a 440th model to this item would be exactly the \"breadth is not the constraint\" failure the audit names.\n\n### 5.2 One migration, two changes\n\n`packages/db/prisma/migrations/20260810120000_helpdesk_message_fk_and_delivery/migration.sql`\n\n**(a) The wrong foreign key.** `support_ticket_messages.ticket_id` references `helpdesk_tickets(id)` while every writer and reader uses `support_tickets`. Repoint it. Both tables exist; `helpdesk_tickets` has no rows written by any code path I found, so the data move is `DELETE`-free, but the migration must still be written defensively:\n\n```sql\nALTER TABLE \"support_ticket_messages\" DROP CONSTRAINT \"support_ticket_messages_ticket_id_fkey\";\n-- Orphans first: any row whose ticket_id names neither table cannot be re-parented.\nDELETE FROM \"support_ticket_messages\" m\n WHERE NOT EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM \"support_tickets\" t WHERE t.id = m.ticket_id);\nALTER TABLE \"support_ticket_messages\"\n  ADD CONSTRAINT \"support_ticket_messages_ticket_id_fkey\"\n  FOREIGN KEY (\"ticket_id\") REFERENCES \"support_tickets\"(\"id\")\n  ON DELETE CASCADE ON UPDATE CASCADE;\n```\n\nSchema side: `SupportTicketMessage.ticket` becomes `SupportTicket @relation(...)`, and `SupportTicket` gains `messages SupportTicketMessage[]`. `HelpdeskTicket` loses its `messages` back-relation. (Whether `HelpdeskTicket` should exist at all is a separate decision — §11.)\n\n**(b) Delivery state on the message.** A reply box that cannot report a failed send reproduces the defect class this spec exists to remove.\n\n```prisma\nmodel SupportTicketMessage {\n  // … existing fields …\n  deliveryStatus    String    @default(\"INTERNAL\") @map(\"delivery_status\") // INTERNAL, PENDING, SENT, FAILED\n  deliveredAt       DateTime? @map(\"delivered_at\")\n  providerMessageId String?   @map(\"provider_message_id\")\n  deliveryError     String?   @map(\"delivery_error\") @db.Text\n\n  @@index([businessId, deliveryStatus])   // new — the retry sweep's only query\n  @@index([ticketId, createdAt])          // existing\n  @@index([businessId])                   // existing\n}\n```\n\nHouse style honoured: `snake_case @map`, compound `@@index([businessId, …])`, `businessId` with a cascading `Business` relation (present at `:12681`… **and this is a defect** — `SupportTicketMessage.businessId` is a bare `String` with **no `business Business @relation`**. Add it with `onDelete: Cascade`, same as `SupportTicket:4476`).\n\n### 5.3 `BUSINESS_ID_MODELS`\n\nThree models carry `business_id` and are **absent** from `BUSINESS_ID_MODELS` in `packages/db/src/client.ts:81-155`: `SupportTicket`, `SupportTicketMessage`, `HelpdeskTicket`. They are therefore unscoped by the isolation extension — `tenantOperationAllowed` (`:196`) is a `Set.has` on the model name, and a name that is not in the set never matches.\n\n**Nothing catches this.** `apps/server/src/core/prisma/tenant-model-list.spec.ts` asserts (i) every *listed* name is a real model and (ii) every listed model has a `businessId` column. It never asserts the converse — a model *with* `businessId` that is not listed. Verified by reading all four `it()` blocks (`:52-92`). A new model is unscoped by default and the suite stays green.\n\n**This work adds `'SupportTicket', 'SupportTicketMessage', 'HelpdeskTicket'` to `BUSINESS_ID_MODELS`,** with the same guard the file's existing comments demand: the set only hooks `findMany/findFirst/findUnique/update/updateMany/delete/deleteMany/count` on the HTTP path, so `HelpdeskService.replyToTicket`'s explicit `where: { id, businessId, deletedAt: null }` (`:124`) stays — the extension is the second layer, never the first.\n\n**And add the missing direction to the spec** (§7, G6): every model whose block contains `businessId String` must be either listed or on a written exemption list. That is the ratchet that makes the next model's omission a build failure instead of a discovery.\n\n---\n\n## 6. The three layers, for each thing this spec creates\n\n| Capability | MANUAL | SMART (deterministic, no LLM) | AI tool |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| Post a journal entry | `/app/finance/books` — the balanced composer | **Reuse.** `RecurringJournalEntryService` + `/app/finance/recurring-journals` already schedules repeating journals. Nothing to build. | `finance_post_journal_entry` · organize · **T3** · `manualEquivalent: { route: '/app/finance/books', api: 'createJournalEntry' }` |\n| Record a bill | `/app/finance/bills` | **Reuse.** `RecurringExpense.createsBill` already emits `status:'BILL'` (`recurring-expense.service.ts:248`); `BankRuleService` codes bank lines. | `finance_create_bill` · organize · T2 · `{ '/app/finance/bills', 'createBill' }` |\n| Pay a bill | `/app/finance/bills` \"Pay\" | none (a payment is not automated at this tier) | `finance_pay_bill` · execute · **T3** · `{ '/app/finance/bills', 'payBill' }` |\n| Set a pay rate | `/app/payroll` \"Set rate\" | none | `payroll_set_rate` · organize · T2 · `{ '/app/payroll', 'setPayRate' }` |\n| Generate a payroll run | `/app/payroll` \"Generate run\" | **New, small.** A BullMQ **repeatable** job, not `setInterval`: queue `payroll`, `jobId: payroll:generate:${businessId}:${periodEnd}`, `repeat: { pattern, tz }` — exactly the shape already used at `ai/queue.service.ts:113-131`. Idempotent by job key, so N replicas fire it once. | `payroll_generate_run` · execute · **T3** · `{ '/app/payroll', 'generatePayrollRun' }` |\n| Reply to a ticket | `/app/helpdesk/[ticketId]` composer | **New, small.** Auto-acknowledge on `supportTicket.created` — a BullMQ **delayed** job, `jobId: helpdesk:ack:${ticketId}`, cancelled if a human replies first. | `helpdesk_reply_to_ticket` · organize · T2 · `{ '/app/helpdesk/[ticketId]', 'replyToSupportTicket' }` |\n\n**Every timer and retry in this spec is BullMQ with a job key.** The send path is `queue.add('helpdesk-reply-send', payload, { jobId: 'helpdesk:send:' + messageId, attempts: 5, backoff: { type:'exponential', delay: 2000 } })`. No `setInterval`, no `setTimeout`, no state on a module field — delivery state lives in the four Postgres columns from §5.2 and the queue lives in Redis. `bullmq@^5.76.8` is already a dependency (`apps/server/package.json:42`) and Redis is a first-class service in both compose files and in CI (`ci-cd.yml:376`).\n\n**Every query is tenant-scoped by `businessId` explicitly**, in the `where`, at the service — not by the extension. See §5.3 for why the extension is the second layer.\n\n---\n\n## 7. The strengthened parity gate\n\nThe rule changes from *\"the declared page can write something\"* to **\"the declared page performs this tool's write, through the same server handler.\"** Six changes, in the order they should land.\n\n### G1 — declare the function, not just the page\n\n```ts\n// flow-tool-registry.ts\nexport interface ManualEquivalent {\n  /** Route a user can be sent to. May be dynamic: '/app/helpdesk/[ticketId]'. */\n  route: string;\n  /** The exported web api function that screen calls to do THIS tool's job. */\n  api: string;\n}\nexport interface FlowTool { /* … */ manualEquivalent: ManualEquivalent }\n```\n\nKeep `manualEquivalentRoute` as a derived getter so `getManualEquivalentRoute()` and its callers are unchanged. Migration is 245 mechanical edits; ~200 are obvious from §2's method, and the ~15 that are not are the finding.\n\n`canWrite(page)` → `reaches(page, apiName)`: same depth-3 walk, same refusal to recurse into `lib/`, but the named export must appear in an api-layer import. On failure the message prints the names it *did* find — which is how a reviewer sees \"your journal tool's evidence is `createReserveBucket`\" in one line.\n\n**This alone kills defects (a) and (c) from §1.2, and would have failed on day one for all 10 Tier-A/B tools.**\n\n### G2 — the named function must actually write\n\nResolve `apiName` in `apps/web/src/lib/**`, read its body, and require a call to `apiPost | apiPatch | apiPut | apiDelete` in any of their `*Simple` / `*Form` variants. Detected by content, not by name.\n\nRetires `MUTATION_NAME`'s 30-verb list and `GENERIC_WRITE`'s exact-match, and with them defect (b): no more `select*`/`enroll*`/`reply*`/`push*` blind spots, no more `apiPostSimple` invisibility, and the self-test stops depending on `push` being an unrecognised verb.\n\n### G3 — the tool's handler and the manual endpoint must meet\n\nThe check that catches payroll, helpdesk and Tier C — none of which a web import graph can see. New spec, server side: `apps/server/src/modules/ai/tool-manual-parity.spec.ts`.\n\n**G3a (ship first, ~120 lines).** For every write tool, parse its `case '<name>':` block in `flow-orchestrator.service.ts` for `this.getX().method(`. Assert that same `method` is also called from at least one controller method decorated `@Post|@Patch|@Put|@Delete` anywhere under `apps/server/src`. Day-one failures: `setRate`, `generateRun`, `replyToTicket`. *\"KEY can call a service method no HTTP client can reach\"* becomes a build error.\n\n**G3b (later).** The full pairing: map the api function's path template → controller prefix + route decorator → controller method → the service method *it* calls, and assert it is the same `Class.method` the tool handler calls. This is what proves `/app/expenses`'s \"mark paid\" is not `finance_pay_bill`'s manual twin — different handler, different effect. Non-trivial (~300 lines of route-template matching); do it once G3a's failures are cleared and the shape is understood.\n\n### G4 — waivers are typed, owned and dated\n\n```ts\ninterface ParityWaiver {\n  tool: string;\n  kind: 'chat-native' | 'building' | 'detail-route';\n  reason: string;\n  owner: string;\n  until: string;   // ISO date. Past today → the spec fails.\n}\n```\n\n`MANUAL_PARITY_EXEMPTIONS` currently holds seven procurement entries with a shared prose reason and no expiry. Six are true and become unnecessary once G1 accepts a dynamic `route` and `resolvePage` resolves `[requestId]` (it already handles dynamic segments, `:427-431`); the seventh (`procurement_update_request`) is untrue and becomes a dated `kind:'building'` waiver. Same for `CROSS_DOMAIN_ROUTES`, whose 30 entries include at least one factually wrong reason (`commerce_update_product`) that nothing checks — with G1 declaring the function, the whole `crossDomain` check and its exemption table can be **deleted**: naming the function makes the domain question moot.\n\nThis mirrors `nav-reachability.spec.ts`'s \"THE ALLOWLIST IS THE BACKLOG\" (`:41-58`), which took its `todo-connect` count 47 → 39 → 33 → 7.\n\n### G5 — negative controls that test the rule, not two live screens\n\n`assertParityCheckWorks` (:308) uses `/app/accounting` and `/app/contracts` as fixtures. Both are live, nav-linked screens; `/app/accounting` is \"read-only\" only by the accident described in §1.2(b), and `next.config.ts:187` redirects it to `/app/money/books`, a one-line re-export. The control is real, and it is balanced on two moving parts.\n\nReplace with synthetic fixtures under `apps/web/src/lib/__fixtures__/parity/` (excluded from the route scan), one per rule, each asserted in both directions:\n\n| Fixture | Expected |\n|---|---|\n| page importing nothing | fail |\n| page importing a **read** api fn (`fetchBills`) | fail — G2 |\n| page importing a **different write** fn from the same module (`createReserveBucket` for a journal tool) | **fail** — G1, the exact live defect |\n| page importing the right fn directly | pass |\n| page → component → hook → the right fn (depth 3) | pass |\n| page → `lib/client` re-export chain only | fail — the recursion stop that made an earlier gate unfailable |\n| tool handler calling a service method no controller calls | fail — G3a |\n\nKeep the existing reverse control too: point a real write tool at a real read-only screen and assert it fails. A gate that has never failed has not been shown to be able to.\n\n### G6 — the two ratchets\n\n1. **Parity debt is a number that only goes down.** `expect(waivers.length).toBeLessThanOrEqual(N)` with `N` decremented as screens land, and `expect(expired(waivers)).toEqual([])`.\n2. **Tenant coverage gets its missing direction.** In `tenant-model-list.spec.ts`, add: every `model X { … businessId String … }` in `schema.prisma` is either in `BUSINESS_ID_MODELS` or on a written exemption list with a reason (the `Payment` / `MarketplaceOrder` comment at `client.ts:135-155` is already that reason, written in prose — turn it into data). This is the check whose absence let `SupportTicket`, `SupportTicketMessage` and `HelpdeskTicket` go unscoped.\n\n### G7 — surface it to the user\n\n`manualEquivalentRoute` appears in three files under `apps/web/src`, all of them the gate itself (`tool-route-audit.ts`, `tool-routes.spec.ts`, `no-fabricated-screens.spec.ts`). No user has ever seen it. Render it on the tool-result card in `components/key/chat/key-plan-card.tsx` as **\"Do it yourself →\"**. With G1's `api` field, the card can also render the honest alternative: when a tool is waived, it says *\"KEY-only — no manual screen yet\"* instead of linking to a page that cannot do the thing. Cheap, and it converts the invariant into something a user can check.\n\n---\n\n## 8. Tests\n\n| # | Test | Where | Negative control — how I know it can fail |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| T1 | Journal composer posts a balanced entry and refuses an unbalanced one | `apps/web` component test | Feed debits ≠ credits; assert **Post Entry** stays disabled and no request is made. Then feed a balanced pair and assert one POST — a test that only ever sees the disabled case would pass against a button that is always disabled. |\n| T2 | `POST /finance/businesses/:b/journal-entries` writes `FinancialTransaction` + balanced `LedgerEntry` rows | `apps/server/test/*.e2e.test.ts`, **real Postgres** (14 suites already run this way in CI) | Post the same entry twice with the same `reference`; assert the second raises `ConflictException` and the ledger row count is unchanged. Then assert that a *deliberately* unbalanced payload is rejected — with a mock, both pass. |\n| T3 | Paying a bill posts Dr AP / Cr Cash | server e2e, real DB | Assert the ledger delta **before** asserting the 200. Reverse control: call the old `PATCH …/expenses/:id { status:'PAID' }` and assert it now **400s** — this is the test that would have caught §2.3 and it must be spelled as a defect, not a shape. |\n| T4 | Helpdesk reply inserts a message and links to the ticket | server e2e, real DB | Run it **against the pre-migration FK** and assert `P2003`. A test written after the migration proves nothing about what it fixed. |\n| T5 | Reply delivery: `deliveryStatus` transitions PENDING → SENT, and → FAILED on a rejecting provider | server e2e, stubbed transport, real DB | Stub the transport to reject; assert `FAILED` + `deliveryError` and that **no** message reports `SENT`. A stub that always resolves cannot disagree with the code. |\n| T6 | `payroll_set_rate` and its HTTP route hit the same `PayrollService.setRate` | G3a spec | Temporarily delete the new `@Post('rates')` and assert the spec fails. |\n| T7 | Parity gate rules | `tool-routes.spec.ts` + `__fixtures__/parity/` | The seven-row table in §7 G5, each asserted in both directions. |\n| T8 | Every `businessId`-bearing model is scoped or exempt | `tenant-model-list.spec.ts` | Add a throwaway model with `businessId` to a schema fixture and assert the spec fails. |\n\nTwo rules from this repo's own history apply to all eight: **assertions check referents, not shapes** — assert the ledger row, the `deliveryStatus`, the specific tool name in the failure list, never \"an array of length 1\"; and **test one layer down** — real Postgres, stubbed transport. Four fixtures in this repo have already been found asserting shapes the client cannot produce.\n\n---\n\n## 9. Effort\n\n| Item | Days |\n|---|---:|\n| S1 journal screen — mount, delete 3 orphans, nav, period-lock message, type decision | **1.5** |\n| S2 bills screen + `lib/api/bills.ts` + expenses mark-paid repair + PATCH rejection | **3** |\n| S3 payroll — 2 routes, 2 api fns, 2 controls *(after Wave-0 #5)* | **2** |\n| S4 helpdesk — migration, route, detail screen, event + BullMQ delivery, casing fix | **5** |\n| S5 four small controls | **2.5** |\n| S6 repoints + waiver cleanup | **0.5** |\n| G1+G2 gate: `manualEquivalent`, 245 registry edits, `reaches()`, delete `crossDomain` | **3** |\n| G3a handler↔endpoint spec | **1.5** |\n| G4 typed waivers, G5 synthetic fixtures, G6 ratchets | **2** |\n| G7 \"Do it yourself\" card | **1** |\n| Tests T1–T8 | **3** |\n| **Total** | **~25 days** |\n\nG3b (full route pairing) is a further **3–4 days** and should be scheduled only after G3a's failures are cleared.\n\n## 10. Adopt, not build\n\n- **`react-hook-form@^7.77` + `zod@^3.24` + `@hookform/resolvers@^5.4`** — MIT, already in `apps/web/package.json:19,45,54`, used in one file against thousands of `useState` calls. The bill form and the pay-rate form are the right places to make it the house pattern. Zero new dependency.\n- **`bullmq@^5.76.8`** — MIT, already in `apps/server/package.json:42`, already doing repeatable jobs with `tz` and idempotent `jobId`s at `ai/queue.service.ts:113-131`. Copy that call site; do not write a scheduler.\n- **`sonner`, `EmptyState`, `WorkspaceShell`, `SectionCard`, `StatusBadge`** — the existing shells. The orphaned composer already uses them, which is why S1 is 1.5 days.\n- **Do not adopt a table library** for the bills list. `@tanstack/react-table` is not installed and one AP list does not justify it; revisit when three screens want it.\n- **Delivery transport for S4** — reuse whatever the outbound module already speaks (Resend / WhatsApp). Do not add a provider for this.\n\n## 11. Deliberately not doing\n\n- **No new Prisma model, and no new NestJS module.** 439 models and 110 modules; the capability this item needs already exists on the server for 7 of the 10 Tier-A/B tools. The scarce thing is doors, not tables.\n- **Not resurrecting the other three books tabs.** 649 lines that duplicate `/app/financial-flow`, `/app/finance/settings` and `/app/finance/cashflow`. Two chart-of-accounts editors is worse than one.\n- **Not merging `SupportTicket` and `HelpdeskTicket`.** Two ticket models is a real defect and it is not this item's. Repointing one foreign key unblocks the reply; the consolidation — and `CAPABILITY_MAP §3.4`'s stronger recommendation to retire `modules/helpdesk` behind the already-running Chatwoot — is a separate decision with a migration behind it.\n- **Not building T&T statutory payroll.** S3 gives a human the two buttons KEY already has (`setRate`, `generateRun`) over the gross-pay engine that exists. PAYE / NIS / Health Surcharge stay out, per `CAPABILITY_MAP §3.4`.\n- **Not touching the SMART layer's 19 unimplemented actions.** Different gate, different item (Wave-0 #3). This spec asserts nothing about triggers or actions.\n- **Not shipping G3b in this pass.** G3a catches the three tools that have no endpoint at all; the full route pairing is a second instrument for a smaller class and should follow evidence, not precede it.\n- **Not widening the journal `type` enum.** Narrow the tool to `ADJUSTMENT` instead — one type, both layers, and the controller and its list query (`finance.controller.ts:94,121`) already assume it.\n\n---\n\n### The three sentences that say this landed\n\n1. `check-tool-routes` names the api function for all 142 write tools, and the count of dated waivers is smaller than it was last month.\n2. A human posts a journal entry, records a bill and pays it, and each one moves the ledger — asserted against a real Postgres, with the pre-fix path asserted to fail.\n3. Nothing in `apps/web` tells a user to go and ask KEY."
      },
      {
        "key": "04-outbound",
        "title": "Let roles actually send",
        "spec": "# Implementation Spec — Let roles actually send\n\n**Basis:** `C:/kfm` @ `main`. Everything below was read, not recalled; each claim names the file and line that establishes it. Companion to `docs/CAPABILITY_MAP_2026-08-09.md` §3.2 item 20 and §3.5 item 3.\n\n**One sentence:** a new leaf module `outbound` owns the answer to *\"may this actor deliver this message, on this channel, to this person, right now\"* and the durable record of that answer; delivery stays where it already is — `KeyInboxReplySenderService` — reached through a transport registered *into* outbound, so no module edge ever points out of it.\n\n---\n\n## 1. What exists today\n\n### 1.1 The gap, re-derived\n\nThe audit's claim is that no role can deliver a message inside its own risk ceiling. I re-derived it from three files rather than quoting it.\n\n| Outbound tool | Tier | Declared at |\n|---|---:|---|\n| `send_message_with_approval` | 3 | `apps/server/src/modules/ai/flow-tool-registry.ts:541` |\n| `comms_send_broadcast` | 3 | `flow-tool-registry.ts:3492` |\n| `inbox_reply_thread` | 3 | `flow-tool-registry.ts:3512` |\n| `commerce_send_invoice` | 3 | `flow-tool-registry.ts:4208` |\n| `marketing_send_campaign` | 4 | `flow-tool-registry.ts:1915` |\n| `social_publish_post` | 4 | `flow-tool-registry.ts:1968` |\n| `helpdesk_reply_to_ticket` | 2 | `flow-tool-registry.ts:3439` |\n\nRole ceilings, `apps/server/src/modules/ai/role-engine.service.ts`: sales `:192` = 2, finance `:216` = 2, support `:237` = 2, operations `:260` = 2, marketing `:284` = 2, general `:323` = 1, operator `:347` = 3, executive `:371` = 1.\n\nThe clamp is `ai-oversight.service.ts:75` — `maxAutoTier: Math.min(settings.maxAutoTier, def.maxRiskTier)` — and the auto-execute test is `:189`, `tier <= settings.maxAutoTier`. So every T3 outbound tool falls to `:198` `requiresFormalApproval` for all seven non-operator roles. `operator` at 3 clears `commerce_send_invoice`; that is the whole of the product's unapproved outbound surface.\n\nThe one apparent exception, `helpdesk_reply_to_ticket` at T2, is worse than the audit recorded. `HelpdeskService.replyToTicket` (`apps/server/src/modules/helpdesk/helpdesk.service.ts:117-147`) writes a `SupportTicketMessage` with `channel: opts?.channel ?? 'internal'` and calls no sender — but it also reads `supportTicket` at `:123` (table `support_tickets`) while `SupportTicketMessage.ticketId` is a foreign key to `helpdesk_tickets`:\n\n- `packages/db/prisma/schema.prisma:12685` — `ticket HelpdeskTicket @relation(fields: [ticketId], …)`\n- `packages/db/prisma/migrations/20260721044726_support_ticket_messages/migration.sql:22` — `FOREIGN KEY (\"ticket_id\") REFERENCES \"helpdesk_tickets\"(\"id\")`\n\n`grep -rn \"helpdeskTicket\" apps/server/src` returns two hits, both reads (`key-cortex-evolution.service.ts:2146`). **Nothing in the server writes `helpdesk_tickets`.** So the insert at `helpdesk.service.ts:128` takes a P2003 against any database that has that constraint. The one tool that looked like it could reply cannot even write its internal row.\n\n### 1.2 The approval queue does not send either\n\n`flow-orchestrator.service.ts:1522-1532` creates an `AiApprovalItem` with `inputPayload` and **no `planStepId`**. `ai-oversight.service.ts:612` only re-arms execution when `item.planStepId` is set. Resolving from `/app/approvals` goes to `ai.controller.ts:551-596`, which migrates the item to a `KeyActionProposal` with `actionType: 'EXECUTE_TOOL'` and calls `approve({ …, autoExecute: false })` at `:578`.\n\nThere *is* a real executor — `KeyCortexActionExecutorPlugin.canExecute` returns true for `EXECUTE_TOOL` (`key-cortex-action-executor.plugin.ts:33`) and routes bare snake_case names to `orchestrator.executeToolDirectly` at `:91`. It is only reached from `executeApproved` (`key-cortex-approval-orchestrator.service.ts:201`), whose only HTTP door is `POST …/proposals/:proposalId/execute` (`key-action-proposal.controller.ts:110`), whose only web caller is `apps/web/src/app/app/key-autonomy/components/proposal-card.tsx:42`.\n\n**So today: KEY drafts, the item lands in `/app/approvals`, the owner presses Approve, and nothing is sent.** Delivery requires a second act on a different screen against a different model. That is the single strongest argument for the design below: the approval must be attached to *the send*, not to a tool call that has already returned.\n\n### 1.3 What is already built and must be reused\n\n| Asset | Where | What it gives us |\n|---|---|---|\n| **Reply sender** | `apps/server/src/modules/key-inbox/key-inbox-reply-sender.service.ts` (304 lines) | Real per-channel delivery: WhatsApp/SMS `:69`, Gmail with `In-Reply-To` threading `:132`, Meta honestly `NOT_SUPPORTED` `:208`. Persists `sendStatus/providerMessageId/sendError/sentVia` `:256`. Emits `key_inbox.reply_sent` / `reply_failed` `:273`. |\n| **Draft-then-send on a thread** | `key-inbox.service.ts:327-369` — `addReply(…, { mode: 'draft' \\| 'send' })` | A draft is a real `KeyInboxMessage` with `sendStatus: 'DRAFT'`; `mode: 'send'` calls the sender on it. Exactly the two-step this spec needs. |\n| **Manual screen** | `apps/web/src/app/app/key-inbox/page.tsx` (864 lines) — `handleReply` `:221`, Send button `:663`, per-message status badges `:422-441`, retry `:243` | `/app/key-inbox` is in the nav (`apps/web/src/lib/nav-config.ts:120`, `:405`). The manual layer for \"reply to a customer\" already works. |\n| **Do-not-contact enforcement** | `notifications/transactional-email.service.ts:325-344` | The pattern: refuse in the pathway, not in a checklist. |\n| **Consent records** | `communications/consent.service.ts:52` + `ConsentRecord` (`schema.prisma:11155`) | Per-contact, per-channel, per-type grant/revoke. |\n| **BullMQ** | `apps/server/package.json:42` `bullmq@^5.76.8`; live at `ai/queue.service.ts:56` and `business-events/business-event.queue.ts:37` | Durable jobs, `jobId` idempotency, exponential backoff, `delay:`. Already the house pattern. |\n| **Optimistic claim** | `communications/delivery-queue.service.ts:88-112` | Compare-and-swap `updateMany({ where: { id, status } })`. Prior art for a replica-safe claim. |\n| **Approvals screen** | `apps/web/src/app/app/approvals/components/ai-approvals-panel.tsx` (`resolveAiApproval` `:129`), nav at `nav-config.ts:236` | The manual surface for \"release this send\". |\n| **Global infrastructure** | `PrismaModule` (`core/prisma/prisma.module.ts:3` `@Global`), `RedisModule` (`core/redis/redis.module.ts` `@Global`), `BusinessEventModule` (`business-events/business-event.module.ts:8` `@Global`) | A new module can reach all three with `imports: []`. This is what makes a true leaf possible. |\n\n### 1.4 Two live hazards this spec must not inherit\n\n- **Neither sender KEY uses checks `doNotContact`.** I read both files end to end: `ai-message-sender.service.ts` (232 lines) and `key-inbox-reply-sender.service.ts` (304 lines) contain no occurrence. The guard exists only on the transactional-email path.\n- **The SMART layer fabricates the send.** `flow/flow-action.registry.ts:160` returns `{ sent: true, channel, body, direct: true }` and calls nothing. `:143` writes a `ResponseDraft` when approval is required, and `ResponseDraftService.sendDraft` (`communications/response-draft.service.ts:120`) does send — but through `AiMessageSenderService` by `contactId`, so a WhatsApp thread reply goes to `contact.whatsappNumber` and never returns to the thread. The omnichannel drafts API (`communications/omnichannel.controller.ts:103-166`) has **zero web callers** (`grep -rn omnichannel apps/web/src/lib` → nothing).\n\n---\n\n## 2. Module boundary\n\n### 2.1 `outbound` — `apps/server/src/modules/outbound/`\n\n**Owns:** the send *decision* and the send *record*. Nothing else.\n\n- who may send (policy), on which channel, for which purpose, at which tier;\n- the guards — do-not-contact, consent, existing-thread, quiet hours, per-actor daily cap, per-contact cooldown, idempotency;\n- the durable queue that carries an allowed send to delivery, and the retry/expiry attached to it;\n- one row per send *attempt* with the verdict and its reason, in words a human can read on a screen.\n\n**Does not own:** transports, message bodies, threads, tickets, contacts, invoices, approval records. It stores a SHA-256 of the body and a 200-character preview for the audit screen; the body itself lives in the `KeyInboxMessage` the caller already wrote.\n\n### 2.2 Public interface\n\n`apps/server/src/modules/outbound/outbound.contract.ts` — the only file other modules import types from.\n\n```ts\nexport type OutboundChannel = 'email' | 'whatsapp' | 'sms';\nexport type OutboundPurpose = 'reply' | 'reminder' | 'confirmation';\n\n/** actorKey is an opaque string. outbound never imports BusinessRole from ai. */\nexport type OutboundActor =\n  | { kind: 'department_role'; actorKey: string; userId?: string }\n  | { kind: 'job_role';        actorKey: string; assignmentId?: string }\n  | { kind: 'human';           actorKey: string; userId: string }\n  | { kind: 'rule';            actorKey: string; automationId: string };\n\nexport type OutboundTarget =\n  | { kind: 'inbox_thread'; threadId: string; messageId: string }\n  | { kind: 'ticket';       ticketId: string }\n  | { kind: 'invoice';      invoiceId: string };\n\nexport interface OutboundRequest {\n  businessId: string;\n  actor: OutboundActor;\n  target: OutboundTarget;\n  purpose: OutboundPurpose;\n  body: string;\n  subject?: string;\n  toolName?: string;          // for the tier lookup and the audit line\n  idempotencyKey: string;     // caller-supplied; unique per business\n}\n\nexport type SendVerdict = 'ALLOWED' | 'NEEDS_APPROVAL' | 'DENIED';\n\nexport interface SendDecision {\n  sendId: string;\n  verdict: SendVerdict;\n  reason: string;             // machine code + human sentence, e.g. \"do_not_contact: …\"\n  policyId: string | null;\n  queued: boolean;            // true only when the job was actually accepted\n}\n\n/** Implemented by the module that owns the target, registered at bootstrap. */\nexport interface OutboundTransport {\n  readonly targetKind: OutboundTarget['kind'];\n  readonly name: string;                     // recorded on the send row\n  describe(businessId: string, target: OutboundTarget): Promise<TargetFacts | null>;\n  deliver(businessId: string, target: OutboundTarget, msg: { body: string; subject?: string; actorUserId?: string }): Promise<DeliveryResult>;\n}\n\nexport interface TargetFacts {\n  contactId: string | null;\n  channel: OutboundChannel;\n  doNotContact: boolean;\n  lastInboundAt: Date | null;\n  outboundSinceLastInbound: number;\n}\n\nexport interface DeliveryResult {\n  delivered: boolean;\n  providerMessageId?: string;\n  error?: string;\n}\n\n/** Implemented by communications, registered at bootstrap. Absent ⇒ fail closed. */\nexport interface ConsentPort {\n  isRevoked(businessId: string, contactId: string, channel: OutboundChannel): Promise<boolean>;\n}\n```\n\n**Exported providers:**\n\n| Provider | Method | Contract |\n|---|---|---|\n| `OutboundSendService` | `request(req): Promise<SendDecision>` | Decides, persists, enqueues if allowed. Idempotent on `(businessId, idempotencyKey)`. |\n| | `release(businessId, sendId, releasedBy)` | Enqueues a `NEEDS_APPROVAL` send after a human said yes. |\n| | `cancel(businessId, sendId, reason)` | |\n| | `get / list(businessId, filters)` | For the audit screen. |\n| `SendAuthorityService` | `decide(req, facts): SendDecision` (no I/O beyond policy + counters) | Testable in isolation. |\n| `OutboundPolicyService` | `list / upsert / seedDefaults(businessId)` | |\n| `OutboundPortRegistry` | `registerTransport(t) / registerConsent(c)` | Called from the owning module's `onModuleInit`. |\n\n**Emits** (through the global `BusinessEventService`, subject `OutboundSend`):\n`outbound.requested`, `outbound.blocked`, `outbound.approval_required`, `outbound.sent`, `outbound.failed`.\n\n**Listens to** exactly one event: `ai.approval.resolved`. `@OnEvent` needs no import — `EventEmitter2` is injected from the Nest core. The emit is a two-line addition inside `AiOversightService.finalizeResolution` (`ai-oversight.service.ts:600`, after the status update) and inside `KeyCortexApprovalOrchestratorService.approve` (`:133`); neither adds an import anywhere.\n\n### 2.3 Why this creates no import cycle\n\n`outbound.module.ts` is:\n\n```ts\n@Module({ imports: [], providers: [...], exports: [...] })\nexport class OutboundModule {}\n```\n\n`imports: []` is literal and load-bearing. Prisma, Redis and BusinessEvents are `@Global`; nothing else is needed. **A module with no outgoing edge cannot be on a cycle**, because a cycle requires a path leaving it and coming back. Every edge this spec adds points *into* `outbound`: `key-inbox → outbound`, `helpdesk → outbound`, `commerce → outbound`, `communications → outbound`, `ai → outbound`, `flow → outbound`.
