None of those is reversed anywhere.\n\nThree specific temptations, and how each is refused:\n\n1. **\"outbound needs to read the thread.\"** It does not. `OutboundTransport.describe()` returns `TargetFacts`; the key-inbox transport reads its own `KeyInboxThread`/`KeyInboxMessage` and the `Contact` it already joins (`schema.prisma:11677`, `thread.contact`). Outbound applies policy to facts and never names another module's table.\n2. **\"outbound needs to check consent.\"** `ConsentPort`, registered by `communications` (which already provides `ConsentService`). If no port is registered, `isRevoked` is treated as `true` for `purpose: 'reminder'` — **fail closed**, the lesson `ai-oversight.service.ts:294-305` writes down. `purpose: 'reply'` is not consent-gated: the customer's inbound message is the consent, and `doNotContact` still applies.\n3. **\"outbound needs to create the approval item.\"** It does not. It records `PENDING_APPROVAL` and emits `outbound.approval_required` with the send id; `ai` listens, creates the `AiApprovalItem` it already knows how to create and route (`ai-oversight.service.ts:383`), and calls back `attachApproval(sendId, approvalItemId)`. Import direction stays `ai → outbound`.\n\nThe one place a cycle could sneak in is the transport for `targetKind: 'ticket'`. Helpdesk cannot deliver anything itself — the only sender it could reach is `AiMessageSenderService`, and `ai.module.ts:50` already imports `HelpdeskModule`, so `helpdesk → ai` would close a loop. **So helpdesk never delivers.** It resolves its ticket's linked inbox thread and issues an `inbox_thread` send; outbound resolves the transport. Helpdesk imports `outbound` and nothing else new. That constraint is also the better data model — see §3.2.\n\n`AiModule` gains `OutboundModule` in `imports` with **no** `forwardRef`. The 68 deferrals are not added to.\n\n---\n\n## 3. Data\n\n### 3.1 New models — `packages/db/prisma/schema.prisma`\n\nHouse style throughout: `snake_case` `@map`, `businessId` with a cascading `Business` relation, compound `@@index([businessId, …])`.\n\n```prisma\nmodel OutboundPolicy {\n  id         String   @id @default(cuid())\n  businessId String   @map(\"business_id\")\n  business   Business @relation(fields: [businessId], references: [id], onDelete: Cascade)\n\n  actorKind String  @map(\"actor_kind\")  // department_role | job_role | human | rule\n  actorKey  String  @map(\"actor_key\")   // 'support' | <jobRoleId> | 'any'\n  channel   String                       // email | whatsapp | sms | any\n  purpose   String                       // reply | reminder | confirmation\n\n  mode        String  @default(\"APPROVE\") @map(\"mode\") // AUTO | APPROVE | DENY\n  maxRiskTier Int     @default(2)         @map(\"max_risk_tier\")\n\n  requireExistingThread    Boolean @default(true) @map(\"require_existing_thread\")\n  inboundWithinHours       Int?                   @map(\"inbound_within_hours\")\n  maxUnansweredOutbound    Int?                   @map(\"max_unanswered_outbound\")\n  dailyLimit               Int?                   @map(\"daily_limit\")\n  perContactCooldownHours  Int?                   @map(\"per_contact_cooldown_hours\")\n  quietHoursStart          String?                @map(\"quiet_hours_start\") // \"21:00\", business tz\n  quietHoursEnd            String?                @map(\"quiet_hours_end\")\n  approvalTimeoutHours     Int     @default(24)   @map(\"approval_timeout_hours\")\n\n  enabled         Boolean  @default(true)\n  updatedByUserId String?  @map(\"updated_by_user_id\")\n  createdAt       DateTime @default(now()) @map(\"created_at\")\n  updatedAt       DateTime @updatedAt      @map(\"updated_at\")\n\n  sends OutboundSend[]\n\n  @@unique([businessId, actorKind, actorKey, channel, purpose])\n  @@index([businessId, enabled])\n  @@map(\"outbound_policies\")\n}\n\nmodel OutboundSend {\n  id         String   @id @default(cuid())\n  businessId String   @map(\"business_id\")\n  business   Business @relation(fields: [businessId], references: [id], onDelete: Cascade)\n\n  idempotencyKey String @map(\"idempotency_key\")\n\n  actorKind   String  @map(\"actor_kind\")\n  actorKey    String  @map(\"actor_key\")\n  actorUserId String? @map(\"actor_user_id\")\n  toolName    String? @map(\"tool_name\")\n\n  targetKind String  @map(\"target_kind\")   // inbox_thread | ticket | invoice\n  targetId   String  @map(\"target_id\")     // threadId | ticketId | invoiceId\n  targetRef  String? @map(\"target_ref\")    // messageId, when the target has one\n  contactId  String? @map(\"contact_id\")\n\n  channel String\n  purpose String\n\n  verdict       String  // ALLOWED | NEEDS_APPROVAL | DENIED\n  verdictReason String  @map(\"verdict_reason\")\n  policyId      String? @map(\"policy_id\")\n  policy        OutboundPolicy? @relation(fields: [policyId], references: [id], onDelete: SetNull)\n\n  approvalItemId String?   @map(\"approval_item_id\")\n  releasedBy     String?   @map(\"released_by\")\n  releasedAt     DateTime? @map(\"released_at\")\n\n  status            String    @default(\"PENDING\")  // PENDING | PENDING_APPROVAL | QUEUED | SENT | FAILED | BLOCKED | EXPIRED | CANCELLED\n  transport         String?\n  providerMessageId String?   @map(\"provider_message_id\")\n  error             String?\n  attempts          Int       @default(0)\n\n  bodyHash    String  @map(\"body_hash\")     // sha256; the body lives on the message row\n  bodyPreview String? @map(\"body_preview\")  // first 200 chars, for the audit screen\n\n  queuedAt  DateTime? @map(\"queued_at\")\n  sentAt    DateTime? @map(\"sent_at\")\n  createdAt DateTime  @default(now()) @map(\"created_at\")\n  updatedAt DateTime  @updatedAt      @map(\"updated_at\")\n\n  @@unique([businessId, idempotencyKey])\n  @@index([businessId, status, createdAt])\n  @@index([businessId, actorKind, actorKey, createdAt])  // daily cap\n  @@index([businessId, contactId, purpose, createdAt])   // per-contact cooldown\n  @@index([businessId, targetKind, targetId])\n  @@map(\"outbound_sends\")\n}\n```\n\nBack-relations on `Business` (the file's convention, `schema.prisma:241` onward):\n\n```prisma\n  outboundPolicies OutboundPolicy[]\n  outboundSends    OutboundSend[]\n```\n\n### 3.2 One column and one FK repair\n\n```prisma\nmodel SupportTicket {\n  // …\n  inboxThreadId String? @map(\"inbox_thread_id\")\n  @@index([businessId, inboxThreadId])\n}\n```\n\nAnd `SupportTicketMessage.ticket` is repointed from `HelpdeskTicket` to `SupportTicket` (§1.1). `helpdesk_tickets` is written by nothing, so the data migration is a no-op `DELETE`-free repoint; the SQL drops and recreates one constraint.\n\n### 3.3 Migration\n\n`packages/db/prisma/migrations/20260812090000_outbound_send_authority/migration.sql` — timestamped in the house format (`20260809170000_add_user_deleted_at_banned_at` is the current head).\n\n```sql\nCREATE TABLE \"outbound_policies\" (…);\nCREATE UNIQUE INDEX \"outbound_policies_business_id_actor_kind_actor_key_channel_purpose_key\"\n  ON \"outbound_policies\"(\"business_id\",\"actor_kind\",\"actor_key\",\"channel\",\"purpose\");\nCREATE INDEX \"outbound_policies_business_id_enabled_idx\" ON \"outbound_policies\"(\"business_id\",\"enabled\");\nALTER TABLE \"outbound_policies\" ADD CONSTRAINT \"outbound_policies_business_id_fkey\"\n  FOREIGN KEY (\"business_id\") REFERENCES \"businesses\"(\"id\") ON DELETE CASCADE ON UPDATE CASCADE;\n\nCREATE TABLE \"outbound_sends\" (…);\nCREATE UNIQUE INDEX \"outbound_sends_business_id_idempotency_key_key\"\n  ON \"outbound_sends\"(\"business_id\",\"idempotency_key\");\n-- + the four compound indexes above\nALTER TABLE \"outbound_sends\" ADD CONSTRAINT \"outbound_sends_business_id_fkey\" …;\nALTER TABLE \"outbound_sends\" ADD CONSTRAINT \"outbound_sends_policy_id_fkey\"\n  FOREIGN KEY (\"policy_id\") REFERENCES \"outbound_policies\"(\"id\") ON DELETE SET NULL;\n\nALTER TABLE \"support_tickets\" ADD COLUMN \"inbox_thread_id\" TEXT;\nCREATE INDEX \"support_tickets_business_id_inbox_thread_id_idx\"\n  ON \"support_tickets\"(\"business_id\",\"inbox_thread_id\");\n\nALTER TABLE \"support_ticket_messages\" DROP CONSTRAINT \"support_ticket_messages_ticket_id_fkey\";\nALTER TABLE \"support_ticket_messages\" ADD CONSTRAINT \"support_ticket_messages_ticket_id_fkey\"\n  FOREIGN KEY (\"ticket_id\") REFERENCES \"support_tickets\"(\"id\") ON DELETE CASCADE ON UPDATE CASCADE;\n```\n\n### 3.4 Tenant isolation — required, and not automatic\n\n**Both new models are added to `BUSINESS_ID_MODELS` in `packages/db/src/client.ts:81`**, in a block with a one-line reason, alongside the finance cluster at `:128`.\n\nA new model is unscoped by default and `tenant-model-list.spec.ts` does not catch an unlisted one — it checks that listed names exist (`:58`) and that listed models have a `businessId` column (`:68`), in that direction only. So the entry is written by hand and pinned by name in a test (§5).\n\nTwo things the entry does **not** buy, restated from the file's own comment at `client.ts:120-127`:\n\n- the extension is HTTP-only, so on the BullMQ worker path `activeBusinessId()` is undefined and the entry is inert. **Every query in `OutboundSendWorker` carries an explicit `where: { businessId }`**, and the worker's first act is to re-read the send row by `{ id, businessId }` from the job payload.\n- `create` is not hooked. `businessId` on every `OutboundSend` is bound from the route param or the caller's already-validated `businessId`, never from a request body.\n\nAdjacent finding, recorded not fixed: `KeyInboxMessage` is **not** in `BUSINESS_ID_MODELS` (only `KeyInboxThread`, `client.ts:99`). `KeyInboxService` scopes explicitly at every site I read (`:73`, `:87`, `:334`, `:375`), so this is not a live hole — but it is one explicit `where` away from being one.\n\n---\n\n## 4. The three layers\n\n### 4.1 The decision, in order\n\n`SendAuthorityService.decide()` walks these and stops at the first that fires. Each writes its own `verdictReason` code, and the code is what the audit screen shows.\n\n| # | Guard | Verdict |\n|---:|---|---|\n| 1 | No enabled policy for `(actorKind, actorKey, channel, purpose)`, and none for the `('any', 'any')` fallback | `DENIED no_policy` |\n| 2 | `policy.mode = DENY` | `DENIED policy_denies` |\n| 3 | `getToolTier(toolName) > policy.maxRiskTier` | `NEEDS_APPROVAL tier_exceeds_policy` |\n| 4 | `policy.mode = APPROVE` | `NEEDS_APPROVAL policy_requires_approval` |\n| 5 | `describe()` returned null (target gone / not this tenant) | `DENIED target_not_found` |\n| 6 | `facts.doNotContact` | `DENIED do_not_contact` |\n| 7 | `purpose = 'reminder'` and consent revoked, **or no `ConsentPort` registered** | `DENIED consent_revoked` / `consent_unavailable` |\n| 8 | `requireExistingThread` and `facts.lastInboundAt = null` | `DENIED no_inbound_history` |\n| 9 | `lastInboundAt` older than `inboundWithinHours` | `NEEDS_APPROVAL inbound_stale` |\n| 10 | `outboundSinceLastInbound >= maxUnansweredOutbound` | `NEEDS_APPROVAL unanswered_streak` |\n| 11 | Now outside quiet hours, in `business.timezone` (`schema.prisma:160`, default `America/Port_of_Spain`) | `NEEDS_APPROVAL quiet_hours` |\n| 12 | `count(OutboundSend where actor, status=SENT, createdAt >= today) >= dailyLimit` | `NEEDS_APPROVAL daily_limit` |\n| 13 | A `SENT` row for the same `(contactId, purpose)` inside `perContactCooldownHours` | `NEEDS_APPROVAL cooldown` |\n| — | otherwise | `ALLOWED` |\n\nTwo gates, not one. `AiOversightService.evaluate` still decides whether KEY may *call* the tool. `SendAuthorityService` decides whether *this actor* may *deliver* this message *now*, and it runs for callers that never touch oversight at all — the flow runner, the automation executor, a cron. That is the actual hole: `flow-action.registry.ts:160` returns `sent: true` today without passing any gate.\n\n### 4.2 Default policy matrix\n\nSeeded per business by `OutboundPolicyService.seedDefaults()` on first read, editable on the manual screen. `AUTO` still means \"auto within the tier the role already has\" — nothing here raises a role's ceiling.\n\n| Actor | Channel | Purpose | Mode | Tier | Conditions |\n|---|---|---|---|:--:|---|\n| `support` | thread's own | reply | **AUTO** | 2 | existing thread; inbound within 72 h; ≤ 3 unanswered outbound; not DNC |\n| `support` | any | reminder | DENY | — | support does not chase money |\n| `operations` | thread's own | reply | **AUTO** | 2 | existing thread; inbound within 48 h |\n| `sales` | thread's own | reply | APPROVE | 2 | existing thread only — a sales reply is a commercial statement |\n| `finance` | email, whatsapp | reminder | **AUTO** | 2 | invoice overdue with a non-zero balance; 1 per invoice per 7 days; business hours |\n| `finance` | thread's own | reply | AUTO | 2 | existing thread |\n| `marketing` | any | any | DENY | — | broadcast stays T4 and outside this module |\n| `general` | any | any | DENY | — | ceiling 1; `general` is pinned on every web chat request (`role-engine.service.ts:303`) |\n| `executive` | any | any | DENY | — | ceiling 1, and \"never execute operational actions\" |\n| `operator` | thread's own | reply, confirmation | **AUTO** | 3 | existing thread |\n| `job_role` (any position) | thread's own | reply | APPROVE | from `JobRolePolicyService.envelopeForJobRole` (`structure/job-role-policy.service.ts:49`) | until a business opts a position up |\n| `rule` (automation) | thread's own | reply | APPROVE | 2 | a rule that sends unattended is a product decision, made once, per business |\n\n### 4.3 New KEY tools\n\nThree, all `family: 'execute'`, all **riskTier 2**. No existing tool's tier changes.\n\nThe rule that makes T2 honest: **a tool's tier is a function of how much the model chooses.** `send_message_with_approval` (`flow-tool-registry.ts:541`) lets the model choose the recipient, the channel and the words — that is cold outreach and stays T3. `inbox_send_reply` chooses only the words: the recipient is whoever wrote in, the channel is the one they used, the thread already exists and the sender cannot be redirected. Narrowing the choice is what earns the tier, and it is why nothing else widens.\n\n```ts\n{\n  name: 'inbox_send_reply',\n  description: 'Send a reply to the customer inside an existing inbox thread, on the channel they used. Cannot start a conversation, cannot choose the recipient, cannot change channel. Refused by the send policy if the thread has no inbound message, the contact is do-not-contact, or the role is not permitted to send.',\n  family: 'execute',\n  riskLevel: 'medium',\n  riskTier: 2 as RiskTier,\n  manualEquivalentRoute: '/app/key-inbox',\n  changedEntities: ['keyInboxMessage', 'outboundSend'],\n  followOnSuggestions: ['inbox_update_thread_status'],\n  parameters: { type: 'object', properties: {\n    threadId: { type: 'string', description: 'Existing inbox thread ID' },\n    body:     { type: 'string', description: 'Reply text, in the words the customer will read' },\n  }, required: ['threadId', 'body'] },\n  outputSchema: { type: 'object', description: 'Send outcome', fields: {\n    sendId:   { type: 'string',  description: 'Outbound send record ID' },\n    verdict:  { type: 'string',  description: 'ALLOWED | NEEDS_APPROVAL | DENIED' },\n    delivered:{ type: 'boolean', description: 'True only when the provider accepted it' },\n    reason:   { type: 'string',  description: 'Why, in the case it was not delivered' },\n    messageId:{ type: 'string',  description: 'The draft/sent message on the thread' },\n  } },\n}\n```\n\n`helpdesk_send_reply` — same shape, `ticketId`, `manualEquivalentRoute: '/app/helpdesk'`. Returns an honest error, never a fabricated success, when `ticket.inboxThreadId` is null: *\"This ticket has no customer channel — it was raised internally. Link it to an inbox thread first.\"*\n\n`commerce_send_payment_reminder` — `invoiceId`, optional `message`, `manualEquivalentRoute: '/app/commerce'`.\n\n**Why `commerce_`, not `finance_`.** `check-tool-routes` domain parity (`apps/web/src/lib/tool-route-audit.ts:118-123`) takes `t.name.split('_')[0]`, strips `/` and `-` from the route and requires containment. The screen with the actual reminder button is `FinancePipeline`, mounted at `apps/web/src/app/app/commerce/page.tsx:41`, whose `handleSendReminder` (`apps/web/src/app/app/finance/hooks/use-pipeline-actions.ts:247`) posts to `POST /commerce/businesses/:id/invoices/:id/send-reminder` (`commerce.controller.ts:1036`). Naming it `commerce_*` makes the parity claim true rather than allowlisted, and points the \"do it yourself\" link at the button that exists. (`/app/finance/page.tsx` is a four-line `redirect('/app/financial-flow')` — declaring it would pass the checker and mislead a user.)\n\n**Route resolution, verified against the checker's own rules:** `/app/key-inbox` → `apps/web/src/app/app/key-inbox/page.tsx` exists and calls `replyToThread` (`page.tsx:224`) → `POST …/threads/:id/reply` (`apps/web/src/lib/api/key-inbox.ts:192`), so `canWrite` (`tool-route-audit.ts:531`) resolves. `next.config.mjs` declares no `redirects()`, so `redirectedAway` (`:127`) cannot fire for any of the three.\n\n**Existing tools, adjusted without changing a tier:**\n\n- `helpdesk_reply_to_ticket` keeps its name and T2 but becomes what it actually is: `family: 'organize'`, description *\"Add an internal note to a support ticket. Not sent to the customer — use `helpdesk_send_reply` for that.\"*, and `HelpdeskService.replyToTicket` hard-codes `channel: 'internal'` rather than defaulting to it. Two tools for one action is the failure mode the registry warns about at `flow-tool-registry.ts:4347`; these are two different actions, and the descriptions say so on both sides.\n- `inbox_reply_thread` (T3) is retired from `FLOW_TOOLS` and its handler at `flow-orchestrator.service.ts:4685` deleted. It is strictly dominated: same action, higher tier, and it reports `sent: result.sendResult?.success ?? true` — **`?? true`**, so a draft-mode return with no `sendResult` reports a send. That is the tool-honesty defect class exactly, and deletion is the honest fix, as it was for `schedule_action` (`tool-honesty-sweep.spec.ts:71`). Tool count goes 245 → 247.\n\n**Role allowlists** (`role-engine.service.ts`): `inbox_send_reply` reaches support/operations/general/operator through the existing `inbox_*` and explicit entries; add it to `sales` `:190` and `finance` `:214` explicitly, and to `general.blockedTools` `:314` and `executive.blockedTools` `:370`. The role gate stays the coarse filter; the send policy is the fine one.\n\n### 4.4 The manual layer\n\n1. **`/app/key-inbox`** — add one button. A message with `sendStatus === 'DRAFT'` currently renders a label and nothing else (`page.tsx:425`). It gets a **Send** control next to the existing FAILED **Retry** (`:427-440`), calling the same `replyToThread(…, \"send\")` the composer already calls at `:224`. This is the manual equivalent of `inbox_send_reply` and the release valve for anything KEY drafted.\n2. **`/app/approvals`** — the existing `AiApprovalsPanel` gains, for items whose `module === 'outbound'`, the message preview and the send's `verdictReason`, so the approver sees *what* will be sent and *why* it stopped. Approving already calls `resolveAiApproval` (`ai-approvals-panel.tsx:129`); the `ai.approval.resolved` listener does the rest.\n3. **`/app/settings/sending`** — the policy matrix, one row per `(actor, channel, purpose)` with mode, tier and the numeric guards. New page + `nav-config.ts` entry under Settings. This is the screen that makes the capability configurable rather than hard-coded, and it is the only place the matrix in §4.2 can be changed.\n4. **`/app/settings/sending/log`** — the `OutboundSend` list: who, to whom, on which channel, verdict, reason, provider id. The record that proves it happened.\n\n### 4.5 The smart layer\n\n`flow/flow-action.registry.ts:136` `send_message` is rewired to `OutboundSendService.request()` with `actor: { kind: 'rule', actorKey: 'automation', automationId: ctx.automationId }`, and returns the real `SendDecision`. The fabricated `{ sent: true, direct: true }` at `:160` is deleted. `flow` imports `OutboundModule`; `outbound` imports nothing, so no cycle.\n\nThe action's entry in the SMART registry declares `requiresPolicy: 'outbound'`, so a business that has never opened `/app/settings/sending` gets `DENIED no_policy` with a sentence naming the screen — a refusal with a next step, not a silent green run.\n\n### 4.6 Scheduling — BullMQ only\n\nQueue `outbound-send`, constructed exactly as `business-events` is (`business-event.queue.ts:37`), on the shared `REDIS_CLIENT`.\n\n- **`jobId = sendId`.** BullMQ drops a duplicate `jobId`, so N replicas enqueuing the same send produce one job. Combined with `@@unique([businessId, idempotencyKey])`, a retried tool call is a no-op rather than a second message.\n- **Retries:** `attempts: 4`, `backoff: { type: 'exponential', delay: 5000 }`. No `setInterval` anywhere in this module.\n- **Approval expiry:** when a send goes `PENDING_APPROVAL`, one delayed job is added with `delay: approvalTimeoutHours * 3600e3` and `jobId: 'expire:' + sendId`. It flips `PENDING_APPROVAL → EXPIRED` if still pending. **No repeatable job, no cron, no sweep** — the timer is per send and dies with it.\n- **Worker starts in `onApplicationBootstrap`, not `onModuleInit`.** Nest runs every `onModuleInit` before any `onApplicationBootstrap`, so every transport has registered before the first job is claimed. A job whose transport is missing **throws** — it does not report success and does not silently no-op — so it retries and then lands in `failed` where the queue stats can see it.\n- **Claim:** the worker's first statement is `updateMany({ where: { id, businessId, status: 'QUEUED' }, data: { status: 'SENDING', attempts: { increment: 1 } } })` and it returns early on `count === 0`. Compare-and-swap, the pattern already proven at `delivery-queue.service.ts:98`. Belt and braces with `jobId`; it costs one statement.\n- **Redis down:** `enableOfflineQueue: false` (`core/redis/redis.module.ts`) means `queue.add` *rejects* rather than hanging. `request()` catches, leaves the row `PENDING` with `queued: false`, and returns `NEEDS_APPROVAL queue_unavailable`. The send does not vanish and does not silently succeed.\n\n---\n\n## 5. Tests\n\n### 5.1 Unit — `outbound/send-authority.service.spec.ts`\n\nTable-driven over the thirteen guards: one case per row of §4.1, each asserting the **verdict and the reason code**, not just the verdict. A guard that fires for the wrong reason is a guard that will be deleted by the next person who reads it.\n\n### 5.2 Integration, real Postgres — `apps/server/test/outbound-send.integration.test.ts`\n\nSits with the existing real-database suites (`cortex-context-v2.integration.test.ts`, `tenant-scope-extension.integration.test.ts`). Real Prisma, real policy rows, **stubbed transport** — one layer down, per the house rule that a mock which cannot disagree with you proves nothing.\n\n1. Support role, thread with an inbound message 2 h old → `ALLOWED`, job enqueued, stub transport called once, `OutboundSend.status = 'SENT'`, `providerMessageId` persisted.\n2. Same request replayed with the same `idempotencyKey` → **one** `OutboundSend` row, **one** transport call.\n3. Contact with `doNotContact = true` → `DENIED do_not_contact`, transport call count **0**.\n4. `purpose: 'reminder'` with no `ConsentPort` registered → `DENIED consent_unavailable`, transport calls **0**.\n5. `general` role → `DENIED no_policy`.\n6. `PENDING_APPROVAL` → emit `ai.approval.resolved` → status `SENT`, transport called once. Rejected → `CANCELLED`, transport calls **0**.\n7. Transport returns `{ delivered: false, error }` → status `FAILED`, `error` persisted, and **`SendDecision.delivered` is false**. A failed delivery must never surface as a success.\n8. Cross-tenant: business B's `threadId` with business A's `businessId` → `DENIED target_not_found`, transport calls **0**.\n\n### 5.3 The negative control\n\nThe failure mode this whole spec exists to prevent is *\"the record says sent and nothing left the building.\"* So the control is spelled as a real defect and must fail:\n\n```ts\nit('the suite fails when a blocked send still reaches the transport', async () => {\n  // Simulates the exact bug: authority consulted, result discarded.\n  const authority = { decide: async () => ({ verdict: 'DENIED', reason: 'do_not_contact', … }) };\n  const service = new OutboundSendService(prisma, queue, registry, authority as never, { ignoreVerdict: true });\n\n  await service.request(req);\n\n  // The assertion the real tests make. It must FAIL here.\n  expect(transport.deliver).not.toHaveBeenCalled();\n});\n```\n\nWrapped in `expect(async () => { … }).rejects` so a green suite proves the assertion is load-bearing. Without this, test 3 above passes against a service that never wires the transport at all — the exact \"assertions check referents, not shapes\" failure the repo has hit repeatedly.\n\nA second control for the honesty claim: temporarily point `deliver` at a stub returning `{ delivered: false }` and assert the suite goes red at test 7. If it stays green, test 7 is checking a shape.\n\n### 5.4 Static gates (extensions of existing specs)\n\n| Spec | Addition |\n|---|---|\n| `core/prisma/tenant-model-list.spec.ts` | `expect(listed).toContain('OutboundSend'); expect(listed).toContain('OutboundPolicy');` — same shape as the `AiGoal`/`AiPlan` pin at `:92`. The list check cannot catch an *omission*, so the omission is pinned by name. |\n| `ai/tool-honesty-sweep.spec.ts` | (a) the three new handlers must bind the decision — `/const decision = await this\\.getOutbound\\(\\)\\.request\\(/`; (b) each must return `delivered: decision.delivered`, never a literal `true`; (c) `expect(orchestrator).not.toMatch(/sent: result\\.sendResult\\?\\.success \\?\\? true/)` pins the deleted `?? true`; (d) `expect(getToolByName('inbox_reply_thread')).toBeUndefined()`. |\n| `ai/flow-tool-honesty.spec.ts` | every declared tool has a handler — satisfied by construction; the three new tools land in its existing sweep. |\n| `apps/web/src/lib/__tests__/tool-routes.spec.ts` | runs unchanged; the three routes resolve to writable pages (§4.3). Expected counts move 245 → 247. |\n| `core/event-bus/event-wiring.spec.ts` | `ai.approval.resolved` and the five `outbound.*` names are emitted **and** listened to, so `KNOWN_DEAD` does not grow. The spec's \"these are now emitted, remove them\" branch (`:103`) is unaffected — none of the new names are in the list. |\n| **New:** `outbound/module-is-a-leaf.spec.ts` | Reads `outbound.module.ts`, asserts `imports: []` literally and that no file under `modules/outbound/` imports from `../ai`, `../key-inbox`, `../helpdesk`, `../communications`, `../commerce` or `../flow`. This is the guarantee in §2.3 turned into a build failure. Negative control: a fixture file with such an import must make it fail. |\n\n---\n\n## 6. Effort, and what to adopt\n\n**Twenty working days.** Sequenced so each phase ships something that works.\n\n| Phase | Work | Days |\n|---|---|---:|\n| 0 | `outbound.contract.ts`, module, both models + migration, `OutboundPolicyService` with seeds, `SendAuthorityService`, `OutboundPortRegistry`, queue + worker, `BUSINESS_ID_MODELS` entries | 4 |\n| 1 | `KeyInboxOutboundTransport` (`describe` + `deliver` over the existing reply-sender), `inbox_send_reply` tool + handler, retire `inbox_reply_thread`, the `ai.approval.resolved` emit + listener, the **Send** button on a DRAFT message | 4 |\n| 2 | Helpdesk: FK repair, `inboxThreadId`, `helpdesk_send_reply`, `helpdesk_reply_to_ticket` demoted to an honest internal note | 3 |\n| 3 | `CommerceOutboundTransport` over the existing `send-reminder` endpoint, `commerce_send_payment_reminder`, `ConsentPort` registration from `communications` | 2 |\n| 4 | SMART: `flow-action.registry.ts` `send_message` rewired, fabricated success deleted | 1 |\n| 5 | `/app/settings/sending` + `/app/settings/sending/log` + nav | 3 |\n| 6 | Tests §5, including both negative controls | 3 |\n\n**Adopt: nothing new.** Every dependency this needs is already in the tree and already used:\n\n- **BullMQ** — MIT, `apps/server/package.json:42` `^5.76.8`, live at `ai/queue.service.ts:56` and `business-events/business-event.queue.ts:37`. Durable timers, `jobId` idempotency and multi-replica safety come free; this spec adds no scheduler and no container.\n- **ioredis** — MIT, `package.json:55`, already `@Global` with the offline-queue behaviour documented at length in `core/redis/redis.module.ts`.\n- **Prisma** — the policy and the ledger are two tables in the Postgres that already exists.\n\n**Explicitly not adopting:** no ESP, no message-queue broker, no Chatwoot-for-this. `docs/CAPABILITY_MAP_2026-08-09.md` §3.4 already refuses each, and this capability is a *decision layer over transports that exist*, not a transport.\n\n---\n\n## 7. Deliberately not doing\n\n| Not doing | Why |\n|---|---|\n| **Lowering any existing tool's tier** | `role-engine.service.ts:315-322` records what a silent tier change costs: `general.maxRiskTier` moved 1 → 2 as a merge artifact and nothing in a conflict review would have shown it. New narrow tools at T2 widen exactly what was reasoned about. |\n| **Broadcast, campaigns, social publishing** | `comms_send_broadcast` T3, `marketing_send_campaign` T4, `social_publish_post` T4 all stay where they are. One-to-many outbound has a different blast radius, a different consent regime (`List-Unsubscribe`, Wave 0 #11) and a different manual screen. This module is 1:1 only. |\n| **A second draft/approval store** | `ResponseDraft` exists (`schema.prisma:11128`) with a full approve/reject/send API (`omnichannel.controller.ts:103-166`) and **no web caller**. Adding a third is how the repo got two `FullBusinessContext` interfaces. The draft is a `KeyInboxMessage` with `sendStatus: 'DRAFT'` — the artefact the shipped screen already renders. `ResponseDraft`'s consolidation into this path is a follow-up, named and not started. |\n| **Reconciling the two approval systems** | `AiApprovalItem` and `KeyActionProposal` both exist and `ai.controller.ts:601` migrates between them. This spec touches neither: it listens for one resolution event and releases its own send. Whichever system wins later, the send record is unaffected. |\n| **A helpdesk delivery path** | It cannot have one without closing a cycle through `ai` (§2.3). Tickets send through their linked inbox thread or refuse honestly. |\n| **The `general` role sending anything** | It is pinned on every web chat request (`role-engine.service.ts:303`) and has the widest allowlist in the file. It is the wrong place to open an outbound door. Sending is a specialist act; picking up the specialist hat is the price. |\n| **Fixing the 51 other `setInterval` schedulers** | Out of scope. This module adds zero and demonstrates the replacement shape — `jobId` + `delay` + compare-and-swap claim — on one real capability. |\n| **Statutory / regulatory send rules (TCPA, GDPR windows)** | Quiet hours, do-not-contact and consent are modelled as *policy fields a business sets*, not as an engine that knows the law. Same reasoning as the payroll refusal in §3.4 of the capability map: a compliance engine is an annual maintenance obligation. |\n\n---\n\n## Where an engineer starts, in order\n\n1. `apps/server/src/modules/ai/role-engine.service.ts:190-384` — the eight ceilings, and where `inbox_send_reply` goes.\n2. `apps/server/src/modules/ai/ai-oversight.service.ts:69-202` — the clamp and the tier ladder this sits beside, not inside.\n3. `apps/server/src/modules/key-inbox/key-inbox-reply-sender.service.ts` — the whole file; this is the thing being reused, unchanged.\n4. `apps/server/src/modules/key-inbox/key-inbox.service.ts:327-369` — `addReply`, the draft/send seam.\n5. `apps/server/src/modules/business-events/business-event.queue.ts:26-78` — the BullMQ shape to copy.\n6. `packages/db/src/client.ts:81-153` — the set, and the comment explaining what it does not protect.\n7. `apps/web/src/app/app/key-inbox/page.tsx:415-445` — the four lines where the Send button goes.\n8. `apps/server/src/modules/helpdesk/helpdesk.service.ts:117-147` — read it next to `packages/db/prisma/migrations/20260721044726_support_ticket_messages/migration.sql:22`. That pair is the smallest, sharpest thing in this document, and it can be fixed in an afternoon before anything else here starts."

        "key": "05-ingestion",
        "title": "Ingestion that matches reality",
        "spec": "# Ingestion that matches reality\n\n**Implementation spec — statement extraction and historical import**\nRepo `C:/kfm` @ `main`. Written against the code on 2026-08-09. Companion to `docs/CAPABILITY_MAP_2026-08-09.md` §2.2 and §3.2 items 24–26, and `docs/OPEN_GAPS_2026-08-09.md`.\n\n---\n\n## 0. The one sentence\n\nA business cannot start here until its history is here, and today nothing can carry it in: the statement parser reads four text formats and silently skips the PDF a Trinidad bank actually sends, and twelve of twenty-two connectors have no inbound path at all. This spec adds **one leaf module that turns bytes into structured rows** and **one module that stages a provider's history before it touches the ledger**, using docling and BullMQ which are already running, and adds no edge to the 68-`forwardRef` graph.\n\n---\n\n## 1. What exists today\n\nEverything below was established by opening the file. Line numbers are from the working tree at `main`.\n\n### 1.1 The statement path — good, and blind to PDF\n\n| Thing | Where | State |\n|---|---|---|\n| Format sniffing | `apps/server/src/modules/finance/bank-statement-parsers.ts:74-87` | Reads content, not filename. Falls through to `return 'csv'` at **:86** for anything it does not recognise — including a PDF. |\n| OFX / QIF / MT940 parsers | same file, `:105`, `:168`, `:233` | Genuinely good. FITID and `:61:` reference give exact dedupe. MT940 comma-decimal handled at `:266`. |\n| CSV parser + column profile | `bank-import.service.ts:137-220` | Per-account `importProfile` on `FinancialAccount.metadata`. Debit/credit split columns handled at `:182-183`. |\n| Ingest + dedupe + audit | `bank-import.service.ts:251-416` | Two dedupe sets (external id, then heuristic), `createMany({skipDuplicates})` backstop, honest `exactDedupe` flag. |\n| DB-level dedupe | `packages/db/prisma/migrations/20260808140000_bank_transaction_external_id_unique/migration.sql` | Partial unique index on `(account_id, rawData->>'externalId')`. |\n| HTTP upload | `finance.controller.ts:599-623` | **`:613` is `file.buffer.toString('utf8')`.** This is where a PDF dies. |\n| Drive/Gmail sweeps | `statement-source.service.ts:170-260` | `:196` and `:243` also `.toString('utf8')`. |\n| Extension gate | `statement-source.service.ts:92` | `STATEMENT_EXT = /\\.(ofx\\|qfx\\|qif\\|sta\\|mt940\\|txt\\|csv)$/i` — **no `pdf`**. |\n| Nightly sweep | `statement-source.service.ts:389` | `@Cron('0 5 * * *')`. One process only; a second replica double-sweeps. |\n| Manual screen | `apps/web/src/app/app/finance/reconciliation/page.tsx:172-189` | Real. `accept=\".csv,.ofx,.qfx,.qif,.sta,.mt940,.txt,…\"` — **no `.pdf`**. |\n| KEY tools | `flow-tool-registry.ts:1330-1429` | Six `reconcile_*` tools, all pointing at `/app/finance/reconciliation`, all with handlers at `flow-orchestrator.service.ts:2480-2569`. |\n\n**The precise failure, not the approximate one.** A PDF dropped in a configured Drive folder does not error. `looksLikeStatement` (`statement-source.service.ts:153-159`) rejects it on the extension, `result.skipped += 1`, and the sweep returns `imported: 0`. `reportHealth` (`:288-372`) only raises a flag after `QUIET_DAYS_BEFORE_CONCERN = 45` days. **A business that connects a Drive folder of PDF statements sees a green connection and nothing at all for six weeks.** Uploaded by hand it is louder but still wrong: `detectFormat` returns `'csv'`, `parseBankCsv` throws `BadRequestException('CSV is missing a Date column')`, and the user is told their statement has no date column.\n\n### 1.2 docling — running in production, wired to two callers, and truncating\n\n`apps/server/src/modules/ingestion/document-parsing.service.ts` is a 93-line client over `quay.io/docling-project/docling-serve` (`docker-compose.production.yml:170-181`, `DOCLING_URL` at `:90`). It works. Three defects make it unusable for a statement as written:\n\n- **`:77` `jsonContent.tables.slice(0, 10)`** — a 4-page statement has more than 10 table fragments.\n- **`:79` `JSON.stringify(t.data ?? t).slice(0, 2000)`** — each table is truncated to 2000 characters *of JSON*, which is perhaps fifteen rows. Then handed to an LLM as a string.\n- **`:69-72`** — empty markdown returns `null`, and both callers treat `null` as \"fall back to raw bytes\". That is how the `xlsx` class of defect (`key-cortex-document.service.ts:828`, against a package in no lockfile, typechecked by the hand-written stub at `apps/server/src/types/xlsx.d.ts`) embeds a binary ZIP as mojibake and reports success.\n\nBoth existing callers reach it through the container escape hatch rather than a module import: `ai/document-intelligence.service.ts:152` and `contracts/contract-clause.service.ts:58`, both `this.moduleRef.get(DocumentParsingService, { strict: false })`. They do that because `IngestionModule` imports `AiModule`, `CommerceModule` and `KeyInboxModule` (`ingestion.module.ts:14`) and a real import would close a cycle.\n\n`DOCLING_URL` is **not** in `.env.example` (checked; `S3_ENDPOINT` at `:119` and `MCP_REMOTE_SERVERS` at `:134` are). Local dev silently falls back to `http://localhost:5001`.\n\n### 1.3 Connectors — the inbound side, re-derived\n\n`core/connectors/connector-sync-modes.ts:43-79` classifies all 22. Counting connectors whose every mode is `STATUS_ONLY`, `PUSH_OR_PUBLISH` or `UNSUPPORTED`: `google_calendar`, `google_business_profile`, `linkedin`, `twitter`, `tiktok`, `stripe`, `paypal`, `wipay`, `quickbooks`, `xero`, `mailchimp`, `klaviyo` — **12**, matching the audit. `sync-engine.service.ts:298-334` is the placeholder that returns `recordsRead: 0` and is written to the job as `status: 'success'` at `:129-141`.\n\nBe precise about what is and is not missing:\n\n- **Stripe/PayPal/WiPay have a live webhook path** (`payments.service.ts`, `webhooks.controller.ts`) for payments taken *after* connection. There is no path for anything before it.\n- **The API clients already exist.** `stripe.connector.ts:217-240` (`stripeRequest`), `:319-386` (`fetchLiveStripeTransactions` — charges + refunds, `limit` capped at 100, **no `starting_after` pagination**). `paypal.connector.ts:230` (`getAccessToken`), `:247` (`listRecentTransactions`). `quickbooks.connector.ts:382-412` (`listChartOfAccounts` — a working QBO query endpoint). `xero.connector.ts:217` (`xeroHeaders`), `:259` / `:318` / `:348` (Contacts, Invoices, Accounts).\n- **WiPay has no transaction-list API.** `wipay.connector.ts:112` posts to `https://tt.wipayfinancial.com/plugins/payments/request` — a payment-*request* plugin. There is nothing to backfill from. See §8.1.\n- **QuickBooks and Xero take a pasted access token**, not an OAuth grant (`quickbooks.connector.ts:29-31`, `xero.connector.ts:30`). An Intuit token lives about 60 minutes. A 24-month backfill does not.\n\n### 1.4 The two things already built that this spec plugs into\n\n- **`EntityResolutionService`** — exported by `ConnectorModule`, which is `@Global` (`connector.module.ts:43`, exports at `:82-106`). Every connector already resolves contacts through it. So does the importer.\n- **`ContactImport` / `ContactImportContact`** (`schema.prisma:3260-3300`, service at `crm-import.service.ts`) — a staged import with a job row and per-record rows, for contacts, already handling csv/xlsx/pdf/vcf. It is the shape to copy, and it does the whole parse **inline in the HTTP request** (`crm-import.service.ts:238-306`) with no queue. It also uses `pdf-parse` (`:5`) — a third PDF path, alongside docling and nothing.\n\n### 1.5 The two constraints, verified against the code\n\n- **Tenant scoping.** `BUSINESS_ID_MODELS` at `packages/db/src/client.ts:81-153` holds 77 names; `tenantOperationAllowed` at `:196-199` is a `Set.has` on the model name. `apps/server/src/core/prisma/tenant-model-list.spec.ts` checks **listed→exists** (`:58`) and **listed→has businessId** (`:68`). It does not check the other direction. `IngestionItem` and `ContactImport` both have `businessId` and are on neither list — **a new model is unscoped and nothing fails.** Confirmed by reading both files.\n- **Scheduling.** BullMQ `^5.76.8` is in `apps/server/package.json:42` and used properly in three places: `ai/queue.service.ts:113-132` (repeatable job, `jobId: cron:${triggerId}`, `repeat: { pattern, tz }`), `business-events/business-event.queue.ts`, `temporal-flow/temporal-flow-memory.queue.service.ts`. `RedisModule` is `@Global` (`core/redis/redis.module.ts:53`), so a queue costs zero module edges.\n\n---\n\n## 2. Module boundaries\n\nTwo new modules. The genome thesis is why they are two and not one: a salon imports contacts and card payments, an accountant imports a chart of accounts and a trial balance, an insurance broker imports policy PDFs. The *extraction* of bytes is identical for all six work shapes; the *scopes* a business backfills are per-genome configuration, which is a list on the run, not a branch in the code.\n\n### 2.1 `DocumentExtractionModule` — new, leaf\n\n```\napps/server/src/modules/document-extraction/\n  document-extraction.module.ts     imports: []          ← the whole cycle argument\n  docling.client.ts                 HTTP only, no DI beyond env\n  document-extraction.service.ts    the public interface\n  extraction-run.repository.ts      writes ExtractionRun\n  document-extraction.service.spec.ts\n  module-graph.spec.ts\n```\n\n**Owns:** the `ExtractionRun` table, the docling contract, and one rule — *no extraction may report success with zero extracted content*.\n\n**Public interface** (the only exported symbols):\n\n```ts\nexport type ExtractionPurpose = 'bank_statement' | 'contract' | 'contact_import' | 'generic';\n\nexport interface ExtractedTable { pageNo: number | null; rows: string[][]; }\n\nexport interface ExtractionResult {\n  runId: string;\n  parser: 'docling' | 'text';\n  markdown: string;\n  tables: ExtractedTable[];      // NOT truncated, NOT stringified\n  pageCount: number | null;\n  charCount: number;\n  cellCount: number;\n  cached: boolean;               // true when the sha256 was already extracted\n}\n\n@Injectable()\nexport class DocumentExtractionService {\n  /** Throws UnprocessableEntityException when charCount === 0 && cellCount === 0.\n   *  Never returns an empty success. Persists an ExtractionRun either way. */\n  extract(businessId: string, input: {\n    buffer: Buffer; filename: string; mimeType?: string; purpose: ExtractionPurpose;\n  }): Promise<ExtractionResult>;\n\n  health(): Promise<{ reachable: boolean; url: string }>;\n}\n```\n\n**Emits:** `document.extraction.completed`, `document.extraction.refused`.\n**Listens to:** nothing.\n\n**Why it cannot create an import cycle.** Its `@Module({ imports: [] })` is literally empty. It injects `PrismaService` (`PrismaModule` is `@Global`, `core/prisma/prisma.module.ts:4`) and `EventEmitter2` (global). A module with no out-edges cannot be a member of a cycle — that is a property of directed graphs, not a judgement, and `module-graph.spec.ts` asserts the array is empty so it stays true.\n\n**It removes edges rather than adding them.** `IngestionModule` imports `DocumentExtractionModule` and deletes `document-parsing.service.ts`. `ai/document-intelligence.service.ts:152` and `contracts/contract-clause.service.ts:58` drop their `moduleRef.get(..., { strict: false })` and inject `DocumentExtractionService` for real, adding `DocumentExtractionModule` to `AiModule` and `ContractsModule` imports — safe, because it is a leaf. **Net: −2 container escape hatches, 0 new cycles.**\n\n### 2.2 `HistoricalImportModule` — new\n\n```\napps/server/src/modules/historical-import/\n  historical-import.module.ts        imports: [DocumentExtractionModule]\n  historical-import.service.ts       the public interface\n  historical-import.queue.ts         BullMQ queue + worker\n  historical-import.controller.ts\n  historical-import.listener.ts      consumes finance's posting result\n  adapters/\n    backfill-adapter.interface.ts\n    stripe-backfill.adapter.ts\n    paypal-backfill.adapter.ts\n    quickbooks-backfill.adapter.ts\n    xero-backfill.adapter.ts\n    file-backfill.adapter.ts         WiPay CSV, and anything else exported by hand\n  dto/start-backfill.dto.ts\n```\n\n**Owns:** `ImportRun`, `ImportRecord`. Nothing else writes those tables.\n\n**Public interface:**\n\n```ts\nexport type ImportProvider = 'quickbooks' | 'xero' | 'stripe' | 'paypal' | 'file';\nexport type ImportScope =\n  | 'contacts' | 'chart_of_accounts' | 'open_invoices' | 'open_bills'\n  | 'payments' | 'opening_trial_balance';\n\n@Injectable()\nexport class HistoricalImportService {\n  startBackfill(businessId: string, input: {\n    provider: ImportProvider; scopes: ImportScope[]; since?: Date; requestedById: string;\n  }): Promise<ImportRunSummary>;                    // enqueues; returns immediately\n\n  listRuns(businessId: string): Promise<ImportRunSummary[]>;\n  getRun(businessId: string, runId: string): Promise<ImportRunDetail>;\n  applyRun(businessId: string, runId: string, actorUserId: string): Promise<ApplyResult>;\n  cancelRun(businessId: string, runId: string): Promise<ImportRunSummary>;\n}\n```\n\n**Emits:** `import.run.staged`, `import.trial_balance.staged`, `import.run.applied`, `import.run.failed`.\n**Listens to:** `import.trial_balance.posted` (finance's reply).\n\n**Why it creates no cycle.** Three separate reasons, each sufficient:\n\n1. **Its only declared import is a leaf.** `imports: [DocumentExtractionModule]`, which has `imports: []`.\n2. **Everything else it needs is `@Global`.** `ConnectorModule` is `@Global` (`connector.module.ts:43`) and its `exports` (`:82-106`) already include `ConnectorCredentialsService`, `EntityResolutionService`, `StripeConnector`, `PayPalConnector`, `QuickbooksConnector`, `XeroConnector`. `PrismaModule` and `RedisModule` are `@Global`. A `@Global` provider is injectable without an `imports` entry, so **no edge appears in the graph**.\n3. **It never imports `FinanceModule`, `CrmModule` or `CommerceModule`** — the three that would close a cycle (`commerce.module.ts:67,72` already `forwardRef`s `FinanceModule`). Contacts go through `EntityResolutionService`. Invoices and payments are written to `Invoice`/`Payment` with `externalAccountingSource` / `externalAccountingId` — the same columns `quickbooks.connector.ts:311` already writes. **The ledger is the one thing it must not touch directly**, so it does not:\n\n```\nHistoricalImportModule  --emit import.trial_balance.staged-->  [event bus]\n                                                                   |\n                                          finance/historical-import.listener.ts\n                                          (new PROVIDER in FinanceModule, not a new import)\n                                                    calls PostingService.post()\n                                                                   |\n[event bus]  <--emit import.trial_balance.posted-------------------+\n     |\nHistoricalImportModule listener stamps its own ImportRecord rows\n```\n\nEach module writes only its own tables. `FinanceModule` gains a provider, not an import. The import module gains a listener, not an import. **Zero new module edges in either direction.**\n\n4. **Nothing imports `HistoricalImportModule` except `app.module.ts`.** The KEY tool handler reaches `HistoricalImportService` through the pattern already used for exactly this at `flow-orchestrator.service.ts:453-456` (`getBankMatching()`, `getStatementSources()`, `getReceivables()`): `this.moduleRef.get(HistoricalImportService, { strict: false })`. `AiModule` is imported with `forwardRef` by `FinanceModule` (`finance.module.ts:57`); an `AiModule → HistoricalImportModule → …` edge would be the expensive mistake, and `moduleRef` avoids it entirely. The honest cost is that this is a runtime lookup, not a compile-time contract — §6 pays for it with a boot test.\n\n### 2.3 Changes to `FinanceModule` (no new module)\n\nBank statements belong to finance. The capability map's own refusal list says *do not add another domain module* — 110 exist and 76 have no cron, loop or listener. So the PDF work lands inside finance:\n\n- `finance.module.ts:57` — add `DocumentExtractionModule` to `imports`. Leaf; no cycle.\n- `finance.module.ts` providers — add `HistoricalImportPostingListener`.\n\n---\n\n## 3. Data\n\n### 3.1 `ExtractionRun` — owned by `DocumentExtractionModule`\n\n```prisma\nmodel ExtractionRun {\n  id         String   @id @default(cuid())\n  businessId String   @map(\"business_id\")\n  business   Business @relation(fields: [businessId], references: [id], onDelete: Cascade)\n\n  purpose    String                       // bank_statement | contract | contact_import | generic\n  filename   String\n  mimeType   String   @map(\"mime_type\")\n  byteSize   Int      @map(\"byte_size\")\n  sha256     String                       // of the bytes, so the same file is never re-parsed\n  parser     String                       // docling | text | refused\n  pageCount  Int?     @map(\"page_count\")\n  charCount  Int      @default(0) @map(\"char_count\")\n  tableCount Int      @default(0) @map(\"table_count\")\n  cellCount  Int      @default(0) @map(\"cell_count\")\n  durationMs Int      @default(0) @map(\"duration_ms\")\n  error      String?\n\n  createdAt  DateTime @default(now()) @map(\"created_at\")\n\n  @@unique([businessId, sha256, purpose])\n  @@index([businessId, createdAt])\n  @@index([businessId, purpose, parser])\n  @@map(\"extraction_runs\")\n}\n```\n\nThe unique on `(businessId, sha256, purpose)` is not hygiene — docling takes seconds per page, and the nightly sweep re-reads the same Drive folder. A hit returns `cached: true` and makes no HTTP call.\n\n`parser: 'refused'` is written **before** the exception is thrown, so a refusal is a row an operator can count, not a log line nobody reads.\n\n### 3.2 `ImportRun` / `ImportRecord` — owned by `HistoricalImportModule`\n\n```prisma\nmodel ImportRun {\n  id             String   @id @default(cuid())\n  businessId     String   @map(\"business_id\")\n  business       Business @relation(fields: [businessId], references: [id], onDelete: Cascade)\n\n  provider       String                        // quickbooks | xero | stripe | paypal | file\n  scope          String                        // one scope per run — a run is resumable, a bundle is not\n  idempotencyKey String   @map(\"idempotency_key\")  // `${provider}:${scope}:${sinceIso ?? 'all'}`\n  status         String   @default(\"QUEUED\")   // QUEUED RUNNING STAGED APPLYING APPLIED FAILED CANCELLED\n\n  since          DateTime?\n  until          DateTime?\n  cursor         Json?                          // provider paging state, written after EVERY page\n\n  recordsFetched Int      @default(0) @map(\"records_fetched\")\n  recordsStaged  Int      @default(0) @map(\"records_staged\")\n  recordsApplied Int      @default(0) @map(\"records_applied\")\n  recordsSkipped Int      @default(0) @map(\"records_skipped\")\n  recordsFailed  Int      @default(0) @map(\"records_failed\")\n\n  error          String?\n  requestedById  String?  @map(\"requested_by_id\")\n  appliedById    String?  @map(\"applied_by_id\")\n\n  startedAt      DateTime? @map(\"started_at\")\n  stagedAt       DateTime? @map(\"staged_at\")\n  completedAt    DateTime? @map(\"completed_at\")\n  createdAt      DateTime  @default(now()) @map(\"created_at\")\n  updatedAt      DateTime  @updatedAt @map(\"updated_at\")\n\n  records        ImportRecord[]\n\n  @@unique([businessId, idempotencyKey])\n  @@index([businessId, status])\n  @@index([businessId, provider, scope])\n  @@index([businessId, createdAt])\n  @@map(\"import_runs\")\n}\n\nmodel ImportRecord {\n  id         String   @id @default(cuid())\n  businessId String   @map(\"business_id\")\n  business   Business @relation(fields: [businessId], references: [id], onDelete: Cascade)\n\n  runId      String    @map(\"run_id\")\n  run        ImportRun @relation(fields: [runId], references: [id], onDelete: Cascade)\n\n  provider   String                       // denormalised from the run ON PURPOSE — see the unique below\n  entityType String    @map(\"entity_type\") // contact | invoice | bill | payment | account | journal\n  externalId String    @map(\"external_id\") // the provider's own id\n\n  raw        Json                          // exactly what the provider returned\n  normalised Json?                         // what `normalise()` made of it — pure, diffable\n  status     String    @default(\"STAGED\")  // STAGED | APPLIED | SKIPPED_DUPLICATE | FAILED\n  targetId   String?   @map(\"target_id\")   // the KEYFLOWOS row it became\n  error      String?\n\n  createdAt  DateTime  @default(now()) @map(\"created_at\")\n  appliedAt  DateTime? @map(\"applied_at\")\n\n  @@unique([businessId, provider, entityType, externalId])\n  @@index([businessId, runId, status])\n  @@index([businessId, status])\n  @@map(\"import_records\")\n}\n```\n\n`provider` is denormalised onto the record so the unique key can be `(businessId, provider, entityType, externalId)`. That is what makes re-running a backfill a no-op at the **database** level rather than in application memory — the same lesson `20260808140000_bank_transaction_external_id_unique/migration.sql` records, learned the hard way.\n\n`Business` gains three back-relations beside `ingestionItems` at `schema.prisma:271`:\n\n```prisma\n  extractionRuns ExtractionRun[]\n  importRuns     ImportRun[]\n  importRecords  ImportRecord[]\n```\n\n### 3.3 Tenant scoping — the part that is not optional\n\n**All three models go into `BUSINESS_ID_MODELS` in `packages/db/src/client.ts:81-153`, in a new block with a dated comment**, in the style of the finance block at `:112-135`:\n\n```ts\n  // ── Ingestion / import cluster, added 2026-08-12 ────────────────────────\n  // Every read path for these is an HTTP controller (historical-import.controller)\n  // or a BullMQ worker. The extension is HTTP-only, so it hardens the first and\n  // is inert on the second — the worker's own `where: { businessId }` is the\n  // only scope there, and it comes from the ImportRun row, never from job data.\n  'ExtractionRun', 'ImportRun', 'ImportRecord',\n```\n\n**And the reason this list needs a gate, not a habit.** `tenant-model-list.spec.ts` today checks only that listed names exist and have a `businessId` column. It cannot catch an *unlisted* model — verified by reading `:49-80`. `IngestionItem` (`schema.prisma:2127`) and `ContactImport` (`:3260`) are both proof: they have `businessId`, they are not on the list, and every gate is green.\n\nAdd the third direction as a **ratchet**, because the honest direction fails ~360 times today:\n\n```ts\n// tenant-model-list.spec.ts — new block\nconst UNLISTED_BY_DEFAULT = readFileSync('unlisted-tenant-models.txt'); // frozen 2026-08-12\nit('a NEW model with a businessId is either scoped or explicitly exempted', () => {\n  const withBusinessId = [...schemaModelsWithBusinessId()];\n  const surprises = withBusinessId.filter(\n    (m) => !listed.includes(m) && !UNLISTED_BY_DEFAULT.includes(m),\n  );\n  expect(surprises,\n    'these models have a businessId, are not in BUSINESS_ID_MODELS, and are not on ' +\n    'the frozen backlog — so they are unscoped by accident. Add them to the set, or ' +\n    'to unlisted-tenant-models.txt with a one-line reason.',\n  ).toEqual([]);\n});\n```\n\nThe frozen file *is* the honest backlog, in the exact shape the doorless-screen allowlist already takes. This is half a day and it is the highest-leverage half day in the spec: it converts \"unscoped by default\" into \"unscoped only on purpose\", permanently.\n\n### 3.4 The migration\n\n`packages/db/prisma/migrations/20260812090000_extraction_and_import_runs/migration.sql` — generated by `prisma migrate dev`, then hand-annotated in house style with *why* each unique exists (the `20260808140000` migration is the template: 27 lines of comment, 3 of SQL).\n\nNo expression indexes are needed, so `migrate diff` will be truthful about this one.\n\n---\n\n## 4. The three layers\n\n### 4.1 PDF bank statements\n\n#### MANUAL — `/app/finance/reconciliation` (exists, `page.tsx`)\n\n1. `page.tsx:177` — `accept` gains `.pdf,application/pdf`.\n2. `page.tsx:185` — the title text gains PDF.\n3. New panel `components/extracted-statement-review.tsx`, rendered when the import response carries `needsReview: true`. It shows the extracted rows in a table, the opening/closing balance docling read out of the header, the arithmetic, and two buttons: **Import these 47 rows** / **Cancel**. It calls `confirmStatementExtraction(businessId, accountId, extractionRunId)`.\n\nThat is the whole manual layer. A person can do everything KEY can.\n\n#### SMART — the sweep, moved off `@Cron`\n\n`statement-source.service.ts`:\n\n- `:92` `STATEMENT_EXT` gains `pdf`.\n- `:153` `looksLikeStatement(filename, content: string)` → `looksLikeStatement(filename, buffer: Buffer)`. First four bytes `%PDF` ⇒ statement candidate, no text decode at all.\n- `:196` and `:243` stop calling `.toString('utf8')` and pass the `Buffer` to a new `bankImport.ingestFile()`.\n- **`:389` `@Cron('0 5 * * *')` is deleted.** In its place, a BullMQ repeatable registered in `onModuleInit`, copying `ai/queue.service.ts:113-132` exactly:\n\n```ts\nawait this.sweepQueue.add('statement-sweep', {}, {\n  jobId: 'statement-sweep:nightly',           // the job key — one fire per schedule, N replicas\n  repeat: { pattern: '0 5 * * *', tz: 'America/Port_of_Spain' },\n  removeOnComplete: { count: 50 }, removeOnFail: { count: 50 },\n});\n```\n\nThe `tz` is not decoration: `ai/queue.service.ts:127` hardcodes `'UTC'`, so \"5am\" is currently 1am in Trinidad. Fix it here for the queue this spec owns and say so; do not silently change the other one.\n\nThe existing quiet-source health row (`:288-372`, writing `KeyCortexMemory` with `type: 'integration_health'`) is reused unchanged and gains one case: **a PDF that extracted but failed its balance check writes a health row immediately**, not after 45 days, because that is a specific detected fault rather than silence.\n\n#### AI — no new tool\n\n`reconcile_sweep_statements` (`flow-tool-registry.ts:1413-1429`, handler at `flow-orchestrator.service.ts:2550-2569`) already covers this. Its description says \"This reads Drive or Gmail — it does not contact the bank\", which remains true, and `statement-source-tools.spec.ts:85` pins that sentence.\n\n**KEY gets no tool that accepts a file, deliberately.** A tool call carries JSON, not bytes. Inventing `import_statement_file(base64)` would put megabytes through a model's context to reach a service the HTTP endpoint already reaches. The tool is the sweep; the door is the upload.\n\n#### The parser work itself\n\n`bank-statement-parsers.ts` — pure, no I/O, unit-testable, matching the file's existing character:\n\n```ts\nexport type StatementFormat = 'ofx' | 'qif' | 'mt940' | 'csv' | 'pdf';\n\n/** Sniff BYTES first. A PDF is not text and must never reach a text parser. */\nexport function detectFormatFromBuffer(buffer: Buffer): StatementFormat | 'binary_unknown' {\n  if (buffer.subarray(0, 5).toString('latin1') === '%PDF-') return 'pdf';\n  // A NUL byte in the first 4KB means this is not a text statement. Returning\n  // 'csv' here is what produced \"your statement has no Date column\" for every\n  // binary file the product has ever been handed.\n  if (buffer.subarray(0, 4096).includes(0x00)) return 'binary_unknown';\n  return detectFormat(buffer.toString('utf8'));\n}\n\nexport interface BalanceProof {\n  opening: Prisma.Decimal; closing: Prisma.Decimal; sumOfRows: Prisma.Decimal; agrees: boolean;\n}\n\n/** Tables + markdown from docling → statement rows. Pure. */\nexport function parsePdfStatement(\n  tables: ExtractedTable[], markdown: string,\n): ParseOutcome & { balanceProof: BalanceProof | null };\n```\n\n`ParseOutcome` (`:57-63`) gains `balanceProof: BalanceProof | null`; the other three parsers return `null` and `bank-statement-parsers.spec.ts` is updated to say so.\n\n**The balance proof is the control that makes PDF import trustworthy.** Every Trinidad bank statement prints an opening and a closing balance. If `sumOfRows !== closing - opening`, the extraction dropped or duplicated a row, and *we know it arithmetically* rather than hoping. `BankImportService.ingestFile` refuses to insert when `agrees === false` and returns `{ needsReview: true, extractionRunId, balanceProof, rows }`. The user confirms on the screen, or does not.\n\n`exactDedupe` stays `false` for PDF — there is no FITID — and the existing UI warning at `page.tsx:95-99` already says the right thing.\n\n### 4.2 Historical import\n\n#### MANUAL — `/app/accounting/import` (new page)\n\n`apps/web/src/app/app/accounting/import/page.tsx`. `/app/accounting` already exists (`nav-config.ts:189`, \"Accounting sync\") and is **push-only** today — \"Push invoices and customers to QuickBooks or Xero\". This is its missing half.\n\nThree panels:\n1. **Start** — provider picker (QuickBooks / Xero / Stripe / PayPal / Upload a file), scope checkboxes, a \"history from\" date. Calls `startImportBackfill()`.\n2. **Runs** — a table of `ImportRun` rows with live counts. `recordsFetched / recordsStaged / recordsSkipped / recordsFailed`, and the run's `error` where present.\n3. **Review and apply** — for a `STAGED` run, the `ImportRecord` rows grouped by entity type, each showing `raw` beside `normalised`, with **Apply** and **Discard**. Apply calls `applyImportRun()`.\n\nThe nav gets `{ label: \"Import history\", href: \"/app/accounting/import\", icon: Download }` under the Finance section beside `Accounting sync`, so the screen is not doorless (86 of 176 static routes currently are).\n\n#### SMART\n\nDeterministic, no LLM, and — importantly — **no new entry in the SMART trigger/action registry**, which advertises 25 actions against 6 implemented. Adding a 26th advertised action to a list that already lies is the wrong move; that registry is Wave-0 work and this spec does not depend on it.\n\nWhat is automatic here:\n- The BullMQ worker pages through the provider, checkpointing `cursor` after **every page**, so a token expiry or a 429 resumes rather than restarts.\n- Retries are BullMQ's: `attempts: 5, backoff: { type: 'exponential', delay: 5000 }`.\n- A run that reaches `STAGED` and is not applied within 7 days writes an `integration_health` `KeyCortexMemory` row, reusing `reportHealth` (`statement-source.service.ts:288-372`) verbatim — \"148 records from QuickBooks are staged and waiting for you to confirm them\".\n\n#### AI — three tools\n\nRegistered in `flow-tool-registry.ts` (`FlowTool` interface at `:23-43`), handlers as new `case` clauses in `flow-orchestrator.service.ts` `executeToolAction`, resolved via `private getHistoricalImport() { return this.moduleRef.get(HistoricalImportService, { strict: false }); }` beside `getStatementSources()` at `:456`.\n\n| name | family | riskLevel | riskTier | manualEquivalentRoute | changedEntities |\n|---|---|---|---|---|---|\n| `import_list_runs` | `read` | low | 1 | `/app/accounting/import` | — |\n| `import_start_backfill` | `execute` | medium | **2** | `/app/accounting/import` | `importRun` |\n| `import_apply_run` | `execute` | high | **3** | `/app/accounting/import` | `invoice`, `payment`, `contact`, `chartOfAccount`, `financialTransaction` |\n\n**Tier reasoning, stated so it can be argued with.** `import_start_backfill` writes only into `import_runs` and `import_records` — its own tables, reversible by deleting a run, invisible to the ledger. That is tier 2, the same tier as `reconcile_sweep_statements`. `import_apply_run` writes invoices, payments and a balanced opening journal in bulk, and a person cannot undo it in a minute. That is tier 3, requiring approval — the same tier as `send_message_with_approval`. Every outbound tool in the product is T3+; a bulk write into the general ledger is not less serious than an email.\n\n**`check-tool-routes` passes without an exemption.** `t.name.split('_')[0]` is `\"import\"`; `/app/accounting/import` lowercased with `/` and `-` stripped is `appaccountingimport`, which contains `import` (`tool-route-audit.ts:118-123`). The page imports its mutations from `@/lib/client`, which `isApiLayerFile` recognises, so `canWrite` (`:531-541`) resolves. No `CROSS_DOMAIN_ROUTES` entry, no `MANUAL_PARITY_EXEMPTIONS` entry.\n\n**The handlers return what happened.** Copying the discipline of `statement-source-tools.spec.ts:96-113`: `import_start_backfill` returns `{ runId: run.id, status: run.status, queued: true }` from the service's own row, never a literal, and never `success: true`.\n\n### 4.3 The Gmail attachment gap, closed cheaply\n\n`ingestion-orchestrator.service.ts:81` writes `attachments` as JSON. `gmail-ingestion.service.ts:490-512` fills it with `{ name, mimeType, sizeBytes, externalId }` — metadata only. `commerce/gmail.service.ts:604-619` (`getAttachment`) fetches the bytes and correctly handles base64url. **Both halves exist and nothing joins them.**\n\nThe join: `StatementSourceService.sweepGmail` already calls `getAttachment` (`:242`). Extend the same call to the *ingestion* path so an emailed invoice PDF reaches `DocumentExtractionService` instead of being recorded as a filename. Two days, and it is the difference between \"we saw an attachment\" and \"we read it\".\n\n---\n\n## 5. What the adapters actually do\n\n```ts\nexport interface BackfillPage {\n  raw: unknown[];\n  nextCursor: Json | null;    // null ⇒ done\n  rateLimitDelayMs?: number;  // honour the provider's own backoff header\n}\n\nexport interface BackfillAdapter {\n  readonly provider: ImportProvider;\n  readonly scopes: ImportScope[];\n  fetchPage(businessId: string, scope: ImportScope, cursor: Json | null, since?: Date): Promise<BackfillPage>;\n  /** Pure. No I/O, no Prisma. This is the function the unit tests exercise. */\n  normalise(scope: ImportScope, raw: unknown): NormalisedRecord;\n}\n```\n\n`fetchPage` calls the existing connector classes. Each needs **one** new public method, and nothing else:\n\n| Adapter | Reuses | New public method to add |\n|---|---|---|\n| Stripe | `stripe.connector.ts:217-240` `stripeRequest` | `listChargesPage(businessId, { startingAfter, createdGte, limit })` — the existing `fetchLiveStripeTransactions` (`:319`) has no `starting_after` and caps at 100 |\n| PayPal | `paypal.connector.ts:230` `getAccessToken` | `listTransactionsPage(businessId, { startDate, endDate, page })` against `/v1/reporting/transactions` (PayPal caps a query at 31 days — the adapter walks months) |\n| QuickBooks | `quickbooks.connector.ts:382-412` query pattern | `queryPage(businessId, entity, { startPosition, maxResults })` — QBO uses `STARTPOSITION`/`MAXRESULTS`, not a cursor |\n| Xero | `xero.connector.ts:217` `xeroHeaders` | `listPage(businessId, endpoint, { page, ifModifiedSince })` — Xero pages at 100 and wants `If-Modified-Since` |\n| File | `DocumentExtractionService` + `csv-parse` | — (this is the WiPay path, and the escape hatch for any provider we do not support) |\n\n`normalise` is pure and lives in the adapter file. That is deliberate: it is the only part of an importer that is worth unit-testing exhaustively, and it needs no database, no network and no Nest.\n\n### The scopes, and why they are these six\n\nThe migration an accountant actually performs, not a mirror of the competitor's database:\n\n1. **`chart_of_accounts`** → `ChartOfAccount` rows with the provider's id in `externalId`. No mapping designer (§8.3).\n2. **`opening_trial_balance`** → **one** balanced journal at the cutover date, emitted as `import.trial_balance.staged` and posted by finance's listener through `PostingService`, with `sourceType: 'import'`, `sourceId: runId`, `kind: 'opening_balance'`. `buildExternalRef` (`posting.service.ts:48-56`) makes re-posting a `ConflictException` rather than a doubled ledger.\n3. **`contacts`** → `EntityResolutionService.resolveContact`, plus a `ContactExternalMapping` row (`quickbooks.connector.ts:373-377` already writes these).\n4. **`open_invoices`** → only unpaid/partially-paid AR at the cutover. Paid history is not re-created; it is already in the opening balance.\n5. **`open_bills`** → the same for AP.\n6. **`payments`** → Stripe/PayPal charges and refunds, so reconciliation has something to match against.\n\n---\n\n## 6. Tests, and the negative control for each\n\nThe repo's standard is that a control must be spelled like a real defect and be shown to fire. Every item below names what it would catch.\n\n### 6.1 `document-extraction.service.spec.ts` (unit, stubbed `fetch`)\n\n- Given a docling response with `md_content: \"\"` and no tables ⇒ **throws** `UnprocessableEntityException`, and an `ExtractionRun` row exists with `parser: 'refused'`.\n- **Negative control:** the same test with a response containing one table of three rows asserts it does **not** throw and returns `cellCount > 0`. Without this, a guard written as `throw new Error()` unconditionally would pass the first assertion forever. Both assertions must move together.\n- A response with 40 tables returns **40** tables, and the largest one is returned whole. This is the assertion that would have caught `document-parsing.service.ts:77` (`slice(0, 10)`) and `:79` (`slice(0, 2000)`).\n\n### 6.2 `bank-statement-parsers.spec.ts` — extend the existing file\n\n- `detectFormatFromBuffer(Buffer.from('%PDF-1.7…'))` ⇒ `'pdf'`.\n- `detectFormatFromBuffer` on a buffer containing a NUL byte ⇒ `'binary_unknown'`, **not** `'csv'`. This is the exact assertion that would have caught the current behaviour.\n- `parsePdfStatement(fixtureTables, fixtureMarkdown)` on a real Republic Bank docling response (checked in as `__fixtures__/republic-statement.docling.json`, account number and names redacted) ⇒ 47 rows, correct signs, `balanceProof.agrees === true`.\n- **Negative control — the one that matters:** delete one row from the fixture's tables and assert `balanceProof.agrees === false` and `sumOfRows` differs by exactly that row's amount. The control is spelled as the real defect — a dropped line in an extracted table — and it fires on exactly that.\n\n### 6.3 `apps/server/test/bank-statement-pdf-ingest.integration.test.ts` (real Postgres, stubbed `fetch`)\n\nExtends the shape of `apps/server/test/bank-statement-ingest.integration.test.ts` — real `db` from `@keyflow/db`, prefixed fixtures, `cleanup()`. Only the docling HTTP boundary is stubbed. (Per the recurring lesson: a mock cannot disagree with you; the DB can.)\n\n- A PDF whose balance proof agrees inserts rows with correct `Decimal` signs and dates.\n- Re-ingesting the identical PDF inserts **zero** — the `ExtractionRun` unique on `sha256` means docling is not even called the second time (`fetch` stub call count is 1, not 2).\n- A PDF whose proof disagrees inserts **zero rows** and returns `needsReview: true`.\n- **Negative control:** the disagreeing PDF, confirmed via `confirmStatementExtraction`, inserts all rows — proving the refusal is a gate and not an inability.\n- Business B cannot import into business A's account by naming its id, matching the existing test's fourth case.\n\n### 6.4 `historical-import.idempotency.integration.test.ts` (real Postgres)\n\n- Stage the same 50-record Stripe page twice ⇒ `import_records` count stays 50, `recordsSkipped` = 50 on the second run. The constraint doing the work is `@@unique([businessId, provider, entityType, externalId])`.\n- `applyRun` twice ⇒ `Invoice` count unchanged the second time.\n- **Negative control:** apply a record with a *different* `externalId` and assert the `Invoice` count **does** increase by one. Without it, \"count unchanged\" is also what a broken apply that writes nothing produces — which is the `sync-engine.service.ts:298` defect wearing a green badge.\n\n### 6.5 `module-graph.spec.ts` (source-level, both new modules)\n\n- `DocumentExtractionModule`'s `imports` array is empty.\n- `HistoricalImportModule`'s `imports` array names exactly `['DocumentExtractionModule']`.\n- No file under `apps/server/src` outside `modules/historical-import/` contains `from '.*historical-import.module'` except `app.module.ts`.\n- **Negative control:** the parser is run against an inline fixture string containing `imports: [FinanceModule, DocumentExtractionModule]` and must report it. This is the same defect class the existing `check-tool-routes` guards against with `assertParityCheckWorks` (`tool-route-audit.ts:308`) — a detector nobody proved can detect.\n\n### 6.6 `core/event-bus/event-wiring.spec.ts` — no new entries in `KNOWN_DEAD`\n\n`import.trial_balance.staged` and `import.trial_balance.posted` must each resolve to ≥1 emitter and ≥1 listener. **They must not be added to `KNOWN_DEAD` (`:50`).** The product has 11 dead listeners; a spec that ships two more with an allowlist entry has learned nothing.\n\n### 6.7 `apps/server/test/app-module-boots.integration.test.ts` — already covers the `moduleRef` risk\n\nThe `strict: false` lookup for `HistoricalImportService` fails at *call* time, not boot time, which is precisely the failure mode this suite is weakest against. So add one assertion to `import-tools.spec.ts` in the style of `statement-source-tools.spec.ts:225-231`: every `import_*` tool has a `case '<name>':` in `flow-orchestrator.service.ts`, **and** `historical-import.module.ts` is present in `app.module.ts`'s import list. Source-level, one second, closes the gap the container escape hatch opens.\n\n### 6.8 `tenant-model-list.spec.ts` — the third direction (§3.3)\n\n**Negative control:** the frozen `unlisted-tenant-models.txt` is checked with a fixture model name that is not in the schema, asserting the reader rejects a stale entry — the same drift that let `ConversationThread` and `MessageThread` sit in `BUSINESS_ID_MODELS` naming nothing.\n\n---\n\n## 7. Adopt, do not build\n\n| Need | Adopt | Licence | Status | Note |\n|---|---|---|---|---|\n| PDF/scan → markdown + tables | **docling-serve** `quay.io/docling-project/docling-serve` | MIT | **Already running** — `docker-compose.production.yml:170-181` | **Pin the tag.** It is `:latest` today, the same defect the capability map flagged for Chatwoot (`:latest-ce` bundles a commercially-licensed `enterprise/` dir). Pin to a released tag and record it. Also add `DOCLING_URL` to `.env.example` — it is missing. |\n| Durable timers, retries, one-fire-per-schedule | **BullMQ** `^5.76.8` | MIT | Installed, used at `ai/queue.service.ts:56` | Repeatable jobs with a `jobId` fire once across N workers by construction. This is the leader election the product does not have. |\n| CSV | **csv-parse** `^5.4.0` | MIT | Installed, `bank-import.service.ts:2` | — |\n| XLSX | **ExcelJS** `^4.4.0` | MIT | Installed, `crm-import.service.ts:3` | Route the importer here. Then delete `apps/server/src/types/xlsx.d.ts` and `key-cortex-document.service.ts:828`, which imports a package in no lockfile behind a hand-written stub. |\n| PDF text fallback when docling is down | **pdf-parse** `^1.1.1` | MIT | Installed, `crm-import.service.ts:5` | Text only — it cannot do tables, so it is a fallback for `purpose: 'generic'` and is **never** used for a bank statement. A statement with no table structure must refuse, not guess. |\n| Object storage for staged files | **@aws-sdk/client-s3** → MinIO | Apache-2.0 / (MinIO AGPL, sidecar only) | `core/object-storage/objectStorage.ts` | Unmodified image, S3 API over the network. Never fork. |\n\n**Considered and declined**, so nobody re-opens it:\n\n- **Nango** (Apache-2.0 core) — its value is OAuth for the other 18 connectors. The four providers this spec needs already have working credential storage (`ConnectorCredentialsService`) and working API clients in-repo. Adopting Nango here means rewriting four working clients to gain nothing. It is the right answer for a *different* task.\n- **Plaid / Ocrolus / statement-parsing SaaS** — no Trinidad coverage worth the integration, and the statements leave the box. The whole reason `bank-statement-parsers.ts:11-16` exists is that the aggregator answer does not apply to this market.\n- **`ofx-js`, `node-ofx`, `mt940-js`** — the in-repo parsers are 40 lines each, sniff from content, and dedupe on FITID. They are better than the packages.\n- **Airbyte** — platform is Elastic License v2; it forbids providing it as a managed service, which is what this is.\n\n---\n\n## 8. What I am deliberately not doing\n\n### 8.1 Not building a WiPay backfill\n\n`wipay.connector.ts:112` is a payment-*request* plugin endpoint. WiPay publishes no transaction-list API. Pretending otherwise would produce a connector that returns zero and reports success — the exact `sync-engine.service.ts:298-334` defect this spec is written against. **WiPay history arrives as the merchant-portal CSV export through `file-backfill.adapter.ts`**, and the UI says so in the provider picker: *\"WiPay — export a CSV from your WiPay dashboard and upload it here. WiPay does not offer a history API.\"* One honest sentence beats a fake connector.\n\n### 8.2 Not building Intuit / Xero OAuth refresh\n\nBoth connectors take a pasted token (`quickbooks.connector.ts:29-31`, `xero.connector.ts:30`). An Intuit access token lives ~60 minutes; a 24-month backfill does not. **The mitigation is architectural, not authentication:** `ImportRun.cursor` is written after every page, so an expiry fails one page, BullMQ backs off, and the run resumes exactly where it stopped once a human re-pastes the token. A 401 sets `ImportRun.error = 'The QuickBooks token expired — paste a fresh one on Connect and press Resume'` and writes an `integration_health` row, rather than a silent zero.\n\nProper OAuth refresh for both is roughly 5 days and is a separate piece of work, correctly sized with Nango in scope.\n\n### 8.3 Not building a chart-of-accounts mapping designer\n\nImport the provider's COA as-is with `externalId` preserved; the accountant remaps in `/app/finance/settings`, which exists. A mapping UI is a week, nobody has asked for it, and the businesses this serves have 30-account charts.\n\n### 8.4 Not importing 24 months of journal detail\n\nOnly: chart of accounts, **one** opening trial balance journal at cutover, open AR, open AP, and payments for reconciliation. Importing a competitor's journal detail means importing their posting rules, and a wrong journal in your own ledger is worse than a correct opening balance. This is what an accountant does at a migration, and it is a fraction of the work of the thing people imagine.\n\n### 8.5 Not sending bytes through KEY\n\nNo tool accepts a file. See §4.1.\n\n### 8.6 Not touching the other 51 `setInterval` schedulers\n\nOne `@Cron` is converted — `statement-source.service.ts:389` — because this spec rewrites that method anyway. The other 51 are a named, separate piece of work (capability map §3.3 item 27, 3–4 weeks). Converting one as a side effect of a feature is how the pattern spreads; converting fifty is a project.\n\n### 8.7 Not adding a SMART-layer trigger or action\n\nThe registry advertises 25 actions against 6 implemented and logs `outcome: 'success'` either way. Adding to it before it is deduplicated makes the lie larger.
