A shipped template that cannot run is the same lie in a friendlier wrapper.\n\n### 5.3 Negative controls\n\nEach is a fixture through `auditAutomationVocabulary`, asserting the finding **appears** — spelled like a real defect, not described in a comment. The repo's own idiom (`event-wiring.spec.ts:135-158`) is to prefer a *property* canary over one pinned to a specific bug, because a detector pinned to a defect stops working the day the defect is fixed. Controls NC-1..NC-4 are property canaries; NC-5 is the one exception and it is deliberate.\n\n- **NC-1** — registry with `{ type:'send_sms', status:'implemented' }` + executor source containing no `case 'send_sms'` → must yield `ADVERTISED_UNIMPLEMENTED`. Asserts the *inclusion direction* still detects.\n- **NC-2** — executor source containing `case 'secret_action':` absent from the registry → must yield `UNDECLARED_CASE`. Asserts the *reverse direction* still detects. Without this, the cheapest way to green the build is to delete registry entries.\n- **NC-3** — action declaring `configFields: []` whose case body reads `action.notificationType` → must yield `UNDECLARED_CONFIG_FIELD`. This is §1.3(a) spelled as a test.\n- **NC-4** — a `renderCapabilityPrompt` stub returning a hand-typed constant → set-equality must fail. Without it, `expect(promptTriggers).toEqual(implementedTriggers)` passes trivially when both derive from the same array; the control proves the assertion is checking the *referent* (the prompt actually spliced into the controller) and not the shape.\n- **NC-5** — a trigger marked `implemented` with `origin:'event'` whose name is not in the analyser's live set → must yield `NO_EMITTER`. Uses `contact.inactive`, which today has no emitter; if it ever gains one, the control must be repointed, and the assertion message says so.\n\n### 5.4 Behavioural tests, against a real database\n\nNot mocks. A mock cannot disagree with the thing being tested, and this whole item is about a system agreeing with itself while being wrong. `apps/server/test/` already runs against real Postgres in CI.\n\n`apps/server/test/automation-run-ledger.e2e.test.ts`:\n\n1. Playbook with `[{type:'add_tag',tag:'x'},{type:'send_sms'}]`, fire `contact.created` → `AutomationRun.status === 'PARTIAL'`, action 0 `PERFORMED`, action 1 `UNIMPLEMENTED`. **Today this run logs `outcome:'success'`.**\n2. `send_email` with no `notificationType` → `MISCONFIGURED`, not `PERFORMED` and not silently skipped.\n3. `POST /playbooks` with `trigger:'schedule.daily'` while retired → 400, no row written. Then with `trigger:'contact.created'` → 201.\n4. `POST …/key/build` with `{ proposedFlow: { triggerEvent: '' } }` → 400. Today it writes a row.\n5. Two concurrent `executePlaybooks` calls on the same event → one `AutomationRun`, one set of effects. This is the replica-safety assertion and it must run before any second replica exists.\n6. Condition `contact.is_vip` (advertised at `automation-constants.ts:123`, unimplemented) → `SKIPPED` with `skipReason: 'CONDITION_UNKNOWN'`, distinguishable from `CONDITION_UNMET`.\n\n### 5.5 The derivation script\n\nKept in the repo at `apps/server/scripts/automation-vocabulary-report.ts` so the numbers in §1 are re-derivable rather than quoted. It prints the five-surface table and the 32→16→8 ladder, and calls `analyseEventWiring()` for the emitter column rather than grepping — grepping is what gave me the wrong answer for `invoice.sent` on the first pass.\n\n---\n\n## 6. What to build first, and in what order\n\n### 6.1 Tranche 0 — the honest registry (13 days, ships alone)\n\n| | Work | Days |\n|---|---|---:|\n| 1 | `@keyflow/shared/automation` package; both prompts generated; both web builders repointed; `playbook-panel.tsx:10-97` deleted | 5 |\n| 2 | Server + web gates, five negative controls | 3 |\n| 3 | Run ledger: 2 models, migration, `executeAction` return type, runs endpoint, run-history tab, health strip repointed | 4 |\n| 4 | Condition honesty: registry-gate the 16→7, `CONDITION_UNKNOWN` | 1 |\n\nAfter Tranche 0 the product advertises 8 triggers and 6 actions, all of which work, and cannot regress. **That is the deliverable.** Everything below adds capability; this makes the existing claim true.\n\n### 6.2 Tranche 1 — the actions whose implementation is already injected (3 days)\n\nRanked by demand (shipped-template usage, counted from `AUTOMATION_TEMPLATES`) against cost (does the code exist, and does it need a new module edge):\n\n| Action | Templates using it | Implementation | Edge | Days |\n|---|---:|---|---|---:|\n| `log_timeline` | 0 | **already implemented** as `LOG_EVENT` at `automation-executor.service.ts:476-488` under a name advertised nowhere. Rename. | none | 0.25 |\n| `add_note` | 0 | `crm.addNote` (`crm.service.ts:1673`); `CrmService` injected at `:26` | none | 0.25 |\n| `update_contact` | 0 | `crm.updateContact` (`crm.service.ts:637`); injected | none | 0.5 |\n| `schedule_followup` | 2 | `crm.addTask` with a `dueDate` — the `create_task` case at `:216-231` already does this | none | 0.25 |\n| **`send_notification`** | **6** | `NotificationsService.create` (`notifications.service.ts:12`) | **`NotificationsModule` already imported at `flow.module.ts:19` and exports it at `notifications.module.ts:25` — inject only** | 0.5 |\n| `send_email` config fix | 10 | not a new action; wire `notificationType` through `configFields` | none | 1 |\n\n`send_notification` is the highest-demand missing action in the product — six of the twenty shipped templates (`daily-summary`, `weekly-review`, `monthly-report`, `overdue-followup`, `payment-received-thankyou`, `lead-score-alert`) are built on it — and it costs half a day and zero new module edges. It is the first thing to build.\n\nThe `send_email` config fix is not a new action and is the single highest-value line in this section: ten of twenty templates use `send_email`, it is already implemented, and today every human-built instance is a no-op.\n\n### 6.3 Tranche 2 — `delay`, and the schedule triggers (8 days)\n\n`delay` is the only action the manual builder gives a config field for (`playbook-editor.tsx:341-353`) and it is unimplemented. It is also the one that cannot be done with a timer.\n\n**BullMQ, not `setInterval`.** `apps/server/package.json:42` already has `bullmq@^5.76.8`; `flow-runner.service.ts:283` already asks for it in a comment while `:284` caps waits at 30 seconds, so \"wait 3 days then follow up\" completes instantly. Redis is a first-class service in both compose files and in CI (`ci-cd.yml`, the `redis:7-alpine` service).\n\n- Queue `automation-actions`, constructed in `FlowModule` from `REDIS_CLIENT` (§2.2 — not `QueueService`, to avoid the `AiModule` edge).\n- `delay` does not sleep. It writes `AutomationRun.status='WAITING'`, `resumeFrom=<next ordinal>`, `resumeAt`, and enqueues with `jobId: 'automation-resume:' + runId + ':' + ordinal` and `delay: totalMs`. **The job key is derived from persisted state, so N replicas enqueue the same key and BullMQ keeps one.**\n- The worker reloads the run by id, re-reads the playbook, and resumes at `resumeFrom`. No in-memory continuation; all state is in Postgres, none in a module field.\n- `schedule.daily` / `.weekly` / `.monthly` become `origin:'schedule'` triggers: one repeatable per business per cadence, `jobId: 'automation-schedule:' + businessId + ':' + cadence`, `repeat: { pattern, tz }` — exactly the shape at `ai/queue.service.ts:113-131`, which already upserts by removing the prior repeatable key. Three more shipped templates become real.\n\nThis tranche is also the first distributed-lock-free scheduler in the repo, and it is the template for retiring the other 52.\n\n### 6.4 Tranche 3 — the nine triggers that are one line away (2 days)\n\n`quote.sent`, `quote.accepted`, `quote.viewed`, `payment.received`, `booking.completed`, `booking.cancelled`, `campaign.sent`, `form.submitted`, `post.published` all have live emitters (§1.2, via the repo analyser) and no `executePlaybooks` call. Each needs an `@OnEvent` and a payload→context mapping. `post.published` needs only two lines added to the listener that already exists at `:804`.\n\nAfter Tranche 3: **20 of 32 triggers implemented** (8 + 9 + 3 scheduled), and 11 of 25 actions.\n\n### 6.5 What gets retired instead of built\n\nMarked `status:'retired'` in the registry, removed from every menu and both prompts, existing rows disabled by the §3.3 migration with an explanation on the capability panel.\n\n**14 actions** — `send_sms` (no SMS provider is connected anywhere in the server), `notify_staff`, `move_pipeline`, `update_segment`, `enroll_campaign`, `request_review`, `create_invoice`, `send_quote_reminder`, `assign_staff`, `create_content_draft`, `create_booking_followup`, `schedule_reengagement`, `remove_tag` (advertised on exactly one surface, `automation.controller.ts:182`), and `LOG_EVENT` (folded into `log_timeline`).\n\n**12 triggers** — `contact.inactive`, `contact.no_activity_30d`, `lead.scored`, `quote.not_accepted_xdays`, `recurring.failed`, `booking.scheduled`, `booking.reminder`, `campaign.opened`, `campaign.not_opened_14d`, `subscriber.joined`, `segment.changed`, `staff.assignment_missing`.\n\nThose twelve share a property worth naming: **none of them is an event.** Each is a derived state — \"no activity for 30 days\", \"not opened in 14 days\", \"24 hours before\" — and needs a scheduled sweep that compares a clock against a table, not a listener. They are the *next* work item (they are also, not coincidentally, the shape of the contract-renewal sweep at capability-map §3.1 item 2), and they need Tranche 2's queue to exist first. They stay in the registry as `status:'planned'` so the gate records the intent while the UI advertises nothing.\n\n**9 conditions** — the unimplemented half of `CONDITION_OPTIONS` (`automation-constants.ts:117-136`), including `contact.is_vip` and `contact.has_overdue_and_booking`.\n\nFour shipped templates reference retired vocabulary and are removed from `AUTOMATION_TEMPLATES` in the same edit: `lead-nurture` (`enroll_campaign`), `booking-followup` (`request_review`), `booking-cancelled-reengagement` (`schedule_reengagement`), `quote-followup-reminder` (`send_quote_reminder`).\n\n### 6.6 Effort\n\n| Tranche | Days |\n|---|---:|\n| 0 — registry, gates, ledger, conditions | 13 |\n| 1 — five actions + the `send_email` config fix | 3 |\n| 2 — `delay` + schedule triggers on BullMQ | 8 |\n| 3 — nine one-line triggers | 2 |\n| KEY tools (2 new + 1 schema repair) | 1 |\n| Retirements, migration, template cleanup | 2 |\n| **Total** | **29 days** |\n\nTranche 0 alone (13 days) is shippable and is the item as scoped. The capability map sized the registry at 3 days; that is item 1 of Tranche 0 in isolation, and it is correct — the other 10 days are the ledger and the gate, without which the registry is a fourth list to drift.\n\n### 6.7 Adopt, not build\n\n**Nothing new is installed.** The two dependencies this needs — `bullmq@^5.76.8` (`apps/server/package.json:42`, MIT) and `zod@^3.24.0` (`:75`, MIT) — are already present, and `bullmq` is already used with repeatable jobs and idempotent job ids at `ai/queue.service.ts:113-131`. Redis is already a service in `docker-compose.yml`, `docker-compose.production.yml` and CI.\n\nWhat **not** to adopt, consistent with capability-map §3.4: **n8n is not open source** (Sustainable Use License, explicitly restricts hosting for third parties — which is what a SaaS is). **Windmill is AGPL-3.0** — sidecar only. **Temporal** (MIT) and **Trigger.dev v3** (Apache-2.0) are the honest answers *if* the engine outgrows BullMQ; it has not, and adopting a durable-execution platform to fix a vocabulary drift would be a category error.\n\n---\n\n## 7. What this deliberately does not do\n\n- **Does not touch the flow-canvas engine.** `AutomationFlow` / `FlowRunner` / `FlowActionRegistry` is a second engine with its own six actions. The gate *checks* it (§5.1, last two rows — including `send_message` returning `{sent:true}` with no sender at `flow-action.registry.ts:160`), but no action is built there and the two engines are not merged. Merging them is a separate item and a larger one.\n- **Does not build the twelve derived-state triggers.** They need scheduled sweeps, not listeners (§6.5). They are `planned` in the registry, invisible in the UI.\n- **Does not build `send_sms`.** There is no SMS provider in the server. Building the action first is how `send_email` ended up implemented-and-unreachable.\n- **Does not fix the other 52 `setInterval` schedulers.** This spec creates zero new timers and moves two things to BullMQ. `DeliveryQueueService` (`communications/delivery-queue.service.ts:64`) — which drains the `OutboundDelivery` rows `send_whatsapp` and `send_email_campaign` write — is still a `setInterval` and still double-fires on a second replica. Named here so it is not mistaken for solved.\n- **Does not replace `ActivityService`.** Activity rows keep being written for the feed; they are derived from the run row instead of being the only record. Its `(prisma.client as any).activity` cast and swallowed `catch` (`flow/activity.service.ts:34-62`) are a separate defect, and `Activity` is missing from `BUSINESS_ID_MODELS` — flagged, not fixed here.\n- **Does not add a capabilities HTTP endpoint.** The web imports the registry at build time from `@keyflow/shared/automation`. An endpoint would be a second source of truth reachable over the network, which is the problem this document exists to remove.\n- **Does not delete a single user's playbook.** Rows are disabled with a reason. Deleting a record of what a customer asked for destroys the best available evidence about what to build next — which is precisely what `@@index([businessId, actionType, status, startedAt])` exists to capture."

Also `ai.listener.ts:14` — `private readonly events: Array<{event, payload}> = []`, appended on all 44 `@OnEvent` handlers and never trimmed. Both are module-field state; the second is an unbounded one. Neither is in scope here, but the new module must not reproduce either.\n\n### 1.4 Where the context string is consumed\n\n`businessGraph.buildContextString(snapshot)` at four sites:\n\n- `flow-orchestrator.service.ts:1292–1294` (chat turn)\n- `flow-orchestrator.service.ts:1672–1673` (streaming turn)\n- `intent-parser.service.ts:143–144`\n- `planner.service.ts:82–83`\n\n`getSnapshot` itself has **18 call sites across 10 files** (`ai.controller.ts:283,837`, `graph-actions.controller.ts:50,80,261`, `strategic-intelligence.service.ts:134,464,562,696,858`, `pro-auto-monitor.service.ts:145`, `profile-intelligence.service.ts:104`, `workspace-recommendations.service.ts:37`, plus the four above and `flow-orchestrator.service.ts:3121`). **This is why the plan below does not rewrite `BusinessGraphService`.**\n\n### 1.5 What already exists and must be reused, not rebuilt\n\n| Need | Reuse | Path |\n|---|---|---|\n| Trial balance / account balances | `LedgerBalanceService.getTrialBalance` — one `groupBy` + one `findMany` | `finance/ledger-balance.service.ts:59–93` |\n| AR aging, basis-aware, ledger-reconciled | `ReceivablesService.getAging` | `finance/receivables.service.ts:220` |\n| Cash, bills due, tax reserved | `FinanceOverviewService.getOverview` (already Redis-cached 60s at `:363`) | `finance/finance-overview.service.ts` |\n| Cash headroom | `SafeToSpendService.calculate` | `finance/safe-to-spend.service.ts:25` |\n| AP aging | `ExpensesService.getPayablesAging` | `expenses/expenses.service.ts:357` |\n| COA lookup by key | `ChartOfAccountsSeederService.getBySystemKey` | `chart-of-accounts-seeder.service.ts:179` |\n| Durable scheduling | **BullMQ**, already a dependency, repeatable-job pattern at `ai/queue.service.ts:113–131`; Queue+Worker on `REDIS_CLIENT` at `business-events/business-event.queue.ts:37,41–50` |\n| Reaching a service with no DI edge | `moduleRef.get(X, { strict: false })` — the orchestrator's own pattern, `flow-orchestrator.service.ts:450–482, 775` |\n\nThree modules are `@Global()` and therefore add no edge to anyone's import graph: `core/prisma/prisma.module.ts:4`, `core/redis/redis.module.ts:53`, `core/event-bus/event-bus.module.ts:4`. This is load-bearing for §3.\n\n---\n\n## 2. What the standing picture should contain\n\nA business owner does not want counts. Per the product thesis, every business here is *a book of relationships with an obligation clock*. The picture is therefore **a set of priced obligations, each with a clock and a named next act** — and it must say when it does not know something.\n\nEight slices. Each is one rendered line or nothing.\n\n| Slice | The facts | Clock | Source |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| `cash` | cash+bank+processor balance; safe-to-spend; runway months | — | `FinanceOverviewService`, `SafeToSpendService` |\n| `ledger` | `debits − credits` over all entries (must be exactly 0); date of last posting; open accounting period; **`coverage`** — money events in the last 30d that produced a `FinancialTransaction`, over all money events | period close date | `LedgerBalanceService`, `AccountingPeriodService` |\n| `receivables` | overdue total, count, oldest days, largest debtor | oldest due date | `ReceivablesService.getAging` |\n| `payables` | bills due ≤7d, ≤30d, already overdue | earliest `dueDate` | `Expense{status:'BILL'}` |\n| `pipeline` | open deals, weighted value (`value × probability`), count slipped past `expectedCloseAt` | earliest slipped date | `Deal` (`schema.prisma:2754`) |\n| `payroll` | current unpaid run: `totalGross`, `itemCount`, `periodEnd` | `periodEnd` | `PayrollRun` (`:2372`) |\n| `stock` | SKUs at/below `reorderAt`; stock value; SKUs with no `InventoryStock` row | — | `InventoryStock` (`:5691`) |\n| `service` | open tickets, age of oldest; bookings today / next 7d; overdue project tasks | oldest ticket `createdAt` | `SupportTicket` (`:4467`), `Booking`, `ProjectTask` |\n\nPlus, on the same row, **`deltas`** — for each fact whose value moved since the last non-identical picture, the old value and the direction. This is the only part that makes an unprompted judgement possible: \"the number that changed\" is what a chief of staff opens with, and today nothing in the product stores yesterday's number.\n\n### 2.1 The one non-negotiable semantic\n\n**A slice that could not be computed is never a zero.**\n\n`PulseSlice.unavailableReason` is a first-class field, `BusinessPulse.degraded` is a `String[]` of slice keys, and `render()` emits `⚠ payables: unavailable (Prisma P2021)` rather than omitting the line. This is the direct answer to the defect class in `OPEN_GAPS` — `getFullContext`'s `Promise.allSettled` (`key-cortex-context-v2.service.ts:305–317`) turned eight rejections into eight empty slices, hourly, in production, and nothing said so.\n\nThe same rule catches a subtler case: `ReceivablesService.getAging:222` is an unbounded `findMany`, capped at 1000 rows by the default-take extension (`packages/db/src/client.ts:61–74`). At 1001 open invoices the AR total silently understates. The contributor asserts `invoices.length < 1000` and sets `unavailableReason: 'AR truncated at the 1000-row default take'` — a visible degradation instead of a wrong number.\n\n### 2.2 `ledger.coverage` — the picture tells the truth about itself\n\nOnly 5 of 18 money-touching modules post to the ledger (CAPABILITY_MAP §1.3(1)). The pulse must not pretend the books are complete. `coverage` = count of last-30d `Invoice{status:PAID}` + `Expense` + `PayrollRun{status:PAID}` + goods receipts that have a matching `FinancialTransaction` (join on `sourceType`/`sourceId`, indexed at `schema.prisma` `@@index([businessId, sourceType, sourceId])`), over the total. When it is below 1.0 the rendered line says so:\n\n```\nLedger: balanced. 61% of money events posted (payroll, purchasing not booked).\n```\n\nThat single line makes KEY's own blind spot part of KEY's context, which is worth more than pretending otherwise.\n\n---\n\n## 3. The module boundary\n\n### 3.1 `PulseModule` — `apps/server/src/modules/pulse/`\n\n```\npulse/\n  pulse.contract.ts              ← imports only @nestjs/common types. No module imports. Ever.\n  pulse.module.ts                ← @Global, imports: []\n  pulse.service.ts               ← the public interface\n  pulse-contributor.registry.ts\n  pulse-render.ts                ← pure function, no I/O, no injection\n  pulse-refresh.queue.ts         ← BullMQ Queue + Worker\n  pulse-invalidation.listener.ts ← @OnEvent → enqueue, never compute\n  pulse.controller.ts            ← GET, for the manual screen\n```\n\n**Owns:** the `BusinessPulse` table, the slice contract, the refresh schedule, the render budget. Nothing else.\n\n**Does not own:** any business fact. It owns no query against `ledger_entries`, `deals`, `payroll_runs` or `inventory_stocks`. Those queries live in the modules that own those tables, behind the contributor interface.\n\n### 3.2 Public interface\n\n```ts\n// pulse.contract.ts — the whole boundary\nexport type PulseSliceKey =\n  | 'cash' | 'ledger' | 'receivables' | 'payables'\n  | 'pipeline' | 'payroll' | 'stock' | 'service';\n\nexport interface PulseFact {\n  key: string;                       // 'overdue_ar'\n  label: string;                     // 'Overdue AR'\n  value: number | string;\n  unit: 'money' | 'count' | 'days' | 'percent' | 'text';\n  dueAt?: string | null;             // the obligation clock, ISO. null = no clock\n  actionRoute?: string;              // a real /app route\n  actionTool?: string;               // a real FLOW_TOOLS name\n}\n\nexport interface PulseSlice {\n  key: PulseSliceKey;\n  facts: PulseFact[];\n  line: string | null;               // ≤160 chars, or null to omit from the prompt\n  unavailableReason?: string;        // set => this slice is DEGRADED, never zero\n}\n\nexport interface PulseContributor {\n  readonly sliceKey: PulseSliceKey;\n  /** Budget: ≤4 Postgres queries, ≤400ms. Enforced by pulse-budget.spec.ts. */\n  contribute(businessId: string, now: Date): Promise<PulseSlice>;\n}\n\nexport const PULSE_CONTRIBUTOR_TIMEOUT_MS = 1_500;\nexport const PULSE_RENDER_MAX_CHARS = 900;\n```\n\n```ts\n// pulse.service.ts\nexport class PulseService {\n  /** One indexed read. Never throws. Hard-bounded at PULSE_READ_BUDGET_MS (250). */\n  get(businessId: string): Promise<PulseView>;\n\n  /** Pure. No I/O. Elides empty slices, keeps degraded ones, truncates at 900 chars. */\n  render(view: PulseView): string;\n\n  /** Enqueue. Debounced by jobId. Returns in <5ms. */\n  requestRefresh(businessId: string, reason: string, slices?: PulseSliceKey[]): Promise<void>;\n\n  /** Compute now. Used by the worker and by pulse_get_picture({ refresh: true }). */\n  computeNow(businessId: string, slices?: PulseSliceKey[]): Promise<PulseView>;\n}\n\nexport class PulseContributorRegistry {\n  register(c: PulseContributor): void;   // called from each contributor's onModuleInit\n  keys(): PulseSliceKey[];\n  list(): PulseContributor[];\n}\n```\n\n**Emits** (EventEmitter2, the existing bus):\n- `pulse.refreshed` `{ businessId, fingerprint, changedSlices, degraded }`\n- `pulse.degraded` `{ businessId, sliceKey, reason }` — so a contributor that starts failing is *loud*. This is the alarm that did not exist when eight getters threw hourly.\n- `pulse.obligation.due` `{ businessId, sliceKey, factKey, dueAt }` — fired when a fact's clock passes. Consumed by nothing on day one; registered as a trigger when the single SMART registry (CAPABILITY_MAP §3.1 item 3) lands. **Declared as a dependency, not pretended.**\n\n**Listens to** (verified to be emitted somewhere in `apps/server/src/modules`, by enumerating all 231 distinct `emit('…')` literals):\n\n```\ninvoice.created  invoice.updated  invoice.paid  invoice.deleted  invoice.overdue\ninvoice.payment_recorded  payment.received  expense.created  bill.created  bill.paid\ncrm.deal.created  crm.deal.stage_changed  crm.deal.won  deal.updated\nquote.accepted  inventory.low  inventory.out  purchaseOrder.received\n```\n\nEach maps to **one** slice key. `invoice.*` → `receivables`; `bill.*`/`expense.*` → `payables`; `payment.*` → `cash`; `crm.deal.*`/`deal.*`/`quote.accepted` → `pipeline`; `inventory.*`/`purchaseOrder.*` → `stock`. `payroll` and `service` have no events today (verified: no `payroll.*`, `ticket.*` or `helpdesk.*` names among the 231) and refresh on the floor tick only — stated here so nobody later assumes they are event-driven.\n\nThe listener **never computes**. It calls `requestRefresh` and returns. No `Array.push`, no counters, no module fields.\n\n### 3.3 Why this creates no import cycle — the argument, and how to check it\n\n`pulse.module.ts` is declared:\n\n```ts\n@Global()\n@Module({\n  providers: [PulseService, PulseContributorRegistry, PulseRefreshQueue, PulseInvalidationListener],\n  controllers: [PulseController],\n  exports: [PulseService, PulseContributorRegistry],\n})\nexport class PulseModule {}\n```\n\n**`imports` is empty.** It needs `PrismaService` (`prisma.module.ts:4` — `@Global`), `REDIS_CLIENT` (`redis.module.ts:53` — `@Global`) and `EventEmitter2` (`event-bus.module.ts:4` — `@Global`). All three are already ambient.\n\nThree consequences:\n\n1. **Out-degree zero.** A node with no outgoing edges cannot lie on a directed cycle. The 68 `forwardRef` deferrals stay 68; the four remaining cycles stay four. There is nothing to argue about.\n2. **In-degree zero too, because it is `@Global`.** A contributor in `FinanceModule` injects `PulseContributorRegistry` without `FinanceModule` listing `PulseModule` in `imports`. `AppModule` registers `PulseModule` once. So the module graph gains **zero edges in both directions** — the same shape `RedisModule` and `EventBusModule` already have, and defensible for the same reason: this is a read model with no domain dependencies, consumed by `ai`, `key-cortex`, `autopilot` and `notifications`.\n3. **No file-level cycle either.** Contributors import exactly one file, `pulse/pulse.contract.ts`, which imports nothing from any module. A `madge`/`dpdm` run cannot produce a cycle through it.\n\n**The check** — `pulse-boundary.spec.ts`, a source-text gate in the house style of `tenant-model-list.spec.ts`:\n\n```ts\nit('PulseModule imports nothing', () => {\n  expect(readFileSync('pulse/pulse.module.ts','utf8')).toMatch(/imports:\\s*\\[\\s*\\]/);\n});\nit('no file under pulse/ reaches into another module', () => {\n  // any `from '../<x>/...'` where <x> !== 'core' is a boundary violation\n  expect(offenders).toEqual([]);\n});\nit('pulse.contract.ts imports only @nestjs/common', () => { ... });\n```\n\nThe direction that matters is the second: it is what stops someone \"just adding a quick `ledgerEntry` query\" inside the pulse module in six months, which is exactly how the 68 `forwardRef`s were earned.\n\n### 3.4 The eight contributors and where they live\n\n| Slice | File | Module | Uses |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| `cash` | `finance/pulse/cash-pulse.contributor.ts` | Finance | `FinanceOverviewService`, `SafeToSpendService` |\n| `ledger` | `finance/pulse/ledger-pulse.contributor.ts` | Finance | `LedgerBalanceService`, `AccountingPeriodService` |\n| `receivables` | `finance/pulse/receivables-pulse.contributor.ts` | Finance | `ReceivablesService.getAging` |\n| `payables` | `expenses/pulse/payables-pulse.contributor.ts` | Expenses | `Expense` aggregate |\n| `pipeline` | `crm/pulse/pipeline-pulse.contributor.ts` | CRM | `Deal` aggregate + groupBy |\n| `payroll` | `payroll/pulse/payroll-pulse.contributor.ts` | Payroll | `PayrollRun` findFirst |\n| `stock` | `continental-ops/pulse/stock-pulse.contributor.ts` | ContinentalOps | `InventoryStock` |\n| `service` | `helpdesk/pulse/service-pulse.contributor.ts` | Helpdesk | `SupportTicket`, `Booking`, `ProjectTask` |\n\nEach is one class, `onModuleInit() { this.registry.register(this); }`, and one `contribute()`. Ordering is irrelevant: registration happens in `onModuleInit`, the worker starts in `onApplicationBootstrap` (which Nest runs after every `onModuleInit`), and `PulseService.get()` is only reached at request time.\n\n`service` reads `Booking` and `ProjectTask`, which Helpdesk does not own. Two options, decide once and write it down: either split it into three contributors in `bookings`, `projects` and `helpdesk` with slice keys `service.tickets` / `service.bookings` / `service.tasks`, or keep one and accept the cross-table read. **Take the split.** Three 40-line files beat one boundary exception, and the slice-key union in the contract makes the completeness gate (§7.1) enforce it.\n\n---\n\n## 4. Data\n\n### 4.1 Prisma model\n\n```prisma\n/// The standing picture — one materialised row per business, read on every KEY\n/// turn. Written only by PulseRefreshWorker; never by a request handler.\n///\n/// `degraded` is the point of this table. A slice that failed to compute is\n/// NAMED here and rendered into the prompt as unavailable. It is never a zero,\n/// because a zero and a failure are indistinguishable to a model — which is how\n/// eight KeyCortexContextV2 getters threw hourly in production without anyone\n/// noticing (see cortex-context-v2.integration.test.ts).\nmodel BusinessPulse {\n  id         String   @id @default(cuid())\n  businessId String   @unique @map(\"business_id\")\n  business   Business @relation(fields: [businessId], references: [id], onDelete: Cascade)\n\n  /// Record<PulseSliceKey, PulseSlice>. Written whole or per-key.\n  slices   Json\n  /// The previous non-identical `slices`, for deltas. Null until the second refresh.\n  previous Json?     @map(\"previous_slices\")\n\n  /// The exact prompt block. Precomputed so a turn does zero string work.\n  rendered      String @db.Text\n  renderedChars Int    @default(0) @map(\"rendered_chars\")\n\n  /// sha1 of the canonical JSON of `slices`. A refresh that produces the same\n  /// fingerprint skips the write, so an idle business writes nothing all day.\n  fingerprint String @db.VarChar(40)\n\n  /// Slice keys whose contributor failed or timed out on the most recent run.\n  degraded String[] @default([]) @map(\"degraded_slices\")\n\n  /// Optimistic-concurrency token. Two per-slice refreshes for the same\n  /// business can be picked up by two workers; the update is conditional on\n  /// this value and retried once on a zero row count.\n  version Int @default(0)\n\n  computedMs  Int      @default(0) @map(\"computed_ms\")\n  refreshedAt DateTime @default(now()) @map(\"refreshed_at\")\n  previousAt  DateTime? @map(\"previous_at\")\n  createdAt   DateTime @default(now()) @map(\"created_at\")\n  updatedAt   DateTime @updatedAt @map(\"updated_at\")\n\n  @@index([businessId, refreshedAt])\n  /// The staleness sweep runs cross-tenant with no business context — this is\n  /// the index it uses, and the only one not led by businessId.\n  @@index([refreshedAt])\n  @@map(\"business_pulses\")\n}\n```\n\nHouse style honoured: `snake_case @map`, `businessId` with a cascading `Business` relation, compound `@@index([businessId, …])`.\n\n### 4.2 Tenant isolation — the required, easily-forgotten step\n\n**`'BusinessPulse'` is added to `BUSINESS_ID_MODELS` in `packages/db/src/client.ts:81–153`**, in a new block with a comment saying why.\n\nThis is not optional and it is not automatic. Verified 2026-08-09 by reading `apps/server/src/core/prisma/tenant-model-list.spec.ts`: it has three assertions (`:58`, `:68`, `:86`), and all three run **listed → schema**. There is no schema → listed assertion. A model that is never added to the set passes the gate silently and is unscoped forever. That is precisely the shape of defect the spec's own header warns about, pointing the other way.\n\nThree notes to write into the block, because each has bitten this file before:\n\n- `businessId` here is the model's `@unique` key. Injection by `withTenantWhere` (`client.ts:187–194`) therefore adds a field that is already present with the same value — a genuine no-op. It does **not** hit the Prisma-6.19 hazard documented at `client.ts:143–148` (an extra scalar in a `WhereUniqueInput` silently returning null), because there is no *extra* scalar.\n- The extension hooks ten read/update operations, not `create`/`upsert`/`aggregate`. The worker's `upsert` is scoped by an explicit `where: { businessId }` bound from the job payload, never from a request body.\n- The sweep runs with no tenant context (`activeBusinessId()` is `undefined`, `client.ts:196–198`), so the extension is inert there by design and the cross-tenant `findMany` on `refreshedAt` works. Stated so nobody \"fixes\" it later.\n\n### 4.3 Migration\n\n`packages/db/prisma/migrations/20260812090000_business_pulse/migration.sql`, written in the house style (prose first, then DDL, `IF NOT EXISTS` throughout — see `20260808140000_bank_transaction_external_id_unique/migration.sql`):\n\n```sql\n-- KEY's standing picture, materialised.\n--\n-- Before this table, every chat turn built its context from 35 aggregate\n-- queries (business-graph.service.ts:140-215, :339-474) that touched\n-- ledger_entries, financial_accounts, deals, payroll_runs, inventory_stocks\n-- and support_tickets exactly zero times. Every proactive judgement KEY made\n-- was made without money in view.\n--\n-- One row per business, refreshed by a BullMQ worker. The turn path reads it\n-- and renders it; it never computes it, because a chat turn must not wait on\n-- eight departments.\n--\n-- degraded_slices is the column that matters. A contributor that fails writes\n-- its key here and the prompt says \"unavailable\", instead of the slice\n-- becoming zeros that read to a model as \"you have no overdue invoices\".\n\nCREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS \"business_pulses\" (\n  \"id\"              TEXT PRIMARY KEY,\n  \"business_id\"     TEXT NOT NULL,\n  \"slices\"          JSONB NOT NULL,\n  \"previous_slices\" JSONB,\n  \"rendered\"        TEXT NOT NULL,\n  \"rendered_chars\"  INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,\n  \"fingerprint\"     VARCHAR(40) NOT NULL,\n  \"degraded_slices\" TEXT[] NOT NULL DEFAULT '{}',\n  \"version\"         INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,\n  \"computed_ms\"     INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,\n  \"refreshed_at\"    TIMESTAMP(3) NOT NULL DEFAULT CURRENT_TIMESTAMP,\n  \"previous_at\"     TIMESTAMP(3),\n  \"created_at\"      TIMESTAMP(3) NOT NULL DEFAULT CURRENT_TIMESTAMP,\n  \"updated_at\"      TIMESTAMP(3) NOT NULL\n);\n\nCREATE UNIQUE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS \"business_pulses_business_id_key\"\n  ON \"business_pulses\"(\"business_id\");\nCREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS \"business_pulses_business_id_refreshed_at_idx\"\n  ON \"business_pulses\"(\"business_id\",\"refreshed_at\");\n-- Led by refreshed_at on purpose: the staleness sweep is the one cross-tenant\n-- reader in this design and it scans by time, not by tenant.\nCREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS \"business_pulses_refreshed_at_idx\"\n  ON \"business_pulses\"(\"refreshed_at\");\n\nDO $$ BEGIN\n  ALTER TABLE \"business_pulses\"\n    ADD CONSTRAINT \"business_pulses_business_id_fkey\"\n    FOREIGN KEY (\"business_id\") REFERENCES \"businesses\"(\"id\")\n    ON DELETE CASCADE ON UPDATE CASCADE;\nEXCEPTION WHEN duplicate_object THEN NULL; END $$;\n```\n\nNo backfill. The read-through path (§5.3) creates a row on first use.\n\n---\n\n## 5. How it stays cheap\n\nTwo budgets, because cost has two axes and only one of them is queries.\n\n### 5.1 Read path — the turn\n\n| Case | Cost |\n|---|---|\n| Steady state | **1 Redis `GET`** on `pulse:v1:{businessId}` (TTL 60s). Zero Postgres. Zero string work — `rendered` is stored, not built. |\n| Redis miss | **1 Postgres query**: `SELECT … FROM business_pulses WHERE business_id = $1` on the unique index. |\n| Cold (no row) | compute inline **once**, under a Redis lock `SET pulse:lock:{businessId} <token> NX PX 30000`, bounded at `PULSE_INLINE_BUDGET_MS = 800`. On timeout or a held lock: return `{ rendered: '', degraded: ['*'] }` and enqueue. **A chat turn never waits twice and never fails because of the picture.** |\n\n`PulseService.get` catches everything and returns an empty view. The picture degrades; the turn does not.\n\n### 5.2 Write path — the refresh\n\n**Per-slice, not whole-picture.** The invalidation listener maps an event to one slice key and enqueues `{ businessId, slices: ['receivables'] }`. Running one contributor costs ≤4 queries, not ~24.\n\nDebounce is a **job key, not a timer**:\n\n```\njobId = `pulse:${businessId}:${sliceKey}:${Math.floor(Date.now() / 60_000)}`\ndelay = 5_000\n```\n\nBullMQ refuses a duplicate `jobId` while the job is waiting, so an invoice import emitting 400 `invoice.created` events in ninety seconds produces **at most two** refreshes of one slice. No `setInterval`, no in-memory debounce map, no per-replica state.\n\n**Floor sweep** — one repeatable job, the pattern from `ai/queue.service.ts:113–131`:\n\n```ts\nawait this.queue.add('pulse-sweep', {}, {\n  jobId: 'pulse:sweep',\n  repeat: { pattern: '*/5 * * * *', tz: 'UTC' },\n  removeOnComplete: { count: 100 },\n  removeOnFail: { count: 50 },\n});\n```\n\nA repeatable job with a stable `jobId` is stored in Redis, not in a process. **N replicas produce one tick.** That is the distributed lock the repo has zero of today, obtained for free from a dependency already installed. The sweep selects up to 500 rows where `refreshedAt < now − 15min` and enqueues a full refresh for each with `jobId = pulse:{businessId}:all:{15-min bucket}`.\n\n**Concurrency.** Two workers can pick up two slice refreshes for the same business. The update is conditional:\n\n```ts\nconst n = await db.businessPulse.updateMany({\n  where: { businessId, version },\n  data: { slices, rendered, fingerprint, degraded, version: { increment: 1 }, refreshedAt: now },\n});\nif (n.count === 0) return this.applySlice(businessId, sliceKey, slice, attempt + 1); // once\n```\n\nNot `updateMany` fire-and-forget — the returned count is checked. (The audit found `updateMany` used without checking the count in the stock path, CAPABILITY_MAP §3.2 item 15; do not repeat it here.)\n\n**Fingerprint short-circuit.** If the recomputed `slices` hashes to the stored `fingerprint`, the worker writes nothing but `refreshedAt`. An idle business costs one `SELECT` per 15 minutes and no row churn.\n\n### 5.3 Steady-state arithmetic, per business per day\n\n- Floor: 96 full refreshes × ~24 queries = **2,304**\n- Events: an active SMB emits perhaps 60 money events/day; debounced to ~25 slice refreshes × 4 = **100**\n- Turn reads: ~0 Postgres (Redis), ~200 Redis GETs\n\n**~2,400 queries/day for a picture that is never more than 15 minutes stale.** For comparison, `assembleSnapshot` costs 35 on every cache miss and today has no cross-replica cache at all.\n\n### 5.4 Token budget — the axis that is easy to forget\n\n`buildContextString` currently emits 13 lines, ~500 characters, ~130 tokens, on every turn of every business forever.\n\nThe pulse block is capped at **`PULSE_RENDER_MAX_CHARS = 900`** (~230 tokens), enforced inside `render()` and asserted by `pulse-render-budget.spec.ts` against a fixture of a maximally loaded business.\n\nIt stays under budget three ways:\n- **Elision.** A slice with nothing to say returns `line: null` and is omitted. A salon with no inventory and no payroll renders five lines, not eight.\n- **Degraded slices are never elided.** They are the ones that change what KEY should say.\n- **Truncation is visible**, never silent: `[+3 slices — ask me]`.\n\nRendered shape:\n\n```\n── PICTURE (as of 09:40, 4m ago) ──\nCash: TTD 84,210 · safe to spend 19,400 · 2.8 months runway\nLedger: balanced · last posted 2h ago · 61% of money events posted (payroll, purchasing not booked)\nReceivables: TTD 31,880 overdue across 9 invoices · oldest 74d · Ramkissoon Ltd TTD 12,400\nPayables: TTD 6,120 due in 7d · TTD 940 already overdue\nPipeline: 14 open · TTD 96,500 weighted · 3 past their close date\nPayroll: run to 15 Aug · TTD 22,300 committed · 6 days out\n⚠ Stock: unavailable (contributor timed out at 1500ms)\nChanged since 08:10: overdue AR +TTD 4,200 (1 invoice tipped past due)\n```\n\nThat last line is the product. Nothing in the repo can produce it today.\n\n### 5.5 Two contained fixes to the existing snapshot\n\nNot a rewrite — 18 call sites forbid that.\n\n1. **Replace the `Map` at `business-graph.service.ts:117` with Redis** (`ai:graph:{businessId}`, 120s, same TTL). Six lines. `invalidateCache` (`:136`) then works across replicas, and `ai.listener.ts:162` starts meaning something on a second instance. Also removes a module field holding tenant data.\n2. **Pass `{ includeEntities: false }` at the four prompt sites** (`flow-orchestrator.service.ts:1292`, `:1672`, `intent-parser.service.ts:143`, `planner.service.ts:82`). `assembleEntityLinks` (`:339–474`) is 5 queries including a 25-contact `findMany` with two nested `take: 20` includes — up to 1,000 joined rows — and `buildContextString` reads **none** of it. Default stays `true`, so the 14 other call sites are untouched. **−5 queries and ~1,000 rows off every cache-missed turn.**\n\nDo not extend `INVALIDATING_DOMAINS` (`ai.listener.ts:7–9`) here. The pulse listener covers `deal`, `bill`, `payment`, `inventory` and `quote` independently, and widening the graph listener changes cache behaviour for 18 callers. Note it in the follow-up, do not smuggle it in.\n\n---\n\n## 6. The three layers\n\n### 6.1 MANUAL — `/app/pulse`\n\n`apps/web/src/app/app/pulse/page.tsx`, fed by `GET /pulse/businesses/:businessId` (`PulseController`, `BusinessGuard` as every other controller).\n\nEight cards, each showing the facts, the clock, and the action link built from `PulseFact.actionRoute`. A degraded slice renders red — \"Stock: unavailable — contributor timed out (1500ms). Last good value 14 SKUs low, 09:10.\" — because the *screen* must not lie either. A `refreshedAt` stamp and a \"Refresh now\" button that calls `POST /pulse/businesses/:id/refresh` (enqueue, then poll; it does not compute in the request).\n\n**Nav entry required.** `apps/web/src/lib/nav-config.ts`, under the \"Money\" section of `operateSections` (`:130`), `{ label: \"Picture\", href: \"/app/pulse\", icon: Activity }`. Without it, `nav-reachability.spec.ts` fails — its allowlist takes three reasons and `todo-connect` is described in the file as work, not an exemption. Do not add an allowlist entry.\n\n### 6.2 SMART — deterministic, no LLM\n\nThe pulse *is* the deterministic layer: eight contributors, plain SQL, no model call, one BullMQ schedule. Two deterministic behaviours ride on it:\n\n- `pulse.obligation.due` is emitted when a fact's `dueAt` passes, by the same worker that refreshes the slice. On day one nothing consumes it. It becomes a real automation trigger when the single trigger/action registry lands (CAPABILITY_MAP §3.1 item 3). **It is not added to `automation-constants.ts` now** — that file advertises 32 triggers against an engine that dispatches on 8, and adding a 33rd fake one is the defect, not the fix.\n- `pulse.degraded` is consumed by `notifications` to open a `CommandItem` when a slice has been degraded for two consecutive refreshes. This is the alarm whose absence let eight getters throw hourly for an unknown length of time.\n\n### 6.3 AI — two tools\n\nAdded to `FLOW_TOOLS` in `apps/server/src/modules/ai/flow-tool-registry.ts`:\n\n```ts\n{\n  name: 'pulse_get_picture',\n  description: 'The current standing picture of the business — cash, ledger health, overdue receivables, bills due, pipeline, payroll committed, stock and service load, with what changed since last time.',\n  family: 'read',\n  riskLevel: 'low',\n  riskTier: 1 as RiskTier,\n  manualEquivalentRoute: '/app/pulse',\n  parameters: { type: 'object', properties: {\n    refresh: { type: 'boolean', description: 'Recompute before returning (slower; use only when the user says the number looks wrong)' },\n  }, required: [] },\n  outputSchema: { type: 'object', description: 'Standing picture', fields: {\n    slices: { type: 'object', description: 'Eight slices keyed by name' },\n    degraded: { type: 'array', description: 'Slice keys that could NOT be computed — treat as unknown, never as zero' },\n    refreshedAt: { type: 'string', description: 'ISO timestamp of the picture' },\n    changed: { type: 'array', description: 'Facts that moved since the previous picture' },\n  } },\n},\n{\n  name: 'pulse_explain_slice',\n  description: 'Show the underlying facts and their sources for one slice of the picture — answers \"why do you say I have $31,880 overdue\".',\n  family: 'read',\n  riskLevel: 'low',\n  riskTier: 1 as RiskTier,\n  manualEquivalentRoute: '/app/pulse',\n  parameters: { type: 'object', properties: {\n    sliceKey: { type: 'string', description: 'cash | ledger | receivables | payables | pipeline | payroll | stock | service',\n                enum: ['cash','ledger','receivables','payables','pipeline','payroll','stock','service'] },\n  }, required: ['sliceKey'] },\n  outputSchema: { ... },\n},\n```\n\nGate compliance, each verified against the checker source:\n\n- `manualEquivalentRoute` is a real page → satisfies `unresolved` (`apps/web/src/lib/tool-route-audit.ts:78–92`, the check that caught four routes pointing at pages nobody built).\n- `family: 'read'` → outside `WRITE_FAMILIES`, so `noManualWrite` (`:94–101`) and `crossDomain` (`:118–123`) do not apply. Both would pass anyway: the domain prefix `pulse` is contained in `/app/pulse`.\n- Handlers go in `executeToolAction` (`flow-orchestrator.service.ts:2251`, switch at `:2256`). Required: `flow-tool-honesty.spec.ts:39–43` reads that exact method by name and collects `case '…':` literals, and fails if a declared tool has none. The five `delegation_*` tools pinned in that file are what happens otherwise.\n- The handler reaches `PulseService` via `this.moduleRef.get(PulseService, { strict: false })`, matching `flow-orchestrator.service.ts:450–482`. No constructor injection, no import edge — belt and braces on top of §3.3.\n- Add both names to the tool whitelist at `intent-parser.service.ts:122` and to `approvedTools` for the roles at `role-engine.service.ts:369`. Tier 1, so `general` and `executive` (ceiling 1) can call them. **A tier-1 read tool that the two lowest-ceiling roles cannot call would be a picture nobody can ask for** — the exact failure the roles lens found on the outbound side.\n\n---\n\n## 7. Tests\n\n### 7.1 Contributor completeness — the gate that matters most\n\n`apps/server/test/pulse-contributors.integration.test.ts`, booting the real `AppModule` (model: `apps/server/test/app-module-boots.integration.test.ts`):\n\n```ts\nit('every declared slice key has a registered contributor', () => {\n  const registered = new Set(app.get(PulseContributorRegistry).keys());\n  const declared: PulseSliceKey[] = ['cash','ledger','receivables','payables','pipeline','payroll','stock','service'];\n  expect(declared.filter(k => !registered.has(k)),\n    'these slices are declared in the contract and produced by nobody — the ' +\n    'picture would render without them and nothing would say so').toEqual([]);\n});\n```\n\n**Why this and not a unit test:** a contributor is registered by being in a module's `providers` array. Deleting it from that array is a one-line diff that typechecks, lints, and produces a picture missing a slice with no error anywhere. The declared-vs-registered set difference is the same shape as `flow-tool-honesty.spec.ts` and `tenant-model-list.spec.ts`, which are the two gates in this repo that have actually caught things.\n\n**Negative control:** remove `PayrollPulseContributor` from `payroll.module.ts`'s `providers`, run, confirm the failure message names `payroll`. Restore. This is the step the memory note \"assertions check referents, not shapes\" exists for — spell the control like a real defect and confirm it fires on the expected key, not merely that something red appears.\n\n### 7.2 Real-database catch path\n\n`apps/server/test/pulse-slices.integration.test.ts`, directly modelled on `apps/server/test/cortex-context-v2.integration.test.ts` and for the identical reason stated in that file's header.\n\nAgainst an empty seeded business, a correct slice and a caught-exception slice are **both** zeros. Asserting the returned shape would pass while every contributor was failing — the exact failure this file exists to prevent. So:\n\n1. Seed a business with one contact, one paid invoice, one `BILL` expense, one open deal, one draft `PayrollRun`, one open ticket, via `db` with `setTenantContextProvider` supplying an ambient `businessId` (the interceptor's `AsyncLocalStorage` only exists on the HTTP path — `cortex-context-v2.integration.test.ts:47–54`).\n2. Run all eight contributors.\n3. **Assert `degraded` is empty.** A contributor that threw appears there by construction; the assertion names the slice.\n4. Assert seeded rows appear: `receivables.facts` contains the invoice, `payables` the bill, `pipeline` the deal, `payroll` the run, `service` the ticket — so a regression to a wrong column name fails here rather than returning an empty slice.\n5. Clean up with `$executeRawUnsafe` DELETE, not `deleteMany` — `Business` and `Contact` are in the soft-delete extension's model list (`packages/db/src/client.ts:19–34`), so `deleteMany` becomes an UPDATE, the row survives, and the next run's `upsert` matches a row with `deletedAt` set. Documented at `cortex-context-v2.integration.test.ts:117–130`; do not rediscover it.\n\n**Negative control:** point one contributor at a column that does not exist (`_sum: { totalGros: true }`), run, confirm the test fails naming `payroll` and *not* naming the other seven. Then confirm that the *shape-only* version of the assertion (`expect(slice.facts).toBeDefined()`) still passes against that same broken build. That second half is the control that proves the test design, not just the test.\n\n### 7.3 Isolation\n\n`pulse-tenancy.integration.test.ts` — seed two businesses with different overdue AR, compute both, assert A's rendered string contains A's number and does not contain B's; assert `db.businessPulse.findUnique` under ambient tenant A cannot return B's row. Negative control: drop `'BusinessPulse'` from `BUSINESS_ID_MODELS` in a scratch copy and confirm the second assertion fails. (It will — and the `tenant-model-list.spec.ts` suite will still be green, which is the point of §4.2.)\n\n### 7.4 Budgets\n\n`pulse-budget.spec.ts` — wrap `db` in a `$extends` query counter and assert:\n- each contributor issues ≤ 4 queries against a seeded business;\n- `PulseService.get` on a warm row issues ≤ 1;\n- a full `computeNow` issues ≤ 26.\n\nNegative control: add a gratuitous `findMany` to one contributor, confirm the count assertion fails naming it.\n\n`pulse-render-budget.spec.ts` — a fixture with all eight slices maximally populated plus three degraded; assert `render()` output ≤ 900 chars, that all three degraded lines survive truncation, and that the truncation marker is present. Negative control: raise the fixture to twelve slices and assert the marker appears and the cap holds.\n\n### 7.5 Debounce\n\n`pulse-debounce.spec.ts` — with a real BullMQ queue on the test Redis, fire 200 `invoice.created` events for one business inside one minute and assert the queue holds ≤ 2 jobs. Negative control: replace the bucketed `jobId` with `randomUUID()` and confirm the count becomes 200. This is the assertion that proves the debounce is a job key and not wishful thinking — and it is the class of thing the `cron-scheduler` note in `OPEN_GAPS` says has never been measured properly.\n\n---\n\n## 8. Effort, and what to adopt\n\n| Work | Days |\n|---|---:|\n| Prisma model, migration, `BUSINESS_ID_MODELS`, tenancy test | 1.0 |\n| `pulse` module skeleton — contract, registry, service, render, controller | 2.0 |\n| BullMQ queue, worker, sweep, per-slice debounce, Redis lock, optimistic version | 2.0 |\n| Ten contributors (eight keys, `service` split three ways) | 4.5 |\n| Wire four prompt sites; Map→Redis on the graph cache; `includeEntities: false` | 1.5 |\n| `/app/pulse` screen + nav entry | 2.0 |\n| Two KEY tools: registry, handlers, intent-parser list, role approval | 1.0 |\n| Tests + the five negative controls | 3.0 |\n| **Total** | **17.0** |\n\n**ADOPT:**\n- **BullMQ** (MIT) — already in `package.json`, already doing repeatable jobs at `ai/queue.service.ts:113–131`. It supplies scheduling, cross-replica single-fire and the debounce. Write no scheduler. This is CAPABILITY_MAP §3.3 item 27 applied to one module, as a proof that the pattern works before the other 52 `setInterval`s move.\n- **ioredis** (MIT), already present, for `SET … NX PX` as the inline-compute lock. **Do not adopt `redlock`** — its guarantees are about multiple independent Redis nodes; there is one (`docker-compose.production.yml:145`, `redis:7-alpine`), so redlock adds a dependency and no safety.\n\n**BUILD:** the contract, the contributors, the render, the screen. There is no off-the-shelf \"SMB obligation snapshot\", and the value is entirely in which facts are chosen.\n\n**Considered and rejected, with the reason:**\n- *Postgres materialised views, one per slice.* `REFRESH MATERIALIZED VIEW` is global, and `CONCURRENTLY` still rewrites every tenant's rows when one business changes. Wrong granularity, and it moves logic to a place the tenant extension cannot see.\n- *`pg_cron`.* Not installed — the image is `pgvector/pgvector:pg16` (`docker-compose.production.yml:130`) and no migration creates the extension. It would also put scheduling somewhere the application cannot introspect, when BullMQ is already there.\n- *Adding a finance slice to `key-cortex-context-v2.service.ts`.* 1,643 lines, 16 callers, a duplicate `FullBusinessContext` interface (`OPEN_GAPS`), and a `Promise.allSettled` that still swallows rejections. Adding to it makes the duplication worse.\n\n---\n\n## 9. What this deliberately does not do\n\n- **Does not make the ledger complete.** The other 13 money modules still do not post (CAPABILITY_MAP §3.2 item 14). The pulse *reports* that as `ledger.coverage` so the incompleteness is visible in KEY's own context rather than papered over by a P&L that looks fine.\n- **Does not fix `safe-to-spend`.** `safe-to-spend.service.ts:68–69` still hard-codes `payrollReserved = 0`. The payroll slice makes `PayrollRun.totalGross` available and the fix becomes two lines — but it is a finance change and belongs in that item, not smuggled in here.\n- **Does not touch `key-cortex-context-v2` or `key-cortex-context-assembly`.** Two services, two identically-named interfaces, one repaired last week. Deciding whether both should exist is an `OPEN_GAPS` item with its own answer.\n- **Does not rewrite `BusinessGraphService`.** 18 call sites. Two contained changes only (§5.5).\n- **Does not extend `INVALIDATING_DOMAINS`** at `ai.listener.ts:7–9`, or trim its unbounded `events` array at `:14`. Both are real; both change behaviour for 18 unrelated callers.\n- **Does not add a trigger to `automation-constants.ts`.** That file advertises 32 triggers against an engine that dispatches on 8. `pulse.obligation.due` is emitted and documented; it is wired when the single registry exists.\n- **Does not add a morning brief, a digest, or alert delivery.** The pulse is the input those need. `MorningBriefingService` already exists and can read it on day two.\n- **Does not add a ninth slice for marketing, contracts or projects-profitability.** Eight is what fits the 900-character budget while staying legible. `MarketingCampaignPlan` has no `actualRevenue` and `renewalDate` is read by nothing — both need their own work before they have a fact worth carrying on every turn.\n\n---\n\n## The measure of whether this shipped\n\nThree sentences, testable:\n\n1. `pulse_get_picture` returns a non-empty `receivables` slice whose overdue total equals what `/app/finance` shows — because both call `ReceivablesService.getAging`.\n2. A contributor that starts throwing appears in `degraded`, in the prompt, and in a `CommandItem` within 30 minutes — instead of becoming zeros for an unknown length of time.\n3. Two replicas produce one sweep tick, and 200 invoice events produce at most two refresh jobs."

The module boundary\n\n**Name:** `DirectiveModule` — `apps/server/src/modules/directive/`.
