the façade\n  directive-authority.service.ts ← membership → per-step verdict\n  directive-planner.service.ts   ← instruction → ordered steps\n  directive-preview.service.ts   ← resolves reads, renders the sentence\n  directive-executor.service.ts  ← BullMQ worker; saga; compensation\n  directive-recipes.ts           ← SMART layer: static, deterministic\n  tool-department.map.ts         ← 245 tools → 13 departments\n  dto/plan-directive.dto.ts      ← class-validator DTO (see §2.5)\n  dto/authorize-directive.dto.ts\n```\n\n### 2.2 What it owns\n\nThe `Directive` and `DirectiveStep` tables; the tool→department map; the authority composition rule; the ordering rule; the preview shape; the `directive-steps` and `directive-expiry` BullMQ queues.\n\nIt owns **no** tool implementations, **no** compensation handlers beyond the two it registers (§5.4), and **no** domain tables.\n\n### 2.3 Public interface\n\n`directive.contract.ts` — types and one token, no Nest decorators, importable by anyone without pulling the module:\n\n```ts\nexport const DIRECTIVE_PORT = Symbol('DIRECTIVE_PORT');\n\nexport type DirectiveVerdict = 'auto' | 'quick_confirm' | 'formal' | 'admin' | 'denied';\n\nexport interface DirectiveStepView {\n  id: string;\n  stepIndex: number;\n  department: string;            // 'payments' | 'bookings' | 'comms' | ...\n  toolName: string;\n  riskTier: 1 | 2 | 3 | 4;\n  summary: string;               // the sentence a human reads\n  args: Record<string, unknown>;\n  reversible: boolean;           // compensationAction !== null\n  verdict: DirectiveVerdict;\n  verdictReason: string;\n  manualEquivalentRoute: string; // getManualEquivalentRoute(tool)\n  status: string;\n}\n\nexport interface DirectivePreview {\n  directiveId: string;\n  instruction: string;\n  planSource: string;            // 'recipe:refund_and_release' | 'llm'\n  steps: DirectiveStepView[];\n  needsAuthorization: DirectiveStepView[];\n  irreversibleSteps: DirectiveStepView[];\n  expiresAt: string;\n  blockedReason?: string;\n}\n\nexport interface DirectivePort {\n  plan(i: { businessId: string; userId: string; instruction: string; sessionId?: string;\n            pageRoute?: string }): Promise<DirectivePreview | { delegatedToChat: true; reply: unknown }>;\n  authorize(i: { businessId: string; userId: string; directiveId: string;\n                 decisions: Array<{ stepId: string; approved: boolean }> }): Promise<DirectivePreview>;\n  run(i: { businessId: string; userId: string; directiveId: string }): Promise<{ directiveId: string; queued: true }>;\n  get(businessId: string, directiveId: string): Promise<DirectivePreview>;\n  abandon(i: { businessId: string; userId: string; directiveId: string; reason: string }): Promise<DirectivePreview>;\n}\n```\n\n### 2.4 Events\n\n**Emits** (EventEmitter2, which is global — no import edge):\n`directive.planned`, `directive.authorized`, `directive.step.completed`, `directive.step.failed`, `directive.completed`, `directive.compensated`, `directive.stranded`, `directive.expired`. Every payload carries `{ businessId, directiveId, userId }`.\n\n**Listens to** exactly one: `directive.requested` — `{ businessId, userId, recipeId, args }`. This is the SMART layer's entry point, so a deterministic `Automation` rule can raise a directive with no LLM and no HTTP. Nothing else is consumed.\n\nPer `OPEN_GAPS_2026-08-09.md` (\"11 dead listeners\"), every one of these names must have a consumer or a documented reason not to; `directive.stranded` gets a consumer in the same PR (a notification), because a stranded directive that nobody is told about is worse than a failure.\n\n### 2.5 Why this creates no import cycle\n\nThe repo carries 102 `forwardRef(...)` occurrences across `*.module.ts` (grep) and four live cycles. This module adds zero.\n\n**The arrow points one way, and the direction is the design.** `DirectiveModule` is a **front door in front of** `AiModule`, never a callee behind it.\n\n```\nAppModule\n   └── DirectiveModule ──> AiModule        (FlowOrchestratorService, AiOversightService — both exported, ai.module.ts exports[])\n                      └──> KeyCortexModule (KeyCortexSagaService, KeyCortexCompensationService,\n                                            KeyIdempotencyService — exported, key-cortex.module.ts:726-729)\n                      └──> PrismaModule, RedisModule\n```\n\nFour mechanical guarantees:\n\n1. **`apps/server/src/modules/ai/**` and `apps/server/src/modules/key-cortex/**` are not edited to add an import of `directive/`.** The web posts to the new controller; the new controller calls the old services. `flow.controller.ts` and `flow-orchestrator.service.ts` gain no new dependency.\n2. **No `forwardRef` appears anywhere in `directive.module.ts`.** If one is ever needed, that is not a fix — it is the alarm that an edge went backwards. Stated in the file header.\n3. **A spec enforces it.** `directive-no-back-edge.spec.ts` greps `apps/server/src` for `modules/directive` or `from '../directive` outside `modules/directive/` and `app.module.ts`, and asserts zero hits. Its negative control greps for `modules/ai` and asserts a non‑zero count — proving the detector is live, in the shape of `tool-route-audit.ts:308‑323` `assertParityCheckWorks`.\n4. `directive.contract.ts` imports nothing from `@nestjs/*` or from any module, so a future consumer can type against the port without an edge.\n\n`flow-tool-registry.ts` is imported directly as a file. Verified: it has **zero import statements** (`grep -n \"^import\"` returns nothing), so this is not a module edge, it is a constant table.\n\n---\n\n## 3. Data\n\nHouse style observed from `schema.prisma`: `businessId` + cascading `Business` relation, `snake_case` `@map`, compound `@@index([businessId, ...])`.\n\n### 3.1 Prisma\n\n```prisma\nmodel Directive {\n  id         String   @id @default(cuid())\n  businessId String   @map(\"business_id\")\n  business   Business @relation(fields: [businessId], references: [id], onDelete: Cascade)\n\n  /// Who typed the sentence. Authority is composed from THIS user's membership,\n  /// never from a department role guessed off the text. Non-nullable on purpose:\n  /// a directive with no author has no authority and must not exist.\n  userId String @map(\"user_id\")\n  user   User   @relation(\"UserDirectives\", fields: [userId], references: [id], onDelete: Cascade)\n\n  instruction String\n  sessionId   String? @map(\"session_id\")\n\n  /// 'recipe:<id>' when a deterministic recipe matched, 'llm' otherwise.\n  /// Read by the tests and by /app/directives — a number that says how often\n  /// the LLM was needed at all.\n  planSource String @map(\"plan_source\")\n\n  /// planned | awaiting_authorization | authorized | running | completed\n  /// | failed | compensated | stranded | abandoned | expired\n  status String @default(\"planned\")\n\n  /// SagaExecution.id. Execution lineage lives in the existing saga tables;\n  /// this column is the join, not a duplicate.\n  sagaId String? @map(\"saga_id\")\n\n  authorizedAt DateTime? @map(\"authorized_at\")\n  startedAt    DateTime? @map(\"started_at\")\n  completedAt  DateTime? @map(\"completed_at\")\n\n  /// Named machine reason for a non-happy ending: 'unorderable',\n  /// 'authority_changed', 'step_failed', 'compensation_unavailable',\n  /// 'expired', 'abandoned_by_user'. Never free text.\n  outcomeReason String? @map(\"outcome_reason\")\n\n  /// The member's resolved authority AT PLAN TIME. Re-derived before the first\n  /// step runs; any widening between preview and run aborts with\n  /// outcomeReason='authority_changed'. A preview a user approved is a promise\n  /// about a specific set of powers, and it must not be honoured under a\n  /// different set.\n  authoritySnapshot Json @map(\"authority_snapshot\")\n\n  /// A directive is a conversation, not a schedule. Unauthorized past this, it\n  /// is expired by the sweep and never executed.\n  expiresAt DateTime @map(\"expires_at\")\n\n  steps DirectiveStep[]\n\n  createdAt DateTime @default(now()) @map(\"created_at\")\n  updatedAt DateTime @updatedAt @map(\"updated_at\")\n\n  @@index([businessId, status])\n  @@index([businessId, userId, createdAt])\n  @@index([businessId, expiresAt])\n  @@index([businessId, sagaId])\n  @@map(\"directives\")\n}\n\nmodel DirectiveStep {\n  id          String    @id @default(cuid())\n  /// businessId is carried on the CHILD as well as the parent, deliberately.\n  /// SagaStep (schema.prisma:7565) has none and is therefore permanently\n  /// outside BUSINESS_ID_MODELS — it can only ever be scoped by walking to its\n  /// parent. This table does not repeat that.\n  businessId  String    @map(\"business_id\")\n  business    Business  @relation(fields: [businessId], references: [id], onDelete: Cascade)\n  directiveId String    @map(\"directive_id\")\n  directive   Directive @relation(fields: [directiveId], references: [id], onDelete: Cascade)\n\n  stepIndex  Int    @map(\"step_index\")\n  department String\n  toolName   String @map(\"tool_name\")\n  riskTier   Int    @map(\"risk_tier\")\n\n  /// The sentence a human reads before it happens. Built by\n  /// DirectivePreviewService from REAL resolved values, not from the raw args.\n  summary String\n  args    Json   @default(\"{}\")\n\n  /// From KeyCortexCompensationService.getCompensatingAction(toolName).\n  /// NULL means nothing in this codebase can undo this step — the ordering\n  /// rule in §5.2 is derived from this column and nothing else.\n  compensationAction String? @map(\"compensation_action\")\n  /// Values captured at PLAN time so the rollback is real: previous booking\n  /// status, previous field value. Same pattern as\n  /// key-cortex-saga-executor.service.ts:141-155, which captures previousValue\n  /// for a blueprint field before overwriting it.\n  compensationPayload Json? @map(\"compensation_payload\")\n\n  /// auto | quick_confirm | formal | admin | denied\n  authorityVerdict String  @map(\"authority_verdict\")\n  authorityReason  String  @map(\"authority_reason\")\n  /// AiApprovalItem.id, created for ROUTING AND NOTIFICATION ONLY. The row in\n  /// this table is the source of truth for whether the step may run.\n  approvalItemId   String? @map(\"approval_item_id\")\n\n  /// pending | authorized | denied | running | completed | failed\n  /// | compensated | stranded | skipped\n  status String @default(\"pending\")\n\n  authorizedByUserId String?   @map(\"authorized_by_user_id\")\n  authorizedAt       DateTime? @map(\"authorized_at\")\n\n  output Json?\n  error  String?\n\n  startedAt   DateTime? @map(\"started_at\")\n  completedAt DateTime? @map(\"completed_at\")\n\n  createdAt DateTime @default(now()) @map(\"created_at\")\n  updatedAt DateTime @updatedAt @map(\"updated_at\")\n\n  @@unique([directiveId, stepIndex])\n  @@index([businessId, status])\n  @@index([directiveId, stepIndex])\n  @@index([businessId, approvalItemId])\n  @@map(\"directive_steps\")\n}\n```\n\nBack‑relations on `Business` (next to `sagaExecutions` at `schema.prisma:435`):\n\n```prisma\n  directives     Directive[]\n  directiveSteps DirectiveStep[]\n```\n\nand on `User`: `directives Directive[] @relation(\"UserDirectives\")`.\n\n### 3.2 Tenant isolation — the explicit bit\n\n**`'Directive'` and `'DirectiveStep'` are added to `BUSINESS_ID_MODELS` in `packages/db/src/client.ts:81‑160`**, in a new block with a comment naming this spec.\n\nThis is not optional and it is not automatic. Verified against `apps/server/src/core/prisma/tenant-model-list.spec.ts`: it asserts (a) every *listed* name is a real model, (b) every *listed* model has a `businessId` column, (c) `AiGoal` and `AiPlan` are listed. **It does not assert that every model with a `businessId` is listed.** A new model is unscoped by default and the suite stays green. §7.5 closes that hole for everyone.\n\nThe extension hooks `findMany/findFirst/...` but *not* `create/createMany/upsert` — stated in the file's own comment at `client.ts:130‑136`. So `businessId` on every write in this module is bound from the route param via `BusinessGuard`, never from the body. See §2.5/§6 on the DTO.\n\n### 3.3 Migration\n\n`packages/db/prisma/migrations/20260812090000_add_directive_tables/migration.sql`. Latest existing is `20260809170000_add_user_deleted_at_banned_at`, so this sorts after. Style follows `20260808140000_bank_transaction_external_id_unique` — a reason at the top, `IF NOT EXISTS` throughout, no destructive statement.\n\n```sql\n-- The multi-department turn: one instruction, ordered steps, per-step authority.\n--\n-- Two tables, and the reason there are two rather than one is the preview.\n-- SagaExecution/SagaStep (added by the Phase 0 saga migration) record what\n-- HAPPENED. These record what is ABOUT TO happen and who is allowed to let it —\n-- the plan a human reads and authorises before any of it runs. Directive.saga_id\n-- is the join between the two, so nothing is duplicated.\n--\n-- Purely additive: no column dropped, no row rewritten, every statement guarded.\n\nCREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS \"directives\" (\n  \"id\"                  TEXT PRIMARY KEY,\n  \"business_id\"         TEXT NOT NULL,\n  \"user_id\"             TEXT NOT NULL,\n  \"instruction\"         TEXT NOT NULL,\n  \"session_id\"          TEXT,\n  \"plan_source\"         TEXT NOT NULL,\n  \"status\"              TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT 'planned',\n  \"saga_id\"             TEXT,\n  \"authorized_at\"       TIMESTAMP(3),\n  \"started_at\"          TIMESTAMP(3),\n  \"completed_at\"        TIMESTAMP(3),\n  \"outcome_reason\"      TEXT,\n  \"authority_snapshot\"  JSONB NOT NULL,\n  \"expires_at\"          TIMESTAMP(3) NOT NULL,\n  \"created_at\"          TIMESTAMP(3) NOT NULL DEFAULT CURRENT_TIMESTAMP,\n  \"updated_at\"          TIMESTAMP(3) NOT NULL,\n  CONSTRAINT \"directives_business_id_fkey\" FOREIGN KEY (\"business_id\")\n    REFERENCES \"businesses\"(\"id\") ON DELETE CASCADE ON UPDATE CASCADE,\n  CONSTRAINT \"directives_user_id_fkey\" FOREIGN KEY (\"user_id\")\n    REFERENCES \"users\"(\"id\") ON DELETE CASCADE ON UPDATE CASCADE\n);\n\nCREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS \"directives_business_id_status_idx\"           ON \"directives\" (\"business_id\", \"status\");\nCREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS \"directives_business_id_user_id_created_idx\"  ON \"directives\" (\"business_id\", \"user_id\", \"created_at\");\nCREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS \"directives_business_id_expires_at_idx\"       ON \"directives\" (\"business_id\", \"expires_at\");\nCREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS \"directives_business_id_saga_id_idx\"          ON \"directives\" (\"business_id\", \"saga_id\");\n\nCREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS \"directive_steps\" (\n  \"id\"                     TEXT PRIMARY KEY,\n  \"business_id\"            TEXT NOT NULL,\n  \"directive_id\"           TEXT NOT NULL,\n  \"step_index\"             INTEGER NOT NULL,\n  \"department\"             TEXT NOT NULL,\n  \"tool_name\"              TEXT NOT NULL,\n  \"risk_tier\"              INTEGER NOT NULL,\n  \"summary\"                TEXT NOT NULL,\n  \"args\"                   JSONB NOT NULL DEFAULT '{}',\n  \"compensation_action\"    TEXT,\n  \"compensation_payload\"   JSONB,\n  \"authority_verdict\"      TEXT NOT NULL,\n  \"authority_reason\"       TEXT NOT NULL,\n  \"approval_item_id\"       TEXT,\n  \"status\"                 TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT 'pending',\n  \"authorized_by_user_id\"  TEXT,\n  \"authorized_at\"          TIMESTAMP(3),\n  \"output\"                 JSONB,\n  \"error\"                  TEXT,\n  \"started_at\"             TIMESTAMP(3),\n  \"completed_at\"           TIMESTAMP(3),\n  \"created_at\"             TIMESTAMP(3) NOT NULL DEFAULT CURRENT_TIMESTAMP,\n  \"updated_at\"             TIMESTAMP(3) NOT NULL,\n  CONSTRAINT \"directive_steps_business_id_fkey\" FOREIGN KEY (\"business_id\")\n    REFERENCES \"businesses\"(\"id\") ON DELETE CASCADE ON UPDATE CASCADE,\n  CONSTRAINT \"directive_steps_directive_id_fkey\" FOREIGN KEY (\"directive_id\")\n    REFERENCES \"directives\"(\"id\") ON DELETE CASCADE ON UPDATE CASCADE\n);\n\n-- The uniqueness is what makes the executor's \"run step N\" idempotent at the\n-- database, not only at the idempotency-key registry.\nCREATE UNIQUE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS \"directive_steps_directive_id_step_index_key\"\n  ON \"directive_steps\" (\"directive_id\", \"step_index\");\nCREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS \"directive_steps_business_id_status_idx\"    ON \"directive_steps\" (\"business_id\", \"status\");\nCREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS \"directive_steps_business_id_approval_idx\"  ON \"directive_steps\" (\"business_id\", \"approval_item_id\");\n```\n\n---\n\n## 4. Permission, composed from the user\n\n### 4.1 The rule\n\n> **Authority is the intersection of (the member's grant) × (the business's ceiling) × (the tool's tier), computed per STEP. The department role is removed from the authority path entirely.**\n\nThe department role survives as a *voice* — `getSystemPromptForRole` (`role-engine.service.ts:610‑634`) still shapes tone in the reply. It stops deciding what exists.\n\n### 4.2 Three inputs\n\n**(a) The member.** `directive-authority.service.ts` reads `Membership` (`schema.prisma:2174`) once per directive:\n\n```ts\nexport interface MemberAuthority {\n  userId: string; businessId: string; role: 'OWNER' | 'ADMIN' | 'STAFF';\n  scopes: Record<string, 'none' | 'read' | 'write' | 'admin'>;\n  maxApprovalTier: 0 | 1 | 2 | 3 | 4;\n}\n```\n\n**The scope map must be reconciled before it is read.** `ModuleScopeGuard.DEFAULT_SCOPES` (`core/auth/module-scope.guard.ts:22‑38`) enumerates **13** keys including `analytics` and `operations`. `IdentityService.PERMISSION_MODULES` (`modules/identity/identity.service.ts:411‑413`) enumerates **11** and has neither, and `inviteTeamMember` writes that short map into `permissionScopes` on every invite (`:506‑513`). The web's own team screen (`apps/web/src/app/app/settings/team/page.tsx:80‑92`) also lists 11. So a member invited as ADMIN resolves `'none'` on `operations`. If this module reads `permissionScopes` naively, **every invited admin is denied every operations step and the feature is dead on arrival for anyone but the founder** (whose membership is upserted with no scopes at all and therefore falls through to `DEFAULT_SCOPES`).\n\nResolution, owned by `DirectiveAuthorityService.resolveScopes()`:\n\n```\nfor each department key D:\n  stored   = membership.permissionScopes?.[D]\n  fallback = DEFAULT_SCOPES[membership.role][D]\n  scopes[D] = stored ?? fallback ?? 'none'\n```\n\nA key **absent** from the stored map falls back to the role default; a key **present** and set to `'none'` is honoured as a deliberate denial. That distinction is the whole fix, and it is one `??` rather than `||`. A spec asserts `PERMISSION_MODULES ⊇ keys(DEFAULT_SCOPES.OWNER)` and fails today — so the reconciliation is visible as a failing gate, not buried in a helper. (Fixing `identity.service.ts` is Wave 0 #5 of the capability map and belongs to that PR; this module must be correct whether or not that lands, which is what the fallback buys.)\n\n**(b) The business.** `AiOversightService.evaluate(businessId, toolName, undefined, undefined, undefined)`. With `role` and `envelope` both undefined the envelope branch (`ai-oversight.service.ts:137‑143`) and the role branch (`:144‑146`) are both skipped, and what returns is the pure business ceiling: `blockedTools`, `blockedModules`, `mode` (`advisory`/`restricted` still return nothing at all), `autonomyLevel`, `approvedTools`, `maxAutoTier`. **`ai-oversight.service.ts` is not modified.** No new parameter, no new import, no edge.\n\n**(c) The tool.** `getToolTier(toolName)` (`ai-oversight.service.ts:95‑108`) and `getToolByName(...).family` from `flow-tool-registry.ts`.\n\n### 4.3 Tool → department\n\n`ai-oversight.service.ts:728‑743` `inferModule` returns a *tool family* (`'organize'`, `'execute'`, `'drafts'`, `'intelligence'`) that is not a permission‑scope key. It cannot be reused for authority. This module owns an explicit map:\n\n```ts\n// tool-department.map.ts\n//\n// KEYS ARE REAL TOOL-NAME PREFIXES AND VALUES ARE REAL PERMISSION-SCOPE KEYS.\n// Both halves are asserted by directive-department-map.spec.ts, because a map\n// whose keys match nothing is the exact defect KeyCortexCompensationService\n// documents at key-cortex-compensation.service.ts:59-81 — eight of ten keys\n// naming tools that did not exist, so the lookup returned undefined for\n// almost everything and the fix changed nothing.\nexport const TOOL_DEPARTMENT: Record<string, string> = {\n  payments_:   'revenue',   commerce_:  'revenue',   finance_:    'revenue',\n  reconcile_:  'revenue',   expenses_:  'expenses',  procurement_:'operations',\n  inventory_:  'operations',bookings_:  'bookings',  calendar_:   'bookings',\n  crm_:        'crm',       deals_:     'crm',       contracts_:  'crm',\n  projects_:   'projects',  time_:      'projects',  marketing_:  'content',\n  social_:     'content',   content_:   'content',   seo_:        'content',\n  store_:      'storefront',marketplace_:'storefront',\n  inbox_:      'crm',       comms_:     'crm',       helpdesk_:   'crm',\n  automations_:'automations',delegation_:'automations',\n  structure_:  'team',      people_:    'team',      payroll_:    'team',\n  documents_:  'crm',       drive_:     'crm',       fetch_:      'analytics',\n  reports_:    'analytics', goals_:     'analytics',\n};\nexport const DEPARTMENT_LABEL: Record<string, string> = { revenue: 'Payments & invoicing', bookings: 'Bookings', crm: 'Customers & comms', /* ... */ };\n```\n\nEvery one of the 245 tools must resolve to exactly one department. Unmatched names fail the build (§7.1) — that is how a 246th tool cannot silently become invisible to every directive.\n\n### 4.4 The verdict, per step\n\n```\ndept  = departmentFor(toolName)\nscope = member.scopes[dept]\ntier  = getToolTier(toolName)\nfamily= getToolByName(toolName).family\n\nif (!business.allowed)                    -> denied  (business reason, verbatim)\nif (scope === 'none')                     -> denied  (\"your account has no access to <label>\")\nif (scope === 'read' && family !== 'read')-> denied  (\"read-only access to <label>\")\n\nceiling = min(business.maxAutoTier,\n              member.maxApprovalTier,\n              scope === 'admin' ? 4 : scope === 'write' ? 2 : 1)\n\nif (tier <= ceiling)                      -> auto\nif (tier === 2)                           -> quick_confirm\nif (tier === 3)                           -> formal\nif (tier === 4)                           -> admin\n```\n\n`Membership.maxApprovalTier` defaults to OWNER 4 / ADMIN 3 / STAFF 0 (`identity.service.ts:421‑425`). So a STAFF member gets `ceiling = 0`: every write in their directive needs a human. That is correct and it is the reason the feature is safe to ship with a wide tool set.\n\n### 4.5 Assembling the tool set without abandoning the ceiling\n\nToday the ceiling is enforced by **hiding**: a tool the role cannot use is never shown to the model (`flow-orchestrator.service.ts:1467`). Hiding produces a plan that is *wrong* — the model says \"I've released the booking\" having silently dropped the refund, because it never saw a refund tool. That is the fabricated‑success class the repo already pins in `flow-tool-honesty.spec.ts`.\n\n**The ceiling moves from visibility to authorization.**\n\n- **Candidate set** = ∪ over all departments `D` where `member.scopes[D] !== 'none'`, of every tool whose department is `D`, filtered by family where `scope === 'read'`. Tools blocked by `business.blockedTools`/`blockedModules` are excluded — a business‑level *no* is a real no, not a \"needs approval\".\n- **Rank and cap at 128** (the OpenAI array limit). The ranking algorithm already exists, unused, at `flow-orchestrator.service.ts:1071‑1088`: tokenise the message, score by tool‑name part overlap, stable‑sort, slice. It is lifted verbatim into `directive-planner.service.ts` as a pure function. The dead original should be deleted in the same PR — a deletion in `ai/`, not an import.\n- Every high‑tier tool in the candidate set carries its tier into the plan, and the plan carries it into the preview. Nothing executes on tier alone.\n\nThree hard caps survive so that \"wider tool set\" does not mean \"unbounded blast radius\":\n\n1. **≤ 6 steps** per directive. Beyond that it is a project, not an instruction.\n2. **≤ 1 irreversible step** (`compensationAction === null`, §5.2).\n3. **≤ 1 money‑out step** — today exactly one tool qualifies, `payments_refund_charge`, and its own registry comment says so (`flow-tool-registry.ts:1296‑1300`: *\"the only tool in the arsenal that moves money out of the business to a third party\"*). The list is a named constant with a spec asserting each entry is a real tool.\n\n---\n\n## 5. Transactional integrity\n\n### 5.1 Execution shape\n\n`run()` enqueues **one BullMQ job for the whole directive**:\n\n```ts\nawait this.directiveQueue.add('directive', { businessId, directiveId }, {\n  jobId: `directive:${directiveId}`,   // ← the distributed lock. Two replicas, one run.\n  attempts: 1,                          // the SAGA retries, not the queue\n  removeOnComplete: { age: 7 * 24 * 3600, count: 1000 },\n  removeOnFail:     { age: 30 * 24 * 3600, count: 500 },\n});\n```\n\n`jobId` is BullMQ's own dedupe key — `queue.service.ts:97‑111` already relies on it for plan steps. `attempts: 1` is deliberate: a whole‑directive retry would re‑run completed steps. Retry is per step, inside the worker, and only for steps the tool registry marks retryable.\n\nThere are **26 `@Cron` jobs and 100 raw `setInterval` call sites in `apps/server/src` outside specs** (both re‑derived by grep today), with zero leader election. This module adds none of either. The expiry sweep is a repeatable BullMQ job using the upsert pattern already at `queue.service.ts:113‑132`:\n\n```ts\nawait this.expiryQueue.add('directive-expiry', {}, {\n  jobId: 'directive-expiry:v1',\n  repeat: { pattern: '*/5 * * * *', tz: 'UTC' },\n});\n```\n\nNo module field holds directive state. `UndoService` (`ai/undo.service.ts:27`) keeps `recentActions` in a `Map` — that is the pattern this module must not copy, and the header says so.\n\n### 5.2 The ordering rule\n\nDerived from data already in the repo, not invented:\n\n> **A directive's steps are ordered so that every step with a compensating action precedes every step without one. Within each group, declared order is preserved. If the dependency graph forbids that ordering, the directive is rejected at plan time with `outcomeReason = 'unorderable'` and the preview shows why.**\n\n`compensationAction` comes from `KeyCortexCompensationService.getCompensatingAction(toolName)` (`key-cortex-compensation.service.ts:82‑132`). Today `payments_refund_charge` has no entry, and the registry explains why in prose (`flow-tool-registry.ts:1296‑1300`): *\"There is no compensating action — a refund cannot be un‑refunded, only re‑charged, which requires the customer to pay again.\"*\n\nFor the thesis sentence this yields:\n\n| # | Department | Tool | Tier | Reversible |\n|---|---|---|---|---|\n| 1 | Bookings | `bookings_cancel_booking` | 3 | **yes**, after §5.4 |\n| 2 | Customers & comms | `inbox_reply_thread` | 3 | best‑effort (`key_inbox.mark_draft`) |\n| 3 | Payments | `payments_refund_charge` | 3 | **no** — last, by rule |\n\nIf step 3 fails, steps 2 and 1 roll back and the customer is not left with a cancelled booking and no money. If step 1 or 2 fails, no money has left. **The ordering rule is the transactional design; the saga is only its bookkeeping.**\n\n### 5.3 Step loop\n\n```\nsaga = KeyCortexSagaService.start({ businessId, sagaType: 'directive',\n                                    correlationId: directiveId, sessionId })\nDirective.sagaId = saga.id\n\nfor i in 0..n-1:\n   KeyCortexSagaService.addStep(saga.id, i, `${dept}.${tool}`, args,\n       step.compensationAction\n         ? { stepName, action: step.compensationAction,\n             payload: { businessId, ...args, ...step.compensationPayload } }\n         : undefined)\n\nre-derive MemberAuthority; if it differs from authoritySnapshot\n   -> abort, status='failed', outcomeReason='authority_changed', no step runs\n\nfor i in 0..n-1 SEQUENTIALLY:\n   idem = { businessId, idempotencyKey: `dir:${directiveId}:${i}`,\n            requestHash: sha256(toolName + stable(args)) }\n   if KeyIdempotencyService.check(idem).status === 'completed' -> reuse, continue\n   step.status='running'\n   try:\n      out = FlowOrchestratorService.executeToolDirectly(businessId, toolName, args)\n      KeyIdempotencyService.complete(idem, out)\n      KeyCortexSagaService.completeStep(saga.id, i, out)\n      step.status='completed'; emit directive.step.completed\n   catch e:\n      KeyIdempotencyService.fail(idem, e.message)\n      KeyCortexSagaService.failStep(saga.id, i, e.message)\n      step.status='failed'; emit directive.step.failed\n      goto ROLLBACK\n```\n\nSequential, not `Promise.all`. `flow-orchestrator.service.ts:1563` runs tool calls concurrently, which is why \"refund then release\" has no defined order today even in the impossible case where one role could do both.\n\n### 5.4 Rollback, and the honest failure\n\n```\nROLLBACK:\n   results = KeyCortexSagaService.compensate(saga.id)     // reverse order\n   reread SagaExecution.status\n   'compensated'              -> Directive.status='compensated'; emit directive.compensated\n   'compensation_failed'      -> Directive.status='stranded';    emit directive.stranded\n   'compensation_unavailable' -> Directive.status='stranded';    emit directive.stranded\n```\n\n`compensate()` at `key-cortex-saga.service.ts:177‑189` already distinguishes these three, and the comment at `:163‑176` records exactly why: *\"Nothing in production writes `SagaStep.compensationAction` … So every real saga skipped every step, returned `[]`, and then recorded that it had rolled back. The audit trail asserted a recovery that never happened.\"*\n\n**This module is the first production writer of `SagaStep.compensationAction`.** That is a reuse, and it is also the fix.\n\n`directive.stranded` is not a log line. Its consumer creates a tier‑4 `AiApprovalItem` titled *\"A directive stopped part‑way and could not be undone\"*, listing each completed step, what it did, and the manual route to reverse it by hand. `ApprovalRoutingService` (via `ai-oversight.service.ts:416/430‑437`) routes it to a real approver and pushes a notification. A partial multi‑department action that nobody is told about is the worst outcome this feature can produce, and it is the one the design spends the most on.\n\n**Two compensations to add, through the public extension point.** `KeyCortexCompensationService.register(actionRef, handler)` is public (`key-cortex-compensation.service.ts:45‑47`). `DirectiveModule` registers two in `onModuleInit`, editing no key‑cortex file:\n\n- `bookings.restore_status` — restores `Booking.status` to `compensationPayload.previousStatus`, captured at plan time by reading the booking. This is what makes `bookings_cancel_booking` reversible, and it is the exact pattern `key-cortex-saga-executor.service.ts:141‑155` already uses to capture `previousValue` before a blueprint write.\n- `directive.noop_marker` — for steps whose tool is a `read`. Never invoked; present so a read step is never counted as \"declared no compensation\".\n\n`getCompensatingAction` is a pure lookup owned by key‑cortex, so mapping `bookings_cancel_booking → bookings.restore_status` is a two‑line addition to its table (`:82‑132`). That table's own docstring already sets the bar: *\"Adding a key here is a promise that the handler below genuinely reverses the effect — do not add one to make a number go up.\"* This one does, and `directive-compensation.spec.ts` (§7.3) proves it against a real database.\n\n---\n\n## 6. Approval — what needs it and what does not\n\n### 6.1 All‑or‑nothing at the front\n\n> **A directive begins only when every step is authorized. There is no partial start.**\n\nThis is the single most valuable decision in the spec and it is a product decision, not a technical one. Three separate approvals arriving at three different moments is exactly how you get a refund issued against a booking that was never released. Collecting them up front turns three interruptions into one review and eliminates most stranded states by construction: you approve the refund *before* the booking is touched.\n\n### 6.2 The four verdicts\n\n| Verdict | When | How it is resolved | Who |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| `auto` | `tier ≤ ceiling` (§4.4) | nothing — pre‑authorized in the preview | — |\n| `quick_confirm` | tier 2 above ceiling | the Allow button on the preview card | the directive's author |\n| `formal` | tier 3 | Approve on `/app/directives/:id`; an `AiApprovalItem` is created for routing + notification | any member with `maxApprovalTier ≥ 3` |\n| `admin` | tier 4, or any irreversible step, or any money‑out step | same surface, higher bar | any member with `maxApprovalTier ≥ 4` |\n\n**Irreversible and money‑out are promoted to `admin` regardless of tier.** `payments_refund_charge` is tier 3; inside a directive it needs a tier‑4 approver. A directive that moves money out is not the same thing as a lone refund on a screen, and the composition is what raises the stakes.\n\n**Self‑approval is refused** when `verdict ∈ {formal, admin}` and the approver is the directive's `userId` *and* `member.maxApprovalTier < 4`. An OWNER approving their own directive is the normal case for a one‑person business and is allowed.\n\n### 6.3 Reusing the approvals surface without owning it\n\n`AiApprovalItem` rows are created via `AiOversightService.createApprovalItem` (`ai-oversight.service.ts:383‑421`) with `planId = directiveId` and `planStepId = directiveStepId` — both existing nullable columns, zero schema change on the approvals side. The row exists **for routing and notification only**; `DirectiveStep.status` is the source of truth for whether a step may run.\n\n`AiApprovalsService` emits nothing on resolve (verified: `grep \"events.emit(\" ai-approvals.service.ts` returns nothing), so there is no event to listen for and no dead listener to create. Instead, `/app/approvals` renders items whose `planId` starts with `dir_` as a **\"Review directive →\"** link to `/app/directives/:id` rather than an inline approve button. One conditional in the existing approvals component; the directive screen stays the only place a directive is authorized.\n\n### 6.4 Expiry\n\n`expiresAt = now + 30 minutes` for `quick_confirm`‑only directives, `now + 24 hours` when any step needs formal/admin approval. The 5‑minute repeatable sweep sets `status='expired'`, `outcomeReason='expired'`, resolves the open `AiApprovalItem` rows, and emits `directive.expired`. An expired directive has run nothing, so there is nothing to compensate.\n\n---\n\n## 7. The three layers\n\n### 7.1 SMART — deterministic, and only as wide as it is real\n\n`directive-recipes.ts` holds a **static** table. No Prisma model, no LLM, no `autoExecutable`‑style field written and never read.\n\n```ts\nexport interface DirectiveRecipe {\n  id: string;\n  match: RegExp;                    // over the instruction\n  steps: Array<{ department: string; toolName: string; bind: string[] }>;\n}\n\nexport const DIRECTIVE_RECIPES: DirectiveRecipe[] = [\n  {\n    id: 'refund_and_release',\n    match: /\\brefund\\b[\\s\\S]{0,60}\\b(booking|appointment|slot|reservation)\\b|\\b(booking|appointment|slot)\\b[\\s\\S]{0,60}\\brefund\\b/i,\n    steps: [\n      { department: 'bookings', toolName: 'bookings_cancel_booking', bind: ['bookingId'] },\n      { department: 'crm',      toolName: 'inbox_reply_thread',      bind: ['threadId', 'body'] },\n      { department: 'revenue',  toolName: 'payments_refund_charge',  bind: ['gateway', 'chargeId', 'amount', 'reason'] },\n    ],\n  },\n  // ...\n];\n```\n\nShip **three** recipes, not thirty. The audit's finding is that `/app/automations` advertises 32 triggers and 25 actions against an engine implementing 8 and 6, and logs `outcome: 'success'` either way. This table exists in code so a spec can enumerate it, and `directive-recipes.spec.ts` asserts every `toolName` is in `FLOW_TOOLS`, every `bind` name is a declared parameter of that tool, and every `department` is a key of `DEPARTMENT_LABEL`. The advertised set and the implemented set are the same array; they cannot drift.\n\nA matched recipe fixes the **step list deterministically**; the LLM is used only to fill `bind` arguments from the conversation. `planSource` records `'recipe:refund_and_release'` vs `'llm'`, so how often the LLM plans at all is a number, not a guess.\n\nEntry from a rule, no HTTP and no LLM: emit `directive.requested` with `{ recipeId, args }`.\n\n### 7.2 MANUAL — `/app/directives`\n\n- `apps/web/src/app/app/directives/page.tsx` — the list: instruction, departments touched, status, when.\n- `apps/web/src/app/app/directives/[id]/page.tsx` — the detail: ordered steps, each with department, sentence, tier, reversible/not, verdict, approver, and per‑step **Approve / Deny**; plus **Run** and **Abandon**.\n- `apps/web/src/lib/api/directives.ts` — thin wrappers over `apiPost`/`apiGet`.\n- Nav entry beside Approvals (`apps/web/src/lib/nav-config.ts:236`).\n\nThe gate at `apps/web/src/lib/tool-route-audit.ts:97‑101` requires the page to import a genuine write from `@/lib/api/*` matching `^(apiPost|apiPatch|apiPut|apiDelete)$` (`:478`). The Approve/Run/Abandon buttons satisfy that with real calls, not a shim.\n\nAnd per‑step, both on the detail screen and on the chat preview card, a **\"Do it yourself\"** link built from `getManualEquivalentRoute(tool)` (`flow-tool-registry.ts:44‑57`). `manualEquivalentRoute` appears in **0** files under `apps/web/src` today — the guarantee that KEY is optional is currently an engineering invariant no user can see. This surface is where it becomes visible.\n\n### 7.3 AI — three tools\n\n| name | family | riskTier | manualEquivalentRoute | changedEntities |\n|---|---|---|---|---|\n| `directive_list` | `read` | 1 | `/app/directives` | — |\n| `directive_get` | `read` | 1 | `/app/directives` | — |\n| `directive_abandon` | `organize` | 2 | `/app/directives` | `['directive']` |\n\n`crossDomain` (`tool-route-audit.ts:118‑123`) takes the prefix before the first `_` — `directive` — and requires it to appear in the route with `/` and `-` stripped: `appdirectives` contains `directive`. Passes without a `CROSS_DOMAIN_ROUTES` exemption. `directive_abandon` is a write, so `noManualWrite` requires a real write on the page — the Abandon button.\n\nHandlers are `case` clauses in `executeToolAction` (`flow-orchestrator.service.ts:2251`) calling the injected `DIRECTIVE_PORT`… **no.** That would be an `ai/ → directive/` edge and it is forbidden by §2.5. The three tools are **bridged**, not cased: registered through `CORTEX_TOOL_BRIDGE` (`flow-tool-registry.ts`), which `flow-tool-honesty.spec.ts:52‑60` already exempts from the case‑clause rule *\"because their handler belongs to the organ adapter that registered it\"*, and which `cortex-tool-bridge.spec.ts` separately proves dispatches. `DirectiveModule` registers the adapter at boot. This is the existing mechanism for exactly this problem, and using it is why the tools cost no edge.\n\nThe three tools are `read`/`organize` on purpose. There is deliberately **no `directive_run` tool** — see §9.\n\n---\n\n## 8. What the user sees before it happens\n\n### 8.1 Route\n\nThe web's `use-key-chat-actions.ts:336` posts to `/ai/businesses/:id/flow/chat` (`client.ts:5714`). It changes to `POST /directives/businesses/:businessId/plan`.\n\n`DirectiveService.plan()` decides in one cheap, zero‑I/O step — same shape as the thalamus at `flow-orchestrator.service.ts:1323`:\n\n```\nif a DIRECTIVE_RECIPE matches                            -> directive\nelse if hasClauseSeparator(msg) && departmentKeywordFamilies(msg).size >= 2\n                                                          -> directive (LLM plan)\nelse -> delegate to FlowOrchestratorService.chat(...)      (unchanged, public at :1260)\n```\n\nSingle‑department turns take exactly today's code path with one extra function call. Plus an explicit UI escape hatch — a **\"Do all of it\"** toggle on the composer — for when the heuristic misses.\n\n**Every write on this controller uses a `class-validator` DTO**, not the inline‑typed `@Body()` at `flow.controller.ts:22‑34`. That inline type is why the global `ValidationPipe` whitelist strips nothing and a client can today supply its own `pageContext.jobRoleEnvelope`, which `ai-oversight.service.ts:137` then trusts ahead of every department check. This module never accepts an authority object from a client. Authority is read from `Membership` by `userId` from `@CurrentUser()` and `businessId` from the route param, behind `AuthGuard` + `BusinessGuard`.\n\n### 8.2 Resolving reads before showing the plan\n\nBefore the preview is returned, the module executes **only** steps whose tool `family === 'read'` and `riskTier === 1`, through `executeToolDirectly`. This is what turns a guess into a preview: `payments_search_transactions` resolves the charge, `bookings_list_bookings` resolves the slot, `crm_search_contacts` resolves the name. Read tools are safe by definition and by tier; nothing else runs.\n\n### 8.3 What renders\n\nNew stream chunk type `directive_preview` on `FlowStreamChunk` and a `directive` field on the response; a new `components/key/chat/key-directive-card.tsx` beside the existing `key-plan-card.tsx`, mounted from `key-message-renderer.tsx:155‑168`.\n\n```\n┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐\n│  One instruction · 3 steps · 3 departments      expires in 24 min     │\n│  \"The client cancelled — refund them and release their booking\"       │\n├──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤\n│ 1 ▸ BOOKINGS                                     can be undone   T3  │\n│     Cancel Anisa Mohammed's Deep Clean, Sat 16 Aug 10:00             │\n│     ✓ pre-authorized — you have admin on Bookings   · Do it yourself →│\n├──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤\n│ 2 ▸ CUSTOMERS & COMMS                          best-effort undo  T3  │\n│     Reply on WhatsApp: \"Hi Anisa — your Saturday appointment is       │\n│     cancelled and your TT$450.00 is on its way back…\"                 │\n│     ✓ pre-authorized                                · Do it yourself →│\n├──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤\n│ 3 ▸ PAYMENTS                            ⚠ CANNOT BE UNDONE       T3  │\n│     Refund TT$450.00 to Anisa Mohammed (Stripe ch_3PqL…9x, 3 Aug)     │\n│     ⏸ needs an admin — money leaves the business    · Do it yourself →│\n├──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤\n│  Steps run in this order. The refund is last on purpose: everything   │\n│  before it can be reversed if something fails.                        │\n│                                                                       │\n│      [ Run all ]   [ Run steps 1–2 only ]   [ Cancel ]                │\n└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘\n```\n\nFour things this card must show and today's `key-plan-card.tsx` does not: **the department**, **whether it can be undone**, **who has to say yes**, and **the order, with the reason for it**. The blue sentence at the bottom is generated from the ordering rule, not written by the model — it is a fact about the plan.\n\nWhile running: the same card, steps flipping to Working… / Done / Failed. On rollback, an inserted row per compensated step: *\"Undone: booking restored to CONFIRMED.\"*\n\n---\n\n## 9. Tests, each with the control that proves it can fail\n\nThe recurring defect in `OPEN_GAPS_2026-08-09.md` is *\"a mock that describes an impossible shape … certifies the broken thing as correct.\"* Every spec below states what it asserts and what would make it fail.\n\n**9.1 `directive-department-map.spec.ts`** — every one of the 245 `FLOW_TOOLS` names resolves to exactly one `TOOL_DEPARTMENT` prefix; every value is a key of `ModuleScopeGuard.DEFAULT_SCOPES.OWNER`; no prefix is a prefix of another. *Control:* add a fixture entry `nonexistent_ → 'crm'` inside the test and assert the checker reports it — proving the checker reads the registry rather than the map.\n\n**9.2 `directive-authority.spec.ts`** — the §0 table, asserted. (a) Under `RoleEngineService.isToolAllowed`, no `BusinessRole` permits both `payments_refund_charge` and `bookings_cancel_booking` — this pins the defect so a well‑meaning widening of `operations.approvedTools` cannot quietly make the whole spec pointless. (b) Under `DirectiveAuthorityService`, an OWNER gets both, with `payments_refund_charge → admin` and `bookings_cancel_booking → formal`. (c) A membership whose `permissionScopes` is the 11‑key map from `identity.service.ts:411` still resolves `operations` to `admin` for an ADMIN — the fallback of §4.2. *Control:* set `maxApprovalTier = 0` and assert the refund flips from `admin` to `denied`, not merely to a different string.\n\n**9.3 `directive-ordering.spec.ts`** — a plan with an uncompensatable step before a compensatable one is rewritten to put it last; a plan where that is impossible is rejected with `outcomeReason='unorderable'`. *Control:* the same plan with the irreversible step already last is **accepted** — otherwise the assertion is satisfied by a checker that rejects everything.\n\n**9.4 `directive-compensation.integration.test.ts`** — real Postgres, in `apps/server/test/` beside `bank-statement-ingest.integration.test.ts`. Three steps, step 3 throws. Assert: `SagaExecution.status === 'compensated'` **and** every step with a non‑null `compensationAction` has `status='compensated'` with a `compensationResult.success === true` **and** the booking row is back at its previous status. Asserting the status string alone is precisely the failure `key-cortex-saga.service.ts:163‑176` documents. *Control:* de‑register `bookings.restore_status` and assert the status becomes `compensation_unavailable` and the directive becomes `stranded` — proving the test distinguishes \"rolled back\" from \"recorded that it rolled back\".\n\n**9.5 `tenant-model-list.spec.ts` — the missing third assertion.** Add: *every model in `schema.prisma` declaring both `businessId String` and `business Business @relation` is either in `BUSINESS_ID_MODELS` or on an `UNSCOPED_BY_DESIGN` list with a written reason.* Seed that list with the exemptions the file already argues for in prose — `Payment` and `MarketplaceOrder` (`client.ts:139‑156`) — so it starts green. *Control:* the test temporarily removes `'Contact'` from its parsed copy of the set and asserts the checker reports it. **This is the highest‑leverage test in the spec**: it converts \"a new model is unscoped by default\" from a fact into a build failure, for every model anyone adds after this one.\n\n**9.6 `directive-single-fire.integration.test.ts`** — two `Worker` instances on one Redis, one directive enqueued twice with the same `jobId`; assert exactly one `SagaExecution` row and one `IdempotencyKey` per step. *Control:* enqueue with `jobId: undefined` and assert two runs — proving the `jobId` is what is doing the work.\n\n**9.7 `directive-no-back-edge.spec.ts`** — §2.5. *Control:* the same grep against `modules/ai` returns a non‑zero count.\n\n**9.8 `directive-recipes.spec.ts`** — §7.1 set‑equality. *Control:* a fixture recipe naming `bookings_teleport_customer` fails.\n\n**9.9 `directive-preview-honesty.spec.ts`** — no `DirectiveStep` may reach `status='completed'` with a null `output`; no directive may reach `status='completed'` with a step still `pending`. The `outcome: 'success'`‑after‑a‑no‑op class, blocked at the schema level rather than by review. *Control:* a fixture writing `completed` with a null output fails.\n\n**9.10** Web: extend `apps/web/src/lib/__tests__/tool-routes.spec.ts` and `nav-reachability.spec.ts` — both already run in CI and will pick the three new tools and the new route up automatically once `/app/directives` exists.\n\n---\n\n## 10. Effort, and what to adopt\n\n| Wave | Content | Days |\n|---|---|---|\n| **A** | `TOOL_DEPARTMENT` map + `DirectiveAuthorityService` + scope reconciliation + specs 9.1/9.2/9.5 | 5 |\n| **B** | `DirectivePlannerService` (recipes, ordering, cap), `DirectivePreviewService` (read resolution), Prisma + migration + `BUSINESS_ID_MODELS` + specs 9.3/9.8 | 5 |\n| **C** | `DirectiveExecutorService`: BullMQ queue, saga wiring, idempotency, compensation registration, expiry sweep + specs 9.4/9.6/9.9 | 5 |\n| **D** | Controller + DTOs + contract + `directive.module.ts` + spec 9.7; the three bridged tools | 2 |\n| **E** | `/app/directives` list + detail, `key-directive-card.tsx`, nav, chat routing switch, \"Do it yourself\" links | 4 |\n| **F** | One `directive.stranded` consumer; `/app/approvals` \"Review directive\" link; docs | 1 |\n\n**22 dev‑days ≈ 4½ calendar weeks** with review. Waves A–B are independently shippable and independently valuable: the authority service alone fixes the invited‑ADMIN lockout for anything that consumes it, and spec 9.5 protects every future model whether or not the rest lands.\n\n**ADOPT: nothing new.** Everything required is installed and running:\n\n- **BullMQ** `^5.76.8` (MIT), `apps/server/package.json:42`, already driving two queues. `jobId` gives single‑fire across replicas by construction. Redis is in both compose files.\n- **The saga tables and services** (in‑repo) — this is the reuse the brief asked for, and this module is their first production driver.\n- **`class-validator` / `class-transformer`** — already the global `ValidationPipe`'s basis.\n\n**Explicitly refused, with the reason:**\n\n- **Temporal** (MIT) / **Trigger.dev v3** (Apache‑2.0). A directive is seconds long, capped at six steps, and expires in ≤ 24 hours. Temporal is a container, a worker SDK and a new mental model for durability BullMQ already provides with a `jobId` — and `docs/CAPABILITY_MAP_2026-08-09.md` §3.4 already says *\"Do not build a workflow engine.\"* Adopting one to avoid building one is the same mistake with a licence attached. Named here as the escape hatch **if and only if** directives ever need to span days or survive a Redis flush.\n- **n8n** — Sustainable Use License, explicitly restricts hosting for third parties, which is what a SaaS is.\n- **Windmill / Cal.com** — AGPL‑3.0. Fine as a network sidecar, never linked into `apps/server`.\n\n---\n\n## 11. What this deliberately does not do\n\n- **Does not touch `flow-orchestrator.service.ts`'s `chat()` or `streamChat()`.** Single‑department turns keep today's behaviour byte for byte. The only edit contemplated in `ai/` is deleting the dead `selectToolsForRequest` (`:1033‑1089`) after its ranking body is lifted, and that is a deletion, not an edge.\n- **Does not delete or rewrite `RoleEngineService`.** Eight roles keep shaping tone and priorities via `getSystemPromptForRole`. They stop deciding what exists. Nothing else in the repo that calls `isToolAllowed` — `conversational-ai.service.ts:105` and the two orchestrator filters — is changed by this spec.\n- **Does not unify the three role systems.** That is Wave 1 #21 of the capability map, three weeks, and it is orthogonal: this module composes authority from `Membership`, which all three systems already hang off.\n- **Does not give KEY a `directive_run` tool.** A tool that runs a plan of tools is a privilege‑escalation primitive: it would let a low‑tier turn assemble a high‑tier directive and then execute it with one tool call. A directive is created by a person or by a deterministic rule, authorized against that person's membership, and run from a screen. The three tools shipped are two reads and one cancel.\n- **Does not make directives durable across days.** `expiresAt` is 30 minutes or 24 hours. \"Refund them next Tuesday\" is a scheduled job, not a directive, and belongs to the BullMQ repeatable work in Wave 2 #27.\n- **Does not do cross‑tenant.** Every query is `businessId`‑scoped, both models are in `BUSINESS_ID_MODELS`, and `skipTenantIsolation()` is not used anywhere in this module.\n- **Does not add retries to irreversible steps.** A failed refund is surfaced, never retried automatically. A gateway timeout that actually succeeded would double‑refund, and the idempotency key protects the *step record*, not the gateway.\n- **Does not fix the 68 `forwardRef`s or the four cycles.** It adds none, and spec 9.7 keeps it that way.\n- **Does not touch payroll, e‑sign, search, BI, or connectors.** Those are §3.4 refusals in the capability map and they still hold.\n\n---\n\n## 12. The one‑line acceptance test\n\n> Anisa Mohammed cancels her Saturday appointment. The owner types *\"the client cancelled — refund them and release their booking\"* into KEY. She sees three steps in three departments, in an order chosen so the money moves last, with the refund marked as the one thing that cannot be taken back and needing her explicit yes. She presses **Run all**. The booking is released, WhatsApp goes out, TT$450.00 goes back. If the refund fails, the booking comes back and she is told so in the same card.\n\nFresha cannot do this because it has no ledger. Xero cannot do this because it has no calendar. Neither can be made to, because they do not share a database. This one does."
      },
