        "key": "09b-cross-department-mechanics",
        "title": "THE THESIS: the exact code path",
        "spec": "# THE THESIS: the exact code path\n\n**Companion to 09a.** 09a argues *why* one hat per turn is the wrong shape. This is the diff.\n\n**Basis:** `C:/kfm` @ `main` (`b5292fb4`), read 2026-08-09. Every line number below was opened, not inferred. Builds on `docs/CAPABILITY_MAP_2026-08-09.md` §3.5(2) and `docs/OPEN_GAPS_2026-08-09.md`.\n\n**The sentence this spec makes executable:**\n\n> *\"The client cancelled — refund them and release their booking slot.\"*\n\n---\n\n## 1. What exists today\n\n### 1.1 The single-role filter, in four places\n\n`apps/server/src/modules/ai/flow-orchestrator.service.ts`\n\n| Line | Code | Effect |\n|---|---|---|\n| **1283** | `const detectedRole = role ?? await this.inferRole(...)` | one `BusinessRole` for the whole turn |\n| **1465–1469** | `detectedRole && detectedRole !== 'general' ? getOpenAiToolDefinitions().filter((t) => this.roleEngine.isToolAllowed(detectedRole, t.function.name)) : getOpenAiToolDefinitions()` | **chat** advertisement |\n| **1869–1873** | *identical expression* | **stream** advertisement |\n| **1506 / 1964 / 1393** | `this.governance.evaluate(businessId, tc.name, undefined, detectedRole, pageContext?.jobRoleEnvelope)` | the gate |\n\n`inferRole` (**:1097–1142**) ends at **:1141** with `return this.roleEngine.detectRoleFromContext(detectionCtx)` — one value.\n\n### 1.2 The scores already exist and are thrown away\n\n`role-engine.service.ts:438–514`. `detectRoleFromContext` builds `scores: Record<BusinessRole, number>` (**:439–442**), then adds:\n\n- route, exact +3 / prefix +2 (**:445–459**)\n- focused item type +2 (**:462–465**)\n- message intent +4, switch keywords +2 (**:468–480**)\n- channel +1 / +0.5 (**:483–486**)\n- previous-role stickiness +3 (**:489–501**)\n\n…and at **:504–513** takes `argmax` and **discards the other seven numbers**. The vector needed to staff a turn is computed on every request and dropped on the floor. That is the whole finding: this is not a missing feature, it is a `return` statement narrower than the function.\n\n### 1.3 `isToolAllowed` never reads `riskTier` — confirmed\n\n`role-engine.service.ts:567–608`: `blockedTools` (exact or trailing-`*` prefix) → `approvedTools` → a `general`-only read escape (**:591–593**) → `ROLE_BASELINE_TOOLS` (**:607**). No tier anywhere.\n\nTier discipline lives *only* in `ai-oversight.service.ts`:\n- `applyRoleCeiling` (**:69–93**) — `Math.min(settings.maxAutoTier, def.maxRiskTier)`, plus the `approvedTools` filter at **:89–91**\n- called once, at **:132**\n- the scope refusal is a separate branch at **:144**\n- the tier ladder at **:167–201**\n\nSo today the two gates are already cleanly separated. **This spec does not merge them.** It changes what each is given.\n\n### 1.4 Neither role can complete the sentence — re-derived, not quoted\n\nRunning `role-engine.service.ts:405–431` and `:468–480` against *\"The client cancelled — refund them and release their booking slot\"*:\n\n| Signal | Match | Score |\n|---|---|---|\n| `detectRoleFromIntent` broad operations pattern (`:413`) | **`slot`** | `operations +4` |\n| `ROLE_SWITCH_KEYWORDS.finance` (`:126`) | **`refund`** | `finance +2` |\n| `ROLE_SWITCH_KEYWORDS.support` (`:127`) | **`refund`** | `support +2` |\n| `ROLE_SWITCH_KEYWORDS.operations` (`:128`) | **`slot`** | `operations +2` |\n\n`operations 6, support 2, finance 2`. `argmax → operations`.\n\nNow the tools:\n\n| Tool | Registry | `operations` | `finance` | `support` |\n|---|---|---|---|---|\n| `payments_refund_charge` | `flow-tool-registry.ts:1292`, **T3**, `/app/payments` | ✗ (no `payments_*`) | ✓ `payments_*` (`:214`) | ✗ (only two exact `payments_list/search` names, `:235`) |\n| `bookings_cancel_booking` | `flow-tool-registry.ts:1860`, **T3**, `/app/bookings` | ✓ `bookings_*` (`:258`), not blocked (`:259`) | ✗ | ✗ **blocked** (`:236`) |\n\n`operations` wins the race and cannot refund. Route the user to `/app/inbox` and `support` wins and can do neither. **No single hat in the eight can execute this sentence.** That is the audit's \"structurally impossible,\" derived from the file.\n\n### 1.5 Five more facts this change turns on\n\n1. **`selectToolsForRequest` (`:1033–1089`) has zero callers.** `grep -rn \"selectToolsForRequest\" apps/server/src apps/web/src` returns exactly one hit — its own definition at `:1033`. It is the *only* code that handles the `jobRoleEnvelope` in advertisement (**:1041–1044**), bridges MCP tools (**:1052–1069**), and clamps to `MAX_TOOLS = 128` (**:1071–1072**). All three are dead. Consequence: a default (`general`) turn ships **all 245** tool schemas, unclamped, and the envelope only ever binds at the *gate*, never at the *menu*.\n2. **`FlowSession` has no role column** (`schema.prisma:7134–7150`). Stickiness is a substring scan for a sign-off string in prior assistant text (`:1105–1127`); the code comment at **:1116–1118** says so and calls the fix \"a migration, not a patch.\"\n3. **The saga machinery is real and unreached.** `KeyCortexSagaService.addStep` takes a `compensation` argument (`key-cortex-saga.service.ts:53–67`) and `compensate()` (**:104–197**) runs it in reverse. Its own comment at **:165–176** records that nothing in production writes `compensationAction`. `KeyCortexSagaExecutorService` has no non-spec caller (grep confirms only `key-cortex.module.ts` registration).\n4. **`getCompensatingAction` has no entry for `bookings_cancel_booking`** (`key-cortex-compensation.service.ts:79–131`) — deliberate, per the file's own rule at **:76–79** (\"Absence is meaningful\"). And `payments_refund_charge` has none either, correctly: `flow-tool-registry.ts:1296–1299` states a refund cannot be un-refunded.\n5. **`escalateStaleApprovals` (`ai-oversight.service.ts:652–697`) has zero callers.** Nothing times out an approval today.\n\n---\n\n## 2. The change, file by file\n\n### 2.1 `role-engine.service.ts` — return the vector\n\n**Extract, do not rewrite, the scorer.** Lift `:439–501` verbatim into a private `scoreRoles(ctx): Record<BusinessRole, number>`. `detectRoleFromContext` becomes `argmax(scoreRoles(ctx))` using the identical loop at `:504–513` — same seed (`scores.general`), same strict `>`, same `Object.entries` order. Its return value is **bit-for-bit what it is today**; that is the point, and it is a test.\n\n**New public surface:**\n\n```ts\nexport interface CrewResolution {\n  primary: BusinessRole;\n  crew: readonly BusinessRole[];          // primary first, then descending score\n  scores: Readonly<Record<BusinessRole, number>>;\n}\n\n/** Deterministic. No I/O. Same inputs on two replicas → same crew. */\nresolveCrew(ctx: RoleDetectionContext): CrewResolution\n\n/** The crew allows a tool iff SOME member allows it on its own terms. */\ncrewAllows(crew: readonly BusinessRole[], toolName: string): boolean\n\n/** Which members. Ordered as `crew`. Empty ⇒ out of scope. */\ngrantorsOf(crew: readonly BusinessRole[], toolName: string): BusinessRole[]\n```\n\n**`isToolAllowed(role, toolName)` keeps its exact signature.** `crewAllows` is defined *on top of it*:\n\n```ts\ngrantorsOf(crew, tool) { return crew.filter((r) => this.isToolAllowed(r, tool)); }\ncrewAllows(crew, tool) { return this.grantorsOf(crew, tool).length > 0; }\n```\n\nWhy that predicate and not \"union grants minus union blocks\": `general.blockedTools` (**:314**) contains `bookings_cancel_booking`, `commerce_send_invoice`, `marketing_send_campaign`. Those are statements about *general's own* authority, not business-wide prohibitions — every one of them is granted by some specialist. A union-veto would make the crew strictly weaker than its weakest member and kill the worked example. Per-member `grants ∧ ¬blocks` is the honest reading, and it is **already the predicate `role-tool-reachability.spec.ts:59` uses** to define an orphan. The two `INTENTIONALLY_UNREACHABLE` tools (`:48`) are blocked by all eight roles, so no crew can ever reach them — that is a proof, and `:64–71` already pins it.\n\n**Crew construction — three clauses, each one an anti-widening clause:**\n\n```ts\nconst CREW_FLOOR = 2;   // one switch-keyword hit; below this is noise\nconst MAX_CREW   = 3;\n\nresolveCrew(ctx) {\n  const scores  = this.scoreRoles(ctx);\n  const primary = this.argmax(scores);                       // unchanged\n  if (primary === 'general') return { primary, crew: ['general'], scores };\n\n  const ceiling = ROLE_DEFINITIONS[primary].maxRiskTier;\n  const crew = [primary, ...ROLE_ORDER\n    .filter((r) => r !== primary && r !== 'general'\n                && scores[r] >= CREW_FLOOR\n                && ROLE_DEFINITIONS[r].maxRiskTier <= ceiling)   // (C)\n    .sort((a, b) => scores[b] - scores[a] || ROLE_ORDER.indexOf(a) - ROLE_ORDER.indexOf(b))\n    .slice(0, MAX_CREW - 1)];\n  return { primary, crew, scores };\n}\n```\n\n- **(A) `general` is a fallback, never a colleague.** If it were a member, its `maxRiskTier: 1` would clamp every multi-role turn to tier 1 (see §2.2) and its enormous allowlist (**:313**) would make the crew meaningless. When `general` is primary, `crew = ['general']` — behaviour identical to today, except the menu narrows (§2.3).\n- **(B) `CREW_FLOOR = 2`** is one switch-keyword hit. A role that scored 0 never joins.\n- **(C) No member may out-rank the primary.** `ROLE_SWITCH_KEYWORDS.operator` (**:131**) matches `do it` — a phrase in half of all real messages. Without (C), \"refund them, do it\" recruits `operator`, whose allowlist (**:345**) includes `execute_custom_logic`. Clause (C) makes `operator` (tier 3) joinable *only* by an `operator`-primary crew. `ROLE_ORDER` is a frozen literal so two replicas resolve identically — no `Object.keys` ordering dependence.\n\n**Prompt.** Add `getSystemPromptForCrew(crew, ctx, onboarding)`: the primary's existing `getSystemPromptForRole` output (**:610–634**, unchanged, still carrying `{{CURRENT_DATE}}`) plus one appended block:\n\n```\nALSO WEARING: Finance Manager, Customer Support.\nYou may use their tools in this turn. Each action is still judged against the\nhat that grants it — the widest hat does not raise the others.\n```\n\n`getSystemPromptForRole` stays, byte-for-byte: `key-identity-and-authority.spec.ts:66` and `:84` call it directly.\n\n### 2.2 `ai-oversight.service.ts` — attribute the ceiling to the grantor\n\n```ts\nprivate applyCrewCeiling(\n  settings: AutonomySettings,\n  crew: readonly BusinessRole[],\n  toolName: string,\n): { settings: AutonomySettings; grantedBy: BusinessRole[]; ceiling: RiskTier } | null {\n  const grantedBy = this.roleEngine.grantorsOf(crew, toolName);\n  if (grantedBy.length === 0) return null;                 // scope refusal\n\n  const defs    = grantedBy.map((r) => this.roleEngine.getRoleDefinition(r));\n  const ceiling = Math.min(...defs.map((d) => d.maxRiskTier)) as RiskTier;   // MIN\n  const auto    = Math.min(...defs.map((d) => d.autonomyLevel));\n\n  return {\n    grantedBy, ceiling,\n    settings: {\n      ...settings,\n      maxAutoTier:   Math.min(settings.maxAutoTier, ceiling) as RiskTier,\n      autonomyLevel: Math.min(settings.autonomyLevel, auto),\n      approvedTools: (settings.approvedTools ?? []).filter((t) => this.getToolTier(t) <= ceiling),\n    },\n  };\n}\n```\n\n**`Math.min` over grantors, never `Math.max`.** This is the load-bearing line. Max would mean \"adding a permissive role to the crew raises the auto-execute band for a tool a strict role also grants\" — escalation by crew composition. Min means **crew membership adds scope and can only ever lower the band.**\n\n**For a one-element crew this is `applyRoleCeiling` exactly** — `min` over a singleton is the element, and the `approvedTools` filter is character-identical. That is the non-regression proof for every path that keeps passing one role.\n\n**`evaluate` signature — widened, not changed:**\n\n```ts\nasync evaluate(\n  businessId: string,\n  toolName: string,\n  mode?: string,\n  role?: BusinessRole | readonly BusinessRole[],   // ← only change\n  envelope?: JobRoleEnvelope,\n): Promise<GovernanceDecision>\n```\n\nNormalised on entry: `const crew = Array.isArray(role) ? role : role ? [role] : []`. All five existing call sites compile and behave identically. `evaluateAutoApproval` (**:204–237**) passes `opts?.role` through unchanged.\n\nBody edits:\n- **:132** → `const applied = crew.length ? this.applyCrewCeiling(businessSettings, crew, toolName) : null;` `const settings = applied?.settings ?? businessSettings;`\n- **:144** → `else if (crew.length && !applied) return { ...blocked, reason: \\`No active role covers \"${toolName}\" — KEY is wearing ${crew.join(', ')}\\` };`\n- Envelope branch (**:136–143**) **untouched.** Position-scoped governance still wins outright. Unifying the three role systems is capability-map item #21 and is explicitly out of scope (§10).\n\n`GovernanceDecision` gains two fields — `grantedBy: BusinessRole[]` and `ceiling: RiskTier` — so the UI, the approval card and `FlowTurn.plan` can name *which hat authorised this step*. Both optional-with-defaults so no consumer breaks.\n\n### 2.3 `flow-orchestrator.service.ts` — one selector, three gates\n\n**Revive the dead selector.** Change `selectToolsForRequest` (**:1033**) param 2 from `detectedRole: BusinessRole | undefined` to `crew: readonly BusinessRole[]`, and **:1046–1049** to:\n\n```ts\ncandidates = all.filter((t) => this.roleEngine.crewAllows(crew, t.function.name));\n```\n\nDelete the `detectedRole !== 'general'` special case. Change the MCP gate at **:1055** to `this.roleEngine.crewAllows(crew, 'mcp__x')`.\n\nThen **replace both live inline filters** (`:1465–1469`, `:1869–1873`) with `await this.selectToolsForRequest(businessId, crew, message + pageContextText, pageContext?.jobRoleEnvelope)`, preserving the `triage?.tier === 'reflex' ? undefined :` guard exactly.\n\nThis is the single most important non-widening statement in the spec, and it runs the other way:\n\n> **Today a `general` turn advertises all 245 tools with no filter and is then refused at the gate** — literally the defect `role-tool-reachability.spec.ts:1–23` was written about. After this change a `general` turn advertises exactly `general`'s allowlist. **Strictly fewer tools, zero change at the gate.** It also restores the 128-cap and the envelope-aware menu, both of which have been unreachable code.\n\n**Authority resolution.** `inferRole` (**:1097–1142**) → `resolveTurnAuthority(...): Promise<CrewResolution>`, returning `this.roleEngine.resolveCrew(detectionCtx)`. `previousRole` now reads `FlowTurn.primaryRole` for the session's most recent turn (§4), falling back to the sign-off scan at **:1105–1127** for sessions with no `FlowTurn` row. `key-identity-and-authority.spec.ts:201–211` asserts `getAllRoles()` appears in that function body — the fallback keeps that true.\n\n**Gates.** `:1393`, `:1506`, `:1964` — pass `crew` where `detectedRole` was.\n\n**Prompt.** `:1327–1336` → `crew.length === 1 && crew[0] === 'general'` keeps `FLOW_SYSTEM_PROMPT`; otherwise `getSystemPromptForCrew`.\n\n**Turn plan.** `:1563–1565` and `:2060–2062` currently fire every approved call in a bare `Promise.all` — no order, no saga, no rollback. Replaced by `runTurnPlan(...)` (§5).\n\n### 2.4 `key-cortex-compensation.service.ts` — one honest addition\n\n`bookings_cancel_booking` is the reversible half of the worked example, and nothing can undo it today. Add, obeying the file's own rule at **:76–79** (\"adding a key here is a promise the handler genuinely reverses the effect\"):\n\n```ts\nbookings_cancel_booking:  'bookings.reinstate_booking',\n'bookings.cancel_booking':'bookings.reinstate_booking',   // planner vocabulary, per :103-118\n```\n\n```ts\nthis.register('bookings.reinstate_booking', async (input) => {\n  const bookingId = (input.output?.id ?? input.parameters?.bookingId) as string | undefined;\n  const prior     = input.output?.previousStatus as BookingStatus | undefined;\n  // No prior status recorded ⇒ nothing to restore. Throw, so compensate()\n  // records 'compensation_failed' rather than a silent 'compensated'.\n  if (!bookingId || !prior || !input.businessId) throw new Error('reinstate: no previousStatus captured');\n  await this.bookings.updateBookingStatus(input.businessId, bookingId, prior);\n});\n```\n\nTwo supporting edits:\n- `bookings.service.ts:171–194` — `updateBookingStatus` already reads the row at **:172–174**; return `previousStatus: booking.status` alongside the update.\n- `flow-orchestrator.service.ts:2973–2976` — return `{ booking: updated, id: updated.id, previousStatus: updated.previousStatus }`.\n\n**Named trap:** `register()` (**:46–48**) is `Map.set` with no collision check, and `'bookings.cancel_booking'` is *already registered* at **:212–217** as the undo-a-*create* compensator. Naming the new handler `'bookings.cancel_booking'` would silently overwrite it and turn \"undo the booking I just made\" into \"reinstate it.\" Add a one-line guard to `register()` that throws on duplicate keys, plus a spec (§7.4).\n\n`payments_refund_charge` gets **no** entry. `flow-tool-registry.ts:1296–1299` states the reason. The saga records `compensation_unavailable` for it, which is the truth.\n\n---\n\n## 3. Module boundary — and why the cycle count does not move\n\n**The change adds zero new module edges.** Named plainly, because the repo has paid 68 `forwardRef`s for the opposite:\n\n`role-engine.service.ts`, `ai-oversight.service.ts`, `flow-orchestrator.service.ts`, `flow-tool-registry.ts` are **all four inside `apps/server/src/modules/ai`.** §2.1–§2.3 is entirely intra-module. There is no new import, no new provider, no new `forwardRef`.\n\n**The saga is reached through a declared contract over an edge that already exists.** `ai.module.ts:97` already lists `forwardRef(() => KeyCortexModule)`. Rather than a ninth `moduleRef.get(..., { strict: false })` reach-in (the orchestrator has eight, e.g. **:436**, **:608**, **:5316**, **:5591**), declare:\n\n```ts\n// apps/server/src/modules/ai/ports/turn-saga.port.ts\nexport const TURN_SAGA = Symbol('TURN_SAGA');\nexport interface TurnSagaPort {\n  start(ctx: { businessId: string; sagaType: string; sessionId?: string; correlationId?: string }): Promise<{ id: string }>;\n  addStep(sagaId: string, i: number, name: string, input: Record<string, unknown>, comp?: SagaCompensation): Promise<unknown>;\n  completeStep(sagaId: string, i: number, out: Record<string, unknown>): Promise<void>;\n  failStep(sagaId: string, i: number, err: string): Promise<void>;\n  completeSaga(sagaId: string): Promise<void>;\n  compensate(sagaId: string): Promise<SagaStepResult[]>;\n}\n```\n\nBound in `AiModule` as `{ provide: TURN_SAGA, useExisting: KeyCortexSagaService }`. `KeyCortexSagaService` already satisfies it (`key-cortex-saga.service.ts:39–197`) — **no change to that file.** Net effect on the graph: **−1 reach-in, +0 edges, +0 forwardRefs.** Cycle count stays at 4.\n\n**Listens to / emits.** The AI module already injects `EventEmitter2`. New emissions, all fire-and-forget and consumed by nobody in this spec (no new listener edges):\n\n| Event | Payload |\n|---|---|\n| `key.turn.planned` | `{ businessId, turnId, crew, steps: [{ toolName, grantedBy, tier, ceiling, compensable }] }` |\n| `key.turn.completed` / `key.turn.failed` | `{ businessId, turnId, sagaId, failedAtIndex? }` |\n| `key.turn.compensated` | `{ businessId, turnId, sagaId, status, undone, unavailable }` |\n\nConsumed: `ai.approval.resolved` (already emitted from `finalizeResolution`, `ai-oversight.service.ts:552–620` — add the emit if absent) → enqueues the resume job.\n\n**Negative control on the boundary:** `apps/server/test/app-module-boots.integration.test.ts` must stay green, and a `madge --circular apps/server/src` run must report **the same 4 cycles** before and after. If it reports 5, the port was wired wrong.\n\n---\n\n## 4. Data\n\n### 4.1 New model\n\n```prisma\nmodel FlowTurn {\n  id         String   @id @default(cuid())\n  businessId String   @map(\"business_id\")\n  business   Business @relation(fields: [businessId], references: [id], onDelete: Cascade)\n\n  sessionId  String   @map(\"session_id\")\n  userId     String?  @map(\"user_id\")\n\n  /// Roles staffed for this turn, primary first. Replaces the sign-off\n  /// substring scan at flow-orchestrator.service.ts:1105-1127, which cannot\n  /// survive a turn that ends in an approval — the assistant text the scan\n  /// reads is the approval notice, not a role sign-off.\n  crew        String[] @map(\"crew\")\n  primaryRole String   @map(\"primary_role\")\n  /// The full score vector. \"Why did KEY wear that hat\" becomes a query,\n  /// not a re-run of eight regexes against a message nobody kept.\n  crewScores  Json     @map(\"crew_scores\")\n\n  /// Ordered steps: [{ index, toolName, args, grantedBy[], tier, ceiling,\n  ///                   compensationAction|null, decision, status, error }]\n  plan        Json     @map(\"plan\")\n  status      FlowTurnStatus @default(PLANNED)\n\n  sagaId          String? @map(\"saga_id\")\n  approvalItemId  String? @map(\"approval_item_id\")\n  failedAtIndex   Int?    @map(\"failed_at_index\")\n\n  /// Idempotency for the BullMQ resume job. jobId = \"turn:{id}\".\n  idempotencyKey String  @map(\"idempotency_key\")\n\n  createdAt DateTime @default(now()) @map(\"created_at\")\n  updatedAt DateTime @updatedAt      @map(\"updated_at\")\n\n  @@unique([businessId, idempotencyKey])\n  @@index([businessId, createdAt])\n  @@index([businessId, sessionId, createdAt])\n  @@index([businessId, status])\n  @@index([businessId, approvalItemId])\n  @@map(\"flow_turns\")\n}\n\nenum FlowTurnStatus {\n  PLANNED\n  AWAITING_APPROVAL\n  RUNNING\n  COMPLETED\n  FAILED\n  COMPENSATED\n  COMPENSATION_UNAVAILABLE\n  EXPIRED\n}\n```\n\nHouse style honoured: `snake_case @map`, `businessId` with a cascading `Business` relation, compound `@@index([businessId, ...])`.\n\n`Business` gains `flowTurns FlowTurn[]`.\n\n### 4.2 Tenant isolation — the explicit statement\n\n**A new model is unscoped by default.** The isolation extension (`packages/db/src/client.ts:81–148`) is a hand-maintained `Set` of **77** names consulted by string at **:198**. `tenant-model-list.spec.ts` checks only that listed names *exist* and *have* a `businessId` — it does **not** flag a model that is absent. Verified 2026-08-09.\n\n**Therefore: add `'FlowTurn'` to `BUSINESS_ID_MODELS` in `packages/db/src/client.ts`, in the same commit as the migration.**\n\nAnd note what that does *not* buy, per the file's own caveat at **:120–137**: the interceptor is HTTP-only, so the BullMQ resume worker has no tenant context and the extension is inert there. **Every query in the worker carries an explicit `where: { businessId }` bound from `FlowTurn.businessId`, never from a job payload field a client could influence.**\n\n`SagaExecution` is likewise absent from the set (it has `businessId`, `schema.prisma:7545`). Out of scope here — but stated, so the next reader does not assume it is covered.\n\n### 4.3 Migration\n\n`packages/db/prisma/migrations/20260810120000_add_flow_turn/migration.sql`\n\n```sql\nCREATE TYPE \"FlowTurnStatus\" AS ENUM (\n  'PLANNED','AWAITING_APPROVAL','RUNNING','COMPLETED',\n  'FAILED','COMPENSATED','COMPENSATION_UNAVAILABLE','EXPIRED');\n\nCREATE TABLE \"flow_turns\" (\n  \"id\" TEXT NOT NULL,\n  \"business_id\" TEXT NOT NULL,\n  \"session_id\" TEXT NOT NULL,\n  \"user_id\" TEXT,\n  \"crew\" TEXT[] NOT NULL,\n  \"primary_role\" TEXT NOT NULL,\n  \"crew_scores\" JSONB NOT NULL,\n  \"plan\" JSONB NOT NULL,\n  \"status\" \"FlowTurnStatus\" NOT NULL DEFAULT 'PLANNED',\n  \"saga_id\" TEXT,\n  \"approval_item_id\" TEXT,\n  \"failed_at_index\" INTEGER,\n  \"idempotency_key\" TEXT NOT NULL,\n  \"created_at\" TIMESTAMP(3) NOT NULL DEFAULT CURRENT_TIMESTAMP,\n  \"updated_at\" TIMESTAMP(3) NOT NULL,\n  CONSTRAINT \"flow_turns_pkey\" PRIMARY KEY (\"id\")\n);\n\nCREATE UNIQUE INDEX \"flow_turns_business_id_idempotency_key_key\"\n  ON \"flow_turns\"(\"business_id\",\"idempotency_key\");\nCREATE INDEX \"flow_turns_business_id_created_at_idx\"  ON \"flow_turns\"(\"business_id\",\"created_at\");\nCREATE INDEX \"flow_turns_business_id_session_id_created_at_idx\"\n  ON \"flow_turns\"(\"business_id\",\"session_id\",\"created_at\");\nCREATE INDEX \"flow_turns_business_id_status_idx\"      ON \"flow_turns\"(\"business_id\",\"status\");\nCREATE INDEX \"flow_turns_business_id_approval_item_id_idx\"\n  ON \"flow_turns\"(\"business_id\",\"approval_item_id\");\n\nALTER TABLE \"flow_turns\" ADD CONSTRAINT \"flow_turns_business_id_fkey\"\n  FOREIGN KEY (\"business_id\") REFERENCES \"businesses\"(\"id\") ON DELETE CASCADE ON UPDATE CASCADE;\n```\n\nAdditive only. No backfill. `OPEN_GAPS` records that `_prisma_migrations` was reconciled by hand on 2026-08-09 — verify `prisma migrate status` is clean *before* generating, not after.\n\n---\n\n## 5. Execution: the turn plan, and where BullMQ goes\n\n### 5.1 Ordering — reversible first, irreversible last\n\n```ts\nprivate orderTurnPlan(calls: FlowToolCall[]): FlowToolCall[] {\n  const compensable = (c) => {\n    const a = this.compensationMap.getCompensatingAction(c.name);\n    return !!a && this.compensationMap.hasHandler(a);   // both, or it is not compensable\n  };\n  return [...calls.filter(compensable), ...calls.filter((c) => !compensable(c))];  // stable\n}\n```\n\nThe rule, and the reason it is not cosmetic:\n\n> If a **terminal** step succeeds and a later step fails, nothing can be undone. If a **compensable** step succeeds and a later step fails, everything before the failure can be undone. Terminal steps therefore go **last**.\n\nIn the worked example: refund first, cancel fails ⇒ money gone, slot still held, no way back. Cancel first, refund fails ⇒ reinstate the booking, business is exactly where it started. Same two tools, same turn; the order is the difference between a recoverable failure and an unrecoverable one.\n\n`hasHandler(a)` is checked, not assumed — `compensate()` at `key-cortex-compensation.service.ts:50–54` returns `{ compensated: false }` for an unregistered ref, and a plan that treats that as compensable would order on a promise nothing can keep.\n\n### 5.2 `runTurnPlan`\n\n```\nwrite FlowTurn (PLANNED) with the ordered plan + per-step grantedBy/tier/ceiling\nsaga = TURN_SAGA.start({ businessId, sagaType: 'flow_turn', sessionId, correlationId: turnId })\nfor each step i:\n  TURN_SAGA.addStep(saga.id, i, step.toolName, step.args, compensationFor(step))  // BEFORE it runs\nif any step requires approval:\n    ONE AiApprovalItem, inputPayload = the whole plan; FlowTurn → AWAITING_APPROVAL\n    enqueue expiry job; return the plan to the user; STOP\nelse:\n    enqueue the run job\n```\n\nCompensation is attached **before** the step runs — the property `CAPABILITY_MAP §1.4` credits the tool layer with, finally exercised on the chat path.\n\n### 5.3 BullMQ, not `setInterval` — three jobs, three keys\n\nQueue `key-turn` on the existing Redis (`queue.service.ts:56–57` already builds two queues on the injected `REDIS_CLIENT`; this is a third, same pattern, `jobId` idempotency copied from **:97–112**).\n\n| Job | `jobId` | Trigger | Notes |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| `turn:run` | `turn:{turnId}` | plan needs no approval, **or** `ai.approval.resolved` = approved | `attempts: 1`. A turn is a sequence of real-world side effects; BullMQ retry would re-refund. Retry is a *human* decision via `turn_rollback` + a fresh turn. |\n| `turn:expire` | `turn-expire:{turnId}` | delayed by `settings.approvalTimeoutHours` | Marks `EXPIRED`, compensates nothing (nothing ran), notifies. **Never auto-approves.** |\n| `turn:compensate` | `turn-comp:{turnId}` | a step failed, or `turn_rollback` | `TURN_SAGA.compensate(sagaId)`; writes the real status back (`COMPENSATED` / `COMPENSATION_UNAVAILABLE`), never an optimistic one — `key-cortex-saga.service.ts:165–186` already distinguishes these and this spec preserves that distinction end to end. |\n\n`jobId` gives dedup by construction: two replicas resolving the same approval enqueue the same id, one job runs. **No `setInterval`, no in-process timer, no module field.** All state is `FlowTurn` + `SagaExecution` + `SagaStep` in Postgres.\n\nThis also gives `escalateStaleApprovals` (**:652–697**, currently zero callers) a real driver for the first time — replaced by `turn:expire`, which is per-item and does not sweep every tenant.\n\n---\n\n## 6. The three layers\n\n### 6.1 MANUAL\n\n**New page: `apps/web/src/app/app/approvals/turns/page.tsx`** (+ `[turnId]/page.tsx`, `error.tsx`, `loading.tsx`, and a nav entry under Approvals in `lib/nav-config.ts` — `OPEN_GAPS` counts 8 doorless screens and this must not become the ninth).\n\nShows, per turn: the crew and why (the score vector from `crewScores`, rendered as \"operations 6 · support 2 · finance 2\"), the ordered steps with **which hat granted each**, each step's tier vs. its ceiling, whether it is reversible, and — per step — the \"do it yourself\" link from `manualEquivalentRoute` (`flow-tool-registry.ts:30–40`; `CAPABILITY_MAP` Wave-0 #9 notes this field appears in **0** files under `apps/web/src` today).\n\nActions: **Approve plan**, **Reject**, and for a completed/partial turn **Roll back**. Each is a real `apiPost` — required, or `tool-route-audit.ts:118–123` will not accept the route as a manual equivalent for a write tool.\n\n### 6.2 SMART\n\nDeterministic, zero model calls:\n\n- `resolveCrew` itself — regexes and integers, `role-engine.service.ts` only.\n- `orderTurnPlan` — a partition on a lookup table.\n- `applyCrewCeiling` — a `Math.min`.\n- the three BullMQ jobs — rule-driven, no LLM.\n\nA rule engine can build and run a turn plan with no AI in the path, which is what makes the trichotomy real for this capability rather than asserted.\n\n### 6.3 AI — two tools\n\n```ts\n{\n  name: 'turn_explain_authority',\n  description: 'Explain which business roles KEY is wearing for the current conversation, why each was chosen, and which tools each one can and cannot authorise. Use this to answer \"why can you not do that\".',\n  family: 'read',\n  riskLevel: 'low',\n  riskTier: 1 as RiskTier,\n  manualEquivalentRoute: '/app/approvals/turns',\n  ...\n}\n{\n  name: 'turn_rollback',\n  description: 'Undo the completed steps of a turn, in reverse order, by running each step\\'s declared compensating action. Steps with no registered compensation are reported as not undone rather than skipped silently.',\n  family: 'execute',\n  riskLevel: 'high',\n  riskTier: 3 as RiskTier,\n  manualEquivalentRoute: '/app/approvals/turns',\n  changedEntities: ['flowTurn', 'sagaExecution'],\n  ...\n}\n```\n\nRoute checks, verified against `apps/web/src/lib/tool-route-audit.ts:118–123`: `crossDomain` requires the tool's domain prefix to appear in the route with `/` and `-` stripped. `turn_rollback` → domain `turn`; `/app/approvals/turns` → `appapprovalsturns` contains `turn`. ✓ (`turn_explain_authority` is `family: 'read'`, so `crossDomain` does not apply, but the route must still resolve.)\n\nDescription checks, verified against `tool-honesty-sweep.spec.ts:181–222`: neither description contains a `send`/`notify` verb landing on an outbound medium, so neither is pulled into the mail-path assertion. Deliberate — a rollback is not a message.\n\nReachability, so `role-tool-reachability.spec.ts:55–62` stays green with **no wildcard added anywhere**:\n\n- `turn_explain_authority` → `ROLE_BASELINE_TOOLS` (`role-engine.service.ts:151–178`). Every hat must be able to say why it is refusing.\n- `turn_rollback` → exact name added to `operations.approvedTools` (**:258**) and `operator.approvedTools` (**:345**). Nothing else. At T3 with `operations.maxRiskTier: 2` it lands in formal approval, which is correct for undoing money-adjacent work.\n\n**`turn_rollback`'s handler must return the saga's real per-step results**, not `{ turnId: args.turnId, rolledBack: true }`. `tool-honesty-sweep.spec.ts:224–302` brace-matches each `case` body and fails any handler whose final `return {` is built only from `args.*` and literals. This handler is exactly the shape that test hunts.\n\n---\n\n## 7. Tests\n\n### 7.1 New — `apps/server/src/modules/ai/crew-authority.spec.ts`\n\n```ts\nit('the sentence that motivated this completes', () => {\n  const { primary, crew } = engine.resolveCrew({\n    route: '/app/inbox',\n    message: 'The client cancelled — refund them and release their booking slot',\n  });\n  expect(primary).toBe('operations');\n  expect(engine.crewAllows(crew, 'payments_refund_charge')).toBe(true);\n  expect(engine.crewAllows(crew, 'bookings_cancel_booking')).toBe(true);\n  // and no single member could\n  for (const r of crew) {\n    expect(engine.isToolAllowed(r, 'payments_refund_charge')\n        && engine.isToolAllowed(r, 'bookings_cancel_booking')).toBe(false);\n  }\n});\n\nit('grantors are named, and they are the right ones', () => {\n  const { crew } = engine.resolveCrew({ route: '/app/inbox', message: '...refund...booking slot' });\n  expect(engine.grantorsOf(crew, 'payments_refund_charge')).toEqual(['finance']);\n  expect(engine.grantorsOf(crew, 'bookings_cancel_booking')).toEqual(['operations']);\n});\n\nit('primary is byte-identical to today\\'s detectRoleFromContext', async () => {\n  for (const ctx of FIXTURES /* 40 real messages × routes */) {\n    expect((await engine.resolveCrew(ctx)).primary).toBe(await engine.detectRoleFromContext(ctx));\n  }\n});\n```\n\n### 7.2 New — `apps/server/src/modules/ai/crew-non-widening.spec.ts`\n\nThe four invariants, each one a way the change could go wrong:\n\n```ts\nit('a crew never reaches a tool no member reaches', () => {\n  for (const crew of ALL_CREWS)                       // every subset up to size 3\n    for (const t of TOOLS)\n      if (engine.crewAllows(crew, t))\n        expect(crew.some((r) => engine.isToolAllowed(r, t))).toBe(true);\n});\n\nit('the two destructive tools stay unreachable by EVERY crew', () => {\n  for (const crew of ALL_CREWS)\n    for (const t of ['commerce_delete_invoice', 'crm_delete_contact'])\n      expect(engine.crewAllows(crew, t), `${t} reachable by ${crew}`).toBe(false);\n});\n\nit('adding a member never raises the ceiling for a tool', () => {\n  for (const crew of ALL_CREWS)\n    for (const extra of ROLES) {\n      const before = ceilingFor(crew, TOOL), after = ceilingFor([...crew, extra], TOOL);\n      expect(after).toBeLessThanOrEqual(before);\n    }\n});\n\nit('no crew member out-ranks its primary', () => {\n  for (const ctx of FIXTURES) {\n    const { primary, crew } = engine.resolveCrew(ctx);\n    const cap = engine.getRoleDefinition(primary).maxRiskTier;\n    for (const r of crew) expect(engine.getRoleDefinition(r).maxRiskTier).toBeLessThanOrEqual(cap);\n  }\n});\n\nit('the crew is deterministic across 100 resolutions', () => { /* two replicas must agree */ });\n```\n\n### 7.3 Updated — `role-tool-reachability.spec.ts`\n\n- Keep every existing test. `isToolAllowed`'s signature is unchanged, so all 179 lines still compile and still mean what they meant.\n- **Add** `turn_explain_authority` to the `BASELINE` list at **:141–150** and `turn_rollback` to a new \"reachable by exactly these roles\" assertion — `expect(ROLES.filter(r => engine.isToolAllowed(r, 'turn_rollback'))).toEqual(['operations', 'operator'])`. An exact-array assertion, so a later wildcard that sweeps it into another role fails.\n- **Add** the bridge test making the spec's own orphan predicate (**:59**) load-bearing at runtime: `expect(engine.crewAllows(ROLES, t)).toBe(ROLES.some(r => engine.isToolAllowed(r, t)))` for all 245. The reachability guarantee and the runtime predicate become the same function; they cannot drift.\n\n### 7.4 Updated — `tool-honesty-sweep.spec.ts`\n\n- The `args`-echo test at **:224–302** picks up `turn_rollback` automatically once the `case` exists. No edit needed; named because it is the constraint on how the handler is written.\n- **Add** a compensator-integrity block, in the same family as the existing \"descriptions do not promise fields the query never selects\":\n  - every value of `getCompensatingAction`'s map is a key in `this.handlers` (the file's own stated rule at **:70–74**, currently unenforced);\n  - `register()` throws on a duplicate key — with the `'bookings.cancel_booking'` collision as the named fixture;\n  - `bookings.reinstate_booking` throws when `previousStatus` is absent, so `compensate()` records `compensation_failed` and not `compensated`. This is the exact class `key-cortex-saga.service.ts:165–186` was written about.\n\n### 7.5 Updated — `key-identity-and-authority.spec.ts`\n\nThe test at **:135–152** (\"evaluate ACTUALLY USES the clamped settings\") source-scans for `applyRoleCeiling(businessSettings, role)`. After §2.2 that string is gone from `evaluate` and the test fails — **correctly**. Repoint it to `applyCrewCeiling(businessSettings, crew, toolName)`.\n\nThis is the highest-risk edit in the spec. Its own comment at **:137–139** records that the assertion exists *because* removing the call from `evaluate()` failed nothing until someone wrote it. Repointing it carelessly — deleting it, or loosening it to `/applyCrew/` — recreates that hole exactly. **The repointed assertion must be re-verified by negative control (§7.7).**\n\n### 7.6 New integration — `apps/server/test/flow-turn-crew.integration.test.ts`\n\nReal Postgres, stubbed gateway/HTTP, following the house pattern (`cortex-context-v2.integration.test.ts`). Asserts against **rows**, not mocks:\n\n1. Two-step approval-gated turn → **one** `AiApprovalItem`, one `FlowTurn` at `AWAITING_APPROVAL`, one `SagaExecution` with **two** `SagaStep` rows, the first carrying a non-null `compensation_action`.\n2. Approve → cancel runs, refund throws → `FlowTurn.status = COMPENSATED`, `failedAtIndex = 1`, the booking's status equals its pre-turn value, `SagaStep[0].status = 'compensated'`.\n3. Refund-only turn (no compensable step) fails → `COMPENSATION_UNAVAILABLE`, **not** `COMPENSATED`.\n4. Tenant: business B's user cannot read business A's `FlowTurn` — via the HTTP path (extension live) *and* via the worker path (extension inert, explicit `where` only). Both, because they are different guarantees.\n\n### 7.7 Negative controls — every one of these must be run and its failure observed\n\n| # | Break | Must fail |\n|---|---|---|\n| 1 | `Math.min` → `Math.max` in `applyCrewCeiling` | `crew-non-widening` \"never raises the ceiling\" |\n| 2 | Drop clause (C) from `resolveCrew` | `crew-non-widening` \"no member out-ranks its primary\" |\n| 3 | `general.maxRiskTier: 1` → `2` | **both** §8 tests, for different reasons |\n| 4 | `crewAllows` → union-grants-minus-union-blocks | `crew-authority` \"the sentence completes\" |\n| 5 | Delete the `applyCrewCeiling` call from `evaluate`, keep the method | repointed `key-identity-and-authority.spec.ts:135` |\n| 6 | Swap `orderTurnPlan` to model order | integration #2 (booking not restored) |\n| 7 | Register `bookings.reinstate_booking` as `'bookings.cancel_booking'` | the duplicate-key guard in §7.4 |\n| 8 | Add `'turn_*'` to `general.approvedTools` | the exact-array assertion in §7.3 |\n| 9 | Remove `'FlowTurn'` from `BUSINESS_ID_MODELS` | integration #4 — **and if it does not, the isolation test is asserting a shape and not a referent, and must be rewritten before this ships** |\n\nControl 9 is the one to run first. `MEMORY.md` records \"assertions check referents, not shapes\" as the recurring green-but-wrong gate, and `tenant-model-list.spec.ts` is already known not to catch an unlisted model.\n\n---\n\n## 8. How this change cannot raise `general.maxRiskTier`\n\n`role-engine.service.ts:315–323` records the incident: the line sat *outside* the conflict markers, so merge `b4717b3b` silently took integration's `2` over main's `1`, and `commit cdae068c` put it back by hand.\n\n**Root cause, established by reading `key-identity-and-authority.spec.ts:90–199`: every existing ceiling assertion is *relative*.** `:111` asserts `exec.maxAutoTier < 4`. `:197` asserts `operator > exec`. **Not one test names a number.** Raising `general` from 1 to 2 preserves every relative fact in the suite. The suite could not have caught it, and did not.\n\nFive defences, at four layers:\n\n**(1) An exact-object literal.** In `crew-non-widening.spec.ts`:\n\n```ts\nit('every role ceiling is exactly what it is', () => {\n  const CEILINGS = Object.fromEntries(engine.getAllRoles().map((d) => [d.id, d.maxRiskTier]));\n  expect(CEILINGS).toEqual({\n    sales: 2, finance: 2, support: 2, operations: 2,\n    marketing: 2, general: 1, operator: 3, executive: 1,\n  });\n});\n```\n\n`toEqual` on the whole object. Any change to any role's ceiling — by merge, by hand, by rebase, in either direction — fails with a diff naming the role and both numbers. There is no relative fact left to hide behind.\n\n**(2) A behavioural assertion from the other side**, so the literal is not the only witness:\n\n```ts\nit('the default hat still confirms before creating an invoice', async () => {\n  const d = await oversight.evaluate(BIZ, 'commerce_create_invoice', undefined, ['general']);\n  expect(oversight.getToolTier('commerce_create_invoice')).toBe(2);\n  expect(d.requiresQuickConfirm, 'T2 auto-executed for the role every user gets').toBe(true);\n  expect(d.ceiling).toBe(1);\n});\n```\n\nRaise `general` to 2 and the literal fails on the number *and* this fails on the consequence — two independent tests, two mechanisms, one root cause. Negative control #3 asserts both fire.\n\n**(3) Structural: crew composition cannot raise a ceiling, only lower it.** `applyCrewCeiling` takes `Math.min` over grantors (§2.2), and `resolveCrew` clause (C) refuses any member out-ranking the primary. There is no arithmetic path from \"add a role\" to \"higher tier.\" The **only** way to raise a ceiling is to edit the literal in `ROLE_DEFINITIONS`, which is what (1) watches.\n\n**(4) `general` is not a crew member.** §2.1(A) — when `general` is primary the crew is `['general']` and behaviour is today's; when it is not primary it is absent. Its ceiling is never averaged, unioned, or maxed with anything.\n\n**(5) The comment stays and gets a pointer.** `role-engine.service.ts:315–323` is amended with `// Pinned by crew-non-widening.spec.ts — change the test in the same commit or it fails.` A comment alone did not hold the line last time; a comment naming the test that will fail is a different object.\n\n**What is explicitly *not* a defence:** CODEOWNERS. `/apps/server/src/modules/ai/ @SaCH-PRO` already covers the file and did not stop the merge — every path in that file resolves to the same single owner. Adding a line buys nothing. The tests are the guard.\n\n---\n\n## 9. Migration path — four gates, each independently revertible\n\nOrdered so nothing widens before the thing that proves it cannot is already in the tree.\n\n| # | Ship | Widens? | Revert |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| **0** | `crew-non-widening.spec.ts` ceiling literal + the `general` behavioural test, against **today's** code | no — pure observation | delete a file |\n| **1** | `resolveCrew` / `crewAllows` / `grantorsOf`; **nothing calls them.** `applyCrewCeiling` added, `evaluate` still calls `applyRoleCeiling`. | **no** — dead code with live tests | delete a file |\n| **2** | Revive `selectToolsForRequest` at `:1465` and `:1869` with `crew = [detectedRole]` (single-element). | **narrows.** `general` drops 245→its allowlist; the 128-cap and envelope menu come back. Gate unchanged. | one-line revert to the inline filters |\n| **3** | `evaluate` takes the crew; `inferRole` → `resolveTurnAuthority`. | **yes — this is the change.** Scope widens, tier does not. | feature flag |\n| **4** | `FlowTurn` + saga + BullMQ + the screen + the two tools. | no new authority; adds ordering, rollback, one approval instead of N | flag |\n\n**Gate 3 is flagged.** `KEY_CREW_MAX = 1 | 2 | 3` (env, default `1` at merge). At `1`, `resolveCrew` returns `[primary]` and `applyCrewCeiling` reduces to `applyRoleCeiling` exactly (§2.2). **Merging gate 3 changes nothing until an operator raises a number**, and raising it from 1→2 is one role at a time.\n\n**The flag reads at request time, never cached in a module field** — a second replica must not carry a stale value.\n\n**The observability that decides when to raise it:** with `KEY_CREW_MAX = 1`, `resolveTurnAuthority` still computes and persists the *would-be* crew into `FlowTurn.crewScores` and emits `key.turn.planned`. After a week you can answer, from rows: how many turns would have gained a second hat, which pairs, and which tools that unlocked — before granting anything. That is the honest version of \"roll it out and watch.\"\n\n**Prerequisite, not optional:** `flow.controller.ts:22–34` types `pageContext` as an inline `@Body()`, so the global `ValidationPipe` whitelist does not strip it and a client can post its own `jobRoleEnvelope` — which `ai-oversight.service.ts:136–143` prefers over the department role. This spec routes more authority through that same object. **Wave-0 #6 (`pageContext` as a validated DTO) ships before gate 3, or gate 3 does not ship.**\n\n---\n\n## 10. The worked example, end to end\n\n**Setup.** Salon. Booking `bk_991`, Saturday 10:00, TTD 450, paid by Stripe charge `ch_7f2a`. Owner is on `/app/inbox` reading the customer's WhatsApp. Business autonomy: `assisted`, `maxAutoTier: 1`, `autonomyLevel: 1`.\n\n**Owner types:** *\"The client cancelled — refund them and release their booking slot.\"*\n\n### Turn resolution\n\n`resolveTurnAuthority` → `scoreRoles`:\n\n```\noperations 6   (broad \"slot\" → +4 at role-engine:413; switch \"slot\" → +2 at :128)\nsupport    5   (route /app/inbox → +3 at :85; switch \"refund\" → +2 at :127)\nfinance    2   (switch \"refund\" → +2 at :126)\n```\n\n`primary = operations`. Clause (C): `operations.maxRiskTier = 2`; `support` 2 ✓, `finance` 2 ✓. Crew = **`['operations','support','finance']`**.\n\nMenu: `crewAllows` union → 118 of 245 tools advertised, clamped to 128 (no clamp needed). System prompt: Operations Manager, *\"ALSO WEARING: Customer Support, Finance Manager.\"*\n\n### The tool calls, in order\n\n| # | Tool | Tier | Grantor(s) | Ceiling | Decision |\n|---|---|:--:|---|:--:|---|\n| 1 | `bookings_list_bookings` (`registry:1826`) | 1 | `operations` (`bookings_*`) | 2 | **auto** — 1 ≤ 2 |\n| 2 | `payments_search_transactions` (`registry:1226`) | 1 | `support`, `finance` | min(2,2)=2 | **auto** |\n| 3 | `bookings_cancel_booking` (`registry:1860`) | 3 | **`operations` only** — `support` blocks it (`:236`), `finance` never granted it | 2 | 3 > 2 → **formal approval** |\n| 4 | `payments_refund_charge` (`registry:1292`) | 3 | **`finance` only** — `payments_*` (`:214`) | 2 | 3 > 2 → **formal approval** |\n\nSteps 1–2 execute in the turn and return `bk_991` (status `CONFIRMED`) and `ch_7f2a` (TTD 450).\n\nSteps 3–4 go to `orderTurnPlan`:\n\n- `bookings_cancel_booking` → `getCompensatingAction` → `'bookings.reinstate_booking'`, handler registered → **compensable**\n- `payments_refund_charge` → no entry (deliberate, `registry:1296–1299`) → **terminal**\n\nOrdered: **cancel, then refund.** The model asked for refund first; the planner reorders, because a refund with a failed cancel cannot be walked back.\n\nOne `FlowTurn` (`AWAITING_APPROVAL`), one `SagaExecution`, two `SagaStep` rows — step 0 with `compensation_action = { stepName: 'bookings_cancel_booking', action: 'bookings.reinstate_booking', payload: { businessId, bookingId: 'bk_991' } }`, written **before** anything runs. **One** `AiApprovalItem` (tier 3), routed by `approval-routing.service.ts`.\n\n### What the user sees — turn 1\n\n> I found the booking and the payment. Both steps need your approval before I run them.\n>\n> **Cancel booking bk_991** — Sat 10:00, Priya Ramkissoon · Tier 3 · authorised by **Operations** (ceiling 2) · *reversible — I can put the slot back*\n> **Refund TTD 450 on ch_7f2a (Stripe)** — Tier 3 · authorised by **Finance** (ceiling 2) · *not reversible — a refund cannot be un-refunded*\n>\n> I cancel first, on purpose: if the refund fails I can restore the booking. The other order leaves you refunded with the slot still held.\n>\n> `[ Approve both ]` `[ Reject ]` · *Do it yourself: /app/bookings · /app/payments*\n\nNo single hat could have offered this. `operations` alone stops after step 3; `support` alone offers nothing.\n\n### Approval → run\n\nOwner approves. `finalizeResolution` emits `ai.approval.resolved`; the listener enqueues `turn:run` with `jobId = turn:{turnId}`. Two replicas, one job.\n\n- **Step 0** — `bookings_cancel_booking(bk_991)` → `updateBookingStatus` returns `{ ..., previousStatus: 'CONFIRMED' }`. `completeStep(saga, 0, { id: 'bk_991', previousStatus: 'CONFIRMED' })`.\n- **Step 1** — `payments_refund_charge(stripe, ch_7f2a)` → Stripe returns `charge_already_refunded`. `failStep(saga, 1, ...)`.\n\n### Compensation\n\n`turn:compensate` enqueued with `jobId = turn-comp:{turnId}`. `TURN_SAGA.compensate(sagaId)` walks `SagaStep` in **descending** `stepIndex` (`key-cortex-saga.service.ts:105–108`):\n\n- step 1 — no `compensationAction`, skipped, counted as non-compensable\n- step 0 — `bookings.reinstate_booking` reads `output.previousStatus = 'CONFIRMED'` → booking restored to `CONFIRMED` → `SagaStep[0].status = 'compensated'`\n\n`compensable = 1`, `steps = 2`, no compensation failed → saga status `compensated`. `FlowTurn` → `COMPENSATED`, `failedAtIndex = 1`.\n\n### What the user sees — turn 2\n\n> The refund did not go through — Stripe says charge `ch_7f2a` was already refunded, on 8 Aug.\n>\n> I put the booking back. **bk_991 is CONFIRMED again**, Sat 10:00, exactly as it was.\n>\n> Nothing moved. If the customer was refunded a week ago you may just want to cancel the slot on its own — say so and I will, or do it at `/app/bookings`.\n\n**What did not happen, and why that is the deliverable:** no money moved on a stale charge; no slot was released against a refund that failed; nothing logged `outcome: 'success'`; and the saga did not record `compensated` for a step it could not undo — it counted one of two and said so, which is the distinction `key-cortex-saga.service.ts:165–186` was written to preserve and which nothing on the chat path had ever exercised.\n\n---\n\n## 11. Effort\n\n| Item | Days |\n|---|---:|\n| §2.1 `resolveCrew` / `crewAllows` / `grantorsOf` / crew prompt | 3 |\n| §2.2 `applyCrewCeiling`, `GovernanceDecision` fields, `evaluate` | 2 |\n| §2.3 revive `selectToolsForRequest`, 2 menus + 3 gates, `resolveTurnAuthority` | 3 |\n| §4 `FlowTurn` + migration + `BUSINESS_ID_MODELS` + isolation test | 2 |\n| §2.4 + §5 turn plan, `TURN_SAGA` port, ordering, reinstate compensator, `previousStatus` | 4 |\n| §5.3 BullMQ `key-turn` queue: run / expire / compensate | 3 |\n| §6.1 `/app/approvals/turns` + nav + per-step \"do it yourself\" | 4 |\n| §6.3 two KEY tools, handlers, registry, route + honesty checks | 1 |\n| §7 five spec files + integration + **all nine negative controls run** | 4 |\n| §9 flag, telemetry, staged rollout | 1 |\n| **Total** | **27** |\n\n≈ **5.5 weeks for one engineer.** Gates 0–2 are ~7 days and narrow the product on their own; they are worth shipping even if 3–4 slip.\n\n### ADOPT rather than build\n\n| Instead of | Adopt | Licence | Note |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| a distributed lock for the resume/expire jobs | **BullMQ** `jobId` dedup + delayed jobs | MIT | **already a dependency**, already used with idempotent `jobId` at `queue.service.ts:97–112`. Redis is in both compose files. Zero new containers. |\n| a hand-rolled JSON shape check on `FlowTurn.plan` and on `pageContext` | **zod** | MIT | installed; used in 1 file against 2,169 `useState` calls (`CAPABILITY_MAP` #29) |\n| a cycle-count assertion of my own | **madge** `--circular` | MIT | dev-dependency only, run in CI next to the boot gate |\n\n### Deliberately NOT adopted\n\n**No external policy engine — not Casbin, OPA or Cerbos.** The policy here is eight static `RoleDefinition` objects in one file that a spec enumerates *exhaustively* (`role-tool-reachability.spec.ts` iterates all 245 × 8). Moving the decision into a policy DSL moves it out of the file the reachability spec reads, and re-opens exactly the list-vs-reality drift class this repo has spent two audits closing — `BUSINESS_ID_MODELS`, the compensation keys, the nav allowlist, the automation trigger vocabulary. The right tool for eight roles is a frozen literal and a `toEqual`.\n\n---\n\n## 12. Deliberately not doing\n\n- **Not unifying the three role systems.** `JobRoleEnvelope` stays the outright override it is at `ai-oversight.service.ts:136–143`. That is capability-map item #21, three weeks, and its own spec. Making a `JobRole` contribute *to* a crew rather than replace it is the right shape and it is not this change.\n- **Not letting any crew send a customer a message inside tier.** Every outbound tool stays T3/T4. That is the plan's item #20 and a separate per-role send policy. A crew that can suddenly send would be exactly the accidental widening this spec is built to prevent.\n- **Not raising any role's `maxRiskTier`.** Not `general`, not `executive`, not one. The eight numbers in §8(1) are the numbers.\n- **Not letting the model choose its own crew.** No `set_role` tool, no crew field in the tool-call schema. Authority is resolved from context by deterministic code before the model is handed a menu. A model that can widen its own permissions has no permissions.\n- **Not persisting crew as a user preference or a DB-backed role table.** A role editor in the UI is precisely how a ceiling gets raised without a code review — the failure mode §8 exists to prevent, re-introduced as a feature.\n- **Not resuming turns across sessions or across days.** A `FlowTurn` expires at `approvalTimeoutHours` and is dead. Re-asking is cheap; a week-old plan executing against changed state is not.\n- **Not retrying a failed turn automatically.** `attempts: 1`. Retrying a side-effect sequence is how you refund twice.\n- **Not touching the 52 `setInterval` schedulers** (`OPEN_GAPS` #3). This spec adds a BullMQ queue and removes zero timers. It is a worked precedent for capability-map #27, not the fix.\n- **Not adding `SagaExecution` to `BUSINESS_ID_MODELS`.** It belongs there and it is a separate, auditable change — the `client.ts:120–137` note is explicit that entries are added after per-call-site verification, not in batches.\n\n---\n\n### Files an engineer opens, in order\n\n```\napps/server/src/modules/ai/role-engine.service.ts               438-514, 567-608, 315-323\napps/server/src/modules/ai/ai-oversight.service.ts              69-93,
