    "plan": "# BUILD PLAN — ten specs, one programme\n\n**Basis:** `C:/kfm` @ `71a6fb0b`, read 2026-08-09. Every number below was re-derived this session against the working tree, not copied from the specs. Where a spec asserted something I could not confirm, I say so.\n\n**Re-derived:** 439 models · 294 `forwardRef` occurrences across 93 files · 26 `@Cron` · 4 `@Interval` · 52 `setInterval` · 48 `setTimeout` · 3 files import `bullmq` · 4 `@Global` modules (`core/prisma:4`, `core/redis:53`, `core/event-bus:4`, `business-events:8`).\n\n---\n\n## 0. The finding that reorders everything\n\nSpec 01 proposes a new `Obligation` table. Spec 10 says the obligation ledger already exists. **Spec 10 is right, and it is stronger than it claimed.**\n\n`CommandItem` (`schema.prisma:7931`) has `dueAt`, `snoozedUntil`, `riskTier`, `requiresApproval`, `executableByKey`, `executionTool`, `executionPayload`, `entityType/entityId`, `contactId`, `expectedValue`, and a unique key at `:7971` — `@@unique([businessId, sourceModule, sourceType, sourceId, actionType])` — that is already an idempotency key. It is **already in `BUSINESS_ID_MODELS`** (`packages/db/src/client.ts:88`). It already has a manual surface: `apps/server/src/modules/command/command.controller.ts` (complete / dismiss / snooze / approve / execute / assign / reopen) and `apps/web/src/app/app/command-center/`.\n\nAnd it has no owner. I grepped every reader and writer:\n\n```\napps/server/src/modules/command/{command.service, command-generator, command-scheduler}.ts\napps/server/src/modules/finance/finance-intelligence.service.ts:782      create\napps/server/src/modules/intelligence/command-bridge.service.ts           findFirst-then-create\napps/server/src/modules/intelligence/entity-resolver.service.ts\napps/server/src/modules/intelligence/health-score.service.ts\napps/server/src/modules/ai/{ai.controller, key-command.service, key-tool-registry.service}.ts\napps/server/src/modules/flow/flow-action.registry.ts\napps/server/src/modules/key-cortex/{key-cortex-compensation, key-cortex-context-v2}.service.ts\napps/server/src/modules/livekit/livekit.service.ts\napps/server/src/modules/site/storefront-conversion.service.ts\napps/server/src/modules/admin-analytics/admin-analytics.service.ts\n```\n\n**Sixteen files across twelve modules write or read one module's table directly.** That is the seven-task-models failure happening again, live, on the table this programme is about to build on. Adding an eleventh writer — or a rival `Obligation` table — is the single worst thing this programme could do.\n\nSo the plan is not \"build the obligation clock.\" It is: **put an owner and a door on the table that already is the obligation clock, then build the recurrence spine and the work shapes behind that door.**\n\n---\n\n## 1. Dependency order — what genuinely blocks what\n\nThere are exactly **three hard blocking edges** in ten specs, plus one reverse gate. Everything else is parallel. The instinct to serialise ten specs is wrong and would cost six months.\n\n**Hard edge A — the tenant gate blocks every new model.**\n`apps/server/src/core/prisma/tenant-model-list.spec.ts` checks two directions (listed→exists at `:58`, listed→has-column at `:68`) and not the third. Confirmed by reading it. Ten specs add roughly fourteen models between them. Ship the third direction first or fourteen models land unscoped and every one needs revisiting. Cost: 1 day. Blocks: 01, 04, 05, 06, 07, 08, 09a, 10.\n\n**Hard edge B — the obligation event contract blocks item 10, not item 1.**\nSpec 10's three work shapes need `work.obligation.raised/settled` and the single `upsert` listener in `command`. That contract is two days. It does **not** need `ObligationSeries`, genome rules, or anything else in spec 01. Item 1's recurrence spine and item 10's work shapes are siblings behind one contract, not a chain. Cost: 2–3 days. Blocks: 01, 10; improves 08.\n\n**Hard edge C — the crew vector blocks the directive spine and blocks MCP.**\n09b changes `role-engine.service.ts` to return the score vector it already computes and discards at `:504-513`, and wires `selectToolsForRequest` (`flow-orchestrator.service.ts:1033`, **zero callers — confirmed by grep across `apps/server/src` and `apps/web/src`**). Without it, 09a's directive has no way to advertise a cross-department tool to the model, and 06's MCP tools remain invisible to all eight roles. Cost: 5–7 days. Blocks: 09a, 06.\n\n**Reverse gate D — the parity gate blocks new tools.**\nSpec 03 rewrites `apps/web/src/lib/tool-route-audit.ts` so `canWrite` matches *the* mutation, not *a* mutation. That immediately fails the existing 142. Ship it in split mode — hard-fail for tools added after a recorded baseline, warn for the existing set — and it goes first instead of last. Cost: 2 days.\n\n**What does NOT block:**\n- Item 2 (ledger completeness) does not block item 8. The opposite: item 8's `ledger.coverage` slice is what makes item 2's absence visible to the owner. Ship 08 first.\n- Item 4 (send) does not need items 1 or 9. It is a leaf. But it **does** block item 7's `send_email`/`send_whatsapp` becoming real, and item 9a's `send_message_with_approval` step.\n- Item 5 (ingestion) blocks nothing at all. It is schedule-critical (no business can start without it), not dependency-critical.\n- Item 3 blocks nothing except through gate D.\n\n---\n\n## 2. Shared contracts — sixteen collisions, resolved\n\n### C1 · Two obligation tables → one\n**Cut spec 01's `Obligation` model.** `CommandItem` is the obligation row. Migration adds: `kind ('SUGGESTION' | 'OBLIGATION')`, `owedToType/owedToId/owedToLabel`, `seriesId String?`, `dischargedAt`, `dischargeRef`, and `@@index([businessId, status, dueAt])`.\n\nSpec 01's `occurrenceKey` is redundant. Series materialisation writes `sourceModule='obligations'`, `sourceType='series'`, `sourceId=<seriesId>`, `actionType=<ISO occurrence date>` — the existing five-tuple unique key gives exactly-once per occurrence with no new index.\n\nSpec 01's status vocabulary is not stored. `PENDING`/`DUE`/`OVERDUE` are a comparison of `dueAt` to `now()`, not states; storing them requires a sweep that flips rows, which is a timer we do not need. `DISCHARGED` = `COMPLETED`; `WAIVED` = `DISMISSED` with the reason the dismiss endpoint already captures.\n\n### C2 · `CommandItem` has twelve writers and no owner → `CommandModule` owns it\nNew spec `apps/server/src/modules/command/command-write-boundary.spec.ts`: no `commandItem.create|update|upsert|delete` outside `apps/server/src/modules/command/`, with a **shrinking** allowlist of the sixteen files above. It fails on a seventeenth. Existing writers migrate to `work.obligation.raised` opportunistically; the allowlist is the ratchet.\n\nDelete `intelligence/command-bridge.service.ts:31-46`'s `findFirst`-then-`create` outright — it is a check-then-act race against a unique index one table away.\n\n### C3 · Two genome tables → one `BusinessGenome`\nSpec 01 proposes `ObligationRule` + static genome packs. Spec 10 proposes promoting `BusinessBlueprint.workflowModel` (`schema.prisma:9025`, inferred at `blueprint.service.ts:1179-1192`, read only at `:649-654` where it becomes prose). One new `genome` module owns `BusinessGenome` (enabled work shapes, vocabulary map, default clocks), seeded from `workflowModel` on first read, plus `ObligationRule` as a child table because rules are queried per event type. Not two profiles.\n\n### C4 · Four recurrence engines → one, or none\n`RecurringInvoice` (`schema.prisma:5032`), `RecurringExpense` (`:4848`), `RecurringJournalEntry` (`:9965`) each have their own `nextRunDate` bookkeeping and their own `setInterval` (`commerce/recurring-invoice.service.ts:20`, `expenses/recurring-expense.service.ts:27`, `finance/recurring-journal-entry.service.ts:42`). `ObligationSeries` would be the fourth table.\n\n**Condition: build `ObligationSeries` only with a committed migration of those three onto it in phase 4.** They keep their tables and lose their timers and their `nextRunDate` columns. A fourth recurrence engine with no consolidation path is precisely how this codebase got here — if the migration is not funded, do not build the spine and let obligations be raised by listeners only.\n\n### C5 · A second outbound stack was about to be built\nSpec 04 did not mention that `OutboundContent` / `OutboundVariant` / `OutboundDelivery` (`schema.prisma:3975`) / `ChannelDestination` / `DeliveryEvent` already exist, that `OutboundDelivery` is already in `BUSINESS_ID_MODELS`, or that `communications/delivery-queue.service.ts` already polls it (`POLL_INTERVAL_MS = 30_000`) through an adapter registry, consumed by `crm-sequence-scheduler.service.ts` and `flow/automation-executor.service.ts`.\n\n**Rename the new module `send-policy`.** It owns the verdict and the attempt record only. Delivery stays in the two places that already do it — `communications/delivery-queue` for campaign/broadcast, `key-inbox/key-inbox-reply-sender.service.ts` for thread replies — both registered *into* `send-policy` as transports. The 30-second poller becomes a BullMQ consumer of the same rows in the same phase.\n\n### C6 · `helpdesk_reply_to_ticket` cannot succeed, found twice\nSpecs 03 and 04 independently found it. `helpdesk.service.ts:123` reads `supportTicket`; the row it then inserts has an FK to `helpdesk_tickets` (`schema.prisma:12685-12689`, migration `20260721044726:22`). Plus `'resolved'|'closed'` vs `RESOLVED|CLOSED`. One fix, owner = 03, week one. Remove from 04's scope.\n\n### C7 · `books-journal-tab.tsx` claimed twice → owner is 03\nKeep it, mount it, delete the other three orphaned tabs (`books-snapshot`, `books-accounts`, `books-cashflow`) — all three duplicate live screens.\n\n### C8 · Two tool→authority taxonomies → keep the eight roles\nCut 09a's `tool-department.map.ts` (245 tools → 13 departments). `role-engine.service.ts:181-371` already carries eight roles with prefix patterns. `DirectiveStep.department` becomes `DirectiveStep.grantorRole`, populated by 09b's `grantorsOf()`. A hand-maintained 245-row map drifts the day someone adds tool 246.\n\n### C9 · 09a and 09b are one item in two designs → sequence them\n09b ships first, alone: crew vector, wired `selectToolsForRequest`, 128-tool clamp. It makes the sentence *plannable*. 09a's durable spine (`Directive`/`DirectiveStep`, preview, saga via the existing `KeyCortexSagaService`) ships second. It makes the sentence *safe*. **Cut 09a's `directive-planner.service.ts`** — the model plans once it can see the tools — **and `directive-recipes.ts`** until three real recipes are asked for.\n\n### C10 · Tool risk tier has three sources and a permissive default\n`FLOW_TOOLS.riskTier`; `ai-oversight.getToolTier` returning **2 on a miss** (`:95-108`); `mcp.types.ts:43`'s `riskTier`, written at `:150` and read by nothing. One `getToolTier`, and **a miss is tier 4, not tier 2**. Tier 2 auto-approves at autonomy ≥ 4 (`ai-oversight.service.ts:179-181`); a permissive default on an unknown name is a hole. Ships with 09b behind the same test, because that is where the blast radius is measurable.\n\n### C11 · Four run ledgers → three, deliberately\n`AutomationRun`/`AutomationRunStep` (07), `DirectiveStep` (09a), `SendAttempt` (04). Different lifecycles, keep them separate. All three also write a `BusinessEvent` through the `@Global` `BusinessEventService.emit()` so there is one place to answer \"what happened at 14:32\". Do not build a fourth generic ledger.\n\n### C12 · Event names → `@keyflow/shared/events`\n07's `TRIGGERS` array becomes a *view* over the shared registry (`playbookTrigger: true`), not a rival list. `work.obligation.raised/settled` is declared there. `apps/server/scripts/event-wiring.ts` and `event-wiring.spec.ts:119-133` point at the shared registry. Also: **delete `apps/web/src/app/app/projects/components/playbook-panel.tsx`'s private 20/14 menus** — two builders writing one `Automation` table is the collision, not the divergence.\n\n### C13 · Three PDF paths → one leaf module\ndocling (`ingestion/document-parsing.service.ts`, reached twice via `moduleRef.get(..., {strict:false})`), `pdf-parse` (`crm-import.service.ts:5`), and the `xlsx` stub (`apps/server/src/types/xlsx.d.ts`, package absent from the lockfile). One `DocumentExtractionModule`, `imports: []`. Because it is a leaf, the two `moduleRef` escape hatches become **real imports** — a net reduction in the deferral graph, and the only spec that shrinks it.\n\n### C14 · Approval has two records and two screens → one record with a re-armable continuation\n`AiApprovalItem` is created at `flow-orchestrator.service.ts:1522-1532` with no `planStepId`, so `ai-oversight.service.ts:612` never re-arms; `/app/approvals` migrates it to a `KeyActionProposal` and calls `approve({autoExecute:false})`, and nothing sends. Spec 04 says the approval must attach to the send. Spec 09a says it must attach to the step. **They are the same answer:** `AiApprovalItem` gains a polymorphic `stepType` + `stepId` that references a durable row (`DirectiveStep`, `SendAttempt`, or `CommandItem`) which the resolve path re-arms. `/app/key-autonomy` becomes a view over the same record. Neither spec would have found this alone.\n\n### C15 · `escalateStaleApprovals` (`ai-oversight.service.ts:652-697`) has zero callers\nIt is the first job on the new queue module in week one. Job key `approval:escalate`. Cheap, visible, proves the scheduler works.\n\n### C16 · `selectToolsForRequest` claimed by 09a and 09b → owner is 09b\n\n---\n\n## 3. The module graph\n\n```mermaid\ngraph TD\n  subgraph GLOBAL[\"@Global — reachable with imports: []\"]\n    P[core/prisma]\n    R[core/redis]\n    EB[core/event-bus]\n    BE[business-events]\n    Q[\"core/queue<br/><b>NEW</b>\"]\n  end\n\n  subgraph L0[\"Layer 0 — new leaves, imports: []\"]\n    GEN[\"genome<br/><b>NEW</b>\"]\n    DX[\"document-extraction<br/><b>NEW</b>\"]\n    SP[\"send-policy<br/><b>NEW</b>\"]\n    DIR[\"directive<br/><b>NEW</b>\"]\n  end\n\n  subgraph L1[\"Layer 1 — new, imports Layer 0 only\"]\n    OBL[\"obligations<br/><b>NEW</b>\"]\n    IMP[\"import-runs<br/><b>NEW</b>\"]\n  end\n\n  subgraph EX[\"Existing modules (~110)\"]\n    CMD[command]\n    AI[ai]\n    KI[key-inbox]\n    ING[ingestion]\n    CON[contracts]\n    COMM[communications]\n    DSP[\"dispatch / casework<br/><b>NEW shapes</b>\"]\n  end\n\n  OBL --> GEN\n  IMP --> DX\n  AI --> DIR\n  ING --> DX\n  CON --> DX\n  KI --> SP\n  COMM --> SP\n\n  OBL -. \"work.obligation.raised\" .-> EB\n  DSP -. \"work.obligation.raised\" .-> EB\n  EB -. \"@OnEvent\" .-> CMD\n  SP -. \"outbound.send.authorized\" .-> EB\n  DIR -. \"directive.step.ready\" .-> EB\n\n  classDef new fill:#1f6f43,stroke:#0d3d24,color:#fff\n  classDef glob fill:#2b4c7e,stroke:#16304f,color:#fff\n  class GEN,DX,SP,DIR,OBL,IMP,Q,DSP new\n  class P,R,EB,BE,Q glob\n```\n\n**Cycle proof.** A cycle requires an edge from a new module back into the existing graph. There are zero solid edges pointing that way. Every new module's `imports:` array contains only Layer 0 modules or nothing. Every connection *from* a new module to the existing system is either (a) the `@Global` event bus, which is a publish with no import in either direction, or (b) a port token the **consumer** implements — `AiModule` imports `DirectiveModule` and provides the executor adapter; `KeyInboxModule` imports `SendPolicyModule` and registers its transport in `onModuleInit`.\n\nTopological order: `[core/queue, genome, document-extraction, send-policy, directive]` → `[obligations, import-runs]` → `[~110 existing]`. New modules occupy the two lowest layers. Nothing above can be reached from below, so no `forwardRef` is added and none is needed.\n\nThe three edges into `document-extraction` (`ingestion`, `contracts`, and later `crm`) **replace** `moduleRef.get(DocumentParsingService, {strict: false})` calls that exist today precisely because `IngestionModule` imports `AiModule`, `CommerceModule` and `KeyInboxModule` (`ingestion.module.ts:14`) and a real import would close a cycle. Moving the parser to a leaf removes two escape hatches.\n\n---\n\n## 4. Scalability review\n\nRule: every timer on BullMQ with a job key; no state in a module field; every new model in `BUSINESS_ID_MODELS`.\n\n**Violations in the ten specs:**\n\n| Spec | Violation | Fix |\n|---|---|---|\n| **08** | Proposes \"deltas — yesterday's number\" without saying where. `BusinessGraphService`'s existing cache is `private readonly cache = new Map()` at `:117` with a replica-local `invalidateCache` at `:136`. | Deltas live in a `BusinessPulseSnapshot` row (listed in `BUSINESS_ID_MODELS`). Pulse recompute is a BullMQ job keyed `pulse:{businessId}:{hourBucket}`, not a listener-triggered recompute. |\n| **04** | Silent on `communications/delivery-queue.service.ts`'s `POLL_INTERVAL_MS = 30_000` `OnModuleInit` poller, which is the delivery path it must reuse and which double-fires on replica 2. | Converting that poller to a BullMQ consumer is in scope for phase 3, not deferred. |\n| **05** | Names `statement-source.service.ts:389` `@Cron('0 5 * * *')` but does not convert it. | Nightly sweep becomes a fan-out job: one scheduler job enqueues one job per business, key `stmt-sweep:{businessId}:{yyyy-mm-dd}`. |\n| **01** | Text truncated before the data section; `BUSINESS_ID_MODELS` membership unverified for its tables. | Moot for `Obligation` (cut). `ObligationSeries` and `ObligationRule` must be listed. |\n| **02** | Correctly reports that `StockMovement`, `InventoryStock`, `GoodsReceipt`, `StockCount`, `PayrollRun`, `PayrollItem`, `RetainerAgreement`, `RetainerPeriod`, `ChangeOrder`, `Warehouse` are absent from the list. **I confirmed all ten are absent.** Spec 02 is about to start writing to every one of them. | Ten additions in week one, each audited at its call sites the way the 2026-08-07 finance cluster was. |\n\n**Clean:** 03 (web + a gate), 07 (static data + one listed model), 09b (pure functions), 10 (the most scalability-literate of the ten — it explicitly refuses `ResilientEmitterService`'s module-field circuit breaker at `business-events/resilient-emitter.service.ts:16-20` and the `command-bridge` race).\n\n**The pre-existing violation the whole programme sits on.** `command-scheduler.service.ts:17`:\n\n```ts\n@Interval(60 * 60 * 1000)\nasync scheduledScan() {\n  const businesses = await (this.prisma.client as any).business.findMany({\n    select: { id: true }, take: 1000,\n  });\n```\n\nOne process, hourly, sequential over every business, **capped at 1000 businesses with no ordering and no cursor**. Spec 10 hangs three new work shapes on the table this drives. Convert it to a BullMQ fan-out before phase 5 or the shapes double-fire on replica 2 and stop existing past business 1000. The `take: 1000` is a silent ceiling; nothing logs when it is hit.\n\n**Cross-spec interaction neither spec saw.** 06 §1.1(7) reports that a dead MCP server adds 10 s to every chat turn, because `flow-orchestrator.service.ts:1056` awaits `listBridgedTools` inside `selectToolsForRequest` with `CONNECT_TIMEOUT_MS = 10_000`. That is dormant today only because `selectToolsForRequest` has **zero callers**. 09b's whole point is to wire it. **09b must not wire the MCP branch** until 06 moves tool listing to a Redis-cached table refreshed by a worker. This is a one-line guard and a landmine if missed.\n\n---\n\n## 5. The sequence\n\nEngineer-days, one engineer, ranges are honest.\n\n**Phase 0 — the gates. 6–11 d.**\nTenant-list third direction with a `DELIBERATELY_UNSCOPED` allowlist carrying a reason per entry (1 d). Ten money models added and audited (2 d). Parity gate v2 in split mode (2 d). `core/queue` module + job-key convention + `approval:escalate` as its first job (2.5 d). Helpdesk P2003 (0.5 d).\n*Makes possible:* every subsequent model is scoped by construction and every subsequent timer has somewhere to go.\n\n**Phase 1 — the spine. 14–24 d.**\nObligation event contract + `command/obligation.listener.ts` + write-boundary spec (3 d). `CommandItem` migration (1 d). `GenomeModule` seeded from `BusinessBlueprint.workflowModel` (3 d). `ObligationSeries` + BullMQ scheduler using `upsertJobScheduler` (5 d). \"Due this week\" query, screen, two KEY tools (3 d). Tests (3 d).\n*Makes possible:* item 10 entirely; item 8's obligation slice; the migration path for the three recurrence engines.\n\n**Phase 2 — the turn. 21–35 d.**\n09b crew vector (5 d). `getToolTier` with no permissive default (1 d). `selectToolsForRequest` wired, MCP branch guarded off, 128 clamp (2 d). `DirectiveModule` spine on the existing `KeyCortexSagaService` (8 d). `AiApprovalItem.stepRef` unification (4 d). Plan card (3 d). Tests with negative controls (4 d).\n*Makes possible:* \"refund them and release their booking\" in one turn; every multi-step recipe after it; the approval record that items 4 and 9a both need.\n\n**Phase 3 — send, and automation honesty. 20–34 d.**\n`SendPolicyModule` (8 d). Transport registration + `delivery-queue` poller onto BullMQ (4 d). `@keyflow/shared/automation` registry + prompt renderer + validators (4 d). `AutomationRun` ledger replacing the unconditional `outcome:'success'` at `automation-executor.service.ts:133-145` (3 d). The nine live-but-undispatched triggers, one `@OnEvent` each (2 d). The `send_email` `notificationType` fix (1 d). Tests (4 d).\n*Makes possible:* any role sending inside its ceiling; playbooks that do what they advertise.\n\n**Phase 4 — the money. 36–62 d.**\nItem 08 first (6–12 d) so `ledger.coverage` makes item 02's absence visible before the work starts. Then thirteen posting recipes, unequal: continental-ops 6 d, procurement/AP 4 d, payroll 3 d, the rest 1–2 d each, period-close guard wiring `AccountingPeriodService.checkClosed` (currently called by nothing outside its own controller) 2 d, tests 6 d.\n*Makes possible:* a P&L that is not a lie; item 08's coverage moving toward 1.0.\n\n**Phase 5 — the intake. 24–40 d.**\n`DocumentExtractionModule` (6 d). Statement PDF path — the `file.buffer.toString('utf8')` at `finance.controller.ts:613` and the `STATEMENT_EXT` regex at `statement-source.service.ts:92` (5 d). `import-runs` staging (6 d). Stripe/PayPal/QuickBooks/Xero backfill including `starting_after` pagination Stripe does not have today (8 d). Tests (5 d).\n*Makes possible:* a business can start here.\n\n**Phase 6 — the shapes. 35–55 d.**\nDispatch (12 d), casework (12 d), genome profiles per vertical (5 d), roster/shift last or never (12 d).\n\n**Deferred indefinitely:** MCP (12–18 d).\n\n**Total 156–261 engineer-days.** Two engineers on phases 3+4 in parallel (they share no files) and 5+6 in parallel puts this at roughly five to seven months. One engineer, nine to twelve.\n\n---\n\n## 6. What not to build\n\n**Adopt:**\n- **BullMQ** (MIT) — already at `apps/server/package.json:42`. Use `upsertJobScheduler` / `removeJobScheduler` / `getJobSchedulers`. `getRepeatableJobs`, which `ai/queue.service.ts:116` uses, is deprecated and removed in v6.\n- **rrule** (BSD-3-Clause) for `ObligationSeries` — do not hand-roll RRULE. **Verify it resolves CJS before adding it.** The boot constraint is real: Node 20.18.1 with CommonJS output, one ESM-only dependency kills the server. If it does not resolve CJS cleanly, restrict series to the six cadences `RecurringInvoice.frequency` already uses and skip RRULE entirely.\n- **docling-serve** (MIT) — already running at `docker-compose.production.yml:170-181`. Retire `pdf-parse` and the phantom `xlsx` stub into it. Add `DOCLING_URL` to `.env.example`, which does not have it today.\n- **class-validator** — already there, for the four write paths in 07 that accept any string.\n\n**Do not adopt:**\n- **A workflow engine** (Temporal, Inngest, Restate) for 09a. `KeyCortexSagaService` (`:41-197`) is written, correct, reverse-order, and records `compensation_unavailable` rather than lying. It has never run in production. Run it. Temporal is right at ten times this volume and wrong on a single Hetzner box — it is a second scheduler and a second operational surface.\n- **An ETL framework** (Airbyte, Singer) for 05. Four API clients already exist (`stripe.connector.ts:319`, `paypal.connector.ts:247`, `quickbooks.connector.ts:382`, `xero.connector.ts:259`). A container per connector is not the trade.\n- **A double-entry library.** `finance/posting.service.ts` is 328 lines, validated, idempotent via `businessId_externalRef`, and handles the P2002 race. Leave it alone.\n- **`@nestjs/schedule`.** Retire it, do not extend it. It is the source of 26 `@Cron` and 4 `@Interval`.\n\n**Cut outright:**\n- Spec 01's `Obligation` model (C1).\n- Spec 09a's `directive-planner.service.ts`, `directive-recipes.ts`, `tool-department.map.ts` (C8, C9).\n- The second playbook builder, `apps/web/src/app/app/projects/components/playbook-panel.tsx` (C12).\n- Three of the four orphaned finance tabs (C7).\n- Spec 05's WiPay backfill — `wipay.connector.ts:112` posts to a payment-*request* plugin. There is no transaction-list API. Say so in the UI.\n\n**Defer:**\n- **Item 06 (MCP) entirely.** Zero SSRF defence exists anywhere in `apps/server/src` (confirmed by negative search: no `169.254`, no `isPrivateIp`, no `ssrf`), zero tests, a 10 s hot-path stall that phase 2 would activate, and no capability the thesis needs. If it ever ships: operator-set env var only, never a tenant-supplied URL.\n- **Roster/shift** (item 10's third shape). Dispatch and casework serve the AC company and the insurance broker the thesis names. Shift serves security-guard businesses that are not in the named set.\n\n---\n\n## 7. The first week\n\nFive days. It ships, it is testable, and by Thursday the owner can open one screen and see a salon's rebook and an accountant's VAT return as the same row with the same clock — which is the thesis, made visible rather than prepared for.\n\n**Day 1 — the tenant gate, and ten money models.**\nThird direction in `tenant-model-list.spec.ts`: every model with a `businessId String` column is either in `BUSINESS_ID_MODELS` or in a new `DELIBERATELY_UNSCOPED` map with a reason string. It starts red against ~270 models, so the map starts full; its value is that **it can only shrink**, and a new model lands in neither and fails. Then add the ten spec 02 named — `StockMovement`, `InventoryStock`, `GoodsReceipt`, `StockCount`, `PayrollRun`, `PayrollItem`, `RetainerAgreement`, `RetainerPeriod`, `ChangeOrder`, `Warehouse` — each audited at its call sites.\n*Negative control:* add a throwaway model with a `businessId` to a schema fixture, prove the test goes red, remove it.\n\n**Day 1–2 — `core/queue`.**\n`@Global` `QueueModule` over `REDIS_CLIENT`. One factory, one convention: job key is `${kind}:${businessId}:${occurrence}`. First consumer is `escalateStaleApprovals` (`ai-oversight.service.ts:652-697`, zero callers today) on `upsertJobScheduler`. Nothing times out an approval in this product right now; by Tuesday something does.\n\n**Day 2–3 — the obligation contract.**\n`work.obligation.raised` / `work.obligation.settled` declared in `@keyflow/shared/events`. `apps/server/src/modules/command/obligation.listener.ts` with the `upsert` on the existing five-tuple. `CommandItem` migration: `kind`, `owedToType/owedToId/owedToLabel`, `seriesId`, `dischargedAt`, `dischargeRef`, `@@index([businessId, status, dueAt])`. `command-write-boundary.spec.ts` with the sixteen-file allowlist.\n\n**Day 3–4 — the query, the screen, the producer.**\n`GET /command/businesses/:businessId/due?window=7d` — obligations ordered by `dueAt`, carrying `owedToLabel`. A \"Due this week\" tab on `/app/command-center`, which already has complete / dismiss / snooze / approve / execute wired. Then **one producer**: `bookings` emits `work.obligation.raised` on `booking.completed` (`bookings.service.ts:214`) with `actionType='REBOOK'` and `dueAt = completedAt + genome.rebookDays` (hardcoded to 42 this week; the genome table is phase 1).\n\nTwo KEY tools, both with a real manual route that is writable today:\n- `obligations_list_due` — family `read`, riskTier 1, `manualEquivalentRoute: '/app/command-center'`\n- `obligations_discharge` — family `organize`, riskTier 2, `manualEquivalentRoute: '/app/command-center'`\n\n**Day 5 — the gate and the broken tool.**\nParity gate v2 in split mode against a recorded baseline of the existing 142. The helpdesk P2003 fix — point `replyToTicket` at `helpdeskTicket` and fix the `'resolved'` case comparison.\n\n**Tests, with the control that proves each can fail.** Run against a real Postgres, not mocks — a silent zero hides in a mock and my own greps have been the unreliable step before.\n\n1. *Idempotency.* Two concurrent `work.obligation.raised` with the same five-tuple produce exactly one row. **Control:** swap `upsert` for `create` and prove P2002.\n2. *Re-raise must not resurrect.* Raise, dismiss, raise again — `status` stays `DISMISSED`. **Control:** add `status: 'OPEN'` to the `update` clause and prove the assertion fires on this test and no other.\n3. *Tenant scope.* Seed two businesses with a due obligation each; the query for A returns A's row and not B's. **Control:** delete the `businessId` from the `where` and prove red — the tenant extension is inert in a worker (`activeBusinessId()` is `undefined` off the HTTP path), so the explicit `where` is the only scope and the test must be the thing that proves it exists.\n4. *The gate fires.* A tool added to the registry with a route that reaches no matching mutation fails CI. **Control:** add one, watch it fail, remove it.\n\n**What week one does not do.** No recurrence spine, no genome table, no crew vector, no send path, no posting recipes. One producer, one consumer, one screen, four gates. Everything in phases 1 through 6 lands behind doors that exist by Friday, and the thing the owner sees on Thursday is a business's obligations in one list for the first time."
  },
